--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 1. Overview

`s3a-kite` is an S3A **add-on pack** that forks Kitesurf's Apache-2.0 Rust core (vendored from [ashleycribb/s3a-Kite](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-Kite) at the pin above) and replaces Kitesurf's Cloudflare TypeScript host with a native host and a UniFFI library inside the S3A mobile app.

s3a-kite **is** the S3A viewer. It reads `.s3a` and Edge files with `s3a-engine` / `s3a-edge` and paints the manifest or header sheet and the warehouse tour with Kitesurf's Rust layout and CPU rasterizer. Wiki Markdown and local HTML are painted by `render_page`. On a hub or PC with a display, an optional **native window** is a Host HTTP API client: it sends the UI token on every request except the tokenless health case (DD-60). The Host HTTP API in §5.2.1 is the only write path and the testable wire contract. It is not a Chrome DevTools server. Headless hosts (Aura Rack, LRS VM, or a lab PC without a display) run with `--headless` and no window. The phone app, in v1, is an S3A / Edge-tile viewer only (DD-45, DD-128). It has no Scholar 3-pass / literature-review flow: no candidate list, select, download, inclusion, snapshots, consent, LRS, or `paper_identity`. There is no phone Scholar Explorer. AI Teacher's Assistant chat and phone paper search are future, unbuilt, and out of scope for this spec (DD-128, Q-15, Q-16); this spec adds no ATs, exports, or interfaces for either.

First product use, on the hub or PC: Scholar's wiki browser for Keshav's 3-pass method. Pass 1 is a human skim of about 15 seconds and is never recorded. Selecting a paper means the researcher wants a closer read; it is not the inclusion decision, and it does not snapshot or fingerprint the paper or enter it in the corpus. Downloading a paper is the inclusion decision and is written to the researcher's working archive even when tracing consent is off. Deselecting a paper is logged as its own event; that paper is not snapshotted or fingerprinted and does not enter the corpus; the researcher is not asked for a reason. Pass 1 skips remain unrecorded. Tracing is consent-only xAPI to `s3a-lrs-server`. AI and the browser only check that things exist and record what the researcher did; humans make the judgments. Custody of the UI token file is the human-control point for every write, including those issued by the native window (DD-32, DD-60).

S3A core stays database-only. No money and no cryptocurrency. Everything lives in the pack.

A v1 **Scholar-mode** host process serves **one researcher**, requires **durable local disk**, and runs on an Aura Rack, the LRS VM, or a lab PC (DD-48, DD-78). Viewer-only mode does not require a data directory. Cloud Run is not a v1 target. The host process itself is specified for Unix (Linux) only in v1 (DD-73).

## 2. Glossary

| Term | Meaning in this spec |
| --- | --- |
| Pack | A domain crate that extends S3A without changing `s3a-core`. See `docs/pack-contract.md`. |
| Kitesurf core | Rust crates `packages/core` (`kitesurf-core`), `packages/renderer-core`, and `packages/kitesurf-cli`, kept and forked. |
| Cloudflare layer | TypeScript `packages/engine` plus `wrangler.jsonc`, the Worker Loader binding `LOADER`, and Dynamic Workers. Replaced, not required at runtime. Upstream CDP also lives in this layer and is **not** reimplemented in v1 (DD-41). |
| Host | The `s3a-kite` process for one researcher on a hub, Aura Rack, LRS VM, or lab PC. |
| Scholar mode | Host started without `--viewer` (§5.2.2). Enables the 3-pass wiki browser and writes. Hub or PC only; not the phone. |
| Viewer-only mode | Host started with `--viewer` and `--view-root`. Opens files under that root. No Scholar writes, no LRS, no `--data-dir`. |
| Mobile library | The same Rust renderer, exposed through UniFFI from crate `s3a-pack-kite`, linked into the S3A Android (and later iOS) app. v1 phone builds are an S3A / Edge-tile viewer only (DD-45, DD-128): no 3-pass / literature-review flow. |
| Hyper-tile archive | A standard S3A file whose `tile_stride` is 128 KiB (the default when the header does not declare an Edge stride). |
| Edge archive | An S3A file detected as Edge: `FLAG_PROFILE_EDGE` **or** a `tile_stride` of 4096 or 8192 (§4.3, §5.4 item 1), with a consistent stride of 4096 or 8192 when the flag is set. Still a valid S3A file (EDGE profile §1.2). A file whose stride is neither 4096, 8192, nor 128 KiB, or whose Edge flags disagree with the stride, is opened as inconsistent / unsupported (AT-66, AT-97), not as a hyper-tile. |
| Micro-tile | One 4,096-byte or 8,192-byte Edge tile (`MICRO_TILE_4KB_SIZE` / `MICRO_TILE_8KB_SIZE`). |
| Lobby | `TourLobby` in `crates/s3a-cli/src/tour.rs`. |
| Floor | One tile in the tour directory (`TourFloorItem`). |
| Warehouse tour | Ashley's name for the animated walk of those floors (DD-04). |
| Stored manifest | The UTF-8 payload of `TileType::MANIFEST` (78). |
| Derived header sheet | For Edge files (no tile 78 in the Edge profile), a labeled plain-text readout of present `FileHeader` and `DeviceIdentityRecord` fields, as listed in §5.4. |
| Pass 1 | The researcher's own skim, about 15 seconds per paper. Never recorded. The 15-second timer is a client-side UI concern; the host has no write-on-timer endpoint. |
| Inclusion | The researcher downloads a paper into the corpus (the inclusion decision). Written to the working archive regardless of tracing consent. The inclusion record is a `HumanLrsRecord` (Scholar tile 10). Select is a separate closer-read event and is not inclusion. |
| Select | The researcher marks a current candidate for a closer read (Pass 2). Logged as its own event with the existing verb IRI `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/opened` (DD-154). It does not write an inclusion record, snapshot, fingerprint, or inclusion-index line, and does not enter the paper in the corpus. |
| Deselect | The researcher drops a selected paper that has not been downloaded. Logged as its own event with the existing verb IRI `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/removed` (DD-154). The paper is not snapshotted or fingerprinted and does not enter the corpus. There is no reason field. A prior Pass 1 skip is a different action and is still not recorded. Deselect does not remove an already-downloaded inclusion from the corpus. |
| Download | The researcher includes a paper in the corpus. Logged with the existing verb IRI `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/saved` (DD-154). This is the write that snapshots, fingerprints, and appends the inclusion record and index line. |
| Paper identity | The stable string used as the idempotency key's paper field: the `SRC-NNNN` stem of a `papers/SRC-*.md` file, or the decimal `paper_id` of a live (non-tombstone) `AcademicPaperRecord` (DD-61). There is no join of an SRC stem to a `paper_id`. A source URL is a property of a candidate, not an identity. |
| Source URL | An `http` or `https` URL taken from the optional dossier YAML key `source_url` (DD-59). `AcademicPaperRecord` stores only hashes and has no URL string. |
| Tracing consent | Permission to emit xAPI to the LRS. Gates only LRS traffic. |
| Working archive | A per-researcher live `.s3a` file that receives inclusion records. Not the corpus. |
| Text layer | Structured `text_runs` and `semantics` returned with every frame, per §5.8. Not OCR. |
| Host HTTP API | The v1 wire contract in §5.2.1. Not CDP. The native window is a client of this API. |
| Inclusion index | The Scholar-mode file `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl`. It is local research data, like the working archive: it stores paper identities, the working-archive `file_uuid`, and a nullable snapshot SHA-256 (DD-61). |
| Tokenless health case | `GET /health` on a loopback or Unix-socket bind when `--behind-proxy` is not set. That is the only unauthenticated success case. |

## 3. Non-goals

### 3.1 This document does not authorize

*   Pushing to [ashleycribb/s3a-Kite](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-Kite), s3a-storage, or s3a-reader. Ashley authorized a local test-first build on 2026-11-16. "Land" in §11 and in `BUILD-PLAN.md` means local branches only.
*   Changing `s3a-engine` on a public branch. The fd-taking snapshot/seal API required by F-13 is a stated local dependency of the write path; any engine work stays on a local branch until Ashley authorizes a push.

### 3.2 v1 product non-goals

*   Changing `s3a-core` record layouts or adding kite tile IDs to core (DD-15).
*   Running s3a-kite, Kitesurf, or a GPU renderer on the microcontroller.
*   BLE / USB / SoftAP transport (Edge Phase 2).
*   Hosting or embedding the existing `s3a-wasm-snapshot` / harness WebAssembly viewer.
*   Rasterizing PDF input (DD-14).
*   Money, cryptocurrency, or writing Bitcoin / OpenTimestamps anchors. Reading an existing on-device SHA-256 seal chain is required for Edge verification.
*   Cloudflare Workers Paid, Wrangler, or Dynamic Workers as a runtime dependency.
*   **A CDP server, agent tokens, or any reimplementation of `packages/engine/src/cdp/` (DD-41).**
*   **Page JavaScript, Boa, Wasmtime, or a wasm32 page-isolate ABI (DD-26 as amended, DD-44).**
*   **Scholar 3-pass / literature-review flow on the phone (DD-39 as amended, DD-45, DD-128):** no candidate list, select, download, inclusion, snapshots, consent, LRS, or `paper_identity` on phone UniFFI. There is no phone Scholar Explorer. AI Teacher's Assistant chat and phone paper search are future, unbuilt, and out of scope for this spec (Q-15, Q-16); this spec adds no ATs, exports, or interfaces for either.
*   **Cloud Run or any other stateless / multi-tenant host as a v1 target (DD-48).**
*   **A general `POST /v1/open` by URL, live remote browsing, or following `http(s)` links from a page view (DD-58, DD-59).** Remote bytes are retrieved only by select/download of a candidate that has a `source_url`.
*   **A Windows host binary (DD-73).** v1 `s3a-kite` is specified for Unix (Linux). Android and later iOS remain the mobile targets.

v1 **does** distribute a Kitesurf derivative, under §6, when Ashley authorizes vendoring the Rust core into s3a-storage and shipping the Android (and later iOS) app.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 4. Grounded facts

Claims about Kitesurf, S3A, Edge, and the phone reader are taken from the trees named above. File paths below are in those trees.

### 4.1 Kitesurf

*   Experimental browser engine that parses HTML, applies CSS, runs page JavaScript, and renders screenshots without Chrome (`README.md`). Licensed Apache-2.0 (`LICENSE`); bundled fonts and crates keep their licenses (`NOTICE`).
*   Cloudflare deployment requires Dynamic Workers (Workers Paid) and `wrangler dev` locally (`README.md` "IMPORTANT" block). `packages/engine/wrangler.jsonc` declares `"worker_loaders": [{ "binding": "LOADER" }]`. Page isolates are started with `env.LOADER.load({...})` in `packages/engine/src/eval/page-script.ts` at line 368 in `235111c` (line 376 in `4a29038`).
*   Commit `235111c` (`engine: slim what each page isolate receives at startup`) changes only TypeScript. Each PageScript and renderer isolate previously received the 27 MB core Wasm as bytes and recompiled it on every navigation. The parent now passes its already compiled `WebAssembly.Module`, and the generated startup-input module embeds `hasHtml` and `htmlLength` instead of the navigated HTML string. Files: `packages/engine/src/eval/page-renderer.ts`, `page-script.ts`, two engine tests, `packages/page-script/src/__ks_startup_probe_input.ts`, and `startup-probe.ts`. This does not change the Rust core and does not provide a native-host page-JS runtime.
*   The engine has no authentication of its own. A person who has the URL can open DevTools, connect Puppeteer, and send outbound requests (`README.md`, Cloudflare deployment warning).
*   Stack, from `README.md` "Technology stack": Blitz (HTML/DOM/layout/paint, MIT OR Apache-2.0); Stylo (CSS, **MPL-2.0**); Boa (page JavaScript inside WebAssembly, Unlicense OR MIT); Linebender libraries (Parley, Vello, Kurbo, Peniko, Apache-2.0 OR MIT); Krilla (PDF output, MIT OR Apache-2.0).
*   `packages/core/Cargo.toml` describes `kitesurf-core` as "Wasm core for kitesurf: DOM data plane + Stylo + Blitz render." `crate-type` is `["cdylib", "rlib"]`. Feature `native-host` is declared and is used to drop `wasm_bindgen` exports (for example `packages/core/src/paint/scene_replay.rs`, `tty/grid.rs`).
*   Page JavaScript evaluation lives in `packages/core/src/js.rs` and `packages/core/src/js/boa_eval.rs`, both gated `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`. There is no native-host Boa entry point in the tree today. `packages/core/Cargo.toml` notes that Boa pulls `getrandom` 0.2, which on `wasm32-unknown-unknown` needs the `js` feature.
*   **Paint backend is CPU, not GPU.** Workspace pins `vello_cpu` 0.3 with features `std`, `text`, `u8_pipeline`, `poly-pnorm`, and `anyrender_vello_cpu` 0.18, both `default-features = false` (`Cargo.toml`). Screenshots use `anyrender_vello_cpu::VelloCpuImageRenderer` (`packages/core/src/paint/screenshot.rs`, `scene_replay.rs`). There is no `wgpu`, Vulkan, Metal, or GLES dependency in those manifests. Raster output is a CPU `vello_cpu::Pixmap` / RGBA buffer, then PNG or JPEG.
*   **Fonts are bundled, not system fonts.** `make_empty_font_context` sets `CollectionOptions { shared: true, system_fonts: false }` (`packages/core/src/resources/fonts.rs`). UA fonts live under `packages/engine/public/fonts/` (the directory is about 19 MB, 55 files, SIL OFL 1.1 per `NOTICE`). Production registration is `register_ua_fonts` from host-supplied bytes; tests `include_bytes!` Noto Sans, Italic, Serif, Mono, and Symbols. Noto Color Emoji (about 1.9 MB) and Noto Sans SC (about 5.9 MB) exist as separate assets. Blitz's `system_fonts` feature is deliberately off in `packages/core/Cargo.toml`.
*   `kitesurf-cli` is a terminal client. It downloads a Wasm scene rasterizer and runs it in **Wasmtime** 36 (`packages/kitesurf-cli/Cargo.toml`, `packages/kitesurf-cli/src/wasm.rs` "Calls the scene-host Wasm ABI through Wasmtime"). That is a client playback path, not the Cloudflare host, and it is not a page-JS runtime.
*   Boa is used to run page `Runtime.evaluate` expressions in the core Wasm (`packages/core/Cargo.toml`). Page `WebAssembly.*` is shimmed in `packages/page-script/src/globals.ts`. **s3a-kite does not use Boa or that shim to run an old S3A viewer.** Opening a `.s3a` file does not instantiate the page JavaScript runtime. **Boa is not used in v1** (DD-26 as amended, DD-44): there is no page JS, no Wasmtime, and no Boa isolate on the host or phone.
*   Upstream CDP lives in TypeScript under `packages/engine/src/cdp/` (modules include `page.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `dom.ts`, `network.ts`, `input.ts`, `target.ts`, `debugger.ts`, `accessibility.ts`, `fetch.ts`, and others).

### 4.2 S3A tour and manifest

*   `s3a-cli tour [--json] [--floor N] [--slice] [--cross] [--history] <archive>` and `s3a-cli tour --html <archive> <out.html>` are implemented in `crates/s3a-cli/src/tour.rs`, `tour_html.rs`, and `tour_html_template.html`.
*   `tour_file` builds a `TourReport` with a `TourLobby`. If a floor has `type_id == TileType::MANIFEST` (`s3a-core`: `MANIFEST = 78`), the UTF-8 payload is `lobby.manifest`. Plain-text output prints `--- PLAIN-TEXT MANIFEST ---` then that payload (`print_tour_report`). The test `test_tour_displays_manifest_in_lobby_and_floor` expects `THIS IS AN S3A FILE` and the seal title/creator.
*   Default mode lists the floor directory. `--floor N` is floor detail (schema fields, records, `withheld_count`). Consent, as described in the `tour.rs` header comment: when consent records exist, records without active `purpose=research` are withheld. Separately, `evaluate_consent_summary` returns `"open / unrestricted (no consent records)"` when there are no consent records, no withdrawal marks, and no Edge consent value. Kite follows that no-records-unrestricted rule for both Edge files (no type 71) and hyper-tile files (no core `ConsentRecord`s) **only subject to the trusted-authentication condition in §5.3 item 4**: an unauthenticated-as-a-whole file with zero consent records is not unrestricted and default-withholds. The **only normative withholding rule** for kite is §5.3 item 4 (hyper-tile) and §5.4 item 6 (Edge, which references that table). This paragraph is a source fact about `tour.rs`, not a second algorithm. v1 has no unfiltered or staff view mode.
*   `tour --html` embeds the same JSON, escaped so archive text cannot break out of a `<script type="application/json">` block, and renders with `textContent`, never `innerHTML` (`tour_html.rs`).
*   The HTML animation: clicking a tile opens `#card`. The card is first placed on the tile's bounding rect at opacity 0.2 with `transition: none`, then on a double `requestAnimationFrame` the transition is restored (unless the hash has `instant`) and the card moves to `left: 6vw; top: 8vh; width: 88vw; height: 84vh; opacity: 1`. CSS: `#card { transition: all .45s cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1); }` (`tour_html_template.html`).
*   The unsealed fallback in `tour.rs` walks tiles at `FILE_HEADER_SIZE + i * HYPER_TILE_SIZE` (512 + *i* × 128 KiB). It does **not** consult `tile_stride`. Edge 4 KiB / 8 KiB archives are therefore not toured correctly by today's CLI scanner. s3a-kite must use `s3a_engine::MmapReader` (`is_edge_archive()`, `tile_stride()`, `header_extent()`, edge tile getters) instead of copying that loop.
*   `tour.rs` function `build_consent_state` contains a real branch `else if f.type_id == 82` that parses the payload as `EdgeConsentRecord` and may set `edge_research_allowed`. That is not only a comment. In `s3a-core`, 82 is `VCS_RESOLUTION`. Edge consent is `TILE_TYPE_CONSENT = 71` (`s3a-edge/src/header.rs`), with a different record layout than core consent. `s3a-engine/src/display_layouts.rs` warns that hyper-tile and edge archives reuse type numbers (64, 71, 75) and must be selected with `MmapReader::is_edge_archive`. s3a-kite must not copy the type-82 mistake. On a hyper-tile file that contains a type-82 tile, CLI `withheld_count` can be wrong; it is not a safe oracle for AT-11. Floor-list fields used by AT-10 (`index`, `type_id`, `record_count`, `size_bytes`, `payload_hash_short`) are not produced by that branch.
*   Timestamps in the tour and in sealed manifests are formatted with `humantime::format_rfc3339_seconds` from Unix epoch seconds (`tour.rs` `format_unix_timestamp`; a zero value prints `genesis`). That is UTC (`…Z`), not America/New_York. Kite uses the same function and timezone for header sheets, events, and snapshot capture time.
*   `FileHeader.version` is a `u16`. The writer sets it to 1. `FileHeader::verify` returns `S3AError::UnsupportedVersion` when it is not 1 (`s3a-core`: display text `Unsupported file version: {n}`). Magic that is not `S3A1` is `InvalidMagic` (`Invalid S3A file magic`). On mobile, `s3a-reader-core` surfaces a non-S3A file as `This is not an S3A file`.

### 4.3 Edge profile

From `docs/spec/S3A-EDGE-PROFILE.md` (cited as **EDGE §…** in this document) and `crates/s3a-edge`:

*   Phase 1 writer is `#![no_std]`, heapless, for MCUs with ≤ 32 KiB SRAM. Micro-tiles are exactly 4,096 or 8,192 bytes. Dual-slot header Variant A is 512 bytes; tile *i* is at `512 + i * tile_stride`. Variant B (`FLAG_COMPACT_HEADER`) uses extent `P = max(256, tile_stride)`. Because Edge strides are only 4096 or 8192, *P* is 4096 or 8192, never 256 or 512.
*   Flags: `FLAG_PROFILE_EDGE`, `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_4KB`, `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_8KB`, `FLAG_COMPACT_HEADER`, `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE`, `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED`. Detection: `FLAG_PROFILE_EDGE` **or** `tile_stride` 4096 or 8192. Torn or unsealed micro-tiles are ignored, never treated as committed history.
*   Equipment event log is type 64, `EquipmentEventRecord` (32 bytes) with `event_type` 0x0001–0x0007 and `severity` 0=Info, 1=Warn, 2=Alarm, 3=Emergency (EDGE §5.2). Type 64 may also hold a 96-byte `DeviceIdentityRecord` (distinguish by `payload_len / record_count`). Every Edge archive MUST contain at least one `DeviceIdentityRecord` (EDGE §6.3) — that is a writer requirement.
*   Canonical `event_type` names (EDGE §5.2.2): `0x0001` `EVENT_MACHINE_START`, `0x0002` `EVENT_MACHINE_STOP`, `0x0003` `EVENT_EMERGENCY_STOP`, `0x0004` `EVENT_FAULT_CODE`, `0x0005` `EVENT_MAINTENANCE`, `0x0006` `EVENT_CALIBRATION`, `0x0007` `EVENT_OPERATOR_CHANGE`.
*   On-device trust is a SHA-256 seal chain (`S3AEDGE1` domain separation) and optional Ed25519 signatures in type 70. There is **no** tile 78 manifest in the Edge tile list (EDGE §12.2). A chain break is `S3AError::SealedChainBreak`: any modified, inserted, or reordered tile breaks the chain immediately (EDGE §6.1).
*   Conformance vectors `MICRO-VEC-01` (`micro_valid_4kb`), `MICRO-VEC-02` (torn tile), `MICRO-VEC-03` (signed seal chain) are generated in `crates/s3a-spec-vectors`.
*   EDGE §10 Phone Reader (Android first): verify CRC32C, payload hash, seal chain, Ed25519; store raw `.s3a`; show an event timeline (faults in red, maintenance in yellow, normal operations in green); optional later LRS sync and in-app consent writes. Phase 2 transports are not implemented. DD-07 colors by *severity* instead of that category coloring; §5.4 states the override and adds a non-color category cue.
*   Primary invariant (EDGE §1.2): every Edge file remains a valid S3A file readable by `s3a-engine` without transcoding.

### 4.4 3-pass, LRS, and writes

From `/workspace/3Pass/README.md` (repo [ashleycribb/3Pass](https://github.com/ashleycribb/3Pass)):

*   Pass 1 is the researcher's own skim. The AI only checks whether a record exists.
*   A paper gets a researcher record only when the researcher selects it for Pass 2/3.
*   `triage_wave.py` lists candidates; it does not score, rank, include/exclude, or record anything. Skipped papers are never recorded. The heading is "Pass 1: Your Skim (~15 s per paper)".
*   AI-extracted text is an AI draft and needs researcher judgment.

`s3a-pack-scholar` already defines the Scholar xAPI profile and tiles 10–15 (`HumanLrsRecord` is tile 10, 64 bytes, including `actor_hash`, `verb_id`, `timestamp_sec`, `object_hash`). `s3a-lrs-server` is the LRS (`POST /xapi/s3a/batch`). A unit test documents the client contract: consent off emits zero requests (`crates/s3a-lrs-server/src/main.rs`, `test_batch_sync_consent_off_client_contract`). The s3a-lrs VM is part of Ashley's lab and is outside the storage repo (`docs/design/research-lab-scenario.md`). Verb IRIs used for inclusion must come from `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs`; this spec does not invent a new IRI (Q-07).

Write APIs already in `s3a-engine`: `TileWriter::create`, `TileWriter::open_append`, `write_hyper_tile` / `write_hyper_tile_ext` / `write_raw_hyper_tile`, `enable_addon`, `try_lock_exclusive`, and `seal_archive` / `seal_archive_with_manifest` / `verify_sealed_archive` / `verify_sealed_bytes`. Opening a sealed archive with `open_append` does not modify it; the first append unseals it by dropping the seal trailer (`unseal_on_write` in `TileWriter`). Durability docs describe S3A as single-writer (`docs/durability.md`). Tiles 10–15 require `ADDON_EDU` (`0x0001`) per `docs/pack-contract.md`. Core `TileType::BLOB` is 68 and `TileType::PROVENANCE` is 72. Scholar pack `create_human_lrs` writes `HumanLrsRecord`s.

### 4.5 S3A mobile app (`s3a-reader`)

[ashleycribb/s3a-reader](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-reader) at `792742c`, with Ashley's 2026-10-27 decisions already recorded (UniFFI + cargo-ndk, Kotlin, Jetpack Compose, minSdk 26, Android first, later iPhone via UniFFI Swift). The Rust crate `s3a-reader-core` opens bytes (`MmapReader::from_bytes`), lists tiles, verifies CRC and signatures via `s3a-engine` (`verify_sealed_bytes` and related APIs in `integrity.rs`), and can leave a quarantined file viewable with a label. The Android shell has no network permission; files come from SAF. Visualization today is a Kotlin "deck plan" (`MapLayout.kt`), not Kitesurf. The published Cargo pin for `s3a-engine` in that tree is still `ff89564`, which is behind the storage commit grounded here.

s3a-kite **replaces that visualization** and becomes the in-app viewer. Verification APIs in `s3a-reader-core` stay and remain the mobile entry to `s3a-engine`. BLE/USB remain out of kite v1.

### 4.6 Pack contract

`docs/pack-contract.md`: browser and WebAssembly integrations stay out of `s3a-core` and `s3a-engine`. Addon bits already allocated: EDU, FINTECH (reserved), ROBOTICS, WEARABLE, GIS, DA, ONTOLOGY. Future packs that need new tile IDs start at 64+ with a new bit; the Clean pack uses existing core tiles and needs no new bit. v1 kite follows the Clean-pack pattern (DD-15, DD-16).

### 4.7 Facts used in v3, v4, and v5 dispositions

*   `s3a-pack-clean` `archive::chunk_tiles` / `unchunk` prefix each BLOB or PROVENANCE tile payload with a 32-byte key, a little-endian `u32` chunk index, and a little-endian `u32` total, then the chunk bytes (`crates/s3a-pack-clean/src/archive.rs`). The core registry describes the same 40-byte field layout for tiles 68 and 72 (`docs/spec/registry.md`). Hyper-tile payload size is `HYPER_TILE_SIZE - HYPER_TILE_HEADER_SIZE` (131,072 − 512).
*   `AcademicPaperRecord.paper_id` is a `u64` described as a hash of DOI / URI. The record has no URL or DOI string field (only `doi_prefix_hash`). `HumanLrsRecord` is 64 bytes: `session_uuid` ([u64; 2]), `timestamp_sec` (u64), `actor_hash` (**u64**), `verb_id` (u32), `decision_score` (f32), `ai_trace_coord`, `object_hash` (**u32**), `flags` (u32).
*   `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs` defines `hash_iri(&str) -> u64` as FNV-1a 64-bit with offset basis `0xcbf29ce484222325` and prime `0x100000001b3`. Scholar tests often use literal hashes; this function is the pack's existing string-to-`u64` hash. Verb IRIs and extensions `EXT_PAPER_ID`, `EXT_SOURCE_URL`, and `EXT_CONTENT_SHA256` live in the same file.
*   3Pass dossiers at `wiki/papers/SRC-XXXX.md` use YAML front matter whose keys in the grounded tree are `source_id`, `title`, `author`, `year`, `drive_path`, `provenance`, `wiki_category`, `theories` (and occasionally `PRACTICE`). None of the 287 dossiers in that tree has a `url` or `source_url` key. `drive_path` values are Windows Google Drive paths, not host-local paths.
*   `s3a-lrs-server` authenticates with HTTP Basic (`Authorization: Basic …`) from a credentials file of lines `user:password[:scopes[:archive]]`, or `--auth-user` / `--auth-pass`. It exposes `POST /xapi/statements` and requires `X-Experience-API-Version` of `1.0.x` (tests use `1.0.3`). `POST /xapi/s3a/batch` is a signed binary delta sync, not a JSON statement post.
*   Edge type-70 tiles hold one or more `DeviceSealRecord`s (144 bytes): `tile_index`, `generation`, `seal_hash`, `prev_seal_hash`, `signature` (Ed25519 over the 32-byte `seal_hash`), CRC. `MmapReader::edge_seal_records` returns each record with its `container_tile`. `edge_seal_chain` recomputes `compute_seal_hash` per tile in index order and stops at the first CRC-failed tile. Whenever any type-70 record exists, each stored `seal_hash` is compared to that recomputed hash, whether or not `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set; a mismatch is a `chain_break` whose `break_index` is (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none (DD-150, AT-220). In an Edge file that contains at least one type-70 `DeviceSealRecord`, an Edge writer may sign every *N* tiles; tiles not covered by a verified record are untrusted. In a hyper-tile, or an Edge file with no type-70 records, a valid floor of a file that is not unauthenticated as a whole is trusted (§5.3 item 4).
*   `tour.rs` `evaluate_consent_summary` returns status `"open / unrestricted (no consent records)"` when there are no consent records, no withdrawal marks, and no Edge consent value. That is the no-records behavior kite follows for Edge and hyper-tile files **subject to the trusted-authentication condition in §5.3 item 4** (DD-67, DD-147): an unauthenticated-as-a-whole file with zero records is not unrestricted.
*   `s3a-reader-core/src/verify.rs` defines `VerificationStatus` {`Unverified`, `Verified`, `SelfSigned`, `Unsigned`, `Quarantined`} and `SignerTrust` {`Embedded`, `Pinned`}. `verify(pinned_keys)` treats a non-empty pinned list as the trust anchor: a signer not in the list fails ("signer key not in pinned list"). An empty pin list yields at best `SelfSigned` (a signature against a `DeviceIdentityRecord` key is not independently trusted). s3a-vault `contacts.json` marks a contact `VERIFIED` only after `vault contact add` of the full 32-byte key; unpinned issuers are `UNKNOWN ISSUER`. S3A-EDGE-PROFILE.md §543 refers to a "cached or provisioned public_key."
*   `kitesurf-cli` downloads a Wasm scene rasterizer for playback; that download path is not used in v1 kite (DD-44). `boa_eval.rs` / `js.rs` remain wasm32-only; v1 does not invent a Wasmtime import ABI for them and does not depend on Boa or Wasmtime.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 5. Architecture

### 5.1 Pack layout, names, and FFI (draft: DD-01)

These names are fixed for tests-first work:

| Thing | Name |
| --- | --- |
| Pack crate | `s3a-pack-kite` (`crates/s3a-pack-kite` in s3a-storage) |
| Host binary | `s3a-kite` |
| UniFFI surface | exported by `s3a-pack-kite` (`cdylib` / `staticlib`) |
| Crate types | `rlib`, `cdylib`, and `staticlib` |
| Error type | `KiteError` (message string + a stable code) |

FFI / pack API (every `render_*` returns `Result<RenderFrame, KiteError>`). `device_pixel_ratio` is a parameter on every `render_*` (DD-69). A `KiteContext` holds the UA font set and is required by every `open*` call (DD-122). `max_open_bytes` is required on every `open*` (DD-123):

| Function | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `KiteContext::new(ua_fonts) -> Result<KiteContext, KiteError>` | Build a context from the required UA font bytes (a list of `(name, bytes)`) using the process clock. If a required font is missing, returns `KiteError` that names it (AT-42). There is no process-global font state; tests create a context per test. The shipped host and mobile binaries build one context at startup with the `include_bytes!` set by calling `new`. |
| `KiteContext::new_with_clock(ua_fonts, clock) -> Result<KiteContext, KiteError>` | Same as `new`, plus a clock that returns Unix UTC seconds. Pack tests use this (AT-161, DD-110). It is **not** exported on phone UniFFI. Withholding and Edge expiry are evaluated against this clock at each pack render. The host candidate recompute uses the process clock, not a pack-injected clock (AT-161). |
| `open(ctx, bytes, name_hint, max_frame_pixels, max_open_bytes, pinned_keys) -> Result<Handle, KiteError>` | Open an archive or document already loaded as bytes. `name_hint` is an optional filename (used only for kind detection, §5.2.5). `max_frame_pixels` is the cap in §5.8. `max_open_bytes` is the cap on the input length; exceeding it is `KiteError` with code `file_too_large`. `pinned_keys` is a (possibly empty) list of 32-byte Ed25519 public keys; an empty list is the omitted pin list (DD-79, DD-98). The host always calls `open` or `open_path` and runs the §5.4 algorithm itself (DD-115). It does not prefer `open_verified`. After `open` / `open_path`, whether the file is unauthenticated as a whole for consent is the §5.3 item 4 / DD-148 predicate applied to the pack-computed report — the same predicate `open_verified` applies to a supplied report. |
| `open_verified(ctx, bytes, name_hint, max_frame_pixels, max_open_bytes, report) -> Result<Handle, KiteError>` | Same as `open`, but kite uses the supplied `KiteVerificationReport` for the label, `seal_status`, quarantine banner, and valid floors, and does **not** recompute verification (DD-80, AT-137). Valid floors are the complement of `invalid_tile_indices` in `0 .. tiles_total-1`. The file is unauthenticated as a whole for consent under the **single definition** in §5.3 item 4 / DD-148, applied to this supplied report (the same predicate pack `open` / `open_path` apply to the pack-computed report). Tiles at or after `break_index` (when present) are unauthenticated (they are not valid floors); that does not by itself make the whole file unauthenticated. After the `invalid_report` checks below, `invalid_tile_indices` includes every such index, so the complement definition and this sentence agree. The report type includes `status: unverified`. A report whose `status` is `unverified` is a `KiteError` (the caller must run verify first) (AT-154). The pack also validates the supplied report against the opened bytes: every index in `invalid_tile_indices` and `torn_tile_indices` is in `0 .. tiles_total-1`; `torn_tile_indices` is a subset of `invalid_tile_indices`; if `break_index` is `Some`, it is `< tiles_total` and every index at or after `break_index` is in `invalid_tile_indices`; if `quarantine_reason` is `signer_not_pinned` or `missing_signature`, `invalid_tile_indices` is the full range `0 .. tiles_total-1`; if `status` is `quarantined`, `quarantine_reason` is not `None`; if `quarantine_reason` is `Some`, `status` is `quarantined`; if `quarantine_reason` is `chain_break`, `signature_known_signer`, or `crc`, `break_index` is `Some`; a hyper-tile report does not use `quarantine_reason` `signature_known_signer`, `chain_break`, `missing_signature`, or `signer_not_pinned`, and does not use `status` `verified` or `self_signed` (with or without a `quarantine_reason`); an Edge report does not use `quarantine_reason` `seal_trailer`; and the bytes are an archive kind (`hyper-tile` or `edge`, not `wiki` / `html` / `other` / `unsupported`). Bytes that classify as `unsupported` are `KiteError` with code `not_archive` (AT-97), not `invalid_report`. A violation of the checks above is `KiteError` with code `invalid_report` (AT-211). `open_verified` does not recompute verification or the unsigned-tail union; after these checks it uses the supplied floor list as given and applies the DD-148 predicate to that report. |
| `open_path(ctx, fd, canonical_path, validated_root, max_frame_pixels, max_open_bytes, pinned_keys, scholar_roots, name_hint) -> Result<Handle, KiteError>` | Open bytes from a file descriptor the caller has already authorized under DD-146. `fd` is that host-validated open descriptor (regular file; the host has `fstat`ed it and has not consumed it). `canonical_path` is the absolute canonical path the host proved by the dirfd walk. `validated_root` is the allowed request root that walk started from (absolute, already canonicalized). The pack **reads from `fd` only**; it does not reopen `canonical_path` by name, and it does not `lstat` or `openat` that path again (DD-146, AT-101, AT-202). It performs the single bounded read with `pread` from offset 0 (or an `lseek` to 0 followed by `read`), at most `max_open_bytes` + 1 bytes from `fd` (`file_too_large` if more arrive), and uses `from_bytes` (no path mmap in v1, DD-75). The pack does not depend on the fd offset left by the host. `name_hint` is `Option<String>`: the optional `/v1/open` `name` when the host has one, otherwise `None` (the pack then uses the basename of `canonical_path`). Kind detection uses that hint. `pinned_keys` is as in `open`. `scholar_roots` is `Option<ScholarRoots>` with fields `wiki_root` and `corpus_root` (absolute, already canonicalized by the caller) and `shadowed_by_wiki: bool` (DD-139). When `Some`, the pack uses those roots, `canonical_path`, `validated_root`, `shadowed_by_wiki`, the YAML front matter read from the same `fd` bytes, and this call's `max_open_bytes` to decide `paper_identity` under the §5.5 dossier checks below. The pack does **no path I/O** beyond the already-open `fd` (it does not probe `{wiki_root}/<same relative path>`). When `None`, `paper_identity` is `None`. The Scholar host performs the DD-146 walk and passes `Some` with `--wiki-root`, `--corpus-root`, and `shadowed_by_wiki` computed as specified in §5.5: true if and only if a qualifying item-1 wiki candidate exists at the same relative path. Viewer-only and any caller without roots pass `None`. Phone UniFFI does not export `open_path` (DD-109, AT-99). |
| `verification_report(handle) -> Result<KiteVerificationReport, KiteError>` | Return the `KiteVerificationReport` for an open archive handle (read-only, pure; no I/O, no mutation). After `open` or `open_path`, the report is the one the pack computed, including the valid-floor list (`invalid_tile_indices` is the complement of those floors in `0 .. tiles_total-1`). After `open_verified`, the report is the supplied report, not a recomputation (AT-137). Unknown handle, or a non-archive handle (`wiki` / `html` / `other` / `unsupported`), is `KiteError` (`unknown_handle` / `not_archive`). `kind: "unsupported"` is not an archive for this function: `tiles_total` is not defined (AT-97). This function is exported on phone UniFFI (DD-109, AT-99). AT-165 compares this value after pack `open` to a reader-core `verify` mapped by the table below. |
| `close(handle) -> Result<(), KiteError>` | Release a handle and its last-frame cache. Unknown handle is an error (`unknown_handle`). Drop of a UniFFI handle object also releases it. |
| `render_manifest(handle, width, height, scroll_y, device_pixel_ratio)` | First view of a `.s3a` / Edge file (manifest, labeled absent/quarantine/unsupported state, or Edge header sheet). `view=banner` is an alias for this function (DD-81). |
| `render_page(handle, width, height, scroll_y, device_pixel_ratio, select_enabled)` | Wiki Markdown, HTML, or `kind: "other"` documents (the `page` view). `select_enabled` is true only when Scholar-mode `/v1/select` is available (false on the phone and in viewer-only mode; DD-91). A Select control is painted only when `select_enabled` is true **and** `paper_identity(handle)` is `Some` (AT-101, AT-144). |
| `proceed_to_warehouse(handle)` | Leave the first `.s3a` / Edge view. No-op (not an error) if already in the warehouse. The §5.2.6 matrix applies: `KiteError` `not_archive` if the handle is a page (`wiki` / `html` / `other`); `KiteError` `unsupported` if the handle is `kind: "unsupported"`. |
| `render_warehouse(handle, width, height, scroll_x, scroll_y, device_pixel_ratio)` | Warehouse floor grid at the given scroll offset. |
| `render_floor_transition(handle, floor_index, t_seconds, width, height, scroll_x, scroll_y, instant, device_pixel_ratio)` | Floor-to-detail animation for `floor_index`. `scroll_x` and `scroll_y` are the warehouse scroll at click time; they define the start rectangle in §5.8 (DD-18). |
| `render_floor_detail(handle, floor_index, width, height, scroll_y, device_pixel_ratio)` | Floor N records, including the **per-floor** `withheld_count` (§5.3 item 4). `floor_index` is the tile `index` (the same field as AT-10), not an ordinal among only valid floors (DD-82). Withheld payloads do not appear in `text_runs`, `semantics`, pixels, or hits (DD-125). |
| `hit_test(handle, view, floor, x, y) -> Result<Hit, KiteError>` | Map a point in the last frame of `(view, floor)` for this handle to a `Hit`. `floor` is `Option<u32>`: required (`Some`) on `floor-transition` and `floor-detail`; `None` on other views. On a floor-scoped view, omitted `floor` is the same error as HTTP `400` `missing_param`. `x` and `y` are finite `f64` CSS pixels (§5.8). A finite point outside the last frame's CSS box returns `Hit::None`. A non-numeric or non-finite `x` or `y` is `KiteError` with code `invalid_param` (HTTP `400` `invalid_param`). The §5.2.6 matrix applies to `hit_test` and is checked before `no_frame` (DD-125). |
| `paper_identity(handle) -> Option<String>` | `Some(stem)` if and only if this handle was opened with `open_path` and `scholar_roots: Some(...)`, and the supplied `canonical_path` would be a member of item 1 of the §5.5 candidate set under those same roots and the same rules, using `validated_root` as the root of the walk: `canonical_path` is under `validated_root`; it matches the non-recursive glob `{root}/papers/SRC-*.md` under `wiki_root` or `corpus_root`; the bytes the pack `pread`s from `fd` have present, parseable UTF-8 YAML front matter (missing, unparseable, or non-UTF-8 front matter is `None`); YAML `source_id`, if present, equals the `SRC-NNNN` stem; size ≤ `max_open_bytes`. The pack does **not** `lstat` or reopen `canonical_path`, and does not probe any other path, to make this decision (the host already `fstat`ed the fd as a regular file and computed `shadowed_by_wiki`). **Wiki wins** means a **qualifying item-1 wiki candidate exists** at the same relative path (a regular file under the shared safe-open rule, with parseable UTF-8 YAML front matter, matching `source_id` if present, and size ≤ `max_open_bytes`) — not merely that some directory entry exists there. The host sets `shadowed_by_wiki` to true if and only if that qualifying wiki candidate exists; the pack then returns `None` for a handle whose `canonical_path` is the losing (`corpus_root`) copy. The wiki copy may still be `Some`. If a file exists at the wiki path but is not a qualifying candidate (symlink, bad or missing front matter, oversize, or a failed safe-open), `shadowed_by_wiki` is false and the corpus copy may be `Some` (AT-207, AT-188, AT-101). Otherwise `None`. `open` of bytes yields `None`. There is no "bound paper page" identity. |

`Hit` is one of: `Continue`, `Floor(n)`, `Select { paper_id }`, `Link { href }`, `None`. `hit_test` is **pure**: it does not change handle state (DD-97).

*   `Continue` is only defined on `manifest`, `header`, or `banner` first views (the first-view aliases of `render_manifest`) when the handle's `kind` is not `"unsupported"`. A `kind: "unsupported"` banner paints no Continue control; `hit_test` there returns `None`, and `POST /v1/proceed` remains `409` `unsupported` (AT-97). After a `Continue` hit, the client must call `proceed_to_warehouse` / `POST /v1/proceed` to enter the warehouse. A Continue hit alone leaves the handle on the first view (AT-152).
*   `Floor(n)` is only defined on `warehouse`. `n` is the tile `index`. Invalid or omitted tiles are not hit-testable as `Floor(n)` (they may be listed in the banner text only).
*   `Select { paper_id }` is only defined on `view=page` when `select_enabled` was true for the last `render_page` and `paper_identity(handle)` is `Some`. The returned `paper_id` is that identity. A Select hit does **not** write an inclusion record; the host or native window must call `POST /v1/select` with the UI token (DD-58, DD-60). In viewer-only mode and on the phone, `select_enabled` is false and no Select control is painted (AT-144).
*   `Link { href }` is defined on `view=page` for a link box. `href` in the `Hit` and in the corresponding `SemanticNode` is the value the client uses: for a relative or root-relative link the pack **strips the fragment and the query from the raw attribute, then percent-decodes the path, then resolves**: a relative href is resolved against the **parent directory of the opened page**; a root-relative href (`/x.md`) is resolved against the **allowed request root of the opened page** (`validated_root`), not the filesystem root (AT-160). For `http`, `https`, and `file://` it is the raw attribute with `#fragment` stripped. For a handle opened from bytes (`open`, `open_verified`, or a host open of raw bytes with no filesystem path), `href` is the raw attribute with `#fragment` stripped in every case; it is not resolved against a parent directory (there is none) (AT-206). The pack and the host do **not** fetch or open the link automatically. If the resolved path canonicalizes inside an allowed request root, the UI may then call `POST /v1/open` with that path (AT-160). `http(s)` and `file://` hrefs are not opened or fetched from the page view in v1. The page view also loads **no subresources of any kind** (network or local) in v1 (DD-118, AT-121, AT-167).
*   `hit_test` on `floor-detail` or `floor-transition` (when a prior frame for that view **and floor** exists) returns `None`. Without a prior frame it is `409` `no_frame`. The last-frame cache is keyed by `(handle, view, floor)`. First-view aliases `manifest`, `header`, and `banner` share one normalized view key, `first`, so a `banner` frame followed by `hit_test` with `view=manifest` (or the reverse) hits that frame (AT-196). `floor` is `None` for non-floor views; `Some(_)` on a non-floor view is `400` `invalid_param`, not ignored (the FFI matches the HTTP rule).
*   A `hit_test` whose `view` is not valid for the handle's kind and state under §5.2.6 returns the same error as `/v1/frame` for that cell (`view_mismatch`, `not_archive`, or `unsupported`), checked **before** `no_frame` (AT-172).

v1 UniFFI on the phone exports `KiteContext::new`, `open`, `open_verified`, `verification_report`, `close`, `proceed_to_warehouse`, the render functions, `hit_test`, and the `KiteVerificationReport` type (F-04, DD-128, AT-99). It does not export `KiteContext::new_with_clock`, `open_path`, `paper_identity`, select, download, skip, consent, or write (DD-109). The app may run `s3a-reader-core` `verify(pinned_keys)` and pass the mapped `KiteVerificationReport` to `open_verified`, or call `open` and let the pack run the §5.4 algorithm. Pack `open` and a mapped reader-core `verify` of the same bytes and pin list must agree on `status`, `seal_status`, `invalid_tile_indices`, `break_index`, `torn_tile_indices`, and `quarantine_reason` (DD-115, AT-165). The pack does not depend on `s3a-reader-core`. `KiteVerificationReport` is a pack type with fields `status` (`verified` / `self_signed` / `unsigned` / `quarantined` / `unverified`), `seal_status` (`sealed` / `unsealed` / `seal_failed`), `tiles_crc_failed`, `invalid_tile_indices: Vec<u32>`, `break_index: Option<u32>`, `torn_tile_indices: Vec<u32>`, `quarantine_reason: Option<QuarantineReason>`, and `detail`. `QuarantineReason` is one of `crc`, `seal_trailer`, `chain_break`, `signature_known_signer`, `signer_not_pinned`, `missing_signature`. Pack `invalid_tile_indices` is every tile index that is **not** in the valid floor list. The range is always `0 .. tiles_total-1`, where `tiles_total` is the number of tile indices `MmapReader` enumerates, including torn and uncommitted trailing tiles, and may differ from the header `tile_count`. When no tile is a valid floor, the list is every index in that range. This is the only range; the pack does not use `tile_count` for `invalid_tile_indices`. The app maps a post-verify reader-core `VerificationReport` (s3a-reader `792742c`, `s3a-reader-core/src/verify.rs` and `integrity.rs`) by this table (DD-130):

| `KiteVerificationReport` field | Mapping from reader-core |
| --- | --- |
| `status` | `VerificationStatus`: `Unverified` → `unverified`, `Verified` → `verified`, `SelfSigned` → `self_signed`, `Unsigned` → `unsigned`, `Quarantined` → `quarantined` |
| `seal_status` | `SealInfo.state`: `NotSealed` → `unsealed`, `Sealed` → `sealed`, `Damaged` → `seal_failed` |
| `tiles_crc_failed` | `Vec<u32>`: increasing tile indices of committed tiles whose payload CRC failed (copied from reader-core `tiles_crc_failed`, which has this type). |
| `invalid_tile_indices` | The union, in increasing order, of: `torn_tile_indices`; `tiles_crc_failed`; uncommitted indices (index ≥ header `tile_count`, or after the first torn, CRC-failed, or chain-stop tile); and every index at or after `break_index` when that field is `Some`. The range is `0 .. tiles_total-1`, where `tiles_total` is the number of tile indices the reader enumerates, including torn and uncommitted trailing tiles; it may differ from the header `tile_count`. Both pack text and AT-165 / AT-195 use this name (`tiles_total`), not `tile_count`. If `quarantine_reason` is `signer_not_pinned` or `missing_signature`, the list is every index in that range. After that union is filled, if any authentic type-70 `DeviceSealRecord` exists, every index greater than the highest authentic signed `tile_index` is also added (the unsigned tail; AT-210), except a type-70 container tile whose `DeviceSealRecord`(s) all verify, which is exempt. The mapping is not a pure field copy. If the reader cannot open the file, the list is empty. |
| `break_index` | As in the §5.4 / DD-150 derivation table (pack `open` is normative): the **minimum** over every applicable cause that sets one. Reader-core `SealInfo` or `checks` supply inputs to that table (torn-only, whether or not the chain is active, CRC, `chain_break`, or `signature_known_signer` `tile_index`); they are not copied blindly, and a later higher-priority reason does not move `break_index` later. Hyper-tile committed CRC sets a candidate `break_index` at the first failed committed tile. |
| `torn_tile_indices` | Increasing indices of tiles that satisfy the §5.4 / DD-100 **torn predicate**: index ≥ header `tile_count`, or the header is absent, is erased (`0xFF` fill), or fails header validation, or the tile is a partial trailing tile. The field contains only those indices. Tiles that are uncommitted solely because they follow a torn or CRC stop, but do not themselves satisfy the predicate, are in `invalid_tile_indices` and are **not** in `torn_tile_indices` (AT-165). The mapping does not treat a reader `tile_at` `Err` as torn by itself (a committed CRC failure is `tiles_crc_failed`, not torn). |
| `quarantine_reason` | When `status` is `quarantined`, the highest-priority applicable reason, else `None`. Priority order (DD-150): `signer_not_pinned`, `missing_signature`, `seal_trailer`, `signature_known_signer`, `chain_break`, `crc`. Grounded in `SignatureCheck.detail` (including "signer key not in pinned list", "Ed25519 signature invalid", "seal hash does not match recomputed chain"), `SealInfo.state == Damaged`, and `tiles_crc_failed`. A mid-chain "Ed25519 signature invalid" after an earlier authentic record is `signature_known_signer`, not `chain_break`. |
| `detail` | `String`: copied from reader-core `integrity_detail`. |

Pack `open` uses the same `quarantine_reason` priority order as this table (`signer_not_pinned` > `missing_signature` > `seal_trailer` > `signature_known_signer` > `chain_break` > `crc`) (DD-115, DD-150). `SignerTrust` and `SignatureCheck` themselves are not copied as raw fields; pack `status` and `quarantine_reason` encode pinned versus embedded and the quarantine cause. `unverified` is a legal `status` value (verify has not been called) but `open_verified` returns `KiteError` for it (DD-116, DD-99). AT-165 lives in the out-of-workspace s3a-reader test crate with AT-38 / AT-39 / AT-43 / AT-63 and uses this table.

The pack vendors the Kitesurf Rust core from [ashleycribb/s3a-Kite](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-Kite) at `235111c6fe10f2d63f3476bf5e3d19b4c7089415`. The TypeScript engine, Wrangler, Worker Loader, and TypeScript CDP are not runtime dependencies.

`s3a-core` MUST NOT depend on this pack. The pack depends on `s3a-engine`, `s3a-edge`, `s3a-pack-scholar`, and the forked Kitesurf crates.

**Test locations and pins (DD-121).** Pack ATs are split so the s3a-storage workspace stays green before `s3a-pack-kite` exists: `cargo metadata`, `cargo test`, and every other cargo command at the workspace root must resolve (a workspace member must not depend on a crate that does not yet exist, including via an optional or feature-gated dependency). Tests must not be edited to make them pass. Path dependencies are allowed only in local developer checkouts. "Land" means local branches; nothing is pushed until Ashley says so. Android/iOS ATs run only on a job that has the NDK or Apple toolchain; they are skipped, not failed, on a host-only CI job, and the skip is reported.

- **Workspace member, no pack dependency (`kite-acceptance`).** Host-spawn tests, `cargo tree` / link-gate tests that do not compile against the pack, and the guards that already pass today (AT-22, AT-37, and any other guard that does not require the pack crate to exist) live in a `kite-acceptance` crate that **is** a member of the s3a-storage workspace and has **no** path or git dependency on `s3a-pack-kite`. AT-22 and AT-37 run in this crate today (`cargo test -p kite-acceptance`, and they run under workspace `cargo test`). Tests that spawn `s3a-kite host` fail at **runtime** (not at compile time) if the binary is missing, with a documented "binary not found" failure. AT-40 lives in s3a-storage as the iOS link gate.
- **Out-of-workspace pack-linking crate (`kite-pack-tests`).** Library and unit tests that link `s3a-pack-kite` (including AT-42, AT-95, AT-96, AT-161, AT-205, AT-211, and other pack-API ATs) live in a `kite-pack-tests` crate that is **not** a workspace member (it is listed in `[workspace] exclude`, or lives outside the workspace tree). It has its own `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` and path dependencies into the storage tree and the pack. CI builds it explicitly. Until the pack exists, building this crate fails, and that compile failure is the documented expected fail-first result; it does not fail workspace cargo commands. There is no skeleton `s3a-pack-kite` crate (a stub is forbidden, and it would make AT-01 pass vacuously).
- **s3a-reader tests, outside the reader workspace.** AT-38, AT-39, AT-43, AT-63, and AT-165 live in a separate test crate **outside** the s3a-reader workspace (own `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`, path dependencies). They are **not** feature-gated members of the reader workspace. The reader workspace stays green on the current pin. That out-of-workspace crate builds `s3a-reader-core` against the same `s3a-engine` path or revision as the pack (storage `b7bdcda` or later), not against the reader's older engine pin `ff89564` alone (AT-165, AT-220). CI builds that crate explicitly when those tests are run.
- **Host crate (`crates/s3a-kite`), out of the workspace.** The `s3a-kite` host library and `s3a-kite` binary live in `crates/s3a-kite` (package name `s3a-kite`, lib name `s3a_kite`). That crate is **not** a member of the s3a-storage workspace (it is listed in `[workspace] exclude`, or lives outside the workspace tree, the same rule as `kite-pack-tests`). It has its own `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`. Until the pack exists it has **no** pack dependency, so the unit-test half of AT-183 can land in step 1 of §11: the unit tests of `s3a_kite::egress::egress_allowlist_matches` and `s3a_kite::egress::parse_allowlist_entry` live in this crate's own tests (no network). When the pack is added, this crate may gain a path dependency on `s3a-pack-kite` and remains excluded from the workspace so workspace cargo commands stay green. CI builds it explicitly. Until the crate exists, building it fails, and that compile failure is the documented fail-first result for the unit half of AT-183. The unit-test half of AT-183 does not live in `kite-acceptance`; the host-spawn halves (startup error and end-to-end fixture) do.
- **Test hooks (DD-151, §5.2.7).** In-process fault-injection and pause points used by fail-first ATs are configured only through the `test_hooks` Cargo feature and the `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS` environment-variable control channel. They are not host flags. Release builds, and every build compiled without `test_hooks`, reject the variables at startup (AT-222).

### 5.2 Native host (hub, Aura Rack, LRS VM, lab PC)

The CLI is `s3a-kite host [flags...]` (DD-95). `host` is the only v1 subcommand; flags after `host` are the section 5.2.2 inventory. There is no `s3a-kite` invocation that starts the HTTP listener without `host`.

The host process:

1.  Compiles `kitesurf-core` and `renderer-core` with feature `native-host` and links them as native `rlib`s (DD-02). Wasmtime is not used in v1. Boa is not used in v1.
2.  Requires the flags in the section 5.2.2 inventory for the chosen mode. Missing required flags are an error in every mode. There is no silent port or bind default (DD-03).
3.  Rejects unauthenticated HTTP as in §5.2.1 (DD-11, DD-29, DD-71). There is no CDP listener in v1 (DD-41).
4.  For a candidate **source fetch** (download of a paper that has a `source_url`): the host itself performs the HTTP request, applies the egress policy in §5.2.4, and stores the raw response body as in §5.5. It does not start Boa, Wasmtime, or a page-script isolate. There is no `POST /v1/open` by URL.
5.  For `.s3a` and Edge files: the host calls `render_manifest` / warehouse / floor functions and paints `RenderFrame`s. For wiki Markdown and HTML files: the host calls `render_page`. No Boa, no Wasmtime, no page-script, and **no subresources of any kind** (network or local: no `<img>`, `<link>`, `@font-face`, or Markdown image/file includes are loaded from disk or the network) (DD-77, DD-118, AT-121, AT-167).

**Native window (DD-60).** The window is compiled behind the cargo feature `native-window` and, at runtime, is suppressed by `--headless`. A `native-window` build opens the window unless `--headless` is set. A build without the feature must pass `--headless` or startup fails and names `--headless`. The window is a Host HTTP API client in this process. It attaches the UI token to every request other than the tokenless health case. It does not call select/download/skip/consent through the pack FFI (those functions are not in the pack API). A native window is allowed only when `--bind` is a loopback address or a Unix socket; `--headless` is required for any other bind (loopback and `unix:` binds never use TLS; passing `--tls-cert` or `--tls-key` on them is a startup error that names the flag, so a windowed start must omit those flags). If `--headless` is omitted and no display is available, startup fails and names `DISPLAY`. The HTTP listener is always started.

One Scholar-mode host process serves one `--researcher-id` and one working archive. Durable local disk is required in Scholar mode (DD-48, DD-78).

#### 5.2.1 Host HTTP API (DD-42)

All routes below are the complete v1 HTTP surface. Authentication is checked first (DD-71): a missing or invalid token yields **401** on every route other than the **tokenless health case** (`GET /health` on a loopback or Unix-socket bind without `--behind-proxy`). With `--behind-proxy`, `/health` also requires a token (AT-113, AT-87). AT-50 is the non-loopback TLS bind (without `--behind-proxy`) in which `/health` also requires a token. A valid token on an unknown path yields **404**. AT-06 is "401 without a token on every route other than the tokenless health case; 404 with a valid token on a path not in this table." There is no CDP upgrade.

JSON uses UTF-8 and `Content-Type: application/json`. Errors are `{ "error": "<code>", "message": "<text>" }` with the HTTP status in the table below. `<handle>` is an opaque string minted by `/v1/open`.

Contract defaults for `/v1/frame` query parameters (DD-69), used only when the parameter is omitted: `scroll_x=0`, `scroll_y=0`, `instant=false`, `device_pixel_ratio=1.0`. `w` and `h` have no default and are required. `floor` is required for `floor-transition` and `floor-detail`. `t` is required for `floor-transition` when `instant` is false. `w`, `h`, `scroll_x`, `scroll_y`, `x`, and `y` are **CSS pixels**. The response `width` and `height` are pixmap pixels: `ceil(w * device_pixel_ratio)` by `ceil(h * device_pixel_ratio)` (§5.8, DD-90). Query-field types and ranges (AT-171, AT-141): `w` and `h` are integers ≥ 0 (0 is `invalid_frame_size` at the route tail); `t`, `x`, `y`, `scroll_x`, and `scroll_y` are finite `f64` CSS pixels (`scroll_x` / `scroll_y` may be negative); `device_pixel_ratio` is a finite `f64`; `instant` is the query literals `true` and `false` (`/v1/frame` is a GET with query parameters only; a JSON boolean form does not occur). A present field that is non-numeric, non-finite, the wrong JSON type, or an `instant` value other than those literals is `400` `invalid_param` at the param phase, **including a malformed field that the known view does not use** (`t=abc` on `view=warehouse` is `invalid_param`, not ignored). A present, well-typed `device_pixel_ratio` that is not greater than 0, or a pixmap product that exceeds `--max-frame-pixels`, or a `w` or `h` of 0, is `400` `invalid_frame_size` at the route tail (AT-141). Omitted optional fields still take the contract defaults above.

| Method | Path | Token | Request | Success |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GET` | `/health` | None in the tokenless health case; UI token otherwise | (none) | `200`, `Content-Type: text/plain`, body exactly `ok\n` |
| `POST` | `/v1/open` | UI | `{ "path": "<path>", "name": "<optional hint>" }` | `200` `{ "handle": "<id>", "kind": "hyper-tile"\|"edge"\|"wiki"\|"html"\|"other"\|"unsupported", "first_view": "manifest"\|"header"\|"page"\|"banner" }` — `name`, if set, is the kind-detection hint; otherwise the path basename is used (§5.2.5) |
| `POST` | `/v1/close` | UI | `{ "handle": "<id>" }` | `200` `{ "ok": true }` — unknown handle is `404` `unknown_handle` |
| `POST` | `/v1/proceed` | UI | `{ "handle": "<id>" }` | `200` `{ "ok": true }` |
| `GET` | `/v1/frame` | UI | Query: `handle`, `view` (`manifest`\|`header`\|`page`\|`banner`\|`warehouse`\|`floor-transition`\|`floor-detail`), `w`, `h`, plus the defaults above, `floor` and `t` when required | `200` `{ "png_base64": "<png>", "width": <pixmap w>, "height": <pixmap h>, "text_runs": [...], "semantics": [...] }`. `view=banner` calls `render_manifest` (DD-81). For `floor-transition`, `scroll_x` and `scroll_y` are the warehouse scroll at click time (default 0 only if omitted). An unknown `view` is `400` `invalid_view`. |
| `POST` | `/v1/hit-test` | UI | `{ "handle", "view", "x", "y", "floor": <n or omitted> }` | `200` `{ "hit": "continue"\|"floor"\|"select"\|"link"\|"none", "floor": <n or null>, "paper_id": <string or null>, "href": <string or null> }` — `x`/`y` are CSS pixels; `floor` is a tile index |
| `GET` | `/v1/candidates` | UI | (none) | `200` `{ "papers": [ { "id": "<paper identity>", "title": "<from dossier YAML title, or empty>", "source_url": <string or null>, "on_disk": <bool>, "page_path": <absolute canonical dossier path or null>, "archive_path": <absolute canonical `.s3a` path or null> } ] }` — Scholar mode only; `409` `viewer_only` in viewer-only mode. The list is recomputed at request time (§5.5). |
| `POST` | `/v1/select` | UI | `{ "paper_id": "<paper identity>" }` | `200` `{ "ok": true, "lrs_status": "<sent or queued or skipped or failed or not_configured>" }` — Scholar mode; closer-read event only (DD-154). `409` `viewer_only` in viewer-only; `404` `unknown_paper` if `paper_id` is not in the candidate set recomputed at this request. Select does not write an inclusion record, does not snapshot or fingerprint the paper, does not append an inclusion-index line, and does not enter the paper in the corpus. A repeat select of the same identity in the same working archive does not emit a new LRS statement. `lrs_status` has the same values as for download, with the same meanings (sent / queued / failed / skipped / not_configured). |
| `POST` | `/v1/download` | UI | `{ "paper_id": "<paper identity>" }` | `200` `{ "ok": true, "path": "<absolute path in --download-dir or null>", "snapshot": "<absolute path or null>", "snapshot_error": <string or null>, "fetch_error": <string or null>, "download_error": <string or null>, "lrs_status": "<as select>" }` — same 409/404 as select for viewer_only / unknown_paper. `download_error` is `"no_source"` when the candidate has neither a usable `source_url` nor on-disk bytes (`path` is then null); `"fetch_failed"` when a required fetch failed, including when the sealed snapshot is `snapshot_too_large` (`fetch_error` is `"snapshot_too_large"`, `path` and `snapshot` are null, 2b is skipped; AT-227); `"snapshot_invalid"` when reassembly from an existing snapshot fails, or a recorded, **present** snapshot fails the DD-103 reuse checks (a missing recorded file is a re-fetch, DD-152) (`path` and `snapshot` are then null, `snapshot_error` is `"snapshot_invalid"`, HTTP 200, inclusion kept; AT-180, AT-209); `"source_changed"` when a 2b copy from a pre-opened `drive_path` fd exceeds `--max-open-bytes` mid-copy (`path` is null, the temp is deleted, inclusion kept, no mid-request fetch switch; AT-202); `"download_conflict"` is returned as `409` `download_conflict` with `included: true` and `lrs_status` whenever an inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1 (written on this request or earlier) (AT-94). Bytes for a later download of a previously snapshotted identity are reassembled from that snapshot's BLOB tiles (DD-61). |
| `POST` | `/v1/skip` | UI | `{ "paper_id": "<paper identity>" }` | `200` `{ "ok": true }` — Scholar mode; writes nothing; `404` `unknown_paper` if not in the request-time candidate set; `409` `viewer_only` in viewer-only |
| `POST` | `/v1/deselect` | UI | `{ "paper_id": "<paper identity>" }` | `200` `{ "ok": true, "lrs_status": "<sent or queued or skipped or failed or not_configured>" }` — Scholar mode; logs a deselect event (DD-154, verb `removed`). The body has no reason field, and a reason property is rejected if present (`400` `invalid_request`). `409` `viewer_only` in viewer-only; `404` `unknown_paper` if not in the request-time candidate set; `404` `not_selected` if no prior select of this identity exists in this working archive; `409` `already_included` if a download inclusion for this identity already exists (the inclusion, snapshot, and fingerprint are left in place; deselect does not delete corpus data). A successful deselect writes no snapshot, no fingerprint, and no inclusion record or inclusion-index line, and the paper does not enter the corpus. A repeat deselect of the same identity does not emit a new LRS statement. Pass 1 `/v1/skip` remains a different route and still writes nothing. |
| `GET` | `/v1/consent` | UI | (none) | `200` `{ "state": "on"\|"off", "timestamp": "<RFC3339 seconds UTC or null>" }` — Scholar mode; `409` `viewer_only` in viewer-only. Without `--lrs-url`, this is `200` `{ "state": "off", "timestamp": null }`. |
| `POST` | `/v1/consent` | UI | `{ "state": "on"\|"off" }` | `200` with the same body as GET after the host writes the consent file (a same-state POST is a no-op that keeps the timestamp; a same-state `off` still runs the latch increment / re-cancel / decrement, DD-144) — Scholar mode; `409` `viewer_only` in viewer-only; `409` `lrs_not_configured` if `state` is `on` and `--lrs-url` is unset (reachable at runtime; no consent file is created at any path; AT-116). If `state` is `off` and `--consent-state-file` is not configured, `200` `{ "state": "off", "timestamp": null }` (a no-op). |

Any other path, with a valid token, returns `404` `unknown_path` with the error object. `POST /v1/consent` is UI-token only; v1 has no agent token (DD-41).

**Error catalogue (DD-83, DD-119).** Every error body is `{ "error": "<code>", "message": "<text>" }` and may include extra fields named in this table. JSON that is syntactically valid but has the wrong field types is `400` `invalid_param`, not `malformed_json`. For **every** route, the host evaluates errors in this order: authentication, then `405` `method_not_allowed` (known path, method not in the table), then `413` / `415`, then `409` `viewer_only` (Scholar route on a viewer-only host), then JSON / param errors (`malformed_json`, `missing_param`, `invalid_param`), then route-specific errors. Exception: a header block over `--max-request-bytes` is `413` `request_too_large` **before** authentication (the request is not parsed further); body overflow is `413` after authentication (AT-162, AT-171). A JSON `null` for an optional field equals omission of that field. A JSON `null` for a required field is `400` `missing_param`. After auth, a valid token on a path not in the table is `404` `unknown_path` (that path has no method table, so `405` does not apply). For `/v1/frame` and `/v1/hit-test` the route-specific tail is: `invalid_view`, then `unknown_handle`, then the §5.2.6 matrix (`view_mismatch` / `not_archive` / `unsupported`), then `invalid_floor`, then `invalid_frame_size`, then `no_frame` (DD-125, AT-196). When `view` is not in the enum, `invalid_view` is returned before any other query or body field check, including a missing or mistyped `handle`, `w`, `h`, `floor`, or `t` (an unknown `view` is never `missing_param`, `invalid_param`, or `unknown_handle` merely because those fields are present, omitted, or mistyped). This is still after authentication, `405`, `413` / `415`, `viewer_only`, and (for a POST body) `malformed_json`. A **well-typed** optional query or body field that a known view does not use (`t`, `scroll_x`, `instant`, and the like) is ignored; a malformed one is `400` `invalid_param` (DD-69, AT-171). For `/v1/open`, the route-specific tail after JSON / param errors is: `path_escape`, then `not_found`, then `unreadable`, then `not_regular_file`, then `file_too_large`, then `invalid_magic`, then `too_many_handles` (a missing path does not consume a handle slot).

| HTTP | `error` code | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 400 | `malformed_json` | Request body is not a JSON object (truncated, not JSON, or a JSON array/scalar). |
| 400 | `missing_param` | A required query field (`w`, `h`, `handle`, `view`, `floor`, `t`) or a required JSON body field (`path`, `paper_id`, `handle`, `state`, `x`, `y`, `view`) is absent, or is present as JSON `null`. Omitted `floor` (where required) is this code, not `invalid_floor`. A JSON `null` for an optional field equals omission and is not this error. |
| 400 | `invalid_param` | A present field has the wrong type or is out of range (non-integer or negative `floor`, `w`, or `h`; non-numeric or non-finite `t`, `x`, or `y`; an unknown enum value for consent `state`; a non-absolute, empty, or NUL-containing `/v1/open` `path`; wrong JSON types). `t` is a finite decimal number; a finite value outside `[0, 0.45]` is clamped, not this error (§5.8, AT-171). Supplying `floor` on a non-floor view (`manifest`, `header`, `banner`, `page`, `warehouse`) is this error, for both `/v1/frame` and `/v1/hit-test` (AT-196). |
| 400 | `invalid_view` | `view` is not in the enum above (AT-100). |
| 400 | `invalid_frame_size` | `w`, `h`, or `device_pixel_ratio` fails the cap / positivity rule. |
| 400 | `invalid_magic` | The path is under an allowed root but the bytes have S3A magic and a version or header that cannot be opened (also AT-69's `Unsupported file version: {n}` message). |
| 401 | `unauthenticated` | Missing or invalid token, including on unknown paths (auth first). |
| 403 | `unreadable` | `/v1/open` of a path under an allowed root that the process cannot read (EACCES). |
| 404 | `unknown_path` | Valid token, path not in the table. |
| 404 | `unknown_handle` | Handle not open (including after idle expiry or close). |
| 404 | `unknown_paper` | `paper_id` is not in the request-time candidate set. |
| 404 | `not_found` | `/v1/open` of a missing path under an allowed root (AT-30). |
| 405 | `method_not_allowed` | A known path with a method not in the table (for example `GET /v1/select`). |
| 409 | `viewer_only` | Scholar route on a viewer-only host. |
| 409 | `lrs_not_configured` | Consent `on` without `--lrs-url`. |
| 409 | `unsupported` | `proceed` (or a warehouse/floor view) on a `kind: "unsupported"` handle. |
| 409 | `not_archive` | `proceed` or a warehouse/floor view on a page / other handle. |
| 409 | `no_frame` | `hit_test` with no prior frame for that handle and `(view, floor)`. |
| 409 | `invalid_floor` | `floor` is **present** but is not the tile index of a valid floor (DD-82). An omitted `floor` is `400` `missing_param`. |
| 409 | `addon_missing` | An existing working archive lacks `ADDON_EDU`. The check runs in the step-0/1 hold on **every** select/download, whether or not step 1 is skipped (not at startup) (AT-46). Nothing is written; the body has no `lrs_status`. For `/v1/select` and `/v1/download`, this is evaluated after `unknown_paper` and before any inclusion or snapshot write. |
| 409 | `download_conflict` | A file exists at the download path whose contents do not hash to the name (AT-94). Body includes `included: true` whenever an inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1 (written on this request or earlier), and `lrs_status` when step 3 ran or was skipped (F-20). |
| 409 | `snapshot_conflict` | A **newly fetched** capture's `{sha}.s3a` already exists in `--snapshot-dir` and fails the DD-103 reuse checks (AT-180). This is not used for a sha already **recorded** in the inclusion index: that failure is HTTP 200 with `snapshot_error: "snapshot_invalid"` (and `download_error: "snapshot_invalid"` on a download) (step 0, AT-209). Body includes `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1 (written on this request or earlier), and `lrs_status` when step 3 ran or was skipped. The file is not overwritten. |
| 409 | `path_escape` | A request path or `Link` follow-up canonicalizes outside allowed roots (AT-52, AT-53). An escaping `drive_path` is not this error (AT-150). |
| 409 | `view_mismatch` | `view` is in the enum but is not valid for this handle's state (for example `warehouse` before proceed, or `page` on an archive). Applies to `/v1/frame` and `/v1/hit-test`. |
| 409 | `not_regular_file` | `/v1/open` of a directory, FIFO, device, or a basename matching `s3a-kite-tmp-*` directly inside `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir` (checked after the walk and before the read; AT-171). A user file with that basename under `--view-root`, `--wiki-root`, or `--corpus-root` is not this error. |
| 409 | `file_too_large` | Open bytes exceed `--max-open-bytes` / `max_open_bytes`. |
| 413 | `request_too_large` | The inbound HTTP request header block, or the request body, exceeds `--max-request-bytes`. A header-block excess is returned even without a valid token (the request is not parsed further). |
| 415 | `unsupported_media_type` | A JSON route whose `Content-Type` is present and is not `application/json`, or is missing on a route that requires a JSON body. The media type is compared case-insensitively and parameters are ignored, so `application/json; charset=utf-8` and `Application/JSON` are accepted as `application/json` (AT-171). |
| 429 | `too_many_handles` | Open would exceed `--max-open-handles`. |
| 500 | `write_failed` | Inclusion or snapshot write failed after the preconditions were checked (AT-89), a step-1 index-append failed after the tile-10 write (AT-158; body includes `included: true` and no `lrs_status`), a step-2 / step-2b disk publish or index-append failed after a successful step 1 (AT-212, AT-213, AT-214; body includes `included: true` and `lrs_status`), or a running Scholar host cannot open the working-archive path on select because it is missing or its identity changed (AT-74). The host does not create the archive mid-process. |
| 500 | `lock_failed` | Exclusive lock on the working archive could not be taken (AT-73, AT-89). |
| 500 | `revoke_delete_failed` | `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` could not be deleted on revoke. This is the last response the process may write; it then exits non-zero (DD-138, AT-124). Not used as an in-process degraded state. |
| 500 | `consent_write_failed` | The consent file or `revoke-pending` could not be written (AT-169, AT-189). This is the last response the process may write; it then stops all LRS sending and exits non-zero (DD-138). Not used as an in-process degraded state. |
| 500 | `render_failed` | `KiteError` during rendering. Body includes `kite_code` from `KiteError`. v1 specifies no reachable, deterministic trigger for this code on a correctly configured host; it is not claimed by an AT (it is untestable on purpose). |
| 500 | `internal` | Any other unexpected host failure. Body is still the error object; no secrets or identifiers. |

**Handles (DD-74, DD-92).** `--max-open-handles` is required in both modes and must be an integer ≥ 1. `--handle-idle-secs` is required in both modes and must be an integer ≥ 1. A handle with no `/v1/frame`, `/v1/hit-test`, `/v1/proceed`, or `/v1/close` for that many seconds is closed; the next use is `404` `unknown_handle` (AT-149). The same flag also bounds time from TCP accept, including the TLS handshake, header-read, and inter-byte idle time, on every accepted connection, including unauthenticated peers, as in the §5.2.2 inventory (AT-230). On a non-loopback bind, concurrent connections are capped at 8 (DD-153, AT-231). A further `/v1/open` that would exceed the cap returns `429` `too_many_handles` and mints no handle. `POST /v1/close` releases a handle. On process exit, all handles are dropped. `/v1/frame`, `/v1/hit-test`, `/v1/proceed`, and `/v1/close` on one handle are serialized by the same per-handle lock. The last-frame cache, and `hit_test`'s lookup into it, are keyed by `(handle, view, floor)` everywhere, with first-view aliases normalized to `first` (DD-125, AT-196). If no such frame exists, `hit_test` returns `409` `no_frame`.

**Write mutex (DD-102, DD-112).** Select, download, and consent are serialized by one process-wide mutex that is held **only for the local disk I/O and consent-state access listed here** (step 0 lookup, step 1, snapshot publish, download delivery, consent-file write, `revoke-pending` write, retry-queue mutation, in-flight registry insert/remove, the working-archive read-copy for `/v1/open`, and consent GET/POST); it is **never** held across network I/O (the source fetch or the LRS POST). The numbered-step list in §5.5 names which sub-steps take the mutex; the fetch in step 2 does not. Skip writes nothing and does **not** take the mutex. Candidate-set membership is computed **before** the mutex is taken. The lock-free `lrs_inhibit` latch (DD-144) is not this mutex.

An **in-flight fetch registry** keyed by `(file_uuid, identity)` (not verb; DD-112) prevents a second GET while a fetch for that key is running. A concurrent download waits for the in-flight result and does not start another fetch in the same request. Select does not fetch and does not enter this registry. A request inserts a registry entry only after step 1 has succeeded or been skipped, and only if this request will start the GET. The entry is removed in the **same mutex hold** as the snapshot publish and index append, or as the fetch-failure, `snapshot_conflict`, or step-2 / step-2b disk-write-failure (`write_failed`) outcome. A publish or index-append failure always removes the entry in that hold, so a later waiter cannot hang (AT-212). If step 1 fails, this request must not leave a registry entry (and must not have inserted one) (AT-89). Once a `POST /v1/download` has passed candidate membership (`unknown_paper` has been ruled out), its handler **runs to completion** of steps 0–3, including registry insertion and removal, even if the client disconnects (the handler is not cancelled by a dropped connection; the same rule as revoke, AT-198). Step 1, if it runs, completes (the tile-10 write and the index append, or the defined `500` `write_failed` if the index append fails; AT-158). Step 3, if it is not skipped, still runs, so a consented LRS statement is sent or queued and is not lost because a later request would skip step 3. The in-flight registry entry is always removed in the completion hold. Waiters are woken with the starter's defined outcome and do not hang. A later download of the same key proceeds normally (AT-221). If the process exits fail-closed (DD-138) before that hold, the process is gone (waiters' connections are closed; a new process has no registry).

A waiter receives the starter's outcome (§5.5 step 2). Two concurrent selects of the same identity therefore produce one tile-10 record, at most one snapshot, and **exactly one** source GET (AT-126). Snapshot files are written to a temp file in `--snapshot-dir` and published with an atomic rename.
