**Process exclusivity and archive write lock (DD-114).** These are two different locks:

1.  **Process-lifetime sidecar `flock`s** (host versus host): at Scholar start the host takes a non-blocking exclusive `flock` (`LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`) on `--data-dir/.lock`, a non-blocking exclusive `flock` on `--working-archive.lock` in the archive's parent directory, a non-blocking exclusive `flock` on `--snapshot-dir/.lock`, and a non-blocking exclusive `flock` on `--download-dir/.lock`. When `--consent-state-file` is configured, the host also takes a non-blocking exclusive `flock` on `--consent-state-file.lock` in that file's parent directory. Each of those sidecar lock files is opened (or created if missing) with `openat(dirfd, name, O_CREAT|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK|O_CLOEXEC, 0600)` from that directory's startup dirfd; `fstat` must show a regular file, or startup is an error that names the path, binds nothing, and mutates none of those paths (AT-208). A FIFO, symlink, or other non-regular file at a sidecar lock name therefore fails startup and does not hang. All of these flocks are held until process exit. A blocking `flock` is not used (AT-164). Startup runs in this order, and this is the only exception to "locks first": **(1)** canonicalize and `lstat` every configured path and run the DD-93 / DD-129 / reserved-name / pairwise-distinct checks, with **no creation** (no `.lock` file is created, and no other path is created or truncated); **(2)** take the sidecar locks (each lock file is created mode `0600` if missing); **(3)** any other read, cleanup, or bootstrap. Canonicalization and the step-(1) checks may `lstat` those paths. All startup cleanup, bootstrap (empty inclusion index, consent-file create), `revoke-pending` retry or delete, `lrs-retry/` delete or leftover `{id}.claimed` rename, and temp cleanup run only after the locks are held (step 3). A second `s3a-kite host` whose `--data-dir`, `--working-archive`, `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, or (when configured) `--consent-state-file` canonicalizes to the same path is a startup error that names the flag, binds nothing, and **mutates none of those paths** (AT-164, AT-208). Two hosts therefore cannot share one consent file and race its temp+rename. AT-46 uses a distinct `--data-dir`, a distinct `--working-archive`, a distinct `--snapshot-dir`, and a distinct `--download-dir`.
2.  **Engine `try_lock_exclusive` on the working-archive file** (host versus any other `TileWriter`, including s3a-cli): taken for each tile-10 write (step 1) once the file exists, and on `TileWriter::create`. This is not the sidecar lock. If an external process holds this lock, step 1 returns `500` `lock_failed` and does not truncate the file (AT-73). A second kite host never reaches this case (AT-164).

`/v1/open` of the working archive copies the file under the process-wide write mutex, so the copy cannot tear against a concurrent tile-10 append.

**Consent transitions and in-flight LRS (DD-102, DD-113, DD-138, DD-142, DD-144).** Consent `GET` and `POST` are serialized by the write mutex. The in-memory consent flag is **read and written only while holding that mutex**. Separately, a lock-free **`lrs_inhibit` latch** (DD-144) is not that flag. The latch is a pair of monotonic epoch counters, `epoch` and `cleared_epoch`, initially equal, plus a `revokes_in_flight` counter, initially 0. Inhibit is active iff `epoch != cleared_epoch`. `epoch` only increases; it is never reset. `off` always wins against a concurrent `on`: it is final until a later explicit `POST {"state":"on"}` that **starts after** the revoke has completed (the revoke has released the mutex after persisting, or the process has exited). An `on` records `observed = epoch` and `observed_revokes = revokes_in_flight` when it is received. A same-state `POST {"state":"on"}` (the in-memory flag is already `on` when the request is applied under the mutex) is a no-op: it does not write the consent file, does not change the timestamp, and **never** touches `cleared_epoch` (F-16 of v15; AT-198). A non-no-op `on` qualifies only if `observed_revokes` was 0, `revokes_in_flight` is still 0 at the CAS, and `epoch` still equals `observed`. If it does not qualify, it must not set the flag to `on` and must not write the consent file `on`; it returns the `off` state and writes nothing (AT-198). A qualifying `on` that will persist `on` first unlinks a leftover `revoke-pending` if that path is present as a regular file, `fsync`s the parent directory, and only then writes the consent file `on`. Failure of that unlink or parent `fsync` is treated as a DD-138 fail-closed event: the host does not write the consent file `on`, stops LRS, and exits non-zero (AT-229). Hook point `fail_revoke_pending_remove` also applies to this runtime unlink.

Receipt-time latch actions run **only after** a `POST /v1/consent` has passed every check in the §5.2.1 error-precedence list (authentication through `invalid_param`) and the body is a valid `{"state":"off"}` on a Scholar host (including a same-state `off`, and including `POST off` when `--consent-state-file` is not configured). An unauthenticated, malformed, `viewer_only`, or otherwise rejected request does not increment `epoch` or `revokes_in_flight` and does not cancel in-flight POSTs (AT-198). After those checks, and before the mutex is taken, the host increments `epoch`, increments `revokes_in_flight`, cancels every in-flight LRS POST through the current cancellation token, and swaps in a fresh cancellation token. Until a later qualifying `on` persists, the host inhibits all LRS: no new send; in-flight LRS POSTs are aborted and those requests report `lrs_status: "skipped"`. A qualifying `on` clears inhibit only by a single compare-and-swap of `cleared_epoch` to `observed`, and only if `revokes_in_flight` is 0 and `epoch` still equals `observed`; if the CAS fails or `revokes_in_flight` is not 0, the `on` returns `off` and writes nothing. Every LRS sender (step 3, retry-file write, startup replay) must, in this order: (1) register the attempt with the current cancellation token, (2) re-check that inhibit is inactive (`epoch == cleared_epoch`) and the in-memory flag is `on`, (3) only then start the HTTP POST. If inhibit is active at the re-check, the sender starts no POST, writes no new retry file, and reports `skipped`. Under the mutex a revoke **always** cancels every in-flight LRS POST through the current token again and swaps in a fresh token (so a POST started after the receipt-time swap is still aborted), even when the `off` is a same-state no-op. If the in-memory flag is already `off`, the revoke performs **no persistent writes** (it does not write `revoke-pending`, does not rewrite the consent file, does not delete `lrs-retry/`, and does not change the timestamp). If the flag is `on`, the revoke sets the flag to `off`, then persists, in this order, each persistent file write being a temp file in the parent directory named `s3a-kite-tmp-{pid}-*`, `fsync` of the temp, atomic rename, then `fsync` of the **parent directory**, mode `0600`. After the `lrs-retry/` delete or the `revoke-pending` unlink, the host also `fsync`s that parent directory. These parent-directory `fsync`s complete before the response is written:

1.  Write `--data-dir/revoke-pending` (contents `off` and an RFC3339 timestamp). `revoke-pending` is **present** only when that path is a regular file (a directory or other file type at that name is not "present" and is a startup error that names the path).
2.  Write the consent file to `off`.
3.  Delete `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` (including `.json`, `.claimed`, and `.permanent` files).
4.  Remove `revoke-pending`. If this unlink fails at runtime after steps 1–3 succeeded, the host logs the failure (identifier-free), returns 200 for the revoke, and leaves the file in place (AT-229). A later qualifying `on` in this process must remove that leftover before persisting `on`, as specified above. If the process exits with the leftover still present and no later qualifying `on` has removed it, the next start handles it as in AT-181. The failed step-4 unlink itself is not a DD-138 fail-closed event.

Every revoke decrements `revokes_in_flight` at the **end of its mutex hold**, whether or not it persisted, including a same-state no-op `off` and a `POST off` on a host without `--consent-state-file`. Once receipt-time latch actions have run, the revoke **runs to completion of that hold**, decrement included, even if the client disconnects mid-hold (the handler is not cancelled by a dropped connection) (AT-198). Only a fail-closed exit (DD-138) skips the decrement (the process is gone). A fail-closed exit also increments `epoch` (DD-144).

**Fail closed (DD-138, Ashley, 2026-11-16).** Any failure to write the consent file, to write `revoke-pending`, or to delete `lrs-retry/` on revoke (or any other consent-file write failure, including a failed `POST` `{ "state": "on" }`) stops all LRS sending immediately. The host logs the failure (identifier-free), may write one last `500` `consent_write_failed` or `500` `revoke_delete_failed` for the request that hit the failure, does not accept any further request, and exits non-zero. After the failing hold releases the mutex, in-flight requests receive **no further disk writes** (a pending step-1 inclusion that has not yet taken the mutex must not commit). The host may write `500` `internal` to those in-flight requests if it can do so without further disk I/O; otherwise it closes their connections without a response. Leftover snapshot or download temps are removed by the next start's DD-122 cleanup. There is no in-process recovery or degraded mode: no "refuse LRS for the life of the process" latch, no later `POST on` / `GET` in the same process, and no `409` `revoke_pending`.

If the `revoke-pending` write fails, the host still attempts (best-effort, same mutex hold) the consent-file `off` write and the `lrs-retry/` delete, then exits (AT-189). If the consent-file write fails after `revoke-pending` was written, the host leaves `revoke-pending` in place and exits (AT-169). If queue deletion fails after those writes, the host leaves `revoke-pending` in place and exits (AT-124).

On the next start, if `revoke-pending` is present (a regular file), the host (after taking the sidecar locks, DD-114) treats consent as `off`, increments `epoch` (inhibit becomes active), retries the consent-file write and queue deletion, then **removes `revoke-pending`**; it does not send LRS (AT-181). Failure to remove `revoke-pending` after those retries succeed is a startup error that names `revoke-pending`, binds nothing, and leaves the file in place. A failed consent-file write at that startup is a startup error that names `--consent-state-file`, binds nothing, and leaves `revoke-pending` in place. If queue deletion fails at startup while consent is `off` (including via `revoke-pending`), startup is an error that names `lrs-retry`, and the host binds nothing. If neither `revoke-pending` nor the consent file could be written before the previous exit, the next start may read `on` (the revoke was not durable); that case is observable in the prior process's log.

A Scholar start **without** `--lrs-url` (so `--consent-state-file` is forbidden) takes the sidecar locks first, then deletes `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` and `--data-dir/revoke-pending` if either is present, then continues with tracing unconfigured. Failure to delete either is a startup error that names the path (`lrs-retry` or `revoke-pending`) and binds nothing (AT-199).

Step 3 of select/download, and every retry or replay send, follows the register / re-check / send order in the consent-transitions paragraph (DD-144). If inhibit is active or the flag is `off` at the re-check, no request is sent and no new retry file is written (`lrs_status` is `skipped` when a statement would otherwise have been attempted). Incrementing `epoch` on revoke cancels every in-flight LRS POST via the then-current token (AT-156, including a revoke during a slow LRS POST); the aborted request reports `lrs_status: "skipped"`. An aborted or skipped **replay** leaves its `{id}.claimed` file in place for a revoke delete and performs no rename; if `lrs-retry/` is already gone, it does nothing and must not recreate that directory. `--lrs-timeout-secs` (required when `--lrs-url` is set, integer >= 1) bounds each LRS attempt. The LRS response header block and the LRS response body are each bounded by `--max-open-bytes`. If a complete status line has already been read, an oversized remainder follows the DD-134 status class (2xx or 409 is `sent`; 408, 429, and 5xx are queued; any other 4xx is permanent). If the header block exceeds the cap before a status line is read, the attempt is a permanent failure (`{id}.permanent` written, `lrs_status: "failed"`). The host does not read further in either case (AT-191). `--fetch-timeout-secs` bounds the **entire** source-fetch redirect chain, wall-clock from the first connect to the final body.

Opening the Scholar working-archive path via `/v1/open` uses the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule **under the process-wide write mutex** (so the copy cannot tear against a concurrent tile-10 append): the host opens via the startup parent dirfd, applies the cap and `(dev, ino)` check, and calls pack `open` on those bytes (not `open_path`), so `paper_identity` is `None` (DD-78, DD-146, AT-217).

`kind: "unsupported"` and `first_view: "banner"` are used for an unsupported stride (AT-97) or an inconsistent Edge header (AT-66). `GET /v1/frame?view=banner` (or `view=manifest`) returns the banner text in the text layer. `proceed` on those handles returns `409` `unsupported`.

#### 5.2.2 Modes, flags, and paths

**CLI (DD-95).** The host is started as `s3a-kite host` followed by the flags below. Tests (including AT-04) use that form. There is no other v1 subcommand.

**Flag inventory (DD-106).** Every host flag is in this table. "Required" means a startup error that names the flag if it is missing or invalid. "Forbidden" means a startup error that names the flag if it is passed. Integer flags have no silent default. Test-hook configuration is **not** a flag: it uses the `test_hooks` Cargo feature and environment variables in §5.2.7 / DD-151, the same kind of non-flag test surface as `egress_test`.

| Flag | Type / range | Viewer | Scholar | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `--viewer` | switch | sets the mode | must not be passed | |
| `--bind` | any IP literal (including loopback, `0.0.0.0`, `::`, and a specific interface address such as `192.0.2.10`) or `unix:/absolute/path.sock` | required | required | |
| `--port` | integer 1–65535 | required unless `--bind` starts with `unix:` (then forbidden) | same | |
| `--auth-token-file` | existing file, euid+0600, charset in the token bullet | required | required | |
| `--view-root` | existing directory | required | optional; if set, also an allowed open root | |
| `--log-dir` | existing directory | required | required | |
| `--max-open-handles` | integer >= 1 | required | required | |
| `--max-frame-pixels` | integer >= 1 | required | required | |
| `--handle-idle-secs` | integer >= 1 | required | required | Also bounds time from TCP accept on every connection, including an unauthenticated one, on every bind including a non-loopback TLS bind: the TLS handshake, the time to send the request header block, and idle time between request bytes. The connection is then closed without a response (AT-230). On a non-loopback bind (not a loopback IP and not a Unix socket), concurrent connections are capped at a fixed 8 (DD-153, AT-231); this is not a flag. |
| `--max-request-bytes` | integer >= 1 | required | required | Caps the inbound HTTP request header block and, separately, the request body; either excess is `413` `request_too_large` |
| `--max-open-bytes` | integer >= 1 | required | required | Cap on bytes read by `/v1/open` / pack `open_path` from the host-validated fd (over = `409` `file_too_large`); also caps each of the LRS response header block and LRS response body |
| `--headless` | switch | required unless `--bind` is loopback or `unix:`; required in builds without cargo feature `native-window` | same | |
| `--wiki-root` | existing directory | forbidden | required | Scholar-only (AT-132) |
| `--corpus-root` | existing directory | forbidden | required | Scholar-only |
| `--working-archive` | file path; parent must exist | forbidden | required | Scholar-only |
| `--snapshot-dir` | existing directory | forbidden | required | Scholar-only |
| `--download-dir` | existing directory | forbidden | required | Scholar-only |
| `--data-dir` | existing directory | forbidden | required | Scholar-only |
| `--researcher-id` | non-empty string | forbidden | required | Scholar-only; never logged. In lab mode this is the user's own username or email. In study mode (`--study-id` set) this is a participant code such as `P-017`, never a name or email (DD-155). |
| `--fetch-timeout-secs` | integer >= 1 | forbidden | required | Scholar-only; bounds the whole redirect chain |
| `--max-fetch-bytes` | integer >= 1 | forbidden | required | Scholar-only; decoded body, and separately the fetch response header block |
| `--max-redirects` | integer >= 0 | forbidden | required | Scholar-only |
| `--lrs-url` | `scheme://host[:port]` with an optional path of `/xapi` and an optional single trailing `/` stripped; `https`, or `http` on a loopback IP literal (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, or the IPv4-mapped form of `127.0.0.0/8`) | forbidden | optional | Scholar-only. An `https` hostname that fails to resolve at start is a startup error that names this flag. If set, `--consent-state-file`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, and `--lrs-timeout-secs` are required. `--lrs-actor-homepage` is forbidden (DD-157). A hostname (including `localhost`) with `http`, or `http` on any other address, is a startup error that names `--lrs-url`. The only allowed path is empty, `/`, `/xapi`, or `/xapi/`; any other path, or any userinfo, query, or fragment, is a startup error that names `--lrs-url`. The lab endpoint `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/xapi/` is a valid value (Q-02, DD-65). |
| `--consent-state-file` | path | forbidden | required iff `--lrs-url` is set; forbidden if `--lrs-url` is absent | Scholar-only. Missing file is created as `off` (DD-96) |
| `--lrs-credentials-file` | existing file, euid+0600 | forbidden | required iff `--lrs-url` is set | Scholar-only |
| `--study-id` | non-empty string; when set, Scholar study mode | forbidden | optional | Scholar-only. When omitted, the host is in lab mode. When set, `--researcher-id` must be a participant code, not a name or email (DD-155). |
| `--lrs-actor-homepage` | (removed) | forbidden | forbidden | Removed in v24.1 (Q-13, DD-157). If the flag is passed, startup exits non-zero and names the removed flag. The host builds the actor homepage itself. |
| `--lrs-timeout-secs` | integer >= 1 | forbidden | required iff `--lrs-url` is set | Scholar-only; bounds each LRS attempt |
| `--pinned-keys-file` | existing file, euid+0600, hex keys | optional | optional | Empty/omitted list: at best Self-signed |
| `--tls-cert` | existing file (may be world-readable) | required with `--tls-key` when `--bind` is non-loopback and not `unix:` | same | |
| `--tls-key` | existing file, euid+0600 | as `--tls-cert` | as `--tls-cert` | TLS 1.2 or newer |
| `--behind-proxy` | switch | accepted only with loopback or `unix:` bind | same | With this flag, `/health` requires the token |
| `--egress-allowlist` | comma-separated exact host[:port] | forbidden | optional | Scholar-only; never overrides the deny list |
| `--egress-test-loopback-port` | integer 1–65535 | forbidden | optional; startup error unless the binary was built with Cargo feature `egress_test` | Scholar-only |

**Scholar mode** is the absence of `--viewer`. **Viewer-only mode** is the presence of `--viewer`. There is no separate Scholar switch.

Select, download, skip, and consent routes return `409` `viewer_only` in viewer-only mode. In viewer-only mode, request paths are opened only under `--view-root`. In Scholar mode the allowed request roots are those in DD-89.

**Startup error precedence (DD-127).** When more than one required flag is missing or invalid, the host exits non-zero, binds nothing, and names **one** of the failing flags (no prescribed order). An AT that asserts the name of a particular flag must supply every other flag valid for that mode, so the named flag is the only error (AT-51, AT-125).

`--working-archive` names a file path. At startup the host canonicalizes its **parent directory** (the parent must exist) and `lstat`s the final component. The file itself may be missing. If it exists, it must be a regular file (not a symlink, directory, or other type), or startup is an error that names `--working-archive` (AT-203). The host creates and opens the file via that canonical parent dirfd with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` (it does not follow a replaced final component). If the file existed at startup, the host records its `(dev, ino)` from that `lstat`. The host does **not** keep a startup-lifetime working-archive fd across requests. In **every** step-0/1 hold it re-opens the final component from the startup parent dirfd with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK)` when the file exists. Create uses `openat(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW)` **only at startup** if the file was missing; mid-process, a missing final component is `500` `write_failed` and the host does not create a replacement archive until the next start. A mid-process file whose `(dev, ino)` differs from the recorded identity (for example a delete and `O_CREAT` by some other path) is also `500` `write_failed` until restart; the host does not re-record the identity mid-process. The writer then uses that hold's already-validated fd: an fd-taking `TileWriter` constructor in `s3a-engine` (this does not touch `s3a-core`), or the Linux `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` magic link, which reopens the same inode without re-resolving the name. That `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` option is for the working-archive hold only, which has no host `LOCK_EX` on that fd. Snapshot and download temps must not use it (they are sealed on the same file description that holds `LOCK_EX`; §5.6). On first create at startup the host calls `enable_addon(ADDON_EDU)` and writes the create-time research-purpose `ConsentRecord` (AT-79, §5.6) and then records the created file's `(dev, ino)`. If that startup create fails (the parent is not writable, `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` fails, `enable_addon` fails, or the create-time `ConsentRecord` write fails), startup is an error that names `--working-archive` and binds nothing (AT-78). After the writer is attached and **immediately before each tile-10 write**, the host performs a **name-binding re-check**: `fstatat(parent_dirfd, basename, AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` must succeed, must describe a regular file, and its `(dev, ino)` must equal both `fstat` of the writer's open fd and the recorded identity. A failure (including a mid-request swap of the final component for a symlink into `--corpus-root`) is `500` `write_failed`; the host writes nothing and does not follow, append to, or truncate the symlink target (AT-203, AT-219). A swap **after** that final check can still write to the already-open inode (now possibly unlinked or renamed); it never follows the new name or writes the symlink target (DD-146 residual). The header read for the current `file_uuid` and `addon_mask` in the same hold uses this hold's validated fd, not a by-name reopen (AT-46). If that header read fails because the existing file is not a readable S3A archive or `FileHeader::verify` fails, the request is `500` `write_failed`, nothing is written, and there is no `lrs_status` (AT-74). Startup does not require a pre-existing working archive to be a valid S3A file (the `ADDON_EDU` bit is still not checked at startup). `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, `--log-dir`, `--data-dir`, `--wiki-root`, `--corpus-root`, and `--view-root` must exist as directories at startup (the host does not create them). Each existing directory is canonicalized with `realpath`. A missing or non-canonicalizable required path is a startup error that names the flag.

**Disjoint write and read locations (DD-93, DD-129, AT-145, AT-179).** After canonicalization, `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, `--data-dir`, `--log-dir`, and the working-archive **parent** must be pairwise-disjoint from `--wiki-root`, `--corpus-root`, and `--view-root` (no directory is equal to, or an ancestor directory of, another in the other set: a **path-component prefix** after canonicalization, so `/x/a` and `/x/ab` are disjoint). `--data-dir` and `--log-dir` must also be pairwise-disjoint from `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir`. `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir` must be pairwise-disjoint from each other (neither equal to nor an ancestor directory of the other). `--wiki-root` and `--corpus-root` must be pairwise-disjoint from each other (neither equal to nor an ancestor directory of the other; this keeps `paper_identity` well-defined). When `--view-root` is set in Scholar mode, it must also be pairwise-disjoint from `--wiki-root` and from `--corpus-root` (neither equal to nor an ancestor directory of the other), so a request path lies under at most one of those roots and `validated_root` is unambiguous (AT-179). The working-archive parent must also be pairwise-disjoint from `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, and `--log-dir`. A violation is a startup error that names both flags (AT-179). This keeps the corpus read-only, stops the recursive `*.s3a` walk from picking up snapshots, and keeps identities and logs out of request roots. Viewer-only mode applies the same disjointness between `--log-dir`, `--pinned-keys-file`, the token file, and any TLS files (`--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`) versus `--view-root`. Secret and state files must not sit inside a request root (DD-129).

**Reserved `--data-dir` names (AT-208).** The host reserves `--data-dir/.lock`, `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl`, `--data-dir/revoke-pending`, `--data-dir/lrs-retry/`, and the sidecar lock files `--working-archive.lock` (in the archive parent), `--snapshot-dir/.lock`, `--download-dir/.lock`, and `--consent-state-file.lock` (in the consent file's parent, when `--consent-state-file` is configured). If the canonical path of `--consent-state-file` or `--working-archive` is equal to, or inside, a reserved name, startup is an error that names both flags, binds nothing, and mutates none of those paths (AT-208). The basename of `--working-archive`, and of `--consent-state-file` when configured, must not match `s3a-kite-tmp-*`; a violation is a startup error that names the flag, binds nothing, and mutates none of those paths (AT-208). The canonical paths of `--working-archive`, `--working-archive.lock`, `--consent-state-file`, `--consent-state-file.lock` (when configured), `--auth-token-file`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`, `--data-dir/.lock`, `--snapshot-dir/.lock`, and `--download-dir/.lock` (each, when that flag or path is set) must also be **pairwise distinct**. A violation is a startup error that names both flags, binds nothing, and mutates none of those paths (AT-208). These reserved-name and pairwise-distinct checks run in startup step (1) of §5.2.1, before any sidecar lock file is created. In Scholar mode, if `--max-fetch-bytes` is greater than `--max-open-bytes`, startup is an error that names both flags, binds nothing, and creates no lock file (AT-223).

**Shared safe-open rule (DD-146).** Every host open of an existing artifact at these sites uses the same discipline: `openat` from that directory's startup dirfd, final component `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, `fstat` requires a regular file, and at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes are read. The host never follows a symlink and never blocks on a FIFO, device, or other non-regular file. The sites are: a `drive_path` used as an on-disk source; a snapshot-reuse open of `--snapshot-dir/{sha}.s3a`; a download-dir existing-file hash check of `--download-dir/{sha}`; a step-2b snapshot-reassembly open; the working-archive `/v1/open` copy; a candidate item-1 dossier front-matter read; a candidate item-2 corpus `*.s3a` open; startup cleanup opens of stale `s3a-kite-tmp-*` names in `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir`; sidecar lock opens of `--data-dir/.lock`, `--working-archive.lock`, `--snapshot-dir/.lock`, `--download-dir/.lock`, and `--consent-state-file.lock` (when configured), which also use `O_CREAT` (mode `0600`) as specified in §5.2.1; and host opens of the config and state files listed in this subsection (`--auth-token-file`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--consent-state-file`, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`, and `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl`). Walks under a request root use that root's startup dirfd and directory fds for descent (`openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK)` at each component). Site-specific errors:
- `drive_path`: the path is not a usable on-disk source (`on_disk: false`); the request is not `409` `path_escape` and does not hang the mutex (AT-197, AT-202).
- Candidate item-1 dossier front-matter read and item-2 corpus `*.s3a` open: any failure (symlink, FIFO or other non-regular file, oversize, `ELOOP`, or a read that exceeds the cap) is a **silent skip** of that path (it is not a candidate; the walk does not hang; token or other secret bytes are not read) (AT-218).
- Startup temp cleanup of `s3a-kite-tmp-*` in `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir`: `lstat` / `openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK)`; only a regular file that can be `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`'d is deleted; a FIFO, symlink, or other non-regular file is **skipped** (startup does not hang and does not follow or lock the target) (AT-187).
- Config and state files (`--auth-token-file`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--consent-state-file`, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`): must be regular files, opened with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, and are bounded by `--max-open-bytes` (the same cap; no second numeric cap is invented). A missing required file, a non-regular file, a hang-avoiding open failure, or an oversize file is a startup error that names the flag or path and binds nothing. `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl` is opened under the same safe-open discipline (regular file, `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`) but is **not** bounded by `--max-open-bytes`: the host reads the whole well-formed file. No rotation bound is specified (none is invented). A missing index is created empty as already specified; a non-regular index, or a hang-avoiding open failure, is a startup error that names the path and binds nothing. A well-formed index larger than `--max-open-bytes` is not a startup error (AT-224).
- Snapshot reuse (symlink, FIFO or other non-regular file, oversize, or a failed verify/hash check): reuse fails as `409` `snapshot_conflict` on a newly fetched capture, or HTTP 200 `snapshot_invalid` when the sha is already recorded (AT-215). A concurrent `GET /v1/consent` is not blocked indefinitely (no hang on a FIFO or symlink); it may wait for the write mutex for a bounded copy or reuse check.
- Download-dir existing-file check: a symlink, FIFO, or other non-regular file, a regular file whose contents do not hash to the name, or a regular file larger than `--max-open-bytes` (the hash check reads at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes and cannot complete), is `409` `download_conflict` with `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion exists (AT-216, AT-223). A symlink is not followed.
- Working-archive `/v1/open` copy: the host opens via the startup parent dirfd under this rule, reads at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes (`409` `file_too_large` if more arrive), and re-checks `(dev, ino)` against the recorded startup or create identity (`409` `path_escape` on mismatch, including a swapped symlink). A FIFO, directory, or other non-regular file is `409` `not_regular_file`; `ELOOP` from a symlink is `409` `path_escape`. The host then calls pack `open(ctx, bytes, name_hint=basename, max_frame_pixels, max_open_bytes, pinned_keys)` (not `open_path`, because the working-archive parent is not a request root), so `paper_identity` is `None` (AT-217). A concurrent consent GET/POST is not blocked indefinitely (no hang on a FIFO or symlink); it may wait for the write mutex for the bounded copy.

**Path rule (DD-89, DD-146).** This rule applies to paths that come from **requests**: `/v1/open` `path`, a `Link` follow-up, and a candidate file. The JSON `path` is a plain absolute filesystem path, or a `file://` URL whose path is percent-decoded and then treated as that filesystem path; a `file://` that cannot be so converted is `409` `path_escape` (AT-53). A `path` that is not absolute (after that `file://` conversion, if any), is empty, or contains a NUL is `400` `invalid_param` (AT-171); it is not resolved against any root. After that conversion, those paths must end strictly inside an allowed request root, or be exactly the canonical working-archive path. If, after lexical normalization, the path equals the canonical working-archive path and that file is missing mid-process, `/v1/open` is `404` `not_found` (AT-53). The host does not create the archive on `/v1/open`. If the file exists, the host opens it under the shared safe-open rule via the startup parent dirfd (not from a request-root dirfd; the working-archive parent is disjoint from every request root), copies at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes under the write mutex, re-checks `(dev, ino)`, and calls pack `open` on those bytes as specified in the shared safe-open rule (AT-217). For every other request path the host first **lexically normalizes the absolute path** (resolving `.` and `..` only; no symlink resolution), then picks the **unique** allowed request root that is a path-component prefix of the normalized path. No match, or more than one match, is `409` `path_escape` (AT-52, AT-53). A `..` that crosses from one root into another (`/wiki/../corpus/x`) is therefore resolved in that absolute lexical step, then matched. Allowed roots are stored already canonicalized; `page_path` is canonical. A request path may contain `.` and `..` and is lexically normalized first. A non-canonical prefix of a root (for example `/tmp/...` when the stored root is `/private/tmp/...`) is `409` `path_escape`. The host then **opens first and validates the fd** (DD-146) under the chosen root: it opens the root's dirfd and walks each remaining component with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` (intermediates as directories; the final component with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`), staying beneath the allowed root, then `fstat`s the open fd and requires a regular file. The host does **not** consume that fd: it `fstat`s it (a regular file is required; if `st_size` is greater than `--max-open-bytes`, `409` `file_too_large`) and passes the fd to the pack. The pack performs the single bounded `pread` from offset 0, at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes; if more than `--max-open-bytes` arrive, that is also `409` `file_too_large`. A symlink at any component fails the open: `ELOOP` or `ENOTDIR` from a symlink is `409` `path_escape` for a request path; `ENOTDIR` from a **non-symlink** intermediate component (for example `{root}/dir/file.md/x` where `file.md` is a regular file) is `404` `not_found` (AT-171); `409` `not_regular_file` only if the final fd, once opened, is not a regular file. The host uses that fd; it does not reopen the path by name later and does not `read` or `lseek` it before the pack call. For `/v1/open` and a `Link` follow-up the host then calls pack `open_path` with that fd, the canonical path it proved, and the allowed root the walk started from; the pack `pread`s from offset 0 and does not reopen the path by name (AT-101, AT-202). A resolved `drive_path` is **not** a request path: if it is missing, cannot be canonicalized, escapes the source-table roots, or lands in `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir`, it is silently treated as no usable on-disk source (`on_disk: false`); the select or download is not `409` `path_escape` (AT-150). Allowed request roots are `--wiki-root`, `--corpus-root`, `--view-root`, and, in Scholar mode, `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir`. `..` segments, symlink escapes, and `file://` URLs outside those directories are `409` `path_escape`. Separately, the host may open its own config and state files: the token file, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--consent-state-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--log-dir` files, and `--data-dir` files (the inclusion index and `lrs-retry/`). Those paths are not required to sit inside a request root, and **must not** sit inside one: at startup, if the canonical path of `--auth-token-file`, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--consent-state-file`, `--data-dir`, or `--log-dir` is inside any allowed request root, startup is an error that names both flags and binds nothing (DD-129, AT-179). `--data-dir` and `--log-dir` must also be disjoint from `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir` after canonicalization (a violation is a startup error naming both flags). The working-archive parent must also be disjoint from `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, and `--log-dir`. In viewer-only mode the same rule applies to `--log-dir`, `--pinned-keys-file`, the token file, and any TLS files versus `--view-root`.

**Unix-socket bind (DD-72, DD-108).** `--bind unix:/absolute/path.sock` binds a Unix domain socket at that path. `--port` must not be passed (startup error if it is). The socket's **parent directory** must exist, be owned by the process euid, and must not be group- or world-writable; otherwise startup is an error that names the path (AT-174). The socket file is created with owner euid and mode `0600`. If a file already exists at that path (a stale socket or anything else), startup is an error that names the path; the host does not unlink it (AT-163). The token is still required on every route other than the tokenless health case.

**Health, tokens, TLS, and other secrets (DD-29, DD-52, DD-68, DD-73, DD-85, DD-108).**

*   `GET /health` success is exactly HTTP 200, `Content-Type: text/plain`, body `ok\n`.
*   The token file must be a single line of at least 32 characters from the set `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ -` (the unpadded base64url alphabet), optionally ending in one `\n` or `\r\n`. The host strips that single trailing newline at load; the secret is the remaining characters. Any other whitespace, any other byte, an empty file, or a secret shorter than 32 characters is a startup error that names the file (AT-45, AT-81). Comparison is constant-time on the UTF-8 secret. The Bearer value must be exactly that secret (no added newline).
*   On Unix, these existing files must be owned by the process's effective user and have mode `0600`: `--auth-token-file`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--tls-key`, and `--pinned-keys-file` when that flag is set. `--consent-state-file`, if it exists at startup, must also be `0600` and so owned. A `0644` (or otherwise group/world-readable) file, or a file owned by another user, is a startup error that names the file (AT-117, AT-142). `--tls-cert` may be world-readable. v1 does not specify a Windows host.
*   Files the host **creates** under `--data-dir` (the inclusion index, `lrs-retry/` entries) and the consent file after a host write (including first-run bootstrap) are created with mode `0600` and owner euid.
*   The host never writes secrets or identifiers to `--log-dir`, stdout, or stderr: UI token bytes, LRS passwords, incoming or outgoing `Authorization` header values (including Basic to the LRS), TLS private keys, paper identities, source URLs, or `--researcher-id` (AT-117, AT-142).
*   HTTPS to `--lrs-url` (and any other non-loopback https fetch) is validated against the **process platform / webpki system root store**. v1 does not pin LRS certificates (a later DD may add pinning). Loopback `http` LRS remains allowed for tests.
*   A non-loopback, non-Unix `--bind` (including `0.0.0.0`, `::`, and a specific interface IP) requires `--tls-cert` and `--tls-key`, and requires `--headless`. TLS is **1.2 or newer** (AT-163). Passing `--tls-cert` or `--tls-key` with a loopback or `unix:` bind is a startup error that names the flag (those binds never use TLS). `--behind-proxy` is accepted **only** with a loopback bind or a Unix-socket bind (AT-87). Non-loopback plus `--behind-proxy` is a startup error. With `--behind-proxy`, every route including `/health` requires the token.

**Logs and ephemeral temp (DD-88).** `--log-dir` receives append-only lines for startup (flag *names* only, never flag values that are paths to secrets or token contents), shutdown, and error messages that obey the no-identifier rule above. There is no access log of request bodies or paper ids. The ephemeral temp directory is created with `mkdtemp` and mode `0700` under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` if that variable names an existing directory, otherwise under `/tmp`, with a name prefix `s3a-kite-{pid}-`. It is deleted on a clean exit. Every ephemeral temp directory includes a flock'd marker. At startup the host deletes leftover `s3a-kite-*` directories under those locations that are owned by the same euid **and** whose marker exists and can be taken with an exclusive `flock`. It does **not** delete on a "pid not running" check alone (hosts in different PID namespaces may share `/tmp` or `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`). Names whose pid cannot be parsed are skipped and are not deleted. It must not delete a live temp belonging to another same-euid host (AT-187). The `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` else `/tmp` choice is an explicit contract exception (DD-88, F-29, DD-136).

#### 5.2.3 Consent file (DD-50, DD-96)

`--consent-state-file` is required when `--lrs-url` is set, and is **forbidden** when `--lrs-url` is absent (startup error that names the flag, AT-114, F-30). When `--lrs-url` is set and the consent file is **missing**, the host **creates** it as UTF-8 `off`, a newline, and an RFC3339-seconds-UTC timestamp of the create, mode `0600`, owner euid (AT-151). If the file is missing at the DD-129 check, its parent directory must exist and is canonicalized; the canonical parent joined with the basename is the path checked against the request roots (AT-179). `off` is the privacy-safe state; this is not an invented `on` default. A present but malformed file is a startup error that names the file. `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "on" }` without `--lrs-url` is **reachable at runtime** and returns `409` `lrs_not_configured`; no consent file is created at any path (AT-116). `POST` `{ "state": "off" }` without a configured consent file returns `200` `{ "state": "off", "timestamp": null }` (a no-op). `GET /v1/consent` without `--lrs-url` returns `200` `{ "state": "off", "timestamp": null }`. GET and POST return the host's **in-memory** consent state, read under the write mutex, not a raw reread of a possibly stale file. After a plain startup that did not rewrite the file, GET returns the timestamp stored in the file. If startup created the missing file, GET returns the create timestamp. After a later successful write, GET and POST return the timestamp of that last change. A `POST /v1/consent` whose `state` equals the current in-memory state is a no-op for the consent file: the host does not rewrite the file and the timestamp is unchanged (GET and POST agree on this last-change timestamp). A same-state `off` is still a revoke for the latch: after validation it increments `epoch` and `revokes_in_flight`, re-cancels and swaps the token under the mutex, and decrements `revokes_in_flight` at the end of that hold (DD-144, AT-198). Because a consent-file or `revoke-pending` write failure exits the process (DD-138), a running host has no degraded timestamp case. After a revoke has set the flag under the mutex, GET reports `off` until a later qualifying `on` completes (DD-142, AT-198).

The file format is the exact grammar `("on"|"off") "\n" RFC3339-seconds-UTC "\n"?`: the tokens `on` or `off`, a newline, an RFC3339-seconds-UTC timestamp, and an optional trailing newline after the timestamp. A missing trailing newline is not malformed; any other deviation is a malformed file (a startup error that names the file).

**Consent copy (Q-02, DD-158).** The following text is a draft only. It must not be shown to study participants until Ashley Cribb and the IRB have approved it.

> S3A Navigator — tracing consent (draft, not for use with participants until Ashley Cribb and the IRB approve this text)
>
> This lab copy of S3A Navigator can send a trace of your research activity to the lab Learning Record Store (LRS) at https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/xapi/, using the Experience API (xAPI) version 1.0.3.
>
> If you turn tracing on, the records that may be sent are the research actions you take in Scholar mode, such as selecting a paper for a closer read, downloading a paper into the corpus, or deselecting a paper. Each record names the action and the paper identity. Local records of your work are kept on this machine whether or not you turn tracing on.
>
> You may leave tracing off, or turn it off later. Turning it off stops new records from being sent to the LRS and drops any queued unsent records. Local inclusion records and snapshots already written on this machine are not deleted.
>
> This text is a draft only. It must not be shown to study participants until Ashley Cribb and the IRB have approved it.

The host is the only writer (including first-run bootstrap). It writes the consent file, and `revoke-pending`, with a temp file named `s3a-kite-tmp-{pid}-*` in the same directory, plus `fsync` of the temp, plus atomic rename, plus `fsync` of the **parent directory**, mode `0600`, before the response. `POST /v1/consent` is the only supported toggle after start. Mid-session outside edits are not honored (the host keeps the last state it wrote or read at startup). AT-58 uses `POST /v1/consent`, not an outside editor.

DD-12's earlier "consent on and URL missing, statement fails closed" state is **unreachable** in v6: that combination is a startup error, and the toggle is rejected at runtime.

#### 5.2.4 Egress for host fetches (DD-31, DD-49, DD-64, DD-76, DD-86)

v1 has no page subprocess. Egress rules apply to the host's own select/download fetches of a `source_url`.

**`fetch_error` vocabulary (closed).** When a fetch fails, `fetch_error` is exactly one of: `scheme_not_allowed`, `egress_denied`, `egress_not_allowlisted`, `connect_failed`, `timeout`, `too_many_redirects`, `http_status`, `unknown_encoding`, `headers_too_large`, `body_too_large`, `snapshot_too_large`. When more than one condition could apply, the first failing check in this section's order is reported: `scheme_not_allowed`, then `egress_denied`, then `egress_not_allowlisted`, then `connect_failed`, then `timeout` if the `--fetch-timeout-secs` wall-clock cap is exceeded, then `too_many_redirects`, then `http_status`, then `unknown_encoding`, then `headers_too_large`, then `body_too_large`. `snapshot_too_large` is a publish-time outcome after a successful GET (AT-227). Clients distinguish these values; ATs assert the specific code named below.


*   Only `http` and `https` URLs are fetched. `file://` and any other scheme are rejected (`fetch_error: "scheme_not_allowed"`) and no file is read through the HTTP client (AT-123).
*   The **deny list always applies**, including to allowlisted names and to every redirect hop (DD-64). Denied: any address that is not global unicast, including loopback (IPv4 and `::1`), IPv4-mapped forms of those ranges, `0.0.0.0/8`, `::`, RFC1918, RFC4193, IPv6 site-local (`fec0::/10`), link-local, multicast, broadcast, CGNAT `100.64.0.0/10`, NAT64 / 6to4 translated forms of the same ranges, metadata address `169.254.169.254`, and the configured LRS host plus every IP it resolves to (even if that IP would otherwise be allowed). The hostname `metadata.google.internal` is also denied **by name**, applied to the URL host **before any DNS query**; no lookup and no connect are made (AT-226). The LRS host is resolved at Scholar start and **again at each fetch's connect check and on each redirect hop**; the deny set is the union of the startup resolution and that check's resolution, as computed by the pure function `s3a_kite::egress::lrs_deny_union(startup_ips, connect_time_ips, addr) -> bool` in the host crate (true iff `addr` is in the union; AT-226 is a unit test of that function, with no network and no resolver override). If an `https` `--lrs-url` hostname **fails to resolve at Scholar start**, startup is an error that names `--lrs-url` and binds nothing. Denial of a resolved address is at **connect time**, and again on every redirect hop, before the request is sent (`fetch_error: "egress_denied"`, no snapshot, no TCP connection to that address; AT-54, AT-226). A connect or TLS failure after the address has passed the deny and allowlist checks is `fetch_error: "connect_failed"`.
*   `--egress-allowlist` is a comma-separated list of exact hostnames or exact IP literals, each optionally followed by `:port`. **Ports on IPv6 entries require brackets** (`[2001:db8::1]:443`). An unbracketed entry is parsed as an IPv6 address first: if it parses as a single IPv6 address, it is a **portless exact address** and is valid (so `2001:db8::1:443` is the address `2001:db8:0:0:0:0:1:443`, not host `2001:db8::1` at port 443). An unbracketed entry that is not a hostname, not an IPv4 literal, and does not parse as an IPv6 address (for example `2001:db8::1::443`) is a startup error that names `--egress-allowlist`. No wildcards. Matching uses the **effective port**: the URL's explicit port, or the scheme default (`80` for `http`, `443` for `https`). An entry without a port matches **only port 443 with scheme `https`**. An entry `host:PORT` matches either scheme at that effective port. So `host` matches `https://host/` and `https://host:443/`, but not `http://host/`, `http://host:443/`, or `https://host:8443/`. `host:443` matches `https://host/`, `https://host:443/`, and `http://host:443/`, but not `https://host:8443/`. `host:80` matches `http://host/`, `http://host:80/`, and `https://host:80/`, but not `https://host/` (effective port 443) (AT-183). If the flag is set, the URL's host, scheme, and effective port (and every redirect hop's) must also appear on the list. A hop that is not on the list fails with `fetch_error: "egress_not_allowlisted"`, makes zero connects for that hop, and writes no snapshot (AT-183). The allowlist never overrides the deny list.
*   Loopback fixture servers used by tests are reached only when the binary is built with the Cargo feature `egress_test` and the flag `--egress-test-loopback-port <port>` is set. That flag is a **startup error** in builds without the feature (including release distributions). In an `egress_test` build it excepts only `127.0.0.1` and `::1` at that exact port. Redirects from that fixture to a denied address are still denied (AT-88, AT-109).
*   `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`, and `NO_PROXY` environment variables are **ignored for every host fetch**, including to an allowed `egress_test` fixture (AT-122).
*   Follow at most `--max-redirects` redirects (`--max-redirects` is a required integer ≥ 0). Exceeding it is `fetch_error: "too_many_redirects"` and no snapshot (AT-88).
*   Only a final **2xx** response is stored. A 3xx beyond the redirect budget is `too_many_redirects`; a final 4xx/5xx is `fetch_error: "http_status"`; the error-page body is not snapshotted or downloaded (AT-139).
*   The stored and hashed body is the **payload after `Content-Encoding` is decoded** (`identity`, `gzip`, and `deflate` are supported). Unknown encoding is `fetch_error: "unknown_encoding"` and no snapshot. `--max-fetch-bytes` applies to the decoded body (`fetch_error: "body_too_large"` when exceeded; AT-140). The fetch **response header block** is also bounded by `--max-fetch-bytes`; exceeding it is `fetch_error: "headers_too_large"`, writes no snapshot, and does not store the body. "Raw response body" in earlier wording means this decoded payload.
*   Per-fetch wall-time limit is the required `--fetch-timeout-secs` flag (DD-49). It bounds the **entire redirect chain**, from the first connect to the final decoded body. Exceeding `--fetch-timeout-secs` aborts the fetch, writes no snapshot, and returns `fetch_error: "timeout"` (the inclusion record, if already written, is kept; AT-103). Exceeding `--max-fetch-bytes` is `body_too_large` or `headers_too_large` as above. There is no silent default. There is no page-process CPU/memory limit in v1 because there is no page process.

#### 5.2.5 Kind detection and `first_view` (DD-87)

`open`, `open_verified`, `open_path`, and `/v1/open` classify bytes as follows, in this order. The name hint is the optional `/v1/open` `name`, or the path basename.

1.  If the bytes begin with `S3A_MAGIC` (`S3A1`): apply §5.3 / §5.4. A consistent Edge file is `kind: "edge"`, `first_view: "header"`. An inconsistent Edge file, a contradictory size flag on a non-Edge stride (AT-146), or a stride that is not 4096, 8192, or 128 KiB is `kind: "unsupported"`, `first_view: "banner"`. A version-1 hyper-tile is `kind: "hyper-tile"`, `first_view: "manifest"`. A non-1 `FileHeader.version`, or magic present but a header that cannot be opened, is an error: host `/v1/open` returns `400` `invalid_magic`; pack `open` / `open_verified` / `open_path` return `Err` with engine `Invalid S3A file magic` or `Unsupported file version: {n}` (AT-69, AT-95). This is **not** `kind: "other"`.
2.  Else if the name hint ends with `.md` (ASCII, case-insensitive): `kind: "wiki"`, `first_view: "page"`.
3.  Else if the name hint ends with `.html` or `.htm` (ASCII, case-insensitive): `kind: "html"`, `first_view: "page"`.
4.  Else: `kind: "other"`, `first_view: "page"`. `render_page` shows the exact label `no text conversion in archive` and invents no title, abstract, or raster (DD-14, AT-148). This includes PDFs, extensionless download files, and a name hint ending in `.s3a` (or any other suffix) whose bytes do **not** begin with `S3A_MAGIC`. On the phone, that `.s3a`-named no-magic file is shown as `other` with that same label, not as `This is not an S3A file`.

`open(bytes)` without a name hint, and without S3A magic, returns `Ok` with `kind: "other"` and `first_view: "page"` (AT-95, AT-130). Kind detection in this section runs **before** any §5.3 "not an S3A file" error.

#### 5.2.6 View validity matrix (DD-107, DD-125)

`manifest`, `header`, and `banner` are aliases of `render_manifest` on archive kinds (`hyper-tile`, `edge`, `unsupported`). `view_mismatch` is `409` with that code. `unsupported` never leaves the first view (`proceed` is `409` `unsupported`). The same matrix and codes apply to the pack FFI: `proceed_to_warehouse` and `render_manifest` / `render_page` / `render_warehouse` / `render_floor_transition` / `render_floor_detail` return `KiteError` with `unsupported`, `view_mismatch`, or `not_archive` as this table would for the corresponding `/v1/proceed` or `/v1/frame` request. The §5.2.1 error-precedence list is evaluated before this matrix (a `view=page` request that also includes `floor` is `400` `invalid_param`, not `409` `view_mismatch`; AT-196). This matrix applies to `/v1/frame` and `/v1/hit-test`. The host evaluates the matrix **before** `no_frame`. The last-frame cache for hit-test is keyed by `(handle, view, floor)`, with first-view aliases normalized to `first` (floor is omitted or null when the view has no floor; a supplied `floor` on a non-floor view is `400` `invalid_param`). A warehouse `hit_test` after only a `page` or `manifest` frame is `409` `view_mismatch` or `409` `no_frame` according to this order (AT-172).

| Kind | State | `manifest` / `header` / `banner` | `page` | `warehouse` | `floor-transition` / `floor-detail` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `hyper-tile` | first (before proceed) | 200 | 409 `view_mismatch` | 409 `view_mismatch` | 409 `view_mismatch` |
| `hyper-tile` | warehouse | 200 | 409 `view_mismatch` | 200 | 200 (need `floor`; `t` when required) |
| `edge` | first | 200 | 409 `view_mismatch` | 409 `view_mismatch` | 409 `view_mismatch` |
| `edge` | warehouse | 200 | 409 `view_mismatch` | 200 | 200 (need `floor`; `t` when required) |
| `unsupported` | first only | 200 | 409 `view_mismatch` | 409 `unsupported` | 409 `unsupported` |
| `wiki` / `html` / `other` | page only | 409 `view_mismatch` | 200 | 409 `not_archive` | 409 `not_archive` |

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

#### 5.2.7 Test hooks (DD-151)

Fail-first ATs that need in-process fault injection or a pause use the mechanism in this subsection and no other. The hook surface is **not** a host flag (the §5.2.2 / DD-106 inventory stays the complete flag list).

**Feature.** The host may be built with the Cargo feature `test_hooks`. That feature is **not** in the default feature set. Release distributions, and every other build compiled without `test_hooks`, contain no hook implementation. This is the same rule as `egress_test` (§5.2.4, AT-109).

**Control channel.** Hooks are configured only by environment variables:

*   `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS` — a comma-separated list of `POINT=ACTION` entries, with no whitespace around `,` or `=`. Each `POINT` is a name from the closed list below. Each `ACTION` is exactly one of:
    *   `pause_until_signal` — also requires `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOK_READY` and `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOK_CONTINUE` to be set to two distinct absolute filesystem paths. When the hook fires, the host creates or replaces the READY path as a regular file whose contents are the hook-point name and a trailing newline, then waits until the CONTINUE path exists as a non-empty regular file. The test writes CONTINUE to resume. The host does not delete either path.
    *   `fail_with_errno=N` — `N` is a positive decimal errno (for example `28` for `ENOSPC`). The hooked operation fails as if that errno was returned. This action stays active for the whole process lifetime and fails **every** matching operation at that point.
    *   `fail_once_with_errno=N` — `N` is a positive decimal errno as above. The hooked operation fails as if that errno was returned, but only on the **first** matching operation at that point; later firings at that point are no-ops. This is the one-shot exception to process-lifetime hook actions.
    *   `grow` — valid only on `grow_drive_path_during_copy`. During the step-2b copy the host extends the already-opened `drive_path` so that more than `--max-open-bytes` arrive.
    *   `issue` — valid only on `window_issue_select`. After startup, once `GET /health` has succeeded, the native-window client issues `GET /v1/candidates` and then, if that list is non-empty, `POST /v1/select` for the first listed candidate. Both requests are real Host HTTP API requests and carry the Bearer UI token. There is no "first warehouse frame" precondition.

An unknown `POINT`, an `ACTION` not in that list, an action used on a point that does not accept it, a malformed value, or `pause_until_signal` without both signal-path variables, is a startup error in a `test_hooks` build that names `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS` and binds nothing. A hook action stays active for the whole process lifetime unless it is one-shot (`fail_once_with_errno=N`). Hooks are read only at startup; there is no runtime way to add, clear, or change them.

**Release and non-feature builds.** In a build compiled without `test_hooks` (including release distributions), if `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS`, `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOK_READY`, or `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOK_CONTINUE` is set in the environment (even to an empty value), startup is an error that names the variable, binds nothing, and performs no hooked action (AT-222). This mirrors `--egress-test-loopback-port` (AT-109).

**Closed hook-point list.** These are all the hook points. An AT that needs in-process injection names its point below. Operations a test can perform with ordinary filesystem actions between requests, or in `kite-pack-tests` on an already-open fd, are **not** hook points.

| Hook point | Accepted actions | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `pause_before_snapshot_rename` | `pause_until_signal` | AT-187: pause inside the step-2 publish mutex hold after the locked `--snapshot-dir` temp exists and before the rename |
| `pause_before_name_binding_check` | `pause_until_signal` | AT-219: pause after the writer is attached to the hold's fd and immediately before the name-binding `fstatat` re-check |
| `fail_revoke_pending_write` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-189: fail the `revoke-pending` temp+rename |
| `fail_lrs_retry_delete` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-124, AT-169, AT-199: fail **every** `lrs-retry/` delete, including the revoke path after `revoke-pending` and the consent-file `off` write, and including the startup retry or leftover delete |
| `fail_revoke_pending_remove` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-181, AT-199, AT-229: fail removal of `revoke-pending` at startup, at runtime step 4, and on a qualifying `on` that unlinks a leftover (AT-229 case 1 uses `fail_once_with_errno=N` so a later qualifying `on` in the same process can succeed) |
| `fail_index_append` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-158: fail the inclusion-index append after a successful tile-10 write |
| `fail_snapshot_write_temp`, `fail_snapshot_write_fsync`, `fail_snapshot_write_rename` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-212: fail the step-2 snapshot temp write, fsync, or rename respectively (AT-212 uses `fail_once_with_errno=N` so a later select can retry the fetch) |
| `fail_snapshot_index_append` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-213: fail the snapshot index-line append after a successful rename (AT-213 uses `fail_once_with_errno=N` so a later select can reuse the left file) |
| `fail_download_write` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-214: fail every step-2b download temp write, fsync, or rename in the test |
| `grow_drive_path_during_copy` | `grow` | AT-202: mid-copy growth past `--max-open-bytes` |
| `window_issue_select` | `issue` | AT-125: cause the native-window client to issue `POST /v1/select` |
| `pause_before_lrs_retry_write` | `pause_until_signal` | AT-156 case 3: pause after the LRS response is classified and before the mutex hold that would write the retry file |
| `pause_before_consent_rename` | `pause_until_signal` | AT-187: pause inside a consent-file or `revoke-pending` publish after the locked `s3a-kite-tmp-*` temp exists and before the rename |
| `pause_after_consent_receipt` | `pause_until_signal` | AT-198: pause after receipt-time latch actions (epoch increment, token swap, `revokes_in_flight`) and before taking the write mutex |
| `fail_lrs_retry_write` | `fail_with_errno=N`, `fail_once_with_errno=N` | AT-191: fail a retry-file (`.json` / `.claimed` / `.permanent`) write, fsync, or rename |

**No host hook.** These ATs do not use `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS`:

*   AT-101 lives in `kite-pack-tests`: the test opens a host-validated fd, swaps an intermediate path component for a symlink, then calls pack `open_path` on that fd; the pack must not follow the symlink or reopen the path by name.
*   AT-202's symlink and FIFO replacements of `drive_path` are ordinary filesystem operations after `GET /v1/candidates` and before `POST /v1/download`. Mid-copy growth uses `grow_drive_path_during_copy`.
*   AT-203's post-start replacement of the working-archive final component is an ordinary filesystem operation before `POST /v1/select`. A swap after the writer is attached and before the name-binding re-check uses `pause_before_name_binding_check` (AT-219).
*   AT-217's oversize, symlink, and FIFO cases are set up on the working-archive path before `/v1/open` (ordinary filesystem operations).
*   AT-218's FIFO and symlink replacements happen between two `GET /v1/candidates` requests (ordinary filesystem operations).

### 5.3 Opening a hyper-tile `.s3a` file

1.  Open with `open` / `open_verified` / `open_path` as classified in §5.2.5. `open` and `open_verified` take a name hint, the required `max_frame_pixels`, and (for `open`) `pinned_keys`. The host performs the DD-146 walk and calls `open_path` with the validated fd, the proved canonical path, and the allowed root; the pack reads from that fd into memory and uses `MmapReader::from_bytes` (no path mmap in v1; DD-75), passing `name_hint` (the `/v1/open` `name`, else the basename of `canonical_path`) and the caller's `pinned_keys`. This item applies only when the bytes **begin with** `S3A_MAGIC`. Bytes without that magic are never failed as "not an S3A file" here; they follow §5.2.5 steps 2–4. If `FileHeader.version` is not 1, or the header cannot be opened, fail as in §5.2.5 item 1 (AT-69, AT-95). If `tile_stride` is set and is neither 4096, 8192, nor 128 KiB, or a 4 KiB / 8 KiB size flag is set on a 128 KiB (non-Edge) stride, `/v1/open` returns 200 with `kind: "unsupported"`, `first_view: "banner"`, and a labeled unsupported or inconsistent-header banner; `proceed` returns `409` `unsupported`; the file is not toured as a hyper-tile (AT-97, AT-146). The FFI `open` of such a file returns `Ok` with that handle (not `Err`).
2.  **First view** is `render_manifest(handle, width, height, scroll_y, device_pixel_ratio)` (also reached as `view=banner` or `view=manifest`). If a committed, non-quarantined tile 78 is present and valid UTF-8, paint that exact stored manifest text (the same bytes as `lobby.manifest`, trimmed only as `print_tour_report` already trims). If several committed valid tile-78 tiles exist, use the first in tile order as the manifest; later tile-78 tiles still appear as ordinary floors. If tile 78 is absent, or every tile 78 is quarantined, paint a labeled "manifest absent" or "manifest quarantined" state and do not invent title/creator text. If tile 78 is present but not valid UTF-8, paint a labeled "manifest not UTF-8" state; do not invent replacement text. Long manifest text wraps to the pixmap width and scrolls with `scroll_y`; it is not silently truncated.
3.  The first view stays until `proceed_to_warehouse(handle)` / `POST /v1/proceed`. A Continue `hit_test` does **not** change handle state (DD-97, AT-152). There is no timer auto-advance. After proceed, `render_warehouse` is used. Floor-list fields (`index`, `type_id`, `record_count`, `size_bytes`, `payload_hash_short`) are taken from `MmapReader`, not from a full `tour_file` walk. `payload_hash_short` is the first 8 lowercase hex digits of the SHA-256 of the tile **payload** (for a hyper-tile, the tile payload as `MmapReader` exposes it; for Edge, the micro-tile payload excluding the micro-tile header). The CLI `tour --json` oracle is used only for sealed, type-82-free hyper-tile files (AT-10); AT-15 and AT-21 compute the same function over the fixture bytes and do not use the CLI as an Edge oracle. `withheld_count` is computed by kite as in item 4, never by `tour_file`.
4.  Floor N detail is `render_floor_detail`. **`floor_index`, `Floor(n)`, and `?floor=` are the tile `index` of a valid floor**, not a 0-based position in the visible list (DD-82, AT-10). After invalid tiles are omitted from the valid floor list, the same tile index still identifies that floor. A request whose `floor` is omitted (where required) returns `400` `missing_param`. A request whose `floor` is present but is not the tile index of a valid floor returns `409` `invalid_floor`. The invalid-list is banner text only and is not hit-testable (AT-138).

    **Research-consent evaluation (normative for hyper-tile and Edge; DD-67, DD-111, DD-147).** This table is the only withholding rule for research consent. §5.4 item 6, candidate extraction (DD-117), and consent under `open_verified` use it by reference; they do not define a second algorithm. The pack **must not** implement the type-82 branch in `tour.rs` (that branch is a known bug and is not a ConsentRecord). `scope` and `study_id` exist on core `ConsentRecord`s and are **not** used. `now` is Unix UTC seconds; pack tests inject a clock (AT-161, DD-110) and the running host uses the process clock. Withholding is re-evaluated on each render and each candidate recompute.

    **Walk order.** Parseable consent records are considered in increasing tile index, then increasing record index within the tile. A record is **later** than another if it comes after it in this order.

    **Record classification** (at evaluation time):
    *   Hyper-tile research records are core `ConsentRecord`s with `purpose == Research`. A **grant** has `withdrawn_at == 0` and (`expires_at == 0` or `now <= expires_at`). A **withdrawal** has `withdrawn_at != 0`. An **expiry** has `withdrawn_at == 0`, `expires_at != 0`, and `now > expires_at`.
    *   Edge research records are type-71 `EdgeConsentRecord`s only. A **grant** has `(allowed_purposes & PURPOSE_RESEARCH_ANALYTICS) != 0` and (`expires_at == 0` or `now <= expires_at`). A **denial** is any type-71 record that does not allow that purpose — including a record whose `allowed_purposes` lacks `PURPOSE_RESEARCH_ANALYTICS`. There is no research-neutral type-71 record. An **expiry** would allow the purpose but has `expires_at != 0` and `now > expires_at`.
    *   Non-research hyper-tile `ConsentRecord`s still count as consent records of any kind for the unrestricted test below, but they do not enter the per-subject table as a grant, denial, withdrawal, or expiry. Every type-71 record enters the table for the research purpose (as a grant, a denial, or an expiry).

    **Trusted vs untrusted.** A research record is **trusted** only when it sits on an authenticated valid floor of a file that is not unauthenticated as a whole. Authenticated valid floors are valid floors of a file that is not unauthenticated as a whole. Individual tiles are also unauthenticated when they are not a valid floor, fail a committed CRC, fail a mid-chain signature, or — **only in a file that contains at least one authentic type-70 record** — have index greater than the highest authentic signed `tile_index` (the unsigned tail, AT-210; a verifying type-70 container is exempt, §5.4).

    **Unauthenticated as a whole (single definition; DD-148).** Pack `open` / `open_path` apply this predicate to the pack-computed `KiteVerificationReport`. `open_verified` applies the same predicate, identically, to the supplied report. There is no second definition. A file is **unauthenticated as a whole** if and only if (it is a hyper-tile and `seal_status == seal_failed`) or `quarantine_reason` is `signer_not_pinned` or `missing_signature`. Consequences: every tile of such a file is unauthenticated for consent, even CRC-valid committed tiles that **are** listed as floors under AT-90 (display only). An **unsealed** / Unsigned file (hyper-tile without a trailer, or Edge with the flag clear and no signatures) is **not** unauthenticated as a whole (AT-136, DD-141, AT-154). An Unsigned Edge file with a committed CRC failure (`quarantine_reason: crc`) is **not** unauthenticated as a whole. A chain break does **not** make the whole Edge file unauthenticated: only tiles at or after `break_index` are unauthenticated; tiles before `break_index` that are covered by an authentic record remain authenticated valid floors (AT-194, AT-220). An unsigned tail does **not** make the whole file unauthenticated (AT-210). A recognised hyper-tile end-cap or partial trailer remnant whose verify fails is `seal_failed` and **is** unauthenticated as a whole; a complete trailer absence is not, and is recorded in DD-141. Untrusted grants **never** release.

    **Subject.** For hyper-tile files the subject is `ConsentRecord.subject_id`. A payload record that **has** that `subject_id` is that subject's data. A payload record **without** a `subject_id` is not withheld by this table; it is withheld only by marks (`MARK_CONSENT_WITHDRAWN`, or `MARK_TARGET_ALL_RECORDS` as in `tour.rs`). `AcademicPaperRecord` has no `subject_id` field in the grounded layout (only `paper_id`, hashes, and the other §4.7 fields); it is mark-only, plus the Quarantined-corpus rule in §5.5 (AT-166). For Edge files, type-71 records apply to all 32-byte type-64 event-log payloads as one research-purpose class (there is no per-subject field). The derived header sheet and type-64 `DeviceIdentityRecord` floors are **exempt** (DD-53, AT-86).

    **Unrestricted.** The tour is **unrestricted** (this table withholds nothing; `withheld_count` is 0 aside from marks) **only** when both are true: the file has trusted authentication (it is not unauthenticated as a whole), **and** there is zero consent record of any kind (research or not; core `ConsentRecord` or type 71) in any parseable tile (AT-112, AT-85, AT-107). Any other file is in **evaluated mode** (default-withhold): a subject's data is withheld unless the per-subject rule below shows it. Tampering or any untrusted authentication, when consent records are present, never makes the file unrestricted (AT-185, AT-200). An unauthenticated-as-a-whole file with zero consent records is also not unrestricted and default-withholds subject-bearing / event-log payloads.

    **Per-subject decision (evaluated mode).** A subject's data is **shown** only if both hold:

    1.  The latest **trusted** record for that subject is an unexpired grant, **and**
    2.  No parseable record from any tile (valid or invalid, trusted or not) for that subject is a **later** denial, withdrawal, or expiry.

    Otherwise the subject's data is **withheld**. Any **later** denial, withdrawal, or expiry from any parseable tile (valid or invalid), later than the latest trusted grant for that subject, therefore restricts that subject; a later untrusted grant cannot undo it. An earlier expiry or withdrawal does not withhold a later trusted unexpired grant (AT-185). A withheld payload must not appear in `text_runs`, `semantics`, pixels, or hit-test targets (AT-11, AT-23). `withheld_count` is a non-negative integer with two scopes, both computed by kite as in this item 4, never by `tour_file`, and both painted in concatenated `text_runs` as the exact substring `withheld_count: N` (`N` is the decimal count, no thousands separator):
- **Per-file**, on the first view / banner (`render_manifest`, `view=manifest` / `header` / `banner`): the total number of parseable subject-bearing or event-log payload records this table withholds. It counts only records on valid floors, except when the file has no valid floors, in which case the banner still counts parseable subject-bearing or event-log payload records that this table withholds (AT-134, AT-184, AT-195, AT-204). When valid floors exist, parseable payloads on invalid tiles are not added (AT-194).
- **Per-floor**, on `render_floor_detail` / `view=floor-detail`: the number of such withheld records on that floor only (AT-79, AT-173).
Withdrawn marks still withhold, including on unrestricted files. `withheld_count` **includes** records withheld only by `MARK_CONSENT_WITHDRAWN` or `MARK_TARGET_ALL_RECORDS` (those marks are not a per-subject table decision, but they still withhold and are counted). The unrestricted phrase "0 aside from marks" means the per-subject table withholds nothing and the painted count equals the number of mark-withheld records (AT-11).

    | File authentication | Consent records of any kind in any parseable tile | Outcome |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Trusted | None | **Unrestricted** (show subject-bearing / event-log payloads; marks may still withhold) |
    | Trusted | One or more | **Evaluated:** show a subject only under the per-subject rule; otherwise withhold |
    | Unauthenticated as a whole | None or some | **Default-withhold** subject-bearing / event-log payloads (not unrestricted) |

    *   A lone valid-floor withdrawal or denial withholds that subject (AT-204, AT-23, AT-86). A lone valid-floor expiry withholds that subject (AT-205, AT-161). A lone invalid-tile withdrawal withholds that subject, and every other subject without a later trusted unexpired grant is also withheld because the file is in evaluated mode (AT-184).
    *   AT-136: a later research withdrawal of an earlier trusted grant for the same subject withholds that subject (clause 2, or the latest trusted record is no longer a grant).
    *   AT-200 / AT-185: after a seal is tampered, no grant is trusted; every subject is withheld.
    Golden fixtures are the oracle when type 82 is present. The CLI may be used as a cross-check only on hyper-tile fixtures that contain no type-82 tile.
5.  Archive bytes are painted as text, never executed as HTML or JavaScript (same policy as `tour_html.rs` escaping). No JS context is created (observed as in AT-34: `cargo tree` for the host and Android targets contains no `boa_engine` and no `wasmtime`). BLOB and PROVENANCE floors show metadata only (size, chunk index and total, hash, and PROVENANCE text) and **never** raw payload bytes, including when the captured body is itself an S3A archive (AT-107). A hyper-tile floor whose record layout kite cannot decode (so it cannot find a `subject_id`) shows metadata only (type, size, `record_count`) and never raw payload bytes.
6.  Animation is `render_floor_transition` (§5.8). Seal verification uses `s3a-engine` (`verify_sealed_archive` / `verify_sealed_bytes`). Labels follow the §5.4 table: an unsealed hyper-tile may be opened with `status` Unsigned and `seal_status` unsealed, and still shows the manifest or "manifest absent" state (AT-72). If `verify_sealed_archive` fails on a file that has a seal trailer, `status` is Quarantined and `seal_status` is seal_failed. When **every committed tile passes CRC**, CRC-valid committed tiles **are** listed as valid floors (display only; unauthenticated for consent) (AT-90). When a committed CRC failure is also present, the CRC rule decides the valid floors: the failed CRC tile and every subsequent tile are not floors, even though the trailer check also fails (AT-35). The banner reports the seal and/or CRC failure and no floor is presented as a verified seal. `quarantine_reason` still follows the §5.1 priority (`seal_trailer` outranks `crc`). A sealed-OK hyper-tile (trailer verifies, no device signer) is Unsigned with `seal_status` sealed, not Verified (AT-154). The host must not use the CLI's 128 KiB unsealed scanner on any file.
7.  The only open path is `from_bytes` (a snapshot of the file). Path mmap is **not used in v1** (DD-75). Open is also bounded by `--max-open-bytes` / FFI `max_open_bytes` (`409` `file_too_large` / `KiteError` when exceeded; DD-123, AT-170). A failed open is an error, not a hang.

### 5.4 Opening an Edge micro-tile file

1.  Detect with `MmapReader::is_edge_archive()` (flag or stride 4096/8192). If `FLAG_PROFILE_EDGE` is set but `tile_stride` is not 4096 or 8192, or a size flag disagrees with `tile_stride` (for example `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_4KB` with stride 8192), or both 4 KiB and 8 KiB size flags are set, do not tour as if the header were consistent: `/v1/open` returns 200 with `kind: "unsupported"`, `first_view: "banner"`, and a quarantine / "inconsistent Edge header" state (AT-66). `proceed` returns `409` `unsupported`. `tile_stride` is not silently fixed. If `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is clear, the file is an **unsealed Edge** file: open it, show seal status "unsealed" on the header sheet, and do not auto-quarantine solely for that flag (AT-147).
2.  Verification is performed only by `s3a-engine`.
