On mobile, `s3a-reader-core` may run first; kite consumes a `KiteVerificationReport` (DD-80) and does not compute a second result when that report is supplied, even if a fresh verify would disagree (AT-137). **Invalid tiles** (torn, CRC-failed, chain-broken) are omitted from the valid floor list, counted on the header sheet / banner, and may appear only in a clearly marked invalid list (banner text, not hit-testable). They are never drawn as valid floors. When the seal chain breaks, tiles before the break that already verified remain valid floors; the breaking tile and every subsequent tile are not valid floors. The banner reports the break index. `floor_index` remains the tile `index`.

    **Ed25519 and device public-key provenance (DD-57, DD-79, DD-98, DD-99, DD-100).** Type-70 tiles contain one or more `DeviceSealRecord`s. Each record's `signature` is verified with `verify_signature_raw` against a public key over that record's 32-byte `seal_hash`. The record's `tile_index` is the tile whose chain hash the signature covers. Records are considered in increasing `tile_index` order.

    The pack **must not treat a public key embedded in the file** (`DeviceIdentityRecord.public_key`, or any key carried in the same bytes) as a trust anchor. That is the same rule as `s3a-reader-core` `verify(pinned_keys)` and as s3a-vault contact pinning (`vault contact add` of a full 32-byte key; S3A-EDGE-PROFILE.md §543 "cached or provisioned public_key"):

    *   The trust anchor is the **pinned-key list** passed into `open` / `open_path` as `pinned_keys` (possibly empty). The host loads that list from `--pinned-keys-file` when the flag is set, or passes an empty list if the flag is omitted. The file, when the flag is set, is UTF-8 hex-encoded 32-byte keys, one per line, trailing newline stripped the same way as the token file; empty lines and lines starting with `#` are ignored; it must be euid-owned and mode `0600`. After comments and blanks are ignored, a remaining line that is not exactly 64 hex characters (a 32-byte key) is a startup error that names the file. Zero remaining lines is a valid empty list (the same as omitting the flag) (AT-182). The pack does not read the host flag itself. The phone app passes the same list it used (or would use) for `verify(pinned_keys)`.
    *   If the pinned list is **empty**, a signature that verifies against a key found in the file is at best **Self-signed**, never **Verified**.
    *   If the pinned list is **non-empty**, the signer must be in it or the file-level label is **Quarantined** ("signer key not in pinned list") and **no tile is a valid floor**.
    *   `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` set with no type-70 tile, or with type-70 tiles present but no usable candidate key, is **Quarantined** with `quarantine_reason: missing_signature` (AT-134). The file is unauthenticated as a whole for consent, and **no tile is a valid floor** (same as `signer_not_pinned`; AT-165, AT-195).
    *   `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` **clear**, no signatures, and a non-empty pinned list is **Unsigned** (not Quarantined) (AT-154). Unsigned floors are authenticated for consent. Clearing the flag and dropping type-70 tiles (a signature-strip downgrade to Unsigned) is indistinguishable from a file that was never signed; this pack accepts that downgrade (DD-141). If the flag is clear but type-70 tiles **are** present, the signed-file rules below apply (the file is not treated as Unsigned).
    *   AT-133: a file re-signed with a different key that is not in the pinned list is Quarantined, even if the new key is embedded in a `DeviceIdentityRecord`.

    **Signer attribution (DD-145, AT-201).** A `DeviceSealRecord` carries no signer key or key id; the signer is known only when a key verifies the signature. The algorithm below is **total**: every type-70 record, in every pin-list and key-availability case, maps to exactly one of authentic, `signature_known_signer`, `signer_not_pinned`, `missing_signature`, or `chain_break`. If attribution is impossible, the file is Quarantined (`signer_not_pinned` when the pin list is non-empty; `missing_signature` when the pin list is empty) and no tile is a valid floor.

    1.  **Candidate keys** are the pinned list if it is non-empty, else every `public_key` from every `DeviceIdentityRecord` in the file (all embedded keys; several identity records may disagree). The pack still never treats an embedded key as a trust anchor when the pin list is non-empty.
    2.  A type-70 record is **authentic** if it verifies under any candidate key **and** its stored `seal_hash` equals the recomputed `compute_seal_hash` at that `tile_index`. The hash is recomputed whenever any type-70 record exists, whether or not `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set. A signature that verifies over a stored hash that does not match the tile contents is not authentic (it is a seal-hash mismatch / `chain_break`, not a mere signature failure) (AT-220). An authentic record with `tile_index` *s* **covers** every tile index in `0 ..= s` (the cumulative `compute_seal_hash` at *s* includes every tile ≤ *s*). In an Edge file that contains at least one type-70 `DeviceSealRecord`, a tile is trusted for consent only if it is a valid floor **and** is covered by at least one authentic (verified) seal record. Any tile not covered by a verified record is untrusted (this includes the unsigned tail after the highest authentic `tile_index`, and any gap after the last authentic coverage when a later record fails). In a hyper-tile, or an Edge file with no type-70 records, a valid floor of a file that is not unauthenticated as a whole is trusted. For `chain_break` and `signature_known_signer`, `break_index` is **(the highest `tile_index` of an authentic record that precedes the failing record) + 1**, or `0` if there is no such preceding authentic record. Valid floors and trusted consent end before that index. An Edge writer **may** sign every *N* tiles (sparse `DeviceSealRecord`s); *N* is a writer policy and this spec invents no default *N*. The pack and host do not require one record per committed tile.
    3.  **Non-empty pin list** (exactly one reason). A record that verifies under a candidate key but whose stored `seal_hash` does not equal the recomputed `compute_seal_hash` is `chain_break` at `break_index` = (highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none (F-02, AT-220). A record that fails every pinned key and verifies under an embedded key is `signer_not_pinned` whether or not its stored hash matches (the whole file; no valid floors; `signer_not_pinned` outranks `chain_break`), including when earlier records verified under a pinned key and a later record verifies only under an embedded unpinned key. A record that fails every key (pinned and embedded) is `signature_known_signer` with `break_index` = (highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1 if an earlier record already verified under a pinned key (earlier covered floors may remain); otherwise attribution is impossible and the reason is `signer_not_pinned` (no valid floors).
    4.  **Empty pin list** (exactly one reason). A record that verifies under an embedded key and whose stored `seal_hash` equals the recomputed `compute_seal_hash` is authentic (Self-signed, if the chain is otherwise intact). A record that verifies under an embedded key but whose hash mismatches is `chain_break` at `break_index` = (highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none. A record that fails every embedded key is `signature_known_signer` with that same `break_index` rule if an earlier record already verified under an embedded key (earlier covered floors may remain). If no earlier record is authentic — including when the first type-70 record fails every embedded key, or every type-70 record fails them — the reason is `missing_signature` (no valid floors); `signer_not_pinned` is not used when the pin list is empty. Type-70 tiles present with **no** candidate key at all (empty pin list and no `DeviceIdentityRecord`) is also `missing_signature` (no valid floors).
    5.  `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` set and (no type-70 tile **or** no usable candidate key) is `missing_signature`. The label-table phrase is a disjunction, not a conjunction.
    6.  Records are still considered in increasing `tile_index` order. A later authentic record does not re-validate an earlier failure.

    **Per-tile outcomes vs file-level label (DD-100, AT-17, AT-35, AT-155).**

    *   **Torn or uncommitted** tiles are omitted from the valid floor list, counted on the banner as ignored/torn, and do **not** by themselves make the file Quarantined (AT-17, EDGE "torn tiles are ignored"). For Edge, a tile is torn or uncommitted when its index is ≥ the header `tile_count`, or the header is absent, is erased (`0xFF` fill), or fails header validation, or the tile is a partial trailing tile (DD-100). For a hyper-tile, a tile is torn or uncommitted when it is a partial trailing tile, or its index is ≥ the header `tile_count` (a crash during a working-archive append can leave that tail). That case does not by itself quarantine the file; `break_index` is the first such tile (DD-150). A **committed CRC failure** is a tile whose index is < `tile_count`, whose header validates, and whose payload CRC fails. `torn_tile_indices` uses this predicate, not `tile_at` returning `Err`, and contains only the indices that satisfy it. Tiles that are uncommitted solely because they follow a torn or CRC stop, but do not themselves satisfy the predicate, are in `invalid_tile_indices` and are not in `torn_tile_indices`. The Edge seal chain (`edge_seal_chain`) stops at the first torn or CRC-failed tile; that tile is not a valid floor, and every subsequent tile is uncommitted and not a valid floor. **Whether or not** `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set, every tile after the first torn or uncommitted tile is uncommitted and not a valid floor, and `break_index` is that first torn tile (matching the hyper-tile torn row). A torn-only stop is not a chain break for `seal_status` or `quarantine_reason`: if `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set, `seal_status` remains `sealed`; if the flag is clear, `seal_status` is `unsealed`; the file is not Quarantined solely for that torn tile. A committed CRC failure uses reason `crc` (DD-150) and, when the flag is set, `seal_status` `seal_failed`. If the only stop is a torn tile, `quarantine_reason` is not set to `crc` or `chain_break` solely for that torn tile (AT-17, AT-165, DD-150).
    *   A **CRC failure on a committed** tile (hyper-tile or Edge) makes the file-level label **Quarantined**. That tile and every subsequent tile are not valid floors; earlier CRC-ok committed tiles remain valid floors (AT-35). This CRC floor rule takes precedence over the seal-trailer display rule: even if a seal trailer is present and `verify_sealed_archive` also fails, a CRC-valid tile **after** the failed committed tile is not a floor (AT-35, AT-90, AT-165). For a hyper-tile, `seal_status` is `seal_failed` if a seal trailer is present, otherwise `unsealed`. For Edge, `seal_status` is `seal_failed` when `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set (the CRC stop breaks the chain), otherwise `unsealed` (AT-35). Payload display uses only valid floors. Consent and mark records from parseable invalid or unauthenticated tiles can only restrict, never release (§5.3 item 4, DD-111).
    *   Any **seal-chain break** (`SealedChainBreak`, reordered tiles, or a seal-hash mismatch that is not a mere signature failure and not a mere CRC failure) makes the file-level label **Quarantined** and `quarantine_reason` `chain_break` (subject to the DD-150 priority), **whether or not** `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set. `seal_status` is `seal_failed` when the chain is active, and stays `unsealed` when the chain is inactive. Grants on tiles at or after `break_index` are untrusted. `break_index` is (the highest `tile_index` of an authentic record that precedes the failing record) + 1, or 0 if there is none: a tampered tile in a gap between records is not a trusted floor (AT-220). A mid-chain signature that does not verify is **not** `chain_break`; it is attributed under DD-145 (rules 3/4 yield `signature_known_signer`). A committed CRC failure is `crc`, not `chain_break`, even when it stops an active chain (AT-18, AT-35, DD-111, DD-150). Earlier tiles that already verified may remain valid floors.
    *   A **signature failure after an earlier authentic record** (DD-145 rules 3 and 4: the record fails every candidate key, and an earlier type-70 record already verified under a pinned key, or under an embedded key when the pin list is empty) has `quarantine_reason` `signature_known_signer`. The tile at that `tile_index` and every subsequent tile are not valid floors; earlier tiles that already verified remain valid floors. The file-level **status** is **Quarantined** (it does not stay Verified or Self-signed); `seal_status` is unchanged by this reason alone; the banner reports the break index (AT-155, AT-201). A later type-70 record with a higher `tile_index` does not re-validate an earlier failure. The type-70 container tile itself is not a valid data floor when its signatures fail.
    *   A mid-chain (or any) signature whose signer is **not** in a non-empty pinned list: the file is **Quarantined** and no tile is a valid floor (the signer is not a trust anchor) (AT-155 first case).

    **Unsigned tail (AT-210, DD-150).** In a file that contains any authentic type-70 `DeviceSealRecord`, every tile whose index is greater than the highest authentic signed `tile_index` is not a valid floor, is included in `invalid_tile_indices`, is untrusted for consent (grants on it never release), and is reported on the banner, **except** a type-70 container tile whose `DeviceSealRecord`(s) all verify: that container is exempt from the unsigned-tail rule (it may sit after the `tile_index` it covers). Tiles after that container, if any, remain in the unsigned tail. A normally signed file (for example MICRO-VEC-03) therefore does not show an unsigned-tail banner solely because the last type-70 container follows the tiles it signs (AT-210). The file-level `status`, `seal_status`, and `quarantine_reason` are not changed by the unsigned tail alone (the file may remain Verified or Self-signed, as a torn-only file may). The §5.1 mapping adds those indices after filling the other report fields; it is not a pure field copy.

    **Verification label table (DD-99, DD-116).** `KiteVerificationReport.status` is one of `verified`, `self_signed`, `unsigned`, `quarantined`, `unverified`. `seal_status` is one of `sealed`, `unsealed`, `seal_failed`. `unverified` means verify has not been called; it is a legal report field value and is a `KiteError` from `open_verified` (AT-154). UI labels are **Verified**, **Self-signed**, **Unsigned**, **Quarantined**, plus a separate seal-status text **sealed** / **unsealed** / **seal failed**. `unverified` is never painted as a UI label (the open is rejected). Only Pinned plus a successful signature yields Verified.

    | Kind | Seal / signature situation | Pin list | `status` | `seal_status` |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | Hyper-tile | No seal trailer; `verify_sealed_archive` is not applicable | any | Unsigned | unsealed |
    | Hyper-tile | Seal trailer verifies | any | Unsigned | sealed |
    | Hyper-tile | Seal trailer present (including a recognised end-cap magic or a partial/truncated trailer remnant) and `verify_sealed_archive` fails | any | Quarantined | seal_failed |
    | Hyper-tile | Committed tile CRC failure | any | Quarantined | `seal_failed` if a trailer is present, else `unsealed` |
    | Edge | `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` clear; no auto-quarantine for that flag alone (AT-147) | any | (from signatures below) | unsealed |
    | Edge | Seal chain active and intact (see the intact definition below) | (from signatures) | (from signatures) | sealed |
    | Edge | Seal-chain break with `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` set (`SealedChainBreak`, reordered tiles, or a seal-hash mismatch that is not a mere signature failure and not a mere CRC) | any | Quarantined; earlier valid floors may remain | seal_failed |
    | Edge | Seal-hash mismatch with chain inactive (type-70 present; stored `seal_hash` ≠ recomputed `compute_seal_hash`) | any | Quarantined; earlier valid floors may remain | unsealed |
    | Edge | Committed CRC failure with `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` clear (chain inactive; the CRC stop does not break a chain) | any | Quarantined; earlier CRC-ok floors may remain | unsealed |
    | Edge | Signatures verify against a pinned key | non-empty, signer in list | Verified | (as above) |
    | Edge | Signatures verify against an embedded key | empty | Self-signed | (as above) |
    | Edge | Signature failure after an earlier authentic record (DD-145 rules 3/4) | (as that case) | Quarantined; earlier valid floors may remain | unchanged by this reason alone |
    | Edge | Signatures present, signer not in a non-empty list | non-empty | Quarantined; no valid floors | (as above) |
    | Edge | No signatures, `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear | empty or non-empty | Unsigned | (as above) |
    | Edge | `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` set, and no type-70 tile or no usable candidate key | any | Quarantined (`missing_signature`) | (as above) |
    | Edge | `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear, type-70 tiles present | (from the attribution algorithm) | (from the signed-file rules) | (as above) |

    **Report-field derivation (DD-150; pack `open` / `open_path` is normative).** The table below is the single total derivation of `status`, `seal_status`, `quarantine_reason`, and `break_index` for hyper-tile and Edge. The §5.1 mapping uses the same table. When more than one row applies, `quarantine_reason` is the highest-priority applicable reason: `signer_not_pinned` > `missing_signature` > `seal_trailer` > `signature_known_signer` > `chain_break` > `crc`. This order keeps `signature_known_signer` reachable: a mid-chain signature failure after an earlier authentic record is that reason, not `chain_break`. A committed CRC is always `crc`, not `chain_break`. `break_index` is the **minimum** `break_index` over every applicable row that sets one, independent of the `quarantine_reason` priority (rows that leave `break_index` as None do not contribute). Valid floors end before that index: every index at or after `break_index` (when `Some`) is in `invalid_tile_indices` and is unauthenticated for consent (AT-165). `status` is `quarantined` if any applicable row says so. `seal_status` is `seal_failed` if a hyper-tile trailer or remnant fails verify, or an **active** Edge chain is broken by `chain_break` or by a CRC stop; a `signature_known_signer` reason, a torn-only stop, or a chain-inactive `chain_break` (including a seal-hash mismatch) does not by itself set `seal_failed` (`seal_status` stays `unsealed` when the flag is clear). Otherwise `seal_status` is `sealed` if a hyper-tile trailer verifies, or if Edge `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set and `seal_status` is not `seal_failed`; else `unsealed`. An Edge seal chain, **whether or not** it is active, is **intact** when no committed CRC, torn-stop, `SealedChainBreak`, reorder, or seal-hash mismatch applies. Whenever any type-70 record exists, `compute_seal_hash` is recomputed per tile and compared to the stored `seal_hash`. Zero type-70 `DeviceSealRecord`s are not themselves a `chain_break` and are not `missing_signature` when `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` is clear: there are no seal records to contradict the recomputed chain, so a chain-active unsigned file is `sealed` (AT-165).

    | Situation | `status` | `seal_status` | `quarantine_reason` | `break_index` | Floors / notes |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | Hyper-tile: no trailer and no remnant (no recognised end-cap magic, no partial/truncated trailer); no committed CRC | unsigned | unsealed | None | None | CRC-valid committed tiles are valid floors (AT-72). Complete trailer absence is indistinguishable from never-sealed and from a legitimate first-append unseal (DD-141). |
    | Hyper-tile: trailer verifies; no committed CRC | unsigned | sealed | None | None | CRC-valid committed tiles are valid floors. |
    | Hyper-tile: trailer or remnant present and verify fails; no committed CRC | quarantined | seal_failed | seal_trailer | None | CRC-valid committed tiles are listed as floors (display only); the file is unauthenticated as a whole (AT-90, DD-148). |
    | Hyper-tile: committed CRC; no trailer and no remnant | quarantined | unsealed | crc | first failed committed tile | That tile and every subsequent tile are not valid floors (AT-35). |
    | Hyper-tile: committed CRC, and a trailer or remnant is present (verify also fails) | quarantined | seal_failed | seal_trailer (priority over crc) | first failed committed tile | The CRC rule decides floors (AT-35). The file is unauthenticated as a whole. |
    | Hyper-tile: torn or uncommitted trailing tile only | from the other applicable rows (not Quarantined solely for torn) | from the other applicable rows | None from this row | first torn or uncommitted tile | That tile, if it satisfies the torn predicate, is listed in `torn_tile_indices`. Later indices that do not satisfy the predicate are uncommitted and are listed in `invalid_tile_indices` only, not in `torn_tile_indices`. None of these are valid floors. Status and `seal_status` come from the trailer / CRC rows. |
    | Edge: `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` clear, `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear, no type-70, no CRC | unsigned | unsealed | None | None | Unsigned floors are authenticated for consent (AT-154, DD-141). |
    | Edge: `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` set, `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear, no type-70, no CRC, chain intact | unsigned | sealed | None | None | Unsigned floors are authenticated for consent. Zero type-70 records are not a `chain_break` and are not `missing_signature` when the signed flag is clear (AT-165). |
    | Edge: chain active and intact; signatures verify under a pinned key | verified | sealed | None | None | Signed committed tiles are valid floors. An unsigned tail is still not a valid floor (row below, AT-210). |
    | Edge: chain active and intact; signatures verify under an embedded key; empty pin list | self_signed | sealed | None | None | Same floor rule as the Verified row. |
    | Edge: torn-only stop; chain active; signatures ok through the last committed tile | from signatures (not quarantined solely for torn) | sealed | None | first torn tile | Torn and subsequent tiles are not valid floors (AT-17). |
    | Edge: torn-only; chain inactive | from signatures (not quarantined solely for torn) | unsealed | None from this row | first torn tile | That tile and every subsequent tile are uncommitted and not valid floors, whether or not the chain is active (AT-165). Tiles that satisfy the torn predicate are in `torn_tile_indices`. Later uncommitted tiles that do not satisfy the predicate are in `invalid_tile_indices` only. |
    | Edge: committed CRC; chain active | quarantined | seal_failed | crc | first CRC-failed tile | That tile and every subsequent tile are not valid floors (AT-35). |
    | Edge: committed CRC; chain inactive | quarantined | unsealed | crc | first CRC-failed tile | Same floor rule. Not unauthenticated as a whole (DD-148). |
    | Edge: `SealedChainBreak`, reordered tiles, or seal-hash mismatch (not a mere signature failure, not a mere CRC); chain active | quarantined | seal_failed | chain_break | (highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 | Earlier tiles covered by an authentic record may remain valid floors; tiles at or after `break_index` (including any uncovered gap before the failing record) are untrusted (AT-220). |
    | Edge: chain inactive; type-70 present; signatures verify under a pinned key; every stored `seal_hash` equals the recomputed `compute_seal_hash` | verified | unsealed | None | None | Signed committed tiles are valid floors. An unsigned tail is still not a valid floor (AT-210). |
    | Edge: chain inactive; type-70 present; signatures verify under an embedded key; empty pin list; every stored `seal_hash` equals the recomputed `compute_seal_hash` | self_signed | unsealed | None | None | Same floor rule as the Verified row. |
    | Edge: chain inactive; type-70 present; seal-hash mismatch (stored `seal_hash` ≠ recomputed `compute_seal_hash` at that record's `tile_index`, whether or not the signature verifies over the stored hash) | quarantined | unsealed | chain_break | (highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 | Tiles at or after `break_index` are not valid floors and are untrusted for consent, including a tampered tile in the gap before the failing record; a later type-71 grant on a post-break tile does not release (AT-220). Earlier tiles covered by an authentic record may remain valid floors. |
    | Edge: signature failure after an earlier authentic record (DD-145 rules 3/4) | quarantined | unchanged by this reason alone | signature_known_signer | (highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1 | Tiles at or after `break_index` are not valid floors and are untrusted; earlier tiles covered by an authentic record may remain valid floors (AT-201). |
    | Edge: `signer_not_pinned` (DD-145) | quarantined | unchanged by this reason alone | signer_not_pinned | None | No valid floors; unauthenticated as a whole. |
    | Edge: `missing_signature` (DD-145, including empty pin list + embedded keys + first/all-fail) | quarantined | unchanged by this reason alone | missing_signature | None | No valid floors; unauthenticated as a whole (AT-195). |
    | Edge: unsigned tail — any authentic type-70 exists, and some tile index is greater than the highest authentic signed `tile_index` (except a verifying type-70 container tile) | unchanged by the tail alone | unchanged by the tail alone | unchanged by the tail alone | unchanged by the tail alone | Those indices are added to `invalid_tile_indices` and are untrusted for consent (AT-210). A verifying type-70 container is exempt and remains a valid floor if no other row invalidates it. |

3.  **First view:** a derived header sheet via `render_manifest` (also `view=banner`), clearly labeled as derived from the file header and `DeviceIdentityRecord`, not as a stored tile 78 manifest. Fields are only those present: magic, flags, `tile_stride`, `tile_count`, generation, timestamps (RFC3339 seconds UTC, `humantime::format_rfc3339_seconds`), `file_uuid`, device id, hardware model, firmware version, and the verification label from item 2. Missing fields are omitted, not invented. If no `DeviceIdentityRecord` is present, omit device fields and show a labeled "device identity record absent" note; still open the file (the EDGE §6.3 MUST is on the writer). If several identity records exist, the header sheet uses the first in tile order and later ones remain floors. Device identity fields on the header sheet and on type-64 identity floors are **exempt** from research-purpose record filtering: they identify the file and the device, not a consent-gated payload record (DD-53, AT-86).
4.  **Warehouse tour scaled to micro-tiles:** each committed valid micro-tile is a floor, via `render_warehouse`. `size_bytes` is 4096 or 8192. Offsets follow Variant A (`512 + i * tile_stride`) or Variant B (tile 0 at `P = tile_stride`, which is 4096 or 8192). Visual footprint follows §5.8. The Continue control on the header sheet is a `hit_test` target only; the client must call `proceed_to_warehouse` / `POST /v1/proceed` to reach the tour, same as §5.3 (DD-97, AT-152).
5.  Type 64 with 32-byte records renders as an equipment event log: timestamp (RFC3339 seconds UTC), `event_type` name from EDGE §5.2.2, severity, and a non-color category label derived from `event_type`: **fault** for `0x0003` and `0x0004`; **maintenance** for `0x0005` and `0x0006`; **normal** for `0x0001`, `0x0002`, and `0x0007`; **unknown** for any other value. Color is by severity (DD-07), which **overrides** the category coloring in EDGE §10 (a maintenance event with severity 0 uses the severity-0 color, not a category color). Color is never the only channel. The badge fills are the Okabe-Ito colours in DD-07 (Q-10); AT-19 asserts those fills plus the labels. Each event row's severity badge is a `SemanticNode` with role `label` and label `severity: N` (`N` is the decimal severity) carrying its CSS rect; AT-19 samples the pixmap at that rect's centre, scaled by `device_pixel_ratio`. Type 64 with 96-byte records renders as `DeviceIdentityRecord` using `edge_display_layouts()`. Type 64 with `record_count` 0, or a record size that is neither 32 nor 96, is an "unknown layout" floor, not event columns and not identity columns: it shows metadata only (type, size, `record_count`, record size) and **never** raw payload bytes (AT-67). BLOB and PROVENANCE floors likewise show metadata only, as in §5.3 item 5, including when the captured body is itself an S3A archive (AT-107). Every Edge floor whose type is other than 64 (the 32-byte and 96-byte layouts above), 70, or 71 shows metadata only (type, size, `record_count`) and never raw or decoded payload fields, including under a research grant or a research denial (AT-232). Type 70 and type 71 keep the rules already stated in this section and in §5.3 item 4.
6.  Edge consent uses type 71, not type 82 and not core `ConsentRecord`. Withholding is exactly the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table, applied to the Edge research-purpose class (event-log payloads). Header sheet and type-64 identity floors remain visible (AT-86). `withheld_count` is reported when event-log payloads are withheld (AT-23), under the §5.3 item 4 counting rule (valid floors only, except on a banner with no valid floors; AT-134, AT-194, AT-195).

7.  No transcoding into a 128 KiB hyper-tile file is required in order to view (EDGE §1.2). Import via `import_edge_archive` is a separate, existing engine operation, not the viewer path.

The microcontroller continues to run only `s3a-edge`. It does not link kitesurf or the host (AT-22, DD-06).

### 5.5 Scholar wiki browser (3-pass) — hub and PC only

Phone v1 does not implement this section (DD-45).

*   Wiki Markdown from under `--wiki-root` / `--corpus-root` is parsed with `pulldown-cmark` (tables enabled via `Options::ENABLE_TABLES`; raw HTML disabled or escaped) and painted with `render_page`. No JS, no remote subresources, no JS context. Missing pages are reported as missing. HTML files opened under an allowed root are also `render_page`, with JavaScript disabled and **no HTML subresources of any kind** (network or local: a relative `<img>`, `<link>`, or `@font-face` is not loaded from disk or the network; DD-77, DD-118, AT-121, AT-167). A CommonMark image in a wiki or corpus Markdown page may be loaded only under the DD-118 exception (a destination that resolves inside the allowed request root of the opened page); `http`, `https`, and `file://` image destinations are not loaded (AT-233).
*   **Current candidate list (DD-61, F-07, F-35).** There is no persisted candidate list. `GET /v1/candidates` and the membership check for select, download, and skip **recompute the set at that request**. A `paper_id` that is in the set at request time is accepted even if no prior GET listed it. A `paper_id` that is not in the set at request time is `404` `unknown_paper`, even if an earlier GET listed it (AT-105). The set is the union of:
    1.  every regular file matching the exact glob `{root}/papers/SRC-*.md` (not recursive) under `--wiki-root` and under `--corpus-root`, opened under the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule from that root's startup dirfd (a symlink, FIFO, or other non-regular file, including one that escapes its root, is skipped, not followed, and does not hang the walk; AT-218). Identity is the `SRC-NNNN` stem of the filename; if the YAML `source_id` is present and does not equal that stem, the file is omitted from the list and no paper id is written to logs. A candidate path that cannot be canonicalized inside its root is silently omitted. `GET /v1/candidates` never returns `409` `path_escape`;
    2.  every live (non-tombstone) `AcademicPaperRecord` in every regular hyper-tile `*.s3a` file found by a recursive walk of `--corpus-root`, after applying marks, the Quarantined-corpus rule, and the valid-floor rules (`AcademicPaperRecord` is mark-only and has no `subject_id`; DD-147, AT-166). The walk opens each corpus `*.s3a` with the host pin list from `--pinned-keys-file` (or an empty list if the flag is omitted), using pack `open` / the §5.4 algorithm. **Edge files are skipped for item 2** (this pack does not define Scholar `AcademicPaperRecord` extraction from the Edge profile). A mark-withheld or invalid-tile record is not a candidate. A **Quarantined** corpus file (including a hyper-tile whose seal or CRC failed) contributes **no** `AcademicPaperRecord` candidates (privacy-safe: a file whose seal failed does not leak identities into the list). There is **no join** of an SRC stem to a `paper_id` (v4's "already the same paper" wording is deleted; there is no grounded join key). If the same `paper_id` appears in more than one live, un-withheld record in a non-quarantined file, keep one: sort the containing `.s3a` paths in **bytewise ascending order of their canonical absolute paths**, then take the first record in tile order in the first of those files. The walk uses the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule from the `--corpus-root` startup dirfd, with directory fds for descent (`openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK)`). A symlink file or directory is skipped, not followed, and not descended. A FIFO or other non-regular file, an oversize file, or any other failed open or read is a silent skip; the walk does not hang (AT-218). These size and parse rules apply to **item 1 and item 2**: a file larger than `--max-open-bytes` is skipped as a fail-to-open (the host reads at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes); a dossier whose YAML front matter is missing, unparseable, or not UTF-8 is **omitted** from the list and no paper id is written to logs (AT-188). The YAML parser **rejects aliases and anchors** (no alias-expansion / "billion laughs"); a dossier that uses them is unparseable and is omitted the same way. The host does not invent a numeric alias budget. There is **no** candidate cache in v1 (DD-143): every `GET /v1/candidates` and every membership check is a fresh walk plus a current-clock filter (DD-110). Ashley may reinstate a cache later. Membership for select/download/skip is computed before the write mutex is taken.
    If the same relative path `papers/SRC-XXXX.md` is a **qualifying item-1 candidate** under both roots, **`--wiki-root` wins** (the corpus copy is omitted); there is one candidate. A wiki path that exists but is not a qualifying candidate (symlink, bad or missing front matter, oversize, or a failed safe-open) does not shadow a qualifying corpus copy. Building the list twice writes nothing (AT-24). Each candidate includes `title` (dossier YAML `title` if present; the empty string for `AcademicPaperRecord` candidates), `source_url` (from the optional YAML key `source_url` **only** when it is a valid `http` or `https` URL, else `null`), `on_disk` (true when a usable local file exists; see the source decision table), and `page_path` (the absolute canonical path of the winning-root dossier file, or `null` for an `AcademicPaperRecord` candidate, which has no dossier path), and `archive_path` (the absolute canonical path of the winning corpus `.s3a` file for an `AcademicPaperRecord` candidate, or `null` for a dossier candidate). `POST /v1/open` of a non-null `page_path` yields `paper_identity` equal to that candidate's `id` (AT-101). `archive_path` is not a dossier path and is not a `page_path`; opening it does not yield a `paper_identity`. A `file://`, `data:`, or other non-`http`/`https` value is stored as `null`. The host does not HTTP-fetch that value and does not read the `file://` target from the filesystem. If that candidate also has no usable `drive_path`, download is `no_source`. Such a file is opened only via `/v1/open` of a path under an allowed root (AT-55, AT-123).
*   **Source decision table (DD-59, F-06).** A dossier `.md` file is **not** itself the on-disk source. On-disk bytes, when present, come from a `drive_path`:
    *   an absolute `drive_path`, and a relative `drive_path` after it is resolved against the **parent directory of the dossier file**, is **lexically normalized** (resolving `.` and `..` only; no symlink resolution), then matched to the unique allowed root (`--wiki-root`, `--corpus-root`, or `--view-root` if set) that is a path-component prefix of that normalized path, and is **not** under `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir`. No match, or more than one match, means `on_disk: false`. The walk then starts from that root's startup dirfd with `O_NOFOLLOW` (final component `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`), the same shared safe-open rule as a request path. A relative `drive_path` whose `..` segments would leave every allowed root is `on_disk: false`; it is not `409` `path_escape`.
    A `drive_path` that is missing, cannot be opened as a regular file under DD-146 (walk each component with `O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat` the fd, require a regular file, size at most `--max-open-bytes`), escapes those allowed roots, or canonicalizes inside `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir`, is not a usable on-disk source (`on_disk: false`); select and download of that candidate are not `409` `path_escape` (AT-150, AT-197, AT-202). Listing may use `lstat` for the published `on_disk` field. Select and download **open and validate the `drive_path` fd once per request, before step 0 and outside the write mutex** (final component `O_NONBLOCK`, DD-146). The source-table row for this request uses that result. They copy from that fd in step 2b and do not reopen the path by name later, and they do not switch rows mid-request if the fd later becomes unusable. `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir` hold kite-written artifacts; they remain allowed `/v1/open` roots in Scholar mode.
    A Windows Google Drive path, or any other `drive_path` that does not so canonicalize, is not an on-disk source (`on_disk: false`); select and download of that candidate are not `409` `path_escape` (AT-150). Remote bytes, when present, come from the optional front-matter key `source_url` (draft name; not present in the grounded 3Pass tree; fixtures and future dossiers may set it). `AcademicPaperRecord` typically has neither a `source_url` nor an on-disk file. `POST /v1/open` does not accept a URL. At select/download:

    | `source_url` | usable `drive_path` | Fetch | Snapshot | Download delivery |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | yes | no | yes (unless a snapshot already exists for this identity in the current archive, any verb) | yes, on 2xx decoded body | bytes from the snapshot BLOB tiles |
    | no | yes | no | no | copy of the on-disk file |
    | yes | yes | no (prefer on-disk) | no | copy of the on-disk file (AT-129) |
    | no | no | no | no | `200` with `path: null`, `download_error: "no_source"`; inclusion and index still written (AT-128) |

    If a **usable snapshot** exists for `(file_uuid, identity)` (a non-null `snapshot_sha256` on the last index line for that key that has a non-null `snapshot_sha256` (step 0, DD-152) whose `{sha}.s3a` is present and passes the DD-103 reuse checks), download **delivers from that snapshot** and does not copy a now-usable `drive_path`; the table's "copy of the on-disk file" cells apply only when no such usable snapshot exists (F-14). Fetch is triggered only by download of a request-time candidate in the first row of that table, and only when no usable snapshot exists for the key (a missing recorded `{sha}.s3a` is not usable and is a re-fetch, DD-152). Select does not fetch, snapshot, or fingerprint. If a usable snapshot already exists for the identity in the current working archive, or a fetch for that `(file_uuid, identity)` is already in flight (DD-112), download does not start a second GET and either waits for the in-flight result or reassembles bytes from that snapshot's BLOB tiles (DD-61, AT-104, AT-126).
*   Pass 1 list and skip write nothing. The 15-second timer may be shown in the native UI; it is client-side; expiry does not call a host write endpoint (DD-08). AT-29 waits 15 seconds after only `GET /v1/candidates`, `POST /v1/open`, and `GET /v1/frame` and checks byte-identity of the working archive, `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, `--data-dir`, and `--log-dir`.
*   **Numbered write steps (F-05, DD-84, DD-120).** Select and download share this order. The process-wide write mutex is held only for the local disk I/O and consent-state access named here; it is released before a fetch or LRS POST and re-acquired for the next disk sub-step, and is never held across network I/O.
    Step 0 and step 1 run in **one** mutex hold (DD-102). If this request will **start** a GET, the in-flight registry insert happens in that same hold, **only after** step 1 has succeeded or been skipped (DD-112). The verb is used only for inclusion-record idempotency. Snapshot existence and the in-flight registry are keyed by `(file_uuid, identity)` (DD-112).
    0.  (mutex, with 1) Look up the last matching inclusion-index line for `(file_uuid, identity, verb)`. If a matching line is present, skip step 1. A **usable snapshot** exists if the **last** index line for this `(file_uuid, identity)` (any verb) that has a non-null `snapshot_sha256` names a `{sha}.s3a` that is present and passes the DD-103 reuse checks (DD-104: last matching line wins). `snapshot_invalid` applies only to that last recorded sha: if its file is **present** but fails those checks, this request returns `200` with `snapshot: null` and `snapshot_error: "snapshot_invalid"` (a download also has `download_error: "snapshot_invalid"` and `path: null`), keeps the inclusion record, does not overwrite the file, starts no fetch, and does not fall back to a usable `drive_path` (AT-209), even if an earlier line's sha would be usable. If that last recorded `{sha}.s3a` is **missing** (ENOENT under the shared safe-open rule), there is no usable snapshot: the host does not return `snapshot_invalid` and, if the source table requires a fetch, runs step 2 (DD-152, AT-228), even if an earlier line's file is present but failing. If no line has a non-null sha, there is no usable snapshot. Run step 2 only if no usable snapshot exists and the source table requires a fetch. Run 2b for a download only when there are bytes to deliver: a usable on-disk fd, an existing usable snapshot, or a snapshot this request (or the GET it waited on) has just published. Do not run 2b after a failed reuse check, a fetch failure, `snapshot_too_large`, or `snapshot_conflict`. If a matching verb line was present, do **not** emit a new LRS statement (step 3 is skipped).
    1.  (same mutex hold) Write the inclusion record (tile 10) and append the inclusion-index line (including a null `snapshot_sha256` if no snapshot exists yet). If step 1 fails, this request must not insert a registry entry (AT-89).
    2.  If the source table requires a fetch and no snapshot exists for `(file_uuid, identity)`: if the registry shows a GET for this key is running, wait for its result (mutex released during the wait for a GET already started by another request); this waiter **never** starts a GET in the same request. Otherwise, after step 1 has succeeded or been skipped, insert into the registry in this mutex hold and start one GET (mutex released across the GET). Then, under the mutex, if this request started the GET: write the snapshot (temp file + fsync + atomic rename) or record the fetch-failure (including `snapshot_too_large`), `snapshot_conflict`, or disk-write-failure outcome, **and remove the registry entry in this same mutex hold**, and on a successful publish **and** a successful index append, record the snapshot SHA-256 under **this request's verb** (the index is append-only; there is no in-place rewrite). If this was a DD-152 re-fetch whose captured bytes hash to a `new_sha` different from the previously recorded sha, that append is a new index line with `snapshot_sha256 = new_sha` even when step 1 was skipped (no second tile-10). If they hash to the recorded sha, the existing index line stands and no new line is required. If the temp write, fsync, or rename fails, the temp is deleted, the inclusion is kept, the registry entry is removed in this hold, step 3 still runs on the request that wrote the inclusion line, and the response is `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and `lrs_status` (AT-212). If the rename succeeds and the index append then fails, the published snapshot file is **left** for a later DD-103 reuse, no `snapshot_sha256` is recorded, the registry entry is removed in this hold, the inclusion is kept, step 3 still runs, and the response is the same `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and `lrs_status` (AT-213). A request that only waited for an in-flight GET does **not** append an index line. It receives the starter's outcome: the published snapshot path; a fetch failure as `fetch_error` (including `fetch_error: "snapshot_too_large"`) and, on a download, `download_error: "fetch_failed"`, `path: null`, `snapshot: null` (2b is skipped); `409` `snapshot_conflict` with the waiter's own `included` / `lrs_status`; or `500` `write_failed` with the waiter's own `included` / `lrs_status`. A download waiter has no bytes for 2b when the starter failed, conflicted, or returned `snapshot_too_large`. Retries are governed by step 0's any-verb snapshot test, not by "this request's" line.
    2b.  For a download request only: (mutex) deliver bytes into `--download-dir` via temp + fsync + rename (copy from the already-open DD-146 fd, or reassembly from the snapshot, which the host opens under the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule). For an on-disk copy, the host hashes the bytes as they are written to the temp file from that fd and names the published file from that hash; it does not reopen the source path by name. The fd must already have been accepted as a regular file, and the host reads at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes from it (otherwise there is no usable on-disk source, 2b is not a hanging copy, and a later swap of the path cannot copy unexpected bytes into `--download-dir`; AT-197, AT-202). If reassembly from a snapshot fails, `download_error` is `"snapshot_invalid"`, no file is written in `--download-dir`, and the inclusion record is kept (AT-180). If more than `--max-open-bytes` arrive during the 2b copy from a fd that passed the pre-step-0 cap check, the host deletes the temp, writes no published download file, keeps the inclusion record, does not start a mid-request `source_url` fetch, and returns `200` with `download_error: "source_changed"` (the source-table row is not switched mid-request). If the 2b temp write, fsync, or rename fails, the temp is deleted, no published download file is written, the inclusion is kept, no in-flight registry entry remains from this 2b (registry entries are for a GET and are removed in the step-2 publish hold, before 2b; an on-disk copy never creates one), step 3 still runs on the request that wrote the inclusion line, and the response is `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and `lrs_status` (AT-214). A 2b failure affects **only** the request whose 2b failed. A waiter of the GET already received the step-2 outcome (the snapshot path) and then runs its own 2b; if that waiter's own 2b fails, that waiter gets `500` `write_failed` with its own `included` / `lrs_status`.
    3.  If step 0 did not skip step 3, and `lrs_inhibit` is clear (that inhibit check is lock-free, DD-144) and, under a brief mutex hold, the in-memory flag is `on` at the pre-send check (the flag is read only under the mutex, §5.2.1), emit the LRS statement (no mutex across the HTTP POST). After the POST returns or is aborted because `lrs_inhibit` was set, re-acquire the mutex to record the outcome as in §5.5 "HTTP outcomes" (DD-134). An aborted POST is `lrs_status: "skipped"`. Step 3 still runs after `snapshot_conflict` and `download_conflict` on the request that first wrote the inclusion line; those responses include `lrs_status` (AT-94, AT-180). A later request whose step 0 matches skips step 3 (`lrs_status` is `skipped`, or `not_configured` whenever `--lrs-url` is unset — `not_configured` takes precedence over `skipped`); that includes a later download whose reassembly fails (`snapshot_invalid`, AT-180).

    If step 1 fails, skip 2, 2b, and 3, leave `--download-dir` unchanged, and leave no registry entry (AT-89, AT-127). The earlier DD-56 sentence that kept a download after a failed inclusion is **deleted**; it is unreachable once 2b follows 1. If the fetch fails or times out after step 1, the inclusion record is kept, `snapshot` is `null`, `fetch_error` is set, `download_error` is `"fetch_failed"` on a download, 2b is skipped, and step 3 is still attempted if consent is on at the pre-send check (`EXT_CONTENT_SHA256` is present if and only if the index has a non-null `snapshot_sha256` for `(file_uuid, identity)` at step 3, and is equal to it). A fetch is not started when a **usable** snapshot exists for the identity (DD-152). A repeat retries the fetch when no usable snapshot exists for `(file_uuid, identity)` (any verb) — including when no index line has a non-null `snapshot_sha256`, or when the only recorded `{sha}.s3a` files are missing (DD-152) — and the source table requires a fetch (AT-186, AT-228).
*   Tracing consent on/off, and "zero LRS requests when off", are enforced including on retries (DD-66). Consent toggling is only `POST /v1/consent` (DD-50).
*   Only a UI-token `POST /v1/select` or `POST /v1/download` creates an inclusion record (DD-32, DD-60). "Human select" means "a call authenticated with the UI token", including one made by the native window. AT-28 tests an unauthenticated (401) call.
*   Pass 1 list and skip never write paper ids or URLs into `--log-dir`, and none into `--data-dir` other than `inclusion-index.jsonl`. The retry queue under `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` may hold identities only when consent is on and a sendable or `.permanent` statement file exists (`.permanent` files are kept while consent remains `on` and are deleted with the directory on revoke). Render caches and per-request temp files live in the ephemeral directory of §5.2.2, not under `--data-dir` or `--log-dir`. (AT-61; F-01: the index is local research data, like the working archive, and stays in `--data-dir`.)

**Inclusion record (DD-46, DD-61).** Tile 10 `HumanLrsRecord`: `timestamp_sec` is the write time (Unix seconds); `actor_hash` is `hash_iri(--researcher-id)` as implemented in `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs` (FNV-1a 64-bit; the field is `u64`); `verb_id` is the id of the existing `xapi.rs` verb chosen in Q-07; `object_hash` is CRC32C of the UTF-8 paper identity, stored in the `u32` field (CRC32C is already used in S3A); `decision_score` is 0; `ai_trace_coord` is the default / empty coordinate; flags are `FLAG_ACTIVE`. Because a 32-bit CRC is not a unique pointer to a path or identity, **idempotency is not evaluated from `object_hash` alone.**

**Inclusion index (F-01, F-02, F-03).** `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl` is local research data in `--data-dir`. Each successful inclusion appends one JSON object:

```
{"paper_identity":"<id>","verb_id":<n>,"object_hash":<n>,"timestamp_sec":<n>,"working_archive":"<canonical path>","file_uuid":"<hex of FileHeader.file_uuid>","snapshot_sha256":"<lowercase hex or null>"}
```

The idempotency key is **current working-archive `file_uuid` + paper identity string + verb id**, looked up among index lines whose `file_uuid` equals the open archive's `FileHeader.file_uuid`. Lines whose `file_uuid` or canonical `working_archive` path do not match the open archive are **non-matching** and do not suppress a new inclusion (a deleted and recreated archive has a new `file_uuid`). `snapshot_sha256` is null until a successful snapshot exists. The index is **append-only**: a later successful step 2 appends a new line with the same identity, `file_uuid`, and verb and the new `snapshot_sha256`. Lookup uses the **last** matching line (DD-104). An idempotent second select of an identity that already has a **usable** snapshot returns `200` with `ok: true` and `snapshot` set to `--snapshot-dir/{snapshot_sha256}.s3a` when that last recorded file is present and passes the DD-103 reuse checks (AT-83). If that last recorded file is **present** and the checks fail, it returns `200` with `snapshot: null` and `snapshot_error: "snapshot_invalid"`, keeps the inclusion, and does not fetch or overwrite (AT-209). If that last recorded file is **missing**, the request re-fetches when the source table requires a fetch (DD-152, AT-228). A later download of that identity, when a usable snapshot exists, reassembles bytes from the snapshot BLOB tiles and does not fetch again, even if a `drive_path` is now usable (F-14).

Each index mutation copies the current file (or starts empty) to a temp named `s3a-kite-tmp-{pid}-*` in `--data-dir`, appends the new line, `fsync`s the temp, publishes with an atomic rename, `fsync`s `--data-dir`, and uses mode `0600`. There is no in-place rewrite of a JSONL line.

If the index file is **missing** at startup, the host creates an empty one (mode `0600`) and does **not** skip writes based on existing tile-10 `object_hash` values (duplicates of older records are possible; dropping a real inclusion is not). Creating the empty file is an explicit contract exception (F-29). If the index file is **present but malformed** (not UTF-8, a non-JSON line, a partial last line, or a line missing a required key), startup is an error that names the file; the host does not truncate, skip, or invent recovery (AT-158). The index is **not** subject to `--max-open-bytes`; a well-formed file larger than that cap still starts (AT-224). No rotation bound is specified (none is invented).

Step 1 order is: write the tile-10 record, then append the index line. If the index append fails after the tile-10 write, the response is `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and **no** `lrs_status` (steps 2, 2b, and 3 are skipped), and the record is kept (AT-158). A later select may then write a second tile-10 and emit LRS on that later request (the same duplicate policy as a missing index). The phrase "the inclusion record points at a path" is not used: the index stores the identity string, the archive identity, and the optional snapshot digest.

**Idempotency (DD-34, DD-154, Q-07).** Select, download, and deselect use three existing verb IRIs from `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs` (none invented here): select `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/opened`, download `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/saved`, deselect `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/removed`. Repeating the same identity and verb against the same archive `file_uuid` does not emit a new LRS statement and does not write a second event record of that verb. Select-then-download is two events (closer-read, then inclusion) and a single inclusion record / successful snapshot, written on download (AT-83, AT-234). Idempotency of snapshots applies only to **successful** artifacts: a failed snapshot is retried by a later download of the same paper (AT-186).

**LRS client (DD-65).** When `--lrs-url` is set, the host POSTs to `{lrs-url}/xapi/statements` (`--lrs-url` is the origin; the host appends `/xapi/statements`). `--lrs-url` must be exactly `scheme://host[:port]` with an optional single trailing `/` that is stripped at startup. Any other path (including `/xapi`), userinfo, query, or fragment is a startup error that names `--lrs-url`. The scheme must be `https`, except `http` is allowed only when the host is a **loopback IP literal**: an IPv4 address in `127.0.0.0/8`, IPv6 `::1`, or the IPv4-mapped form of a `127.0.0.0/8` address (for example `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]`). A hostname (including `localhost`) or any other address with `http` is a startup error that names `--lrs-url`. There is no DNS lookup to decide loopback (AT-54 uses IP literals). The request uses HTTP Basic from `--lrs-credentials-file` (the s3a-lrs-server format: lines `user:password[:scopes[:archive]]`; `#` comments and blank lines ignored; kite uses the first credential line). Each credential line is parsed exactly as `s3a-lrs-server` `load_credentials_file` at s3a-storage `b7bdcda` (`main.rs`): the line is split on every `:`. `parts[0]` is the username and `parts[1]` is the password; `parts[2]` (scopes) and `parts[3]` (archive), if present, are ignored by kite. A password cannot contain `:`. A line with fewer than two fields, or with an empty username or empty password, is a startup error that names the file. After comments and blank lines are ignored, a file with no credential line is a startup error that names the file (AT-182). The request also sends `X-Experience-API-Version: 1.0.3` and `Content-Type: application/json`. The body is one xAPI 1.0.3 statement:

*   `id`: a UUID generated at first emit and stored in the retry-queue file, reused on retry (so a replay is the same statement).
*   `actor`: `{ "objectType": "Agent", "account": { "homePage": "<--lrs-actor-homepage>", "name": "<--researcher-id>" } }`. The LRS therefore sees the researcher id; `actor_hash` in the local tile does not anonymize the statement (privacy is the researcher's consent).
*   `verb.id`: the Q-07 IRI from `xapi.rs`.
*   `object.id`: the candidate `source_url` if present; otherwise `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/activities/paper/` plus the percent-encoded paper identity. That host is already used by Scholar verb and extension IRIs in `xapi.rs`; this spec does not mint a new host.
*   `object.definition.extensions` includes `EXT_PAPER_ID` (the identity). It includes `EXT_SOURCE_URL` when a `source_url` exists; the value is the candidate's listed `source_url` (pre-redirect), not a final redirected URL. `EXT_CONTENT_SHA256` is present if and only if, at step 3, the inclusion index has a non-null `snapshot_sha256` for `(file_uuid, identity)`, and the extension value equals that digest (DD-137). An on-disk-only download with no snapshot omits it.
*   `timestamp`: RFC3339 seconds UTC of the inclusion write.

AT-110 asserts this shape. A batch of several statements may be sent as a JSON array, per xAPI, but v1 retries are one statement per request.

**Retry queue (DD-51, DD-66).** Queued LRS statements live on disk under `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` (the directory is created mode `0700` if missing; one file per statement, including the statement `id` and body, mode `0600`) and survive a host restart. If `--data-dir/lrs-retry` already exists at startup, `lstat` (no following) must show a directory; a regular file, FIFO, symlink, or other non-directory is a startup error that names `lrs-retry` and binds nothing. Revoke deletion of that directory uses `unlinkat` of each entry within a directory fd opened with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY` (it does not follow a symlink, and must not delete through a replaced name into `--data-dir`). **Every LRS send, including a startup retry, first checks `lrs_inhibit` (lock-free) and that the in-memory consent state is `on`.** If `lrs_inhibit` is set or consent is `off`, the host does not send and writes no new retry file. If consent is `off` at process start (after the sidecar locks are held), the host deletes the queue and issues zero LRS requests (AT-111). Revoke (`POST /v1/consent` to `off`) deletes that directory, including sendable, claimed, and permanent files. If deletion fails, the host logs, may write one last `500` `revoke_delete_failed`, and exits non-zero (DD-138, AT-124).

HTTP outcomes (DD-134, AT-191, AT-192): any 2xx is success. A `409` is success for **every** emit, first or retry (the statement `id` is host-minted, so 409 means the statement already exists). 408, 429, and 5xx are queued for retry. Any other 4xx is a permanent failure: log it, keep a `.permanent` file, and never retry that statement. No HTTP response (timeout, connection refused, DNS failure, or I/O or reset mid-body) is treated like 5xx: queued if inhibit is inactive and the flag is still `on` in the same mutex hold, else `skipped`. An LRS POST aborted because inhibit became active or consent became `off` is `skipped` under that same rule (no retry file) (AT-156, DD-131). A TLS certificate-validation failure (webpki, name, or chain mismatch) is a permanent failure (`failed`). A retry-file write, fsync, or rename that fails (`{id}.json`, `{id}.claimed`, or `{id}.permanent`) is logged, the attempt is `lrs_status: "failed"`, and the process stays up: this is not a DD-138 fail-closed event (AT-191).

Queue file names (all under `--data-dir/lrs-retry/`, mode `0600`): `{id}.json` is sendable, `{id}.claimed` is an in-flight exclusive claim, and `{id}.permanent` is a permanent failure. Replay is serialized: under the write mutex the host claims a sendable file by renaming `{id}.json` to `{id}.claimed` before releasing the mutex to send; two retries must not POST the same statement concurrently. A completed success deletes the claimed file; a queued outcome renames it back to `{id}.json`; a permanent outcome renames it to `{id}.permanent`. An aborted or skipped replay (`lrs_status: "skipped"`) leaves the `{id}.claimed` file for a revoke delete and performs no rename; if `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` is already gone, the replay does nothing and must not recreate that directory. At Scholar start, after the sidecar lock is held and if consent is `on`, the host renames every leftover `{id}.claimed` back to `{id}.json` before replay (a crash mid-send must not drop a consented statement) (AT-91). Writing a new retry file, and the consent / inhibit check that allows that write, run in the **same mutex hold** (AT-156). If inhibit is active or the flag is `off` in that hold, the host writes nothing and the caller's `lrs_status` is `"skipped"`.

Backoff and a maximum attempt count are Q-09; until Ashley sets them, the host retries at process start (only if consent is `on`) and after each later successful LRS emit, and surfaces a visible error after a failed attempt without dropping a sendable (non-permanent) queue file.

### 5.6 Write path and sealed snapshots

**Target (DD-27, DD-103).** Inclusion records go to `--working-archive`. Snapshots go in `--snapshot-dir` as `{lowercase hex SHA-256 of the captured bytes}.s3a`. If that path already exists, the host **does not overwrite it**. Before reuse it opens the existing file under the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule (startup `--snapshot-dir` dirfd, `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, `fstat` regular file, at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes), runs `verify_sealed_archive`, and checks that the archive holds BLOB chunks whose `blob_key` equals that SHA-256 and that reassemble to bytes hashing to that SHA-256. If the existing file is larger than `--max-open-bytes`, is not a regular file, is a symlink, or fails those checks, reuse fails: `409` `snapshot_conflict` on a newly fetched capture, or HTTP 200 `snapshot_invalid` when the sha is already recorded (AT-215). Any file under `--snapshot-dir` larger than `--max-open-bytes` (for example one planted there) is `file_too_large` on `/v1/open` (AT-170). Kite does not publish a snapshot larger than `--max-open-bytes` (AT-227). This pack does not invent a numeric snapshot-size overhead formula. At publish time, if the sealed snapshot file is larger than `--max-open-bytes`, the host does **not** publish it, deletes the temp, records no `snapshot_sha256`, and the request that produced it returns `200` with `fetch_error: "snapshot_too_large"`; a download also has `download_error: "fetch_failed"`, `path: null`, and `snapshot: null`, and skips 2b. The inclusion is kept, step 3 still runs, and the registry entry is removed so a later select or download may retry; waiters receive this as a fetch failure (AT-227). The startup check `--max-fetch-bytes` ≤ `--max-open-bytes` covers only the download-dir hash check, not snapshot overhead. If every check passes, the existing file is reused, its PROVENANCE document (the first capture's `url` and `captured` time) is left unchanged, and both identities' index lines store the same `snapshot_sha256` (AT-157). If any check fails on a **newly fetched** capture, the host still runs step 3 and returns `409` `snapshot_conflict` with `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1, plus `lrs_status`, keeps the inclusion record, and does not overwrite the file (AT-180). A sha already recorded in the index that fails those checks is HTTP 200 `snapshot_invalid` (AT-209), not this 409. Two `source_url`s that return identical bytes therefore share one snapshot when the existing file verifies. The corpus and wiki are read-only. The snapshot directory is an allowed open path, so AT-31's re-open is allowed.

**API and locking (DD-28, DD-102, DD-105, DD-114).** `TileWriter::create` / `open_append` use the engine `try_lock_exclusive` on the working-archive file for each write (DD-114 lock 2), in addition to the process-lifetime sidecar `flock`s held since startup (DD-114 lock 1). `TileWriter::create` (plus `enable_addon` and the create-time `ConsentRecord`) runs **only at startup**, on the fd from the startup `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` open, never in a request hold. `TileWriter::open_append` runs in the current step-0/1 hold, on that hold's already-validated fd (the `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` magic link or an fd-taking `s3a-engine` constructor; `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` is for this working-archive hold only, not for a snapshot or download temp that already holds `LOCK_EX` on its own fd). The host does not keep a startup-lifetime working-archive fd and does not pass the working-archive path string into a constructor that would re-resolve the final component by name. The name-binding re-check in §5.2.2 runs immediately before each tile-10 write (AT-203, AT-219). `ADDON_EDU` is set on create of the **working archive**. The `ADDON_EDU` check on an **existing** working-archive file runs in the step-0/1 hold on every select/download, whether or not step 1 is skipped (not at startup): a missing bit is `409` `addon_missing`, nothing is written, and the request has no `lrs_status` (AT-46). On `create` of the **working archive only**, kite also writes one core `ConsentRecord` using `ConsentRecord::new` with every field set as follows (AT-79, AT-159, AT-173). Snapshots are not created with this path: they receive no `ConsentRecord` (AT-107) and are encoded as BLOB + PROVENANCE (Snapshot encoding below), not via working-archive `TileWriter::create`.

*   `subject_id` = `actor_id_to_subject_id(hash_iri(--researcher-id))` (the helper in `s3a-core` `consent.rs`; `hash_iri` is the same FNV-1a `u64` as `actor_hash`).
*   `study_id` = 0
*   `purpose` = `ConsentPurpose::Research`
*   `scope` = 0 (all)
*   `granted_at` = Unix UTC seconds of the create
*   `expires_at` = 0 (no expiry)
*   `withdrawn_at` = 0
*   `legal_hold` = 0
*   `_reserved` = seven zero bytes
*   `granted_by` = `hash_iri(--researcher-id)`
*   `prev_consent_hash` = 32 zero bytes (first record)
*   `consent_hash` = `ConsentRecord::compute_hash` over the preceding 104 bytes (as `new` already sets)

`HumanLrsRecord` has **no** `subject_id` field. Inclusion records are therefore not withheld by `ConsentRecord` (they are withheld only by marks, §5.3 item 4). The create-time record is an archive-level research grant for the researcher's `subject_id`, so any later payload record that *does* carry that subject remains granted. Because that grant is a consent record, the working archive is in evaluated mode under the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table, so a payload record that carries a **different** `subject_id` is withheld (AT-173). It is not required for visibility of a file that has **zero** consent records before create (DD-67). AT-79 requires `withheld_count == 0` for the owner's inclusion records.

**Atomic publish and temp cleanup (DD-122).** Snapshot and download files are written to a temp file in `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir` respectively, `fsync`ed, and published with an atomic rename. Temp names are `s3a-kite-tmp-{pid}-*` in that directory. A basename matching `s3a-kite-tmp-*` **directly inside** `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir` is not openable via `/v1/open` (`409` `not_regular_file`, checked after the DD-146 walk and before the read; AT-171). The same basename under `--view-root`, `--wiki-root`, or `--corpus-root` is a normal request path. Every `s3a-kite-tmp-*` temp — snapshot, download, inclusion-index, consent-file, and `revoke-pending` — is held `LOCK_EX` on its own fd from create until publish or delete (there is no separate marker file beside a single temp file). The same temp-name pattern is used for inclusion-index, consent-file, and `revoke-pending` publishes (in `--data-dir` or the consent-file parent). At startup, after the sidecar locks are held, the host deletes a stale same-euid `s3a-kite-tmp-*` **file** in `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, `--data-dir`, and the consent-file parent only if `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB` on that file succeeds. Startup temp cleanup never deletes the configured `--working-archive` or `--consent-state-file` path, even if that basename matches `s3a-kite-tmp-*` (those basenames are also rejected at startup; AT-208). The `--snapshot-dir/.lock` and `--download-dir/.lock` sidecars already exclude a second host from those directories. It does **not** delete on a "pid not running" check alone. Names whose pid cannot be parsed are skipped and are not deleted. It must not delete a live temp belonging to another same-euid host (AT-187). Snapshot and download temps are written and sealed through an fd-taking engine API on the **same file description** that holds that `LOCK_EX`. The host must not reopen a snapshot or download temp via `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` or by path for `TileWriter`, `seal_archive`, or `try_lock_exclusive`: that would be a second file description and would conflict with the host's own `LOCK_EX`. The `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` route remains allowed only for the working-archive hold, which has no host `LOCK_EX` on that fd (the sidecar lock is a different file). Concurrent select/download/consent disk writes are serialized by the process-wide write mutex and de-duplicated by the in-flight fetch registry (AT-126). Skip does not take the mutex.

**DD-63 retired (F-22).** v4's "kite-written snapshot exemption" is deleted. A BLOB+PROVENANCE-only snapshot contains no `ConsentRecord`, so DD-67 already leaves it unrestricted. Research-purpose filtering is never skipped merely because a file sits in `--snapshot-dir`. A third-party archive copied into `--snapshot-dir` is filtered by the same §5.3 / §5.4 rules as any other file (AT-107, AT-108).

**Snapshot encoding (DD-47, F-33).** Captured bytes are stored in core BLOB tiles (68) using the 40-byte prefix in `docs/spec/registry.md` / `s3a-pack-clean` `chunk_tiles`: `blob_key` (32-byte SHA-256 of the **complete** captured bytes), little-endian `chunk_index`, little-endian `total_chunks`, then chunk data. Each tile payload is at most `HYPER_TILE_PAYLOAD_SIZE` bytes, so each chunk is at most `HYPER_TILE_PAYLOAD_SIZE - 40` bytes. Reassembly concatenates chunks `0 .. total_chunks-1` of the same key. The "payload hash" in AT-31 is that SHA-256; it must equal `blob_key` and SHA-256 of the reassembled bytes. A payload larger than 128 KiB is required in AT-82. The snapshot temp is written and sealed on the same file description that holds its `LOCK_EX` (an fd-taking engine API; not `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` or a path reopen — those would conflict with that lock, §5.6 Atomic publish).

PROVENANCE tiles (72) use the same chunk layout. The reassembled provenance **document** (after the 40-byte prefix) is exactly the UTF-8 bytes

```
sha256=<lowercase hex>
url=<source_url>
captured=<RFC3339 seconds UTC>
```

with LF line endings and a final newline. `url=` is followed by the candidate's listed `source_url` (pre-redirect), not a final redirected URL (this document is written only for a fetched snapshot, which always has a URL). There is no "or empty" alternative and no `final_url=` line in v1. `blob_key` of the PROVENANCE tile is the SHA-256 of that document (not of the captured content). Kite seals with `seal_archive` (no seal-generated tile-78 requirement). If a seal manifest is later added, it is the archive's ordinary stored manifest and is not counted against AT-32.

On-disk wiki or corpus sources are not re-snapshotted. Skip does not snapshot and does not write an inclusion record.

**Download names (DD-62).** The destination path is `--download-dir/{lowercase hex SHA-256 of the delivered bytes}` with no added extension (the bytes are the authority). Sanitization is unnecessary because the name is hex only. The existing-file check uses the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule (startup `--download-dir` dirfd, `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, `fstat` regular file, at most `--max-open-bytes` + 1 bytes). If that path already exists, is a regular file no larger than `--max-open-bytes`, and the existing file's SHA-256 equals the name, download is a **success no-op** (the path is returned, the file is not overwritten). If a file exists at that path that is a symlink, a FIFO or other non-regular file, a regular file larger than `--max-open-bytes`, or a regular file whose contents do **not** hash to the name (a planted or corrupt file), download fails with `409` `download_conflict` with `included: true` in the error body whenever a matching inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1 (written on this request or earlier), does not overwrite, and the inclusion write is kept (AT-94, AT-106, AT-216, AT-223). At Scholar start, if `--max-fetch-bytes` is greater than `--max-open-bytes`, startup is an error that names both flags and binds nothing, so a successfully fetched body always fits the existing-file hash-check cap. A `no_source` download writes no file in `--download-dir`.

### 5.7 Mobile build (Android, then iOS)

s3a-kite is the **viewer** inside [s3a-reader](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-reader), as a UniFFI library from `s3a-pack-kite`. The phone is not a literature-review surface (Ashley, 2026-11-16, DD-128). v1 phone builds are an S3A / Edge-tile viewer: no Scholar 3-pass flow, no candidate list, select, download, inclusion, snapshots, consent, LRS, or `paper_identity` (DD-45). Those stay on the hub/PC host. v1 phone UniFFI exports `KiteContext::new`, `open`, `open_verified`, `verification_report`, `close`, `proceed_to_warehouse`, the render functions, `hit_test`, and the `KiteVerificationReport` type (AT-99, DD-80, DD-109, F-04). It does not export `open_path`, `paper_identity`, or any Scholar write. There is no phone Scholar Explorer. AI Teacher's Assistant chat and phone paper search are future, unbuilt, and out of scope for this spec (Q-15, Q-16); this spec adds no ATs, exports, or interfaces for either. The pack does not depend on `s3a-reader-core`; the app may map a post-verify reader-core `VerificationReport` into `KiteVerificationReport` before calling `open_verified`. Pack `open` (the host always uses this) and a mapped reader-core `verify` must agree on `status`, `seal_status`, `invalid_tile_indices`, `break_index`, `torn_tile_indices`, and `quarantine_reason` (DD-115, AT-165). Drop of a UniFFI handle also releases the archive.

**Why this is feasible without a mobile GPU.** Kitesurf already rasterizes on the CPU through `vello_cpu` / `VelloCpuImageRenderer` to an RGBA pixmap. The mobile library paints the manifest, warehouse, and page into a CPU buffer and hands pixels plus the text layer to the UI toolkit. v1 does not add a wgpu or platform GPU backend (DD-21). `cargo tree` for Android and host targets must not include `wgpu` or a Vulkan / Metal / GLES backend at all, not only "on the tour path" (AT-41).

**Fonts (DD-22, DD-35, DD-101).** Keep `system_fonts: false`. There is no process-global font set. `KiteContext::new(ua_fonts)` takes the UA font bytes (Noto Sans, Italic, Serif, Mono, and Symbols). Missing those required UA fonts, or an empty set, is a `KiteError`, not a fallback to device or host fonts (AT-42). AT-42 is a library-level test of `KiteContext::new` with an empty set on the same crate the host links (DD-121). The shipped host and mobile binaries pass the required UA font bytes embedded with `include_bytes!` at startup. There is no test-only cargo feature that compiles the host without the bytes. Noto Color Emoji and Noto Sans SC are optional **bundled asset packs** (Play Feature Delivery, or a sideloaded asset packaged with the APK). They are not fetched over the network. The no-network Android manifest stays in force. When those optional packs are absent, the file still opens: required Latin text is painted with the shipped UA fonts; code points not in those fonts appear as the font's `.notdef` glyph (a visible missing-glyph box), not as an error, and not from system fonts, and with zero network requests (AT-49). Q-05 remains which packs ship in the first Play/App build.

**Android (v1 ship target, already decided for the reader).**

*   UniFFI `cdylib` + Kotlin, cargo-ndk, minSdk 26, ABIs `arm64-v8a` and `x86_64` (DD-23), Jetpack Compose.
*   Compose displays the Rust pixmap (for example `ImageBitmap` / `Bitmap` from RGBA) and exposes the text layer to TalkBack (AT-63). The default screen after open is the kite first view (manifest, header sheet, or page), then the warehouse after Continue for archives (AT-43). The warehouse tour does not use a WebView.
*   SAF / share-sheet open paths already in the app stay the way files arrive. Bytes are passed into `open` via the existing `MmapReader::from_bytes` path (no new scratch-file invention). The Kotlin deck plan is no longer the default (DD-24).
*   When the app has a post-verify reader-core `VerificationReport`, it maps that report into `KiteVerificationReport` and calls `open_verified`. Kite uses the supplied report and does not recompute a disagreeing result (DD-80, AT-137). Valid floors, unauthenticated-as-a-whole, and Edge tiles at or after `break_index` are derived from the report as in the §5.1 `open_verified` row. A report with status `unverified` is a `KiteError`. If the app calls `open` instead, it passes `pinned_keys` and kite runs the §5.4 algorithm. Quarantined files may be opened with a persistent banner; invalid tiles follow §5.4 item 2 (DD-20). SAF / share-sheet bytes go to `open` / `open_verified`; `open_path` is not on the phone surface.
*   The app's current "no network" manifest remains the default. Q-03 draft: not in v1 phone builds; a later DD would be required to add phone network or phone writes.

**iOS (feasible, not the first ship).** Feasible because the paint path is portable `std` Rust plus CPU raster, not Metal. UniFFI already planned for Swift in the reader repo. Follow-on, required once the pack links a `staticlib` for `aarch64-apple-ios` and `aarch64-apple-ios-sim` (AT-40, DD-25). SwiftUI (draft) shows the same RGBA frames and text layer. Same font and no-GPU rules.

**Native-link work the pack must do (not optional, not a stub).** Kitesurf's workspace today pins `getrandom` 0.2/0.3 with `js` / `wasm_js` features, and `fonts.rs` uses `#[wasm_bindgen]`. The `native-host` feature already gates some exports. The pack's fork must compile `kitesurf-core` for `aarch64-linux-android`, `x86_64-linux-android`, and later Apple iOS targets **without** the Wasm `getrandom` JS backend and without requiring a JS host (AT-44). That is a fork change in the pack, with Apache change notices on touched files.

**ESP32 / MCU.** Not a kite viewer target.
