**Budgets (DD-06, Q-06).** The "measure on a named minSdk-26 device before adding a second renderer" trigger stays dormant until Ashley names the device and the numeric budgets (peak RSS, time to first manifest frame, APK size delta versus the current reader). This spec does not invent those numbers.

### 5.8 Render API, warehouse scale, and animation

Every paint function returns a `RenderFrame`, not only pixels (DD-38):

```text
RenderFrame {
  rgba: [u8],           # packed RGBA, length = width * height * 4
  width: u32,
  height: u32,          # pixmap pixels; at scale 1.0, 1 CSS px = 1 pixmap px
  text_runs: [TextRun],
  semantics: [SemanticNode],
}
TextRun { text: String, x: i32, y: i32, width: u32, height: u32 }   # pixmap coordinates; x/y may lie off-pixmap
SemanticNode {
  role: String,         # one of: document, heading, button, label, link,
                        # floor, continue, select, paragraph, card
  label: String,
  x: i32, y: i32, width: u32, height: u32,  # CSS pixels, same space as hit_test x/y and the request's CSS w/h (DD-124, AT-168)
  href: Option<String>,       # set when role is link
  paper_id: Option<String>,   # set when role is select
  floor: Option<u32>,         # set when role is floor
  opacity: Option<f64>,       # set when role is card (floor-transition card; AT-62)
}
```

Acceptance tests that assert on "text" use concatenated `text_runs` as contiguous substrings, never OCR. TalkBack and VoiceOver read `semantics`. When an AT requires a unique withheld string to be **absent from pixels**, the oracle is a **render-model control**. The control pixmap is produced from the **same archive bytes** as the fixture (so CRC, `payload_hash_short`, seal hash, and signatures are identical), by running the same render path for the same view, size, scroll, and `device_pixel_ratio`, except that withheld field values are replaced at the render-model layer (after record decode, before paint) by different bytes of the same length. Derived displays (`payload_hash_short`, CRC, seal or signature status) are taken from the real archive bytes and are not altered in the control. The fixture pixmap must be **byte-identical** to that control pixmap. Tests do not OCR and do not substitute different on-disk payload bytes (that would change those derived values). Absence from `text_runs`, `semantics`, and hit-test targets is checked separately on the original fixture.

Every `render_*` takes `width`, `height`, and `device_pixel_ratio` (DD-69, DD-90). `width` and `height` are **CSS pixels**. `hit_test` `x` and `y` are finite `f64` CSS pixels in that same space (DD-90, AT-171): a finite point outside the last frame's CSS box returns `Hit::None`; a non-numeric or non-finite value is `400` `invalid_param`. The pixmap is `ceil(width * device_pixel_ratio)` by `ceil(height * device_pixel_ratio)` pixels; `rgba` length is pixmap width × pixmap height × 4. `device_pixel_ratio` must be a finite number greater than 0. The product of the pixmap dimensions must be ≤ the required `max_frame_pixels` passed to `open` / `open_verified` / `open_path`, or, on the host, the required `--max-frame-pixels`. A request that violates this, or whose CSS `width` or `height` is 0, is `400` `invalid_frame_size` (AT-141, AT-143). At `device_pixel_ratio = 1.0`, 1 CSS px = 1 pixmap px (DD-37). `/v1/hit-test` `x`/`y` are CSS pixels in the same space as `width`/`height` and as `SemanticNode` `x`/`y`/`width`/`height` (AT-168 covers a `device_pixel_ratio=2` hit-test using CSS coordinates). The same defaults as `/v1/frame` apply when an FFI wrapper omits scroll / instant / dpr, and those defaults are part of the contract: `scroll_x=0`, `scroll_y=0`, `instant=false`, `device_pixel_ratio=1.0`. `select` semantics are present only when `select_enabled` is true (`render_page(..., select_enabled)`; false on a viewer-only host and on mobile; AT-144).

**`instant` (AT-13, AT-93).** When `instant` is true, `render_floor_transition` returns only the end frame (the t = 0.45 layout) and ignores `t_seconds`. When `instant` is false, `t_seconds` is used as below.

**Scale (DD-37, DD-70, AT-16, AT-92, AT-120).** At reference scale 1.0, bays are square. Side lengths are the nearest integer to `32 * sqrt(stride / 4096)` CSS px, so area is only **approximately** linear in stride. Specified integer sides: **32** (4 KiB), **45** (8 KiB, nearest integer to 32√2), **181** (128 KiB, nearest integer to 32√32). AT-16 and AT-92 render the 4 KiB and 8 KiB fixtures **separately** and assert those integer sides; they do not assert exact area doubling. AT-120 uses a 128 KiB fixture and asserts side 181. Mixed-stride warehouses are out of v1 (a file is either Edge 4 KiB, Edge 8 KiB, or hyper-tile 128 KiB). The warehouse is a scrollable grid; `scroll_x` / `scroll_y` reach floors that do not fit the viewport. Floors are not automatically shrunk below the reference scale.

**Animation (DD-18, DD-37, AT-62).** Viewport units map to the frame arguments: `1vw = width / 100`, `1vh = height / 100`. `render_floor_transition` and `/v1/frame?view=floor-transition` take `scroll_x` and `scroll_y`: the warehouse scroll at click time (default 0 only if omitted). The start rectangle is the `floor` `SemanticNode` rect of a `warehouse` frame rendered with the same `width`, `height`, `scroll_x`, `scroll_y`, and `device_pixel_ratio` (it may lie partly or wholly outside `[0, width] × [0, height]`; the pixmap still only contains in-viewport pixels). AT-62 and AT-13 derive the start rectangle that way; they do not depend on an unspecified grid origin, gap, or wrapping rule. Without `instant`, the floor-to-detail card starts at t = 0 on that start rectangle at opacity 0.2, and at t = 0.45 s is at `left = 0.06 * width`, `top = 0.08 * height`, `width = 0.88 * width`, `height = 0.84 * height`, opacity 1, showing **floor-detail content** in the card (not an empty card). Progress between those endpoints, including opacity, is the y-value of CSS `cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)` at unit time `t / 0.45` (at `t = 0.225` s, unit time 0.5, progress is approximately 0.946, so opacity is `0.2 + 0.8 * progress`, approximately 0.957). `t` is clamped to `[0, 0.45]`; values outside that range are not an error. Tests sample t = 0, 0.225, and 0.45 against the `card` `SemanticNode` on `floor-transition` frames: the node's rect is within 1 CSS px of the §5.8 start or end rectangle (or the bezier interpolation of those rectangles at that t), and its `opacity` is within 0.02 of those values (AT-62, AT-13). An off-screen bay still animates from its (possibly off-pixmap) start rect to the end rect.

### 5.9 Robustness

`cargo-fuzz` target `open_and_render` (AT-71) is required, with a **checked-in seed corpus** and a **corpus-replay** CI job that runs every seed to completion (not only a timed fuzz). The timed fuzz duration for CI is 300 seconds per push (Q-14, Ashley, 2026-11-16), in addition to the required seed-corpus replay. Malformed input is an error or quarantine, not a crash. In-process test hooks used by fail-first ATs are specified in §5.2.7 / DD-151 and are compiled out of builds that lack the `test_hooks` feature (AT-222).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 6. License obligations

The pack is a derivative of Kitesurf and must comply with every license Kitesurf already lists in `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`. At minimum:

### 6.1 Apache-2.0 (Kitesurf, and many Rust crates)

From `LICENSE` §4 (Redistribution):

1.  Give recipients a copy of the Apache-2.0 license.
2.  Cause modified files to carry prominent notices that they were changed.
3.  Retain all copyright, patent, trademark, and attribution notices from the source.
4.  If a `NOTICE` file is distributed with the Work, include a readable copy of its attribution notices in a NOTICE in the derivative, or in the documentation, or in a display generated by the derivative. The pack MUST ship Kitesurf's `NOTICE` (copyright "kitesurf / Copyright (c) 2026 Cloudflare, Inc.") plus any added notices. Additional notices must not be construed as modifying the License.
5.  The pack may add its own copyright on modifications.
6.  Trademarks (§6): this license does not grant use of Cloudflare or Kitesurf marks except as needed to describe origin and to reproduce NOTICE.

A generated third-party license inventory (for example `cargo-about` or an equivalent check against the resolved tree, including UniFFI, pulldown-cmark, and other transitive crates in the resolved tree (Wasmtime is not a v1 dependency; DD-44)) ships with the distribution (AT-02, AT-70). A host-directory artifact satisfies AT-70 with those files alone. The Android APK / iOS IPA also shows the inventory on an in-app About / licenses screen.

### 6.2 MPL-2.0 (Stylo, and the Public Suffix List if that engine file is kept)

Stylo is MPL-2.0 (`README.md` Technology stack; `NOTICE` lists stylo among patched crates). The Public Suffix List snapshot is MPL-2.0 (`NOTICE`, `licenses/MPL-2.0-PSL.txt`).

MPL is file-level copyleft:

1.  Modified MPL-covered files (Stylo, Stylo patches under `scripts/patches/`, and the PSL if bundled) must remain MPL-2.0, and their source must be provided (a source offer in the distribution and on the About screen).
2.  The larger work (the pack, Apache-2.0) may link them.
3.  Do not remove MPL notices.

### 6.3 Other NOTICE items the pack must retain if the corresponding assets ship

*   SIL OFL 1.1 for Noto, Inter, and related fonts (`packages/engine/public/fonts/OFL.txt`, `licenses/OFL-1.1-*`).
*   jpeg-encoder: MIT OR Apache-2.0, **and** IJG terms (`licenses/IJG-jpeg-encoder-0.7.0.txt`); "This software is based in part on the work of the Independent JPEG Group."
*   WPT / Webref / TodoMVC / wasm-memprof / anti-slop, if those files remain in the fork.

A release artifact that omits NOTICE, LICENSE, the third-party inventory, Stylo/PSL source availability, or OFL texts fails AT-02 / AT-70. A host-directory artifact satisfies AT-70 with those files alone (a headless host has no in-app About screen). The Android APK / iOS IPA must also include the in-app About / licenses screen.
