## 7. Acceptance tests

Tests are written first. They are split into two classes:

v24.1 (DD-154): `POST /v1/select` records a closer-read event only; it does not write an inclusion record, snapshot, fingerprint, or inclusion-index line. `POST /v1/download` is the inclusion decision and is the write used to obtain a snapshot or inclusion record. Where an earlier AT text in this section still describes `POST /v1/select` as producing those writes, that write is performed by `POST /v1/download` and the AT number is unchanged. ATs revised in place in v24.1 (AT-19, AT-26, AT-71, AT-83, AT-228) and new ATs AT-233–AT-239 are read as written.

*   **Fail-first.** A concrete check that does not hold on today's trees (no `s3a-pack-kite` / `s3a-kite` host / UniFFI kite renderer, or the behavior is not implemented). Implementation must make these pass **without editing the tests**.
*   **Guards.** Checks that already hold on today's trees, or that become meaningful once the pack exists and must then stay green. They are not claimed to fail first. AT-22 and AT-37 already pass today. AT-05, AT-34, AT-36, AT-39, and AT-41 are written so that, until the host or pack crate exists, they are skipped as guards (they do not "pass vacuously" by asserting nothing); once the subject exists, they must pass.

Fixtures: hyper-tile archives built the same way as `crates/s3a-cli/tests/tour_tests.rs` (`write_manifest: true` for AT-08); Edge vectors `micro_valid_4kb`, torn and signed vectors from `s3a-spec-vectors`; an 8 KiB file produced with `EdgeWriter<_, 8192>`. Text assertions use the text layer in §5.8 and must match the fixture bytes as contiguous substrings of concatenated `text_runs` (not only a fuzzy OCR of the pixmap). When a test needs a **successful** fetch and an LRS fixture in the same host, the two bind distinct addresses: the fetch fixture is `127.0.0.1` at the `egress_test` port, and the LRS fixture is `[::1]` or `127.0.0.2` (not `127.0.0.1`). AT-54's denial case is the stated exception (LRS and the fetch fixture may both be `127.0.0.1`; the LRS-host deny rule is by IP and is not overridden by the `egress_test` exception).

Mechanical helpers: AT-03 diffs the vendored Kitesurf tree against pin `235111c6fe10f2d63f3476bf5e3d19b4c7089415` and greps each changed file for the change notice. AT-36 uses `cargo deny` (or equivalent) bans plus a grep on pack-owned source only, not the entire vendored upstream tree.

Negative write tests (AT-28, AT-46, AT-57, AT-73, AT-74, AT-89, AT-94, AT-105) first run a **control** select that succeeds on a valid `ADDON_EDU` working archive in the same harness, then run the failing case, then assert the failing target is unchanged (or, for AT-89, that no snapshot was created). AT-46 uses a **second host process** whose `--working-archive`, `--data-dir`, `--snapshot-dir`, and `--download-dir` are all distinct from host A's (one process serves one archive, DD-48, DD-114; all four directories are distinct, matching AT-46). Same-path second starts are AT-164.

Host-spawn acceptance tests and the today-passing guards AT-22 and AT-37 live in the `kite-acceptance` crate, a s3a-storage workspace member with no dependency on `s3a-pack-kite` (DD-121). Those host-spawn tests fail at runtime if the required binary is missing (they name the environment variable below); they are not skipped as a green default. Because `kite-acceptance` is a workspace member and `crates/s3a-kite` is not, tests do not use `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*`. Each host-spawn **case** uses exactly one of these environment variables, which names the path of a prebuilt `s3a-kite` binary. An AT that has more than one case may use different variables for those cases. If the variable is unset or the path is missing, the AT fails at runtime with "binary not found" naming that variable. Any host-spawn case that needs a successful source fetch, or a loopback fetch fixture, uses an `egress_test` build (`S3A_KITE_BIN_EGRESS_TEST` or `S3A_KITE_BIN_EGRESS_TEST_HOOKS`):

*   `S3A_KITE_BIN` — no `egress_test`, `test_hooks`, or `native-window` (production-like). Default host-spawn ATs that need no test feature (including AT-04, AT-24, AT-25, AT-29, AT-46, AT-52, AT-53, AT-171, AT-172, AT-183's startup-error half, AT-208, AT-223, AT-224, AT-225, AT-226's unresolvable `--lrs-url` case, and AT-125's `--bind 0.0.0.0` without `--headless` case, AT-230, AT-231).
*   `S3A_KITE_BIN_EGRESS_TEST` — Cargo feature `egress_test` only (AT-26, AT-27, AT-31, AT-48, AT-54, AT-82, AT-83, AT-88, AT-102, AT-103, AT-106, AT-109's fixture half, AT-122, AT-126, AT-139, AT-140, AT-157, AT-180, AT-183's end-to-end fixture half, AT-186, AT-209, AT-215, AT-221, AT-226's literal-address and `metadata.google.internal` cases, AT-227, AT-228, AT-191's fixture cases).
*   `S3A_KITE_BIN_TEST_HOOKS` — Cargo feature `test_hooks` only (AT-124, AT-158, AT-181, AT-189, AT-199, AT-202's mid-copy grow, AT-219, AT-222's `test_hooks`-build half, AT-198, AT-229).
*   `S3A_KITE_BIN_EGRESS_TEST_HOOKS` — features `egress_test` and `test_hooks`, for an AT that both uses a loopback fixture and a hook (AT-156, AT-169, AT-187, AT-191's hook case, AT-212, AT-213, AT-214).
*   `S3A_KITE_BIN_NATIVE_WINDOW` — features `native-window` and `test_hooks` (AT-125's window half).
*   `S3A_KITE_BIN_RELEASE` — `--release`, with none of those features (AT-109's release-reject half, AT-222's without-`test_hooks` / release half).

Unit tests in `crates/s3a-kite` (AT-183's `egress_allowlist_matches` and `parse_allowlist_entry` halves, AT-226's `lrs_deny_union`) are compiled against that crate and do not spawn a binary. Pack unit and library tests that link the pack live in the out-of-workspace `kite-pack-tests` crate (own `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock`). AT-38, AT-39, AT-43, AT-63, and AT-165 live in a separate test crate outside the s3a-reader workspace (not feature-gated). The unit-test half of AT-183 lives in the host crate's own tests; its host-spawn halves live in `kite-acceptance`. Android and iOS ATs are skipped, not failed, on a host-only CI job, and the skip is reported. "Land" means local branches; nothing is pushed until Ashley says so. AT-76 is exempt from the same-process control-select requirement (a viewer-only host cannot select); any control write for AT-76 runs on a separate Scholar host, per this paragraph.

### Pack and license

*   **AT-01** (fail-first). `cargo test -p s3a-pack-kite` exists and the crate's Cargo manifest does not cause `s3a-core` to depend on kite or kitesurf (`cargo tree -p s3a-core` has no such edge).
*   **AT-02** (fail-first). The pack's distribution directory, and the Android APK / iOS IPA when those artifacts are built, contain Apache-2.0 `LICENSE`, Kitesurf `NOTICE` (including the Cloudflare 2026 copyright line and the Stylo / PSL MPL notes), OFL text for every shipped font, and IJG text if jpeg-encoder is linked. A CI check lists these files; a missing NOTICE fails.
*   **AT-03** (fail-first). A diff of the vendored Kitesurf tree against `235111c6fe10f2d63f3476bf5e3d19b4c7089415` lists every modified file, and each of those files starts with or includes a prominent "changed from upstream kitesurf `235111c6fe10f2d63f3476bf5e3d19b4c7089415`" notice in addition to the original copyright (Apache §4).
*   **AT-70** (fail-first). A generated third-party license inventory covers the resolved tree (not only Kitesurf's NOTICE). The distribution includes an MPL source offer for Stylo / PSL. A host-directory artifact satisfies this test with those files alone (no in-app About screen is required). An Android APK or iOS IPA also shows those notices on an in-app About / licenses screen.

### Host

*   **AT-04** (fail-first). `s3a-kite host` with every Scholar-mode required flag in the §5.2.2 inventory (and `--bind 127.0.0.1`, an ephemeral `--port`, and `--headless`) starts without Wrangler and without a Worker Loader, and `GET http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` returns HTTP 200, `Content-Type: text/plain`, and body exactly `ok\n`, without a token (the tokenless health case). The test builds the command from the inventory table rather than a copied list. Today there is no such binary.
*   **AT-05** (guard once the binary exists; skipped until then). That binary does not read `packages/engine/wrangler.jsonc` and does not call a `LOADER.load` API.
*   **AT-06** (fail-first). The same host, without a valid token, returns **401** on every route in the §5.2.1 table other than the tokenless health case (no body that looks like a successful frame or handle). With a valid token, a request to `/json`, `/json/list`, `/devtools/*`, or any other path not in the table returns **404**. An `Upgrade: websocket` request to any path not in the table (for example `/devtools/page/1`) returns 404; on a known path, the Upgrade header is ignored, and no protocol switch (101) is ever returned. Without a token, those same unknown paths also return 401 (authentication first; AT-115). There is no CDP listener. An open unauthenticated listener other than the tokenless health case is a failure.
*   **AT-07** (fail-first). Omitting `--bind` in any mode, or omitting `--port` when `--bind` does not start with `unix:`, exits non-zero with an error that names the missing flag. No implicit port is bound. `--bind unix:/path.sock` without `--port` is the AT-113 success case.
*   **AT-50** (fail-first). On a non-loopback bind with TLS (not `--behind-proxy`), `GET /health` without a token is rejected; with a valid Bearer token it succeeds with body `ok\n`.
*   **AT-51** (fail-first). `s3a-kite host --bind 0.0.0.0 --port <ephemeral> --auth-token-file <token>` plus every other Scholar-required flag including `--headless`, without `--tls-cert`/`--tls-key` and without `--behind-proxy`, exits non-zero, names TLS (the cert or key flag), and binds nothing.
*   **AT-45** (fail-first). Startup with an empty `--auth-token-file`, a secret shorter than 32 characters, or a file containing any byte outside `A–Z a–z 0–9 _ -` other than a single trailing `\n` or `\r\n`, exits non-zero, names the token file, and binds nothing (AT-81).
*   **AT-52** (fail-first). A UI-token `POST /v1/open` whose JSON `path` contains `../` that would escape every allowed request root (the lexically normalized path lies under no allowed root, or under more than one) returns `409` `path_escape` and opens no file. A path whose lexical normalization crosses from `--wiki-root` into `--corpus-root` (for example `{wiki-root}/../{corpus-basename}/papers/SRC-0001.md` when those roots are siblings) is accepted and opens that corpus file under the unique matching root (it is not `path_escape`).
*   **AT-53** (fail-first). A UI-token `POST /v1/open` whose JSON `path` is a `file://` URL that, after percent-decoding to a filesystem path, ends outside the configured request roots and is not the canonical working-archive path returns `409` `path_escape` and opens no file. A `file://` URL that cannot be converted to a filesystem path is the same error. A `file://` (or plain path) that equals the canonical working-archive path, when that file exists, opens via the parent dirfd and is copied under the mutex. After bind and `/health`, if that file is then removed mid-process, `/v1/open` of the same canonical path is `404` `not_found` and opens no file (the host does not create the archive on open).
*   **AT-54** (fail-first). `POST /v1/select` of a current candidate whose `source_url` is `http://169.254.169.254/` is blocked; the request does not leave the host; `fetch_error` is `"egress_denied"`; no snapshot is written. In a second case, with the LRS configured as `http://127.0.0.1:<lrs-port>/` and `--egress-test-loopback-port` set, a select whose `source_url` is `http://127.0.0.1:<egress-port>/` is denied (`fetch_error: "egress_denied"`, no snapshot, the request does not leave the host): `127.0.0.1` is the LRS IP, and the `egress_test` exception does not override the LRS-host deny rule. This case uses only existing flags and loopback binds; the spec adds no resolver-override flag, and it does not use a global-unicast destination (a real off-host address is not a CI fixture). Tests that need a **successful** fetch and an LRS in the same host still bind distinct addresses as in §7 and DD-140 (LRS on `[::1]`, fetch on `127.0.0.1`).
*   **AT-55** (fail-first). A current candidate whose YAML `source_url` is a `file://` URL pointing at the token file, the working archive, or a corpus file is listed with `source_url: null`. `POST /v1/select` and `POST /v1/download` of that identity perform no HTTP request and no filesystem read of the `file://` target. If the candidate has no usable `drive_path`, download returns `200` with `download_error: "no_source"` and `path: null`. Those files are opened only through `POST /v1/open` under allowed roots (AT-123).

### Hyper-tile `.s3a`

*   **AT-08** (fail-first). Open a sealed archive produced like `test_tour_displays_manifest_in_lobby_and_floor`. The first frame is produced by `render_manifest`. Its text layer contains the exact stored manifest payload as a contiguous substring (including `THIS IS AN S3A FILE` and the seal title/creator). The warehouse floors are not the first frame.
*   **AT-09** (fail-first). Open a hyper-tile archive with no tile 78. The first frame is a labeled "manifest absent" state and does not contain invented title/creator text. After `proceed_to_warehouse` / `POST /v1/proceed`, `render_warehouse` is the next view. A Continue `hit_test` alone does not change the view (AT-152).
*   **AT-10** (fail-first). After the manifest view, the tour floor list matches `s3a-cli tour --json` on the same **sealed, type-82-free** hyper-tile file: `index`, `type_id`, `record_count`, `size_bytes`, `payload_hash_short`. The CLI is not the oracle for an unsealed file or a file that contains a type-82 tile.
*   **AT-11** (fail-first). Opening floor *N* with `render_floor_detail` on a golden hyper-tile fixture whose `withheld_count` was hand-verified under the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table (type 82 is never consent) shows those records and paints the **per-floor** count as the exact substring `withheld_count: N` in concatenated `text_runs` (`N` is the decimal count, no thousands separator). A unique string that appears only in a withheld payload is absent from `text_runs`, `semantics`, hit-test targets, and pixels under the §5.8 render-model pixmap oracle. The same unique string is also absent from `render_floor_transition` frames at `t=0.225` and with `instant=true` (text, semantics, hits, and the same pixel oracle). Fixtures that contain type 82 are included in this suite and must *not* treat type 82 as Edge or hyper-tile consent. The CLI is not the `withheld_count` oracle when type 82 is present. AT-135 and AT-136 cover only-non-research and revoked-research cases. A further fixture that is otherwise unrestricted (trusted authentication, zero consent records) but contains one payload record marked only with `MARK_CONSENT_WITHDRAWN` or `MARK_TARGET_ALL_RECORDS` paints `withheld_count: 1` on the first view and does not paint that record's payload in `text_runs`, `semantics`, hit-test targets, or pixels (the same §5.8 render-model pixmap oracle).
*   **AT-12** (fail-first). A manifest or record that contains `<script>`, `</script>`, or HTML angle brackets is painted as those characters (text layer). No script from the archive runs. No JS context is created.
*   **AT-13** (fail-first). Without `instant`, the floor-to-detail transition matches §5.8 (AT-62 is the timed sampling of the `card` `SemanticNode`). With `instant=true`, `render_floor_transition` returns only the end frame (the t = 0.45 layout) and ignores `t_seconds` (AT-93); that frame's `card` node rect matches the §5.8 end rectangle (`left = 0.06 * width`, `top = 0.08 * height`, `width = 0.88 * width`, `height = 0.84 * height`) within 1 CSS px, and `opacity` is 1. The start rectangle used when `instant` is omitted is the `floor` `SemanticNode` rect of a `warehouse` frame rendered with the same `width`, `height`, `scroll_x`, `scroll_y`, and `device_pixel_ratio` (AT-62).
*   **AT-62** (fail-first). `render_floor_transition` at t = 0, 0.225, and 0.45 s (`instant=false`) includes a `card` `SemanticNode` whose rect is within 1 CSS px of the start, bezier mid, and end rectangles, and whose `opacity` is within 0.02 of the §5.8 values. The start rectangle is the `floor` `SemanticNode` rect of a `warehouse` frame rendered with the same `width`, `height`, `scroll_x`, `scroll_y`, and `device_pixel_ratio`; the end rectangle is the §5.8 end rectangle (`left = 0.06 * width`, `top = 0.08 * height`, `width = 0.88 * width`, `height = 0.84 * height`).
*   **AT-64** (fail-first). A committed tile 78 that is not valid UTF-8 yields a first frame labeled "manifest not UTF-8" and invents no replacement text.
*   **AT-65** (fail-first). Two committed valid tile-78 tiles: the first view uses the first tile's bytes; the second appears as a floor.
*   **AT-69** (fail-first). A file whose `FileHeader.version` is not 1 fails to open with `Unsupported file version: {n}`.
*   **AT-72** (fail-first). An unsealed hyper-tile with a valid tile 78 opens, the first view's text layer includes the manifest and an unsealed seal status, and no seal trailer is invented.

### Edge micro-tiles

*   **AT-14** (fail-first). Open `micro_valid_4kb.s3a` (MICRO-VEC-01). The reader reports `is_edge_archive` and `tile_stride == 4096`. The first frame is the derived header sheet, labeled as derived, and includes stride 4096 and the committed `tile_count`. It is not presented as tile 78.
*   **AT-15** (fail-first). The warehouse list for MICRO-VEC-01 has exactly the committed micro-tiles. Each floor `size_bytes == 4096`. For each floor *i*, `payload_hash_short` equals the first 8 lowercase hex digits of the SHA-256 of the micro-tile payload (excluding the micro-tile header) at offset `512 + i * 4096` (Variant A); the test computes that function over the fixture bytes and does not use the CLI as an oracle or require a frame field that exposes a raw offset.
*   **AT-16** (fail-first). An 8 KiB `EdgeWriter` fixture shows `tile_stride == 8192` and `size_bytes == 8192`. Rendered **separately** from a 4 KiB fixture at the §5.8 reference scale (`device_pixel_ratio` 1.0), the 4 KiB bay's side is exactly 32 CSS px and the 8 KiB bay's side is exactly 45 CSS px (AT-92). Area is only approximately linear (DD-70). A 128 KiB bay is AT-120.
*   **AT-17** (fail-first). MICRO-VEC-02: only the valid tile(s) appear as floors; the torn tile is omitted and the header sheet reports it as ignored/torn. The file-level `status` is **not** Quarantined solely because of that torn tile (DD-100, DD-150). When `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set, `seal_status` remains `sealed` (a torn-only stop is not a chain break). `break_index` is the torn tile's index when the seal chain stops there; later tiles, if any, are not valid floors.
*   **AT-18** (fail-first). MICRO-VEC-03: seal verification matches `s3a-engine`. A copy with a reordered or mutated tile fails verification, shows quarantine, and does not list the mutated tile (or any subsequent tile) as a valid floor.
*   **AT-19** (fail-first). A type-64 tile of 32-byte `EquipmentEventRecord`s paints timestamp, the EDGE §5.2.2 event name, severity, and the category label from §5.4. Severity 0, 1, and {2, 3} use three pairwise-different badge colors; 2 and 3 share a color. Each event row exposes a `SemanticNode` with role `label` and label `severity: N` carrying its CSS rect; the test samples the pixmap at that rect's centre, scaled by `device_pixel_ratio`. The badge fill colours are the Okabe-Ito values in DD-07 (severity 0 `#0072B2`, 1 `#E69F00`, 2 and 3 `#D55E00`); the test samples those CSS rect centres and asserts the fills, and the `severity: N` labels are always present so colour is never the only signal. A severity-0 maintenance event still carries the label "maintenance".
*   **AT-20** (fail-first). A type-64 tile whose record size is 96 bytes paints `DeviceIdentityRecord` fields (device id, hardware model), not event columns.
*   **AT-21** (fail-first). A compact-header Edge file (`FLAG_COMPACT_HEADER`) has tile 0's `payload_hash_short` equal to the first 8 lowercase hex digits of the SHA-256 of the micro-tile payload (excluding the micro-tile header) at offset 4096 (4 KiB stride) or 8192 (8 KiB stride), not of the bytes at offset 512. The test computes that function over the fixture bytes and does not use the CLI as an oracle.
*   **AT-22** (guard; already passes today). `crates/s3a-edge/Cargo.toml` does not depend on kite or kitesurf. A `no_std` build of `s3a-edge` still succeeds.
*   **AT-23** (fail-first). When an Edge type-71 `EdgeConsentRecord` does not allow the research purpose (including a lone valid-floor denial and no grant anywhere), kite withholds event-log payload records and paints the applicable **per-file** (first view / banner) or **per-floor** (`render_floor_detail`) count as the exact substring `withheld_count: N` in concatenated `text_runs`. A unique string that appears only in a withheld event-log payload is absent from `text_runs`, `semantics`, hit-test targets, and pixels under the §5.8 render-model pixmap oracle. The same unique string is also absent from `render_floor_transition` frames at `t=0.225` and with `instant=true`. The implementation must use the Edge layout, not type 82 and not core `ConsentRecord`. The derived header sheet and type-64 identity floors remain visible (AT-86).
*   **AT-66** (fail-first). A file with `FLAG_PROFILE_EDGE` and a 128 KiB stride, a file with `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_4KB` and stride 8192, and a file with both `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_4KB` and `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_8KB` set, each open as HTTP 200 with `kind: "unsupported"` and `first_view: "banner"`, and are not toured as a consistent Edge or hyper-tile file. `GET /v1/frame?view=banner` (and `view=manifest`) returns a frame whose text layer contains the inconsistent-header banner text.
*   **AT-67** (fail-first). A type-64 tile whose record size is neither 32 nor 96, or whose `record_count` is 0, is shown as an "unknown layout" floor that paints metadata only (type, size, `record_count`, record size) and never raw payload bytes, including when a research denial is present on the file.
*   **AT-68** (fail-first). An otherwise valid Edge file with no `DeviceIdentityRecord` opens; the header sheet omits device fields and is labeled "device identity record absent". An otherwise valid Edge file with two `DeviceIdentityRecord`s uses the first in tile order on the header sheet; the later record remains a floor.

### Scholar 3-pass and snapshots

*   **AT-24** (fail-first). Building the Pass 1 candidate list twice (including `GET /v1/candidates` twice) leaves the corpus archive, the LRS store, and `--log-dir` and `--data-dir` byte-identical to the baseline taken **after** the host has bound and answered `GET /health` (startup may already have created `--data-dir/.lock`, an empty inclusion index, and identifier-free startup log lines; AT-29). There are no new `HumanLrsRecord`s, no xAPI statements, no new snapshot tiles, and no paper ids or URLs in those directories. Candidate walks are a **silent skip**: they write nothing to `--log-dir` (AT-218, §5.5).
*   **AT-25** (fail-first). Dismissing a paper as off-topic / skip (`POST /v1/skip`) writes nothing (the same byte-identity check against the post-`/health` baseline as AT-24, including `--log-dir` and `--data-dir`). `POST /v1/skip` itself never writes a log line. Candidate walks are a silent skip and write nothing to `--log-dir`.
*   **AT-61** (fail-first). After a consent-off select, `--log-dir` contains no paper ids or source URLs, and `--data-dir` contains none other than in `inclusion-index.jsonl` (which records the identity, the working-archive `file_uuid`, and a nullable `snapshot_sha256`). The working archive still has the inclusion record. `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` is empty or absent (consent was off, so no queued statements).
*   **AT-26** (fail-first). Downloading a paper, with tracing consent on and a configured LRS, writes one inclusion statement as specified in §5.5 (verb IRI `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/saved` from `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs`, not a newly invented IRI; actor, object, extensions, and stable `id` as in DD-65 / DD-154) and a Scholar `HumanLrsRecord` (tile 10) for that paper identity in the working archive, plus a snapshot of the AT-31 remote fixture. A select of the same paper is a closer-read statement with verb `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/opened` and does not write a tile-10 inclusion record or a snapshot. AT-110 asserts the statement body.
*   **AT-27** (fail-first). The same select of the AT-31 remote-fetched fixture, with consent off: zero HTTP requests to the LRS, and the working archive still contains the inclusion record. The snapshot of the captured bytes is the file `--snapshot-dir/{lowercase hex SHA-256}.s3a`, not a tile in the working archive. The corpus and LRS store are unchanged.
*   **AT-48** (fail-first). Download of the AT-31 remote-fetched fixture with consent off still delivers the file to `--download-dir` as `{sha256_hex}` and writes the local inclusion record and snapshot, with zero LRS requests.
*   **AT-28** (fail-first). After a successful control select, a call to `/v1/select` or `/v1/download` without a token is rejected and writes nothing (working archive, snapshot dir, download dir, LRS unchanged versus the post-control state).
*   **AT-57** (fail-first). There is no agent-token flag and no CDP Input domain. A request that presents a Bearer value read from a **different token file**, an `X-Agent-Token` header, or a synthesized CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` returns 401 or 404 (per §5.2.1) and writes nothing. (v1 has only the UI token; DD-41.)
*   **AT-29** (fail-first). After only `GET /v1/candidates`, `POST /v1/open`, and `GET /v1/frame` (a candidate page with `view=page`), waiting 15 seconds (no select, download, or skip) leaves the working archive, snapshot dir, download dir, `--log-dir`, and `--data-dir` byte-identical. The host has no write-on-timer endpoint. The candidates body includes `page_path` as specified in §5.5; this test does not write merely because that field is present. Candidate walks are a silent skip and write nothing to `--log-dir`.
*   **AT-30** (fail-first). Opening a configured wiki `index.md` via `/v1/open` and `GET /v1/frame?view=page` (or `render_page`) paints its heading text from the file bytes as a contiguous substring of the text layer. Opening a missing `papers/SRC-9999.md` reports missing and does not invent a dossier.
*   **AT-56** (fail-first). A wiki page opened with `view=page` whose Markdown contains a script tag that would set `document.title` and an image tag with an `http` URL paints those tags as text or omits them as raw HTML; no script runs; no JS context is created; the render issues zero network requests.
*   **AT-31** (fail-first). The fixture is a candidate whose `source_url` points at an `egress_test` loopback server that returns a known body (not an on-disk-only SRC dossier). After select (consent on or off), a sealed snapshot exists under `--snapshot-dir` as `{sha256_hex}.s3a` whose `blob_key` and payload hash equal SHA-256 of that body; `POST /v1/open` on that path is allowed and `verify_sealed_archive` succeeds. Skip of the same fixture does not create a snapshot. On-disk-only inclusion is AT-104.
*   **AT-32** (fail-first). That snapshot's reassembled PROVENANCE document (bytes after the 40-byte tile prefix) is exactly `sha256=<lowercase hex>\nurl=<source_url>\ncaptured=<RFC3339 seconds UTC>\n` (LF, final newline). `blob_key` of the PROVENANCE tile is SHA-256 of that document. No other metadata fields are present. A seal-generated tile 78, if any, is not counted as provenance metadata.
*   **AT-46** (fail-first). After a successful control select on a valid `ADDON_EDU` archive in host A, a **second host process** B is started with a **distinct** `--data-dir`, a **distinct** `--working-archive`, a **distinct** `--snapshot-dir`, a **distinct** `--download-dir`, its own `--bind`/`--port`, and either no `--lrs-url` or a **distinct** `--consent-state-file`, pointed at a file whose `addon_mask` lacks `ADDON_EDU`. Host B **starts** (the `ADDON_EDU` bit is not checked at startup). The `ADDON_EDU` check runs in the step-0/1 hold on every select/download, whether or not step 1 is skipped. Select on B then fails with `409` `addon_missing` in that hold, writes nothing, has no `lrs_status`, and leaves that second file and the LRS store byte-identical. A further case on a host whose working archive already has `ADDON_EDU` (so step 1 is skipped) and whose bit is then cleared mid-process **in place** on the same `(dev, ino)` (not by a rewrite-and-rename, which would be `500` `write_failed`) is the same `409` `addon_missing` on the next select/download. A second process on the same `--data-dir`, `--working-archive`, `--snapshot-dir`, or `--download-dir` is AT-164, not this test.
*   **AT-47** (fail-first). After a successful snapshot write, `verify_sealed_archive` on the snapshot file succeeds.
*   **AT-58** (fail-first). Revoking tracing consent mid-session via `POST /v1/consent` with `{ "state": "off" }` (not by editing the file on disk) drops subsequent LRS requests to zero, including any retry queue (`--data-dir/lrs-retry/` is deleted); the already-written local inclusion record remains.
*   **AT-59** (fail-first). With consent on, an LRS 5xx does not roll back the local inclusion record or snapshot; a retry file is written under `--data-dir/lrs-retry/`; the corpus is unchanged.
*   **AT-60** (fail-first). Selecting the same paper twice in one working archive leaves a single inclusion record and a single snapshot.
*   **AT-73** (fail-first). After a successful control write on a running Scholar host, an **external** `TileWriter` (not a second `s3a-kite host`; that case is AT-164) that holds `try_lock_exclusive` on the working-archive file causes the host's next select/download step 1 to return `500` `lock_failed` and not truncate the file.
*   **AT-74** (fail-first). Scholar-mode select with `--working-archive` omitted is a startup error (AT-77). A running Scholar host whose working-archive path cannot be opened (the file is missing, or its `(dev, ino)` differs from the recorded identity), or whose existing regular-file archive is not a readable S3A file or fails `FileHeader::verify`, fails the select with `500` `write_failed`, writes nothing, and has no `lrs_status`. The host does not create the archive mid-process. Phone UniFFI exports no select/download (AT-99).

### Web pages vs archives

*   **AT-33** (fail-first). Opening a local HTML fixture under `--view-root` (a file that contains a `<script>` setting `document.title` and a static heading) via `POST /v1/open` and `GET /v1/frame?view=page` (or `render_page`) uses native-host layout with JavaScript disabled (DD-44). The script does not run: the text layer contains the static heading and does not contain the scripted title. `cargo tree` for the host does not include `wasmtime` or `boa_engine` (AT-34). This test does not fetch the public Internet.
*   **AT-34** (guard once the pack crate exists; skipped until then). `cargo tree` for `s3a-pack-kite` and for the host binary, on host and Android targets, does not include `boa_engine` or `wasmtime`. The tour is painted by the native `vello_cpu` renderer.

### Safety and scope

*   **AT-35** (fail-first). A hyper-tile or Edge file with a **committed-tile** CRC failure is labeled Quarantined. For a hyper-tile, `seal_status` is `seal_failed` if a seal trailer or remnant is present, else `unsealed`. For Edge, `seal_status` is `seal_failed` when `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set (the CRC stop breaks the chain), else `unsealed`. That failed CRC tile and every subsequent tile are not shown as valid floors; earlier CRC-ok committed tiles remain valid floors. The same floor list holds when the fixture also has a seal trailer and `verify_sealed_archive` fails: the CRC rule decides the floors (a CRC-valid tile after the failed committed tile is not a floor). Consent and mark records in a parseable invalid tile can only restrict, never release (DD-111; grants in that tile are AT-176 / AT-177). A failed Edge Ed25519 signature after an earlier authentic record (DD-145 rules 3/4) makes the file-level status **Quarantined** with `quarantine_reason` `signature_known_signer` (it does not stay Verified or Self-signed, and the reason is not `chain_break`) and invalidates every tile at or after `break_index` = (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none, not only the type-70 tile (gap tiles in that range are untrusted); `seal_status` is unchanged by this reason alone; earlier verified floors may remain (DD-57, DD-100, DD-111, DD-150). An unpinned signer on a non-empty list is AT-155 (whole-file Quarantine, no valid floors). Torn tiles are AT-17, not this test.
*   **AT-36** (guard once the pack crate exists; skipped until then). `cargo deny` (or equivalent) plus a grep on pack-owned source and tests (not vendored Kitesurf) contain no Bitcoin, OpenTimestamps-write, payment, or token-transfer code paths. Reading Edge `seal_hash` values is allowed.
*   **AT-37** (guard; already passes today). No new `TileType` constant is added under `s3a-core` relative to the set at s3a-storage `b7bdcda0df63524ffe205f2ad92cd6a5b69a40f6`.
*   **AT-71** (fail-first). The `open_and_render` cargo-fuzz target exists, a seed corpus is checked in, and a CI corpus-replay job runs every seed to completion (including the AT-08 fixture, MICRO-VEC-01/02/03, and a malformed file) without aborting. A timed `cargo-fuzz` job of 300 seconds runs on every push, in addition to the corpus-replay job (Q-14).

### Mobile library

*   **AT-38** (fail-first). The UniFFI library builds for `aarch64-linux-android` and `x86_64-linux-android` with minSdk 26. `open` + `render_manifest` + `proceed_to_warehouse` + `render_warehouse` return a `RenderFrame` (RGBA plus text layer) or `KiteError`. For the AT-08 fixture, the first frame's text layer contains `THIS IS AN S3A FILE`; for MICRO-VEC-01, the first frame's text layer includes `4096`.
*   **AT-39** (guard once the kite tour Compose screen exists; skipped until then). The Android UI path that shows the tour does not instantiate `android.webkit.WebView` for that screen. A lint or unit test on the Compose screen's view tree fails if a WebView is the tour surface.
*   **AT-40** (fail-first). `cargo build --target aarch64-apple-ios -p s3a-pack-kite` produces a `staticlib` (and the `ios-sim` target also links). Shipping the iOS app remains a follow-on (DD-25); this test is the feasibility gate. The job is skipped, not failed, on a host-only CI runner without the Apple toolchain, and the skip is reported.
*   **AT-41** (guard once the pack crate exists; skipped until then). `cargo tree` for `s3a-pack-kite`, for Android and host targets, does not include `wgpu` or a Vulkan/Metal/GLES backend at all. The linked paint crate is `vello_cpu` / `anyrender_vello_cpu`.
*   **AT-42** (fail-first). `KiteContext::new` given an empty font set returns a `KiteError` that names missing fonts. It does not silently construct a context that would render with system or host fonts (`system_fonts` stays false). This is a library-level test on the same crate the host links, and on the mobile library (DD-101, DD-121). The shipped binaries still embed the required UA fonts with `include_bytes!` and pass that set to `KiteContext::new` at startup.
*   **AT-43** (fail-first). In the Android app, opening the AT-08 fixture shows the kite surface as the default screen after open (first the manifest, then the warehouse after `proceed_to_warehouse`). `MapLayout` is not the default. A Continue `hit_test` alone does not change handle state (AT-152).
*   **AT-44** (fail-first). `getrandom` for Android and iOS targets is not enabled with the `js` / `wasm_js` features. A build without a JavaScript runtime succeeds (the fork change required in §5.7, "Native-link work").
*   **AT-49** (fail-first). A manifest containing CJK characters, with Noto Sans SC and Color Emoji absent, opens successfully; required Latin text is in the text layer; CJK code points are painted as `.notdef` (not from system fonts); zero network requests.
*   **AT-63** (fail-first). The first-view semantics tree includes the manifest heading (or the "manifest absent" label). An Android TalkBack (or equivalent accessibility) test reads that label from the kite surface.

### v3 additions (round-2 dispositions)

*   **AT-75** (fail-first). Every route in the §5.2.1 table exists with the documented request and success response on a Scholar host. A path not in the table returns 404 with the JSON error object and still requires a token except the tokenless health case.
*   **AT-76** (fail-first). Viewer-only mode starts with `--viewer`, `--bind 127.0.0.1`, `--headless`, and every other viewer-required flag in the §5.2.2 inventory (including `--port`, `--auth-token-file`, `--view-root`, `--log-dir`, `--max-open-handles`, `--max-frame-pixels`, `--handle-idle-secs`, `--max-request-bytes`, and `--max-open-bytes`). (`--headless` remains optional on loopback for a `native-window` build with a display; this test always passes it so it runs on display-less CI and on builds without `native-window`.) `POST /v1/select`, `/v1/download`, `/v1/skip`, `/v1/consent` and `GET /v1/candidates`, `GET /v1/consent` return 409 with error code `viewer_only` and write nothing (control write on a separate Scholar host, per §7). Opening a file under `--view-root` with `/v1/open` still succeeds. Starting a viewer without `--max-open-handles` is AT-131.
*   **AT-77** (fail-first). Scholar mode missing any required flag in the §5.2.2 inventory (including flags required only when `--lrs-url` is set, tested in a second start that passes `--lrs-url`) exits non-zero and names the flag. `--headless` is required when `--bind` is not loopback and not `unix:`. `--consent-state-file` without `--lrs-url` is AT-114. `--lrs-url` without `--consent-state-file` is a startup error that names `--consent-state-file`. Scholar start with `--lrs-url` whose scheme is `http` and whose host is a hostname (including `localhost`) or any address that is not a loopback IP literal is a startup error that names `--lrs-url` and binds nothing. `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` and `http://[::1]:<port>/` are accepted (AT-54). Scholar start with `--lrs-url` that includes a path (including `/xapi`), userinfo, query, or fragment is a startup error that names `--lrs-url` and binds nothing. A required directory flag that points at a missing path is a startup error that names the flag. The host does not create those directories.

*   **AT-78** (fail-first). Scholar start with `--working-archive` pointing at a missing file whose parent directory exists succeeds: the host creates the file, calls `enable_addon(ADDON_EDU)`, writes the create-time `ConsentRecord`, and binds `/health`. If the parent directory does not exist, or if that startup create fails (the parent is not writable, `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` fails, `enable_addon` fails, or the create-time `ConsentRecord` write fails), startup is an error that names `--working-archive` and binds nothing.
*   **AT-79** (fail-first). When `--working-archive` is missing at Scholar start, the host creates the file at startup with `TileWriter::create`, `enable_addon(ADDON_EDU)`, and a core `ConsentRecord` whose fields match §5.6 (DD-105). After `/health`, the file exists. The first select appends to that file (`open_append`); it does not create it. Opening that archive in kite lists the owner's inclusion records with `withheld_count == 0`.
*   **AT-80** (fail-first). A snapshot written to `--snapshot-dir` can be opened via `POST /v1/open` with that absolute path. A path outside every allowed root still fails (AT-52).
*   **AT-81** (fail-first). A token file of 32 allowed characters plus a trailing newline is accepted when `Authorization: Bearer` is exactly those 32 characters (no newline). A Bearer value that includes the newline, or a file that contains a space, a slash, or any non-alphabet character other than the single trailing newline, is rejected (the bad file is a startup error).
*   **AT-82** (fail-first). A `POST /v1/select` of a candidate whose `source_url` is an `egress_test` fixture that returns at least 200 KiB of non-repeating bytes produces a sealed snapshot under `--snapshot-dir` whose BLOB tiles use the 40-byte prefix, `blob_key` equals SHA-256 of the full payload, `total_chunks` is greater than 1, concatenating chunks `0 .. total_chunks-1` equals the original bytes, and `verify_sealed_archive` succeeds.
*   **AT-83** (fail-first). `POST /v1/select` then `POST /v1/download` of the same paper identity in one working archive uses two existing verb IRIs (`opened` then `saved`, DD-154). Select writes no inclusion record, no snapshot, no fingerprint, and no inclusion-index line. Download leaves a single inclusion record (tile 10) and a single successful snapshot, and is the only write that fingerprints the paper or enters it in the corpus. If a usable snapshot already exists from an earlier download, the download response's `snapshot` is that path (the file passes the DD-103 reuse checks), bytes are reassembled from that snapshot's BLOB tiles, and no second fetch is performed, even if a `drive_path` is also usable. The index line stores `snapshot_sha256` and the working-archive `file_uuid`. The download file is still delivered under `--download-dir`.
*   **AT-84** (fail-first). With `--lrs-url` set and `--consent-state-file` pointing at an **existing malformed** file, startup is an error that names the file. The host does not invent an `on` default and does not bind a port. A **missing** consent file is AT-151 (created as `off`). A missing `--consent-state-file` flag is AT-77.
*   **AT-85** (fail-first). An otherwise valid Edge file that is **not** unauthenticated as a whole and has zero consent records of any kind (no type-71 and no core `ConsentRecord`) in any parseable tile opens unrestricted: research-purpose filtering withholds no records; `withheld_count` is 0. This matches `tour.rs` "open / unrestricted (no consent records)". A lone valid-floor type-71 denial is AT-23, not this test. An unauthenticated-as-a-whole file is AT-195 / AT-200, not this test.
*   **AT-86** (fail-first). When a type-71 record denies the research purpose, the derived header sheet and type-64 `DeviceIdentityRecord` floors still show device identity fields; event-log payloads are withheld (AT-23).
*   **AT-87** (fail-first). `--bind 0.0.0.0` (or any non-loopback address) plus `--behind-proxy`, even with `--tls-cert` and `--tls-key` present, exits non-zero, names the conflict, and binds nothing. `--behind-proxy` with `--bind 127.0.0.1` starts.
*   **AT-88** (fail-first). `POST /v1/select` of a candidate whose `source_url` is an `egress_test` loopback fixture that responds with a redirect to `http://169.254.169.254/` is blocked at the redirect hop: the second request is not sent, and `fetch_error` is `"egress_denied"`. A select whose `source_url` host resolves to `127.0.0.1` (or `::1`) on a port that is not the `--egress-test-loopback-port` is blocked at connect time, `fetch_error` is `"egress_denied"`, and no TCP connection is made to that address. A third case: an `egress_test` fixture returns more than `--max-redirects` redirects to allowed loopback URLs; the select sets `fetch_error: "too_many_redirects"`, writes no snapshot, and does not follow more hops than the cap. A fourth case: an `egress_test` fixture redirects to a `ftp://` or `file://` `Location`; the select sets `fetch_error: "scheme_not_allowed"`, writes no snapshot, and does not follow that hop (this is the reachable path for that code; a stored `file://` `source_url` is AT-123 and is never fetched).
*   **AT-89** (fail-first). If the inclusion-record write (step 1) fails (for example the working archive cannot be locked), no new snapshot file is created in `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir` is unchanged (AT-127), no LRS request is made, and the error is returned (`500` `write_failed` or `500` `lock_failed`). A later successful select of the same paper then writes the record and snapshot (the failed attempt is not treated as a successful idempotent artifact).
*   **AT-90** (fail-first). A hyper-tile file that has a seal trailer, fails `verify_sealed_archive`, and whose **every committed tile passes CRC** opens with a persistent quarantine banner naming the seal failure. CRC-valid committed tiles **are** listed as valid floors (display only; unauthenticated for consent); no floor is presented as a verified seal. Subject-bearing research payloads on those floors are withheld when the file is unauthenticated as a whole (the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table, AT-200); this test may use a fixture with no subject-bearing payloads. A sealed fixture that also has a committed CRC failure is AT-35, not this test.
*   **AT-91** (fail-first). After AT-59's LRS 5xx, the retry file exists under `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` and is still there after the host process is stopped and started again with the same flags. If the previous process is stopped while a send is in flight, leaving a `{id}.claimed` file, the next start with consent `on` renames that file to `{id}.json` before replay and then retries it. `POST /v1/consent` to `off` deletes that directory.
*   **AT-92** (fail-first). At reference scale 1.0, measured integer bay-rectangle sides are exactly 32 CSS px (4 KiB) and exactly 45 CSS px (8 KiB).
*   **AT-93** (fail-first). Two `render_floor_transition` calls with `instant=true` and different `t_seconds` values return byte-identical pixmaps and the same text layer (the end frame). `t_seconds` is ignored.
*   **AT-94** (fail-first). When `--download-dir` already contains a file at `{sha256_hex}` whose contents do **not** hash to that name, `POST /v1/download` of the paper that would use that name returns `409` `download_conflict` with `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1 (written on this request or earlier) and an `lrs_status` in the error body when step 3 ran or was skipped, does not overwrite that file (byte-identical), and the inclusion write is kept. Same-bytes re-download is AT-106. A symlink, FIFO, or other non-regular file at that path is AT-216.
*   **AT-95** (fail-first). Each `render_*` function in §5.1 returns `Result<RenderFrame, KiteError>`. `open` of a byte string that is not an S3A file (no `S3A_MAGIC`, no name hint) returns `Ok` with kind `other` and `first_view: page`; the process does not abort. `open` of bytes that begin with `S3A_MAGIC` but have an unopenable header (including a non-1 version) returns `Err` and the process does not abort.
*   **AT-96** (fail-first). The pack library exports `KiteContext::new`, `KiteContext::new_with_clock` (host/pack tests only; not on phone UniFFI), `open`, `open_verified`, `open_path`, `close`, `verification_report`, `render_manifest`, `render_page`, `proceed_to_warehouse`, `render_warehouse`, `render_floor_transition`, `render_floor_detail`, `hit_test`, and `paper_identity`. `open` and `open_path` take `pinned_keys` and `max_open_bytes`. `open_path` takes a host-validated fd, the proved `canonical_path`, the `validated_root` of that walk, `scholar_roots: Option<ScholarRoots>` (including `shadowed_by_wiki: bool`), and `name_hint: Option<String>`; it reads from the fd and does no path I/O (DD-146). On a dossier path under the §5.5 glob, `open_path` with `scholar_roots: Some(...)` and `shadowed_by_wiki` false yields `paper_identity` `Some(stem)` computed from `canonical_path` and the YAML in the fd bytes, with no second `lstat`; with `shadowed_by_wiki` true, or `scholar_roots: None`, or on `open` of bytes, `paper_identity` is `None`. On the AT-08 first view, `hit_test` at the Continue control returns `Continue`; a point on empty space returns `None`. `render_page` is AT-100. Phone UniFFI (no `open_path`, no `paper_identity`, and with `close` / `proceed_to_warehouse`) is AT-99.
*   **AT-97** (fail-first). A file whose `tile_stride` is 65536 (neither 4096, 8192, nor 128 KiB) is opened via `/v1/open` as HTTP 200 with `kind: "unsupported"` and `first_view: "banner"`; `GET /v1/frame?view=banner` returns a frame whose text layer contains the unsupported-stride banner text; `POST /v1/proceed` returns 409 `unsupported`; the banner paints no Continue control, and `hit_test` at the usual Continue location returns `None`; the file is not toured as a hyper-tile. Pack `open` of those bytes returns `Ok` with that handle; `verification_report` of the handle returns `KiteError` `not_archive`; `open_verified` of the same bytes returns `KiteError` `not_archive`.
*   **AT-98** (fail-first). `open` / `from_bytes` of the AT-08 fixture succeeds. The pack and host do not depend on a memmap crate for `open_path` (`cargo tree` / source grep on pack-owned code); `open_path` reads from the host-validated fd and uses `from_bytes` (DD-75).
*   **AT-99** (fail-first). The v1 **phone** UniFFI surface exports `KiteContext::new`, `open`, `open_verified`, `verification_report`, `close`, `proceed_to_warehouse`, the render functions, `hit_test`, and `KiteVerificationReport`, and does not export `open_path`, `paper_identity`, select, download, skip, consent-toggle, inclusion, snapshot, or working-archive write functions. A Kotlin / Swift binding test fails if a forbidden function is present or if `proceed_to_warehouse`, `close`, or `verification_report` is missing (F-04).


### v4 additions (round-3 dispositions)

*   **AT-100** (fail-first). `render_page` and `GET /v1/frame?view=page` exist. Opening a wiki dossier (`papers/SRC-*.md`) or the AT-33 HTML fixture returns `first_view: "page"` and a frame whose text layer contains a heading from the file. A `view` not in the §5.2.1 table is `400` `invalid_view`, not a silent manifest frame and not 404.
*   **AT-101** (fail-first). On a paper dossier page, `hit_test` at the Select control returns `Select` with that paper identity; a call to the pack FFI alone does not write an inclusion record. `hit_test` at a link returns `Link { href }` and the host performs no fetch of that href. `GET /v1/candidates` includes a `page_path` for each dossier candidate equal to the absolute canonical winning-root path; `POST /v1/open` of that path walks the path under DD-146 and calls `open_path` with that fd, the proved canonical path, the validated root, and `scholar_roots` including `shadowed_by_wiki` computed from the current qualifying wiki set, and yields `paper_identity` equal to that candidate's `id`. The pack does not reopen the path by name. This mid-call swap lives in `kite-pack-tests` and uses no host test hook (§5.2.7): the test opens a host-validated fd, swaps an intermediate directory component for a symlink, then calls pack `open_path` on that fd; the pack does not follow the symlink and does not reopen the path by name. An `AcademicPaperRecord` candidate has `page_path` `null`.
*   **AT-102** (fail-first). `POST /v1/select` of a current candidate with a `source_url` on an `egress_test` loopback fixture performs one GET of that URL (this is the defined fetch trigger). A second select of the same identity (same verb) does not fetch again if the snapshot already exists.
*   **AT-103** (fail-first). If that fetch fails or exceeds `--fetch-timeout-secs` after the inclusion write, the working archive still has the inclusion record, no snapshot file is created, the response includes `fetch_error` (`"timeout"` when the wall-clock cap is exceeded), and the index has the identity. A further case uses a redirect chain in which each hop is fast but the total time from first connect to final body exceeds `--fetch-timeout-secs`; the result is `fetch_error: "timeout"`, no snapshot, and the inclusion is kept.
*   **AT-104** (fail-first). Select (and download) of an on-disk-only candidate (a `papers/SRC-*.md` whose `drive_path` canonicalizes inside `--corpus-root`, and which has no `source_url`) writes the inclusion record, creates **no** file in `--snapshot-dir`, and download copies the on-disk bytes to `--download-dir/{sha256_hex}`.
*   **AT-105** (fail-first). After a successful control select, `POST /v1/select` (and download, and skip) with a `paper_id` that is not in the **request-time** candidate set (§5.5) returns `404` `unknown_paper` and writes nothing (archive, snapshot dir, download dir, index, LRS unchanged versus the post-control state). The same 404 is returned if an earlier `GET /v1/candidates` listed that id but the file was removed before this request. A select with no prior GET succeeds if the id is in the set at request time.
*   **AT-106** (fail-first). A second download of the AT-31 fixture, after the `{sha256_hex}` file already exists with identical bytes, returns 200, returns that same path, and leaves the file byte-identical (success no-op).
*   **AT-107** (fail-first). Opening a kite-written BLOB+PROVENANCE snapshot (no `ConsentRecord` of any kind, and not unauthenticated as a whole) is unrestricted under DD-147: `withheld_count` is 0. BLOB and PROVENANCE floors are viewable as **metadata only** (size, chunk index and total, hash, and PROVENANCE text) and never paint raw payload bytes (so a captured body that is itself an S3A archive cannot bypass that archive's consent table). The snapshot was not written with a create-time `ConsentRecord` (F-09). This is the same rule as AT-112, applied to a file in `--snapshot-dir`. DD-63 is retired.
*   **AT-108** (fail-first). A third-party hyper-tile archive that contains withheld records, copied into `--snapshot-dir` under any name (including a name that looks like a SHA-256), is still filtered by the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table: those records remain withheld. Sitting in `--snapshot-dir` grants no exemption.
*   **AT-109** (fail-first). An `egress_test` fixture host that is listed in `--egress-allowlist` and that redirects to `http://169.254.169.254/` is still blocked at the redirect hop (the deny list wins). A release build (no `egress_test` feature) rejects `--egress-test-loopback-port` at startup.
*   **AT-110** (fail-first). With consent on, the LRS request is `POST {lrs-url}/xapi/statements` with `Authorization: Basic` from the credentials file, `X-Experience-API-Version: 1.0.3`, and a JSON statement whose `id`, `actor.account`, `verb.id`, `object.id`, `timestamp`, and `EXT_PAPER_ID` match §5.5. When the candidate has a `source_url` that redirected, `object.id` and `EXT_SOURCE_URL` are that candidate's listed pre-redirect `source_url`, not the final redirected URL. When the candidate has no `source_url`, `object.id` is the fallback IRI (`https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/activities/paper/` plus the percent-encoded paper identity) and `EXT_SOURCE_URL` is absent. `EXT_CONTENT_SHA256` is present if and only if, at step 3, the inclusion index has a non-null `snapshot_sha256` for `(file_uuid, identity)`, and the extension value equals that digest: an on-disk-only download with no snapshot omits it; a snapshotted select includes it (DD-137). A retry after an LRS 5xx resends the same `id`.
*   **AT-111** (fail-first). After AT-59 has queued a retry, the host is stopped, the consent file is `off` (or `POST /v1/consent` to `off` was used), and the host is started again: zero LRS requests are made, and the queue directory is deleted, or startup fails naming `lrs-retry` (AT-124).
*   **AT-112** (fail-first). A hyper-tile fixture that is **not** unauthenticated as a whole and has zero consent records of any kind in any parseable tile opens unrestricted: `withheld_count` is 0 and records that would be withheld under an active denial are visible. (Distinct from AT-79, which still checks that `create` writes a research-purpose `ConsentRecord`.) A seal-failed file with subject-bearing payloads is AT-200, not this test. A file whose only records are non-research `ConsentRecord`s is AT-135, not this test.
*   **AT-113** (fail-first). `--bind unix:/path.sock` without `--port` starts; `GET /health` over that socket without `--behind-proxy` returns `ok\n`; with `--behind-proxy`, `/health` without a token is 401. Passing `--port` together with `unix:` is a startup error.
*   **AT-114** (fail-first). `--consent-state-file` without `--lrs-url` (whether the file is missing, `off`, or `on`) is a startup error that names the flag and binds nothing.
*   **AT-115** (fail-first). Without a token, `GET /v1/frame` and `GET /no-such-route` are both 401. With a valid token, `GET /no-such-route` is 404 with the JSON error object.
*   **AT-116** (fail-first). On a Scholar host without `--lrs-url`, `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "on" }` returns 409 `lrs_not_configured` and creates no consent file at any path. `POST` `{ "state": "off" }` on the same host returns `200` `{ "state": "off", "timestamp": null }` (a no-op) and also creates no file.
*   **AT-117** (fail-first). A token file with mode `0644` is refused at startup (names the file, binds nothing). The same refusal applies to `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--tls-key`, `--pinned-keys-file` when those flags are set, and to an existing `--consent-state-file` (AT-142). After a successful authenticated request and a consent-on LRS emit, `--log-dir`, stdout, and stderr contain neither the UI token secret, nor any `Authorization` header value (incoming Bearer or outgoing Basic), nor the LRS password, nor paper identities, nor `--researcher-id`. The other-owner case in AT-142 requires root (or a pre-provisioned other-owner file) and is skipped and the skip is reported otherwise.
*   **AT-118** (fail-first). With `--max-open-handles 1`, a second `/v1/open` before `/v1/close` returns 429 `too_many_handles`. After close, a further open succeeds. `POST /v1/hit-test` before any `/v1/frame` for that view returns 409 `no_frame`.
*   **AT-119** (fail-first). `POST /v1/close` of a valid handle returns 200; a second close or a close of an unknown handle returns 404 `unknown_handle`.
*   **AT-120** (fail-first). At reference scale 1.0, a 128 KiB hyper-tile bay's measured integer side is exactly 181 CSS px.
*   **AT-121** (fail-first). An HTML fixture under `--view-root` that contains an `<img src="http://…">` and a `<link rel="stylesheet" href="http://…">` is opened with `view=page`: the render issues zero network requests and zero local file reads for those tags, and those tags are not fetched as subresources (DD-118). Local path-escape subresources are AT-167.
*   **AT-122** (fail-first). A `source_url` that is the literal `http://[::ffff:10.0.0.1]/` (an IPv4-mapped RFC1918 address) is denied at connect time (`fetch_error: "egress_denied"`). The test does not override the resolver (DD-140 forbids a resolver override). With `HTTP_PROXY` (and `HTTPS_PROXY`) set to an otherwise reachable origin, a denied destination is still not sent through the proxy, and an allowed `egress_test` fixture is reached with **zero proxy hits** (the variables are ignored). A fixture body larger than `--max-fetch-bytes` sets `fetch_error: "body_too_large"` and writes no snapshot.
*   **AT-123** (fail-first). Covered with AT-55: a `file://` `source_url` is listed as `null`, is not fetched over HTTP, and is not read from the filesystem; download is `no_source` when there is no usable `drive_path`.
*   **AT-124** (fail-first). If deleting `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` on revoke fails (hook point `fail_lrs_retry_delete` with `fail_with_errno=N`, after `revoke-pending` and the consent-file `off` write have succeeded; §5.2.7), the process may write one last `500` `revoke_delete_failed`, then exits non-zero, leaves `revoke-pending` in place, and accepts no further request (DD-138). Hook point `fail_lrs_retry_delete` applies to **every** `lrs-retry/` delete, including that subsequent start: a start while the queue remains and consent is `off` (including via `revoke-pending`) is a startup error that names `lrs-retry` and binds nothing when the hook fires, or when the delete otherwise fails. AT-169 covers the consent-file write-failure path.
*   **AT-125** (fail-first). A Scholar host started with `--headless` and a loopback bind, and every other Scholar-required flag in the §5.2.2 inventory, has no GUI window and still serves the Host HTTP API. A start with `--headless` and `--bind 0.0.0.0` also supplies every TLS flag required by the inventory and binds. A build with cargo feature `native-window`, without `--headless`, on a loopback bind: hook point `window_issue_select` with action `issue` (§5.2.7), after `GET /health` succeeds, causes the native window client to issue `GET /v1/candidates` and then `POST /v1/select` for the first listed candidate (the fixture plants at least one current candidate); both requests, and any other window-originated Host HTTP API request, carry the Bearer UI token (the test observes the real Host HTTP API requests). Starting without `--headless` when no `DISPLAY`, `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, or equivalent is available is a startup error that names `DISPLAY`. Starting without `--headless` when `--bind` is `0.0.0.0` is a startup error that names `--headless` (every other required flag, including TLS, is supplied so that flag is the only error). That `0.0.0.0` without `--headless` case runs on `S3A_KITE_BIN` (no `native-window` feature), so a missing display is not in play and the host names `--headless`. A build without the `native-window` feature that omits `--headless` is also a startup error that names `--headless`. If the test host has no `DISPLAY`, `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, or equivalent, the window half of the test is skipped and the skip is reported.

### v5 additions (round-4 dispositions)

*   **AT-126** (fail-first). N concurrent `POST /v1/select` requests for the same identity against one Scholar host, with a `source_url` on an `egress_test` fixture that **counts GETs** and returns **different bytes on each request**, produce exactly one tile-10 record, at most one snapshot file (the same `snapshot_sha256`), and **exactly one** GET (a second fetch would be visible as a second GET and as different snapshot bytes). The write mutex, in-flight fetch registry, and snapshot temp+rename hold.
*   **AT-127** (fail-first). A `POST /v1/download` whose step-1 inclusion write is forced to fail leaves `--download-dir` byte-identical to the start; no snapshot is created; no LRS request is made (extends AT-89 to step 2b).
*   **AT-128** (fail-first). Select and download of a candidate with neither a usable `drive_path` nor a `source_url` (an `AcademicPaperRecord`, or a dossier with no `drive_path` and no `source_url`) write the inclusion record and index line (`snapshot_sha256` null). Download returns `200` with `path: null` and `download_error: "no_source"`, and `--download-dir` and `--snapshot-dir` are unchanged.
*   **AT-129** (fail-first). A candidate with both a usable `drive_path` and a `source_url` is selected and downloaded: the host copies the on-disk file, writes no snapshot, and issues zero HTTP fetches of `source_url`.
*   **AT-130** (fail-first). Kind detection matches §5.2.5: S3A magic with a version-1 128 KiB hyper-tile → `hyper-tile` / `manifest`; consistent Edge → `edge` / `header`; hint `.md` (no magic) → `wiki` / `page`; hint `.html` or `.htm` (ASCII, case-insensitive) → `html` / `page`; hint `.pdf` or no hint (and no magic) → `other` / `page` with the exact label `no text conversion in archive` in the text layer (AT-148). A non-1 S3A version remains AT-69 (`invalid_magic`), not `other`.
*   **AT-131** (fail-first). A viewer-only start that includes every AT-76 flag except `--max-open-handles` is a startup error that names `--max-open-handles` and binds nothing.
*   **AT-132** (fail-first). A viewer-only start is parameterized over **every** Scholar-only (forbidden-in-viewer) flag in the §5.2.2 inventory; each case is a startup error that names that flag and binds nothing.
*   **AT-133** (fail-first). An Edge file whose type-70 signatures verify against a `DeviceIdentityRecord.public_key` that is **not** in `--pinned-keys-file` (the list is non-empty and holds a different key) opens as **Quarantined**. Re-signing the same file with a second unpinned key is also Quarantined. The embedded key is never treated as a trust anchor.
*   **AT-134** (fail-first). An Edge file with `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` set and no type-70 tile (and no usable pinned key that covers the file) opens as **Quarantined** with `quarantine_reason: missing_signature`, and **no tile is a valid floor**. The banner names the missing signatures. Consent grants on that file are ignored: the file is unauthenticated as a whole, so event-log payloads are withheld and `withheld_count` on the banner equals the number of parseable event-log payload records (AT-195).
*   **AT-135** (fail-first). A hyper-tile fixture that is **not** unauthenticated as a whole, whose only `ConsentRecord`s have a purpose other than Research, is **not** unrestricted (consent records of any kind are present). Subject-bearing payloads are withheld under the §5.3 item 4 table (no trusted research grant); `withheld_count` counts them. Marks are not required for this assertion.
*   **AT-136** (fail-first). A hyper-tile fixture with a later research-purpose `ConsentRecord` that withdraws an earlier grant for the same `subject_id` withholds that subject's records under the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table (the latest trusted record is no longer an unexpired grant, or a later parseable withdrawal restricts).
*   **AT-137** (fail-first). `open_verified` of a file, given a `KiteVerificationReport` whose `status` or `invalid_tile_indices` disagrees with a fresh `s3a-reader-core` `verify(pinned_keys)` of the same bytes, still uses the supplied report for the banner and the valid floor list (the pack does not recompute). `verification_report(handle)` on that handle returns the supplied report, not a pack recomputation.
*   **AT-138** (fail-first). After invalid tiles are omitted from the valid floor list, `render_floor_detail` / `GET /v1/frame?view=floor-detail&floor=` / `Floor(n)` use the tile `index`. A request whose `floor` is present but not a valid tile index returns `409` `invalid_floor`. A `floor-detail` or `floor-transition` request with `floor` omitted returns `400` `missing_param`. The invalid-list banner text is not hit-testable as `Floor(n)`.
*   **AT-139** (fail-first). A `source_url` whose final HTTP status is 404 (error-page body of any size) yields `fetch_error: "http_status"`, writes no snapshot, and does not store the error-page body. The inclusion record from step 1 is kept.
*   **AT-140** (fail-first). A `source_url` that returns `200` with `Content-Encoding: gzip` is snapshotted as the **decoded** body. `snapshot_sha256` is SHA-256 of the decoded bytes. `--max-fetch-bytes` is applied to those decoded bytes (a gzip payload whose decoded size exceeds the cap is `fetch_error: "body_too_large"` and writes no snapshot). An unknown `Content-Encoding` is `fetch_error: "unknown_encoding"` and writes no snapshot. The same decoded-body rules apply to `Content-Encoding: deflate` (a deflate payload is snapshotted as the decoded bytes; an oversize decoded body is `fetch_error: "body_too_large"` and writes no snapshot). A fixture whose **response header block** exceeds `--max-fetch-bytes` (with a small body) is `fetch_error: "headers_too_large"` and writes no snapshot.
*   **AT-141** (fail-first). `--max-frame-pixels` is required. `GET /v1/frame` with `w`, `h`, and `device_pixel_ratio` whose pixmap product `ceil(w*dpr) * ceil(h*dpr)` exceeds the cap, or with `w` or `h` equal to 0, or with a well-typed non-positive `device_pixel_ratio`, returns `400` `invalid_frame_size`. A non-numeric or non-finite `device_pixel_ratio` is `400` `invalid_param` (AT-171), not `invalid_frame_size`.
*   **AT-142** (fail-first). `--lrs-credentials-file` or `--tls-key` with mode `0644`, or owned by another user, is a startup error that names the file and binds nothing. The other-owner case requires root (or a pre-provisioned other-owner file) and is skipped and the skip is reported otherwise. After a host write, `inclusion-index.jsonl` and `lrs-retry/` files under `--data-dir` are mode `0600`. Extends AT-117.
*   **AT-143** (fail-first). At `device_pixel_ratio = 2.0`, a request with CSS `w=100`, `h=50` produces a pixmap of 200 by 100 pixels. The FFI `RenderFrame` has `width=200`, `height=100`, and `rgba.len()==80000`. The HTTP `/v1/frame` response's decoded PNG is 200 by 100. The product is checked against `--max-frame-pixels`.
*   **AT-144** (fail-first). In viewer-only mode (and on the UniFFI phone surface), `render_page` is called with `select_enabled=false`: the text/semantics layer has no Select control, and `hit_test` at the usual Select location returns `None`, not `Select`.
*   **AT-145** (fail-first). Scholar start with `--snapshot-dir` (or `--data-dir`, `--download-dir`, `--log-dir`, or the working-archive parent) equal to, or an ancestor directory of (path-component prefix after canonicalization), `--wiki-root` or `--corpus-root` is a startup error that names both flags and binds nothing. The same test includes the disjointness converses required by §5.2.2: `--wiki-root` or `--corpus-root` equal to, or an ancestor directory of, each of those write roots is also a startup error that names both flags. A string-prefix pair that is not a component prefix (`/x/a` vs `/x/ab`) is not this error (AT-179).
*   **AT-146** (fail-first). A non-Edge file with `tile_stride` 128 KiB and `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_4KB` (or `FLAG_TILE_SIZE_8KB`) set opens as `kind: "unsupported"`, `first_view: "banner"`, and is not toured as a hyper-tile.
*   **AT-147** (fail-first). An otherwise consistent Edge file with `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` clear opens (not auto-quarantined solely for that flag); the header sheet shows seal status "unsealed".
*   **AT-148** (fail-first). Opening a PDF (or an extensionless file) under `--view-root` returns `kind: "other"`, `first_view: "page"`, and the page text layer contains exactly the label `no text conversion in archive` and invents no title or abstract.
*   **AT-149** (fail-first). `--handle-idle-secs` is required. After a handle is idle for that many seconds (no frame, hit-test, proceed, or close), the next `/v1/frame` or `/v1/hit-test` returns `404` `unknown_handle`.
*   **AT-150** (fail-first). A `drive_path` that is relative is resolved against the dossier's parent directory and then must canonicalize inside an allowed root; a relative path that escapes is not an on-disk source (`on_disk` is false). The dossier `.md` itself is never treated as the on-disk source. `GET /v1/candidates` lists that candidate with `on_disk: false`. Select of it is not `409` `path_escape`; a `source_url` fetch still proceeds if the source table requires one. Download of it, when there is also no usable `source_url` and no snapshot, has `path: null` and `download_error: "no_source"`, and is not `409` `path_escape`.

### v6 additions (round-5 dispositions)

*   **AT-151** (fail-first). Scholar start with `--lrs-url` and a `--consent-state-file` path that does not yet exist creates that file as `off\n` plus an RFC3339-seconds-UTC timestamp, mode `0600`, owner euid, and the host starts (GET `/v1/consent` returns `off` and that timestamp). A present consent file whose two-line body is otherwise valid (`off` or `on` on the first line, an RFC3339-seconds-UTC timestamp on the second) but has no trailing newline is accepted (GET `/v1/consent` returns that state and timestamp). A present but otherwise malformed consent file is still a startup error that names the file.
*   **AT-152** (fail-first). After a first-view frame of AT-08, `hit_test` at the Continue control returns `Continue`, and a subsequent `GET /v1/frame?view=warehouse` (no `POST /v1/proceed`) returns `409` `view_mismatch`. After `POST /v1/proceed`, `view=warehouse` returns 200.
*   **AT-153** (fail-first). `open` and `open_path` accept `pinned_keys`. Opening AT-133's Edge fixture with a non-empty list that does not include the signer yields Quarantined; opening the same bytes with an empty list yields Self-signed when the signatures verify against the embedded key.
*   **AT-154** (fail-first). A sealed-OK hyper-tile (trailer verifies, no device signer) opens with `status` Unsigned and `seal_status` sealed, and is not labeled Verified. `open_verified` given a report whose `status` is `unverified` returns `KiteError`. An Edge file with `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear, no type-70 signatures, and a non-empty pinned list opens as Unsigned (not Quarantined); if `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is also set and the chain is intact, `seal_status` is `sealed` (AT-165).
*   **AT-155** (fail-first). An Edge file whose type-70 signatures verify against an embedded key that is **not** in a non-empty pinned list is file-level Quarantined and has no valid floors, including tiles before any mid-chain break. The same file with an empty pin list, after a mid-chain signature that fails every embedded key following an earlier authentic record (DD-145 rule 4), is file-level **Quarantined** with `quarantine_reason` `signature_known_signer` (not `chain_break`); earlier floors that already verified may remain (DD-111, AT-201).
*   **AT-156** (fail-first). During a slow `egress_test` fetch (longer than a short `--lrs-timeout-secs` would allow for a later send, and long enough for a concurrent request), a `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "off" }` is accepted; the select completes with zero LRS requests (the pre-send check and in-flight abort both hold). A second case uses a slow LRS fixture and revokes **during the LRS POST** (not only during the source fetch); that in-flight POST is aborted, no new retry file is written, and the select/download response has `lrs_status` `"skipped"`. A third case uses hook point `pause_before_lrs_retry_write` with `pause_until_signal` (§5.2.7): after the LRS POST returns 503 and is classified, and before the mutex hold that would write the retry file, the host pauses; a concurrent `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "off" }` is accepted during that pause, and after CONTINUE the consent / inhibit check that gates the queue write runs inside the same mutex hold as that write (if inhibit is active or the flag is `off`, write nothing), no retry file is created, and `lrs_status` is `"skipped"`. A Scholar start with `--lrs-url` and without `--lrs-timeout-secs` is a startup error that names the flag.
*   **AT-157** (fail-first). Two current candidates with different identities and different `source_url`s return identical bytes. After both are selected, `--snapshot-dir` contains one `{sha256}.s3a`; its PROVENANCE document is the first capture's `url` and `captured` time; both index lines store that same `snapshot_sha256`; the first snapshot file is byte-identical to before the second select.
*   **AT-158** (fail-first). A Scholar start with a present `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl` that has a truncated last line, or a non-JSON line, is a startup error that names the file and binds nothing. After a successful step-1 tile-10 write, a forced index-append failure (hook point `fail_index_append` with `fail_with_errno=N`, §5.2.7) returns `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and no `lrs_status` (steps 2, 2b, and 3 are skipped), and leaves the tile-10 record in the working archive.
*   **AT-159** (fail-first). The create-time `ConsentRecord` of AT-79 has `subject_id == actor_id_to_subject_id(hash_iri(--researcher-id))`, `granted_by == hash_iri(--researcher-id)`, `study_id == 0`, `expires_at == 0`, `withdrawn_at == 0`, `legal_hold == 0`, `prev_consent_hash` all zeros, and `consent_hash` equal to `ConsentRecord::compute_hash`.
*   **AT-160** (fail-first). A wiki page under `--wiki-root` whose Markdown links to a sibling `other.md` via a relative href, and to a section via `other.md#sec`, yields `Link { href }` hits whose `href` is the resolved absolute path of that sibling (fragment and query stripped). A relative href `my%20page.md` resolves to the sibling whose name is `my page.md` (fragment and query are stripped from the raw attribute, then the path is percent-decoded, then resolved). A relative href with a query string (`other.md?x=1`) yields the same path as `other.md` (query stripped before decode). A relative href `a%3Fb.md` resolves to the sibling named `a?b.md` (the `%3F` is not treated as a query separator). A root-relative href `/papers/SRC-0001.md` on a page under `--wiki-root` resolves against that wiki root (not the filesystem root `/`). Following that `href` with `POST /v1/open` opens the sibling. A relative href that would canonicalize outside the allowed roots is not opened (`409` `path_escape` if the UI still posts it).
*   **AT-161** (fail-first). Pack tests inject a clock via `KiteContext::new_with_clock`. A research-purpose `ConsentRecord` whose `expires_at` is in the past relative to the injected `now` is an expiry under the §5.3 item 4 table; that subject's records are withheld. The same fixture with `now` before `expires_at` (and no withdrawal) is shown. Withholding is re-evaluated at a later render after the injected clock advances past `expires_at`. `AcademicPaperRecord` has no `subject_id` and is not omitted by ConsentRecord expiry (it is mark-only; AT-166). There is no candidate cache (DD-143). This test asserts only pack-level clock effects; it does not drive the host candidate list from the injected pack clock.
*   **AT-162** (fail-first). A `POST /v1/open` (or other JSON route) whose body is larger than `--max-request-bytes` returns `413` `request_too_large` and opens no handle. A request whose HTTP header block exceeds `--max-request-bytes` returns `413` `request_too_large` even without a valid token, and opens no handle (same as AT-171).
*   **AT-163** (fail-first). `--bind unix:/path.sock` when a file already exists at that path is a startup error that names the path and does not unlink it. A successful Unix-socket bind creates the socket with mode `0600` and owner euid. A non-loopback TLS host negotiated below TLS 1.2 is refused (the client or handshake fails closed; the host is configured for TLS 1.2 or newer).

The first host keeps serving. The colliding host B is started both with and without `--lrs-url` while A has `revoke-pending` and/or `lrs-retry/*.json` on disk: after B exits, those files are byte-identical to their pre-B contents (B mutates nothing). AT-46 is not this test (it uses distinct paths for all four).
