For fixtures MICRO-VEC-01, MICRO-VEC-02, and MICRO-VEC-03, the AT-133, AT-134, AT-154, AT-155, and AT-201 fixtures, and both an empty and a non-empty pin list: pack `open` followed by `verification_report(handle)`, and a reader-core `verify` of the same bytes and pin list mapped by the §5.1 table (DD-130), yield identical `status`, `seal_status`, `invalid_tile_indices`, `break_index`, `torn_tile_indices`, and `quarantine_reason`. Torn tiles in MICRO-VEC-02 appear in `torn_tile_indices` and in `invalid_tile_indices`, and `break_index` is the torn tile when the chain stops there. Later tiles that do not themselves satisfy the torn predicate (including CRC-valid tiles after a mid-file torn or CRC stop) are in `invalid_tile_indices` and are not in `torn_tile_indices`. A sealed hyper-tile fixture with a committed CRC failure agrees on the AT-35 floor list (the failed tile and every subsequent tile are in `invalid_tile_indices`, including CRC-valid tiles after the failure; only tiles that satisfy the torn predicate are also in `torn_tile_indices`). A file whose signer is not in a non-empty pin list has `quarantine_reason: signer_not_pinned` and `invalid_tile_indices` equal to every index in `0 .. tiles_total-1` (every index the reader enumerates, including torn ones). A `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` file with no type-70 tile and no usable key has `quarantine_reason: missing_signature` and the same all-indices list (AT-134, AT-195). The AT-201 attribution fixtures and the AT-210 unsigned-tail fixture are also in this set and must agree on `status`, `seal_status`, `quarantine_reason`, `invalid_tile_indices`, and `break_index` under the §5.4 / DD-150 derivation table (including `signature_known_signer` rather than `chain_break` for a mid-chain signature failure after an earlier authentic record, and including unsigned-tail indices in `invalid_tile_indices`, with a verifying type-70 container exempt from that tail). A fixture with `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` set, `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear, no type-70, and no CRC agrees on `status` Unsigned, `seal_status` sealed, `quarantine_reason` None, and `break_index` None (the chain is intact with zero seal records). A multi-cause fixture with a `chain_break` at tile 4 and a later `signature_known_signer` at tile 7 agrees on `quarantine_reason: signature_known_signer` and `break_index` 4 (the minimum), and tiles 4–6 are in `invalid_tile_indices`. A chain-inactive Edge fixture with a mid-file torn tile at index *t* and committed tiles after *t* agrees on `break_index` *t*, and every subsequent tile is in `invalid_tile_indices` (not a valid floor). The AT-220 chain-inactive seal-hash-mismatch fixtures (including the sparse *s*/*t* gap and the dense per-tile variant) are also in this set and must agree on `status`, `seal_status`, `quarantine_reason`, `invalid_tile_indices`, and `break_index`. A **hyper-tile** fixture with a partial trailing tile, and a fixture with a tile index ≥ header `tile_count`, is not Quarantined solely for that torn or uncommitted tail; `break_index` is the first such tile; those indices are in `torn_tile_indices` and in `invalid_tile_indices`; `status` and `seal_status` come from the other applicable rows (AT-17 covers Edge torn only).

A control hyper-tile with an un-withheld live record in a non-quarantined archive still lists that identity.

A hit at the pixmap coordinates of the same node, if they differ, is not required to hit.

*   **AT-169** (fail-first). After a successful Scholar start with consent `on`, the **parent directory** of `--consent-state-file` is made non-writable. If the queue delete fails at a startup where consent is `off` (hook point `fail_lrs_retry_delete` with `fail_with_errno=N` is the defined injection, and also applies at this startup), the host exits non-zero, names `lrs-retry`, and binds nothing. A concurrent select already past candidate membership, waiting on the mutex or mid-fetch at the moment of the failure, writes no second tile-10 after the failing hold releases the mutex. The non-writable-parent injection requires a non-root runner, or is skipped and the skip is reported if the process is running as root (the write bit on a directory is not effective for root, the same rule as AT-171). AT-181 covers a restart after the parent is made writable again.

Select of a current candidate with a `source_url` on an `egress_test` fixture fails the GET (`snapshot` is null, `fetch_error` is set, one inclusion record, step 3 may still run). The fixture is then made to succeed. A repeat select of the same identity and verb issues **exactly one** GET, writes no second inclusion record and no second LRS statement, and returns a non-null snapshot. A third select of the same identity and verb issues zero GETs.
*   **AT-187** (fail-first). Hook point `pause_before_snapshot_rename` with `pause_until_signal` (§5.2.7) pauses Scholar host A **inside the step-2 publish mutex hold**, after the `--snapshot-dir` `s3a-kite-tmp-*` temp has been created and `LOCK_EX`-locked and before the rename. (The GET has already returned; this test does not require a `--snapshot-dir` temp to exist during the fetch. An in-flight fetch body may already live in the ephemeral directory of §5.2.2.) While A is paused in that hold, a second Scholar host B with a distinct `--data-dir`, `--working-archive`, `--snapshot-dir`, and `--download-dir`, its own `--bind`/`--port`, and either no `--lrs-url` or a distinct `--consent-state-file`, is started as the same euid. B's startup cleanup must not delete A's live ephemeral temp under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or `/tmp` when those directories are shared. (B has a distinct `--snapshot-dir`, so it does not inspect A's snapshot temp.) A's hook then resumes; the snapshot publishes. A further case, on a fresh host's own `--snapshot-dir` (and likewise `--download-dir`), plants a lockable stale `s3a-kite-tmp-*` file and a separately `LOCK_EX`-locked (live-looking) `s3a-kite-tmp-*` file; after that host starts, only the unlocked stale file is deleted and the locked one remains. A further case starts two Scholar hosts with **distinct** `--consent-state-file` paths in the **same** parent directory, distinct `--data-dir` values, and otherwise distinct write locations: hook point `pause_before_consent_rename` with `pause_until_signal` pauses A inside a consent-file publish after its `s3a-kite-tmp-*` temp has been created and `LOCK_EX`-locked and before the rename; while A is paused there, B's startup cleanup must not delete A's locked consent temp, and a lockable stale sibling `s3a-kite-tmp-*` in that parent is deleted. A last case starts B with the same `--snapshot-dir` (or `--download-dir`) as A and is a startup error that names the flag (AT-164).
*   **AT-188** (fail-first). `GET /v1/candidates` omits a dossier under the §5.5 glob whose YAML front matter is missing, unparseable, or not UTF-8, including a dossier that uses YAML aliases or anchors, and writes no paper id to logs. A regular file under that glob whose size exceeds `--max-open-bytes` is also omitted (skipped as fail-to-open). A control dossier with valid UTF-8 front matter and size under the cap is still listed.
*   **AT-189** (fail-first). After a successful Scholar start with consent `on`, hook point `fail_revoke_pending_write` with `fail_with_errno=N` (§5.2.7) fails the `revoke-pending` temp+rename (it does **not** plant a directory named `revoke-pending` in that hook case. A separate startup case plants `--data-dir/revoke-pending` as a directory or other non-regular file: startup is an error that names the path, binds nothing, and treats no consent as loaded from that path). `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "off" }` then still attempts (best-effort) the consent-file `off` write and the `lrs-retry/` delete, may write one last `500` `consent_write_failed`, and exits non-zero. No `revoke-pending` **regular file** is present. A **new** Scholar host started with the same flags reads the consent file `off` (the best-effort write), binds, sends no LRS, and `GET /v1/consent` is `off`. No request is accepted from the previous (exited) process.
*   **AT-190** (fail-first). Scholar start with `--bind 127.0.0.1` (or `unix:/path.sock` with a legal socket parent) plus `--tls-cert` or `--tls-key` is a startup error that names the TLS flag and binds nothing. `--bind 0.0.0.0` or a specific interface IP without `--tls-cert` and `--tls-key` (and without `--behind-proxy`) is a startup error. `--bind ::` is accepted as an IP literal and follows the same non-loopback TLS rule.
*   **AT-191** (fail-first). An LRS fixture that returns `409` is treated as success on a **first** emit and on a retry (`lrs_status: "sent"`; the statement is not retried). A fixture that returns `400` (not 408 or 429) is a permanent failure: `lrs_status: "failed"`, a `{id}.permanent` file is kept under `--data-dir/lrs-retry/`, and a later retry pass does not POST that statement again. A fixture that returns `503` is queued as `{id}.json` and retried. Fixtures that return `408` and `429` are likewise queued as `{id}.json` and retried. A fixture that returns a complete 2xx or 409 status line and then an oversized remainder (header or body exceeding `--max-open-bytes`) is `lrs_status: "sent"` (the status class is honoured; the host does not read further). A fixture that returns a complete 503 (or 408 or 429) status line and then an oversized remainder is queued; a complete other-4xx status line and an oversized remainder is permanent. A fixture whose LRS response header block exceeds `--max-open-bytes` before a status line is read is a permanent failure: `lrs_status: "failed"`, a `{id}.permanent` file is kept, and a later retry pass does not POST that statement again. Hook point `fail_lrs_retry_write` with `fail_with_errno=N` (§5.2.7) fails a retry-file write, fsync, or rename: the attempt is `lrs_status: "failed"`, the failure is logged, the process stays up, and this is not a DD-138 fail-closed event. The 409 / 400 / 503 / 408 / 429 / oversized-remainder cases use `S3A_KITE_BIN_EGRESS_TEST`; the hook case uses `S3A_KITE_BIN_EGRESS_TEST_HOOKS`.


*   **AT-192** (fail-first). An LRS fixture that accepts the TCP connection but never returns a status, for longer than `--lrs-timeout-secs`, yields `lrs_status: "queued"` and a sendable `{id}.json` when consent remains `on`. A fixture that refuses the connection (nothing listening on the configured LRS address) is also queued. A fixture whose TLS certificate does not validate (name or chain mismatch) is a permanent failure (`lrs_status: "failed"`, a `{id}.permanent` file, no retry POST).
*   **AT-193** (fail-first). `GET /v1/candidates` omits a path that matches the §5.5 glob as a symlink (including a symlink whose target escapes the root), and the response is 200, never `409` `path_escape`. A regular-file control dossier under the glob is still listed.
*   **AT-194** (fail-first). An Edge fixture has a seal-chain break at tile *k* > 0, a type-71 research grant on a verified tile before *k*, and no later denial, withdrawal, or expiry on any parseable tile. Event-log payloads are not withheld (`withheld_count` is exactly 0). Post-break parseable payloads are not counted because the file has valid floors. The same grant placed only on a tile at or after *k* is ignored; a parseable denial on a post-break tile still withholds (DD-111).
*   **AT-195** (fail-first). An Edge fixture has `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` set, no type-70 tile, a type-71 research grant, and parseable event-log payload records (a signature-stripped file). It opens Quarantined with `quarantine_reason: missing_signature`, `invalid_tile_indices` equal to every index in `0 .. tiles_total-1`, and no valid floors. The grant does not release event-log payloads: the file is unauthenticated as a whole, so those payloads stay withheld and `withheld_count` on the banner equals their number (DD-111). A denial in the same file is not required for this assertion.
*   **AT-196** (fail-first). After `/v1/open` of a hyper-tile fixture and a `view=banner` frame, `POST /v1/hit-test` with `view: "manifest"` (no `floor`) returns 200 using that frame, not `409` `no_frame` (aliases share the `first` cache key). The same request with a supplied `floor` returns `400` `invalid_param`. A `view=page` or `view=warehouse` frame request that includes `floor` is also `400` `invalid_param`.
*   **AT-197** (fail-first). A candidate whose `drive_path` canonicalizes inside an allowed root to a FIFO, a device, a directory, or a regular file larger than `--max-open-bytes` has `on_disk: false`. Select and download of that candidate are not `409` `path_escape` and do not hang holding the write mutex.


*   **AT-198** (fail-first). This AT uses `S3A_KITE_BIN_TEST_HOOKS`. Hook point `pause_after_consent_receipt` with `pause_until_signal` (§5.2.7) pauses a consent request after receipt-time latch actions (epoch increment and token swap on a revoke; the same-state / qualify checks on an `on`) and before taking the write mutex, so the other request can be received, or can win the mutex, in a determined order. On a Scholar host with consent `on`, a `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "on" }` and a concurrent `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "off" }` are issued so that the `on` is already in flight when the revoke is received (or is waiting for the write mutex), using that hook to park the first request at READY before the second is sent. After both requests complete, each racing `on` either returned `on` before the revoke's mutex hold (a same-state no-op that wrote nothing) or returned `off`. In every case the final `GET /v1/consent` is `off`, the consent file is `off`, `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` is absent or empty, a subsequent select emits zero LRS requests and writes no retry file, and a startup-style replay of any leftover queue (if the test plants one before the revoke) also emits zero LRS requests. The test asserts only these observable host and disk results; it does not read internal latch state. A later `POST {"state":"on"}` that is issued only after the revoke response has been received may turn consent `on` (`GET` reports `on` and the consent file is `on`). In a second ordering, a revoke is received (epoch incremented, token swapped) while the flag is already `off` from an earlier revoke, and an `on` that is received after that receipt wins the mutex first: after both complete, `GET` is `off`, the consent file is `off`, and a subsequent select emits zero LRS requests (the `on` did not qualify; `revokes_in_flight` was not 0). A later `POST {"state":"on"}` issued after both of those requests have completed returns `on` (`GET` reports `on` and the consent file is `on`): the same-state `off` decremented `revokes_in_flight` at the end of its mutex hold. A same-state `POST {"state":"on"}` while the flag is already `on` does not clear inhibit and does not touch `cleared_epoch`. An unauthenticated or malformed `POST {"state":"off"}` does not increment `epoch` or `revokes_in_flight`. In a third case, a valid `POST {"state":"off"}` is accepted, the host writes READY at `pause_after_consent_receipt` (receipt-time actions have run; the mutex is not yet held), the client closes the socket, and the test then writes CONTINUE: the revoke still runs to completion (decrement included), and a later `POST {"state":"on"}` returns `on`. The second ordering likewise parks the already-`off` revoke at that hook so the concurrent `on` can be received and win the mutex first.
*   **AT-199** (fail-first). After a Scholar host with `--lrs-url` has been stopped leaving `--data-dir/lrs-retry/` and/or `--data-dir/revoke-pending` on disk, a new Scholar start **without** `--lrs-url` (and without `--consent-state-file`) deletes those leftovers, binds, and has tracing unconfigured. If the delete fails (hook points `fail_lrs_retry_delete` and `fail_revoke_pending_remove` with `fail_with_errno=N` are the defined injections for those paths; §5.2.7), startup is a non-zero error that names `lrs-retry` or `revoke-pending` and binds nothing. Scholar start with `--data-dir/lrs-retry` already present as a regular file, FIFO, or symlink is a startup error that names `lrs-retry`, binds nothing, and does not follow or delete through that name.
*   **AT-200** (fail-first). The AT-185 fixture also contains a live research payload for an un-consented subject Y (Y has no research grant on any tile). While the file is intact it is in evaluated mode, so Y is withheld (no trusted grant). After the seal trailer is tampered so `verify_sealed_archive` fails, Y stays withheld: the unique Y string is absent from `text_runs`, `semantics`, hit-test targets, and pixels under the §5.8 render-model pixmap oracle, and `withheld_count` counts Y (and X). The tour does not become unrestricted by the tamper (DD-147).
*   **AT-201** (fail-first). Edge signer-attribution fixtures, also in the AT-165 set, agree with DD-145: (1) a non-empty pin list, earlier type-70 records verify under a pinned key, and a later record verifies only under an embedded unpinned key → `quarantine_reason: signer_not_pinned`, no valid floors; (2) a non-empty pin list, a record fails every key, and an earlier record verified under a pinned key at *s* → `quarantine_reason: signature_known_signer` (not `chain_break`) with `break_index` *s*+1, earlier covered floors remain; (3) a non-empty pin list, a record fails every key, and no earlier authentic pinned record → `signer_not_pinned`, no valid floors; (4) type-70 tiles present, empty pin list, and no `DeviceIdentityRecord` → `missing_signature`, no valid floors; (5) `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear but type-70 tiles present → the signed-file rules apply (not Unsigned solely because the flag is clear); (6) empty pin list, at least one embedded `DeviceIdentityRecord` key, and the first type-70 record (or every type-70 record) fails every embedded key → `missing_signature`, no valid floors (not `signature_known_signer`, not `signer_not_pinned`); (7) empty pin list, an earlier type-70 record verifies under an embedded key at *s*, and a later record fails every embedded key → `signature_known_signer` with `break_index` *s*+1, earlier covered floors remain; (8) a fixture with an authentic record at tile 3, a `chain_break` failing record at tile 4, and a later `signature_known_signer` at tile 7 → `quarantine_reason: signature_known_signer` and `break_index` 4 (the minimum over applicable causes; both causes give 4); every index ≥ 4 is in `invalid_tile_indices` and tiles 4–6 are not valid floors (DD-150, AT-165); (9) a chain-inactive Edge file with type-70 records, including the sparse AT-220 variant (authentic at *s* and *t*, edit at *s*+1, mismatch observed at *t*) → `quarantine_reason: chain_break`, `seal_status: unsealed`, `break_index` *s*+1, and grants on tiles at or after *s*+1 untrusted (AT-220, AT-165); (10) a non-empty pin list, a record that fails every pinned key and verifies under an embedded unpinned key, with a **mismatched** stored `seal_hash` → `quarantine_reason: signer_not_pinned` (not `chain_break`), no valid floors (DD-145).
*   **AT-202** (fail-first). After `GET /v1/candidates` reports `on_disk: true` for a `drive_path`, that path is replaced with a symlink whose target is `--auth-token-file` (ordinary filesystem operations; no host hook), then `POST /v1/download` is issued. The download does not write those token bytes into `--download-dir`, does not hang holding the write mutex, and is not `409` `path_escape`; the path is not a usable on-disk source. The same replacement with a FIFO does not hang the mutex (AT-197). `/v1/open` of a request path that is a symlink, or that is replaced with a symlink before the open, follows DD-146 (`path_escape` or `not_regular_file`) and does not follow the symlink. In a further case, after the pre-step-0 open has accepted a `drive_path` fd under the cap, hook point `grow_drive_path_during_copy` with action `grow` (§5.2.7) makes the file grow so that more than `--max-open-bytes` arrive during the 2b copy: the host deletes the temp, writes no published download file, keeps the inclusion, does not start a mid-request fetch, and returns `200` with `download_error: "source_changed"`.
*   **AT-203** (fail-first). Scholar start with `--working-archive` naming a path whose final component exists as a symlink (including a symlink into `--corpus-root`) is a startup error that names `--working-archive` and binds nothing; the symlink target is not opened or appended to. A missing final component still starts (the parent exists). The host creates and opens the file via the canonical parent dirfd with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)`. After a successful start on a real regular file, the test replaces that final component with a symlink into `--corpus-root` (ordinary filesystem operations; no host hook) and then issues `POST /v1/select`: the host re-opens the final component in that request's step-0/1 hold (it does not keep a startup-lifetime fd) and returns `500` `write_failed` from the openat or the name-binding `fstatat` re-check, does not follow or append to the symlink target, and leaves the target byte-identical (DD-146). A swap that happens after the writer is attached and before the name-binding re-check is AT-219. A swap after that final check is the DD-146 residual (the write may go to the already-open inode; it never follows the symlink).

*   **AT-204** (fail-first). A hyper-tile fixture that is not unauthenticated as a whole, whose only research consent record is a valid-floor withdrawal or denial for subject X (no grant anywhere), and that also contains live research payloads for X and for an unmentioned subject Y, withholds both X and Y under the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table (evaluated mode; no trusted unexpired grant). `withheld_count` counts X and Y. The unique X and Y strings are absent from `text_runs`, `semantics`, hit-test targets, and pixels under the §5.8 render-model pixmap oracle. This is the hyper-tile denial-only case; the Edge denial-only case is AT-23.
*   **AT-205** (fail-first). Pack tests inject a clock via `KiteContext::new_with_clock`. A hyper-tile fixture that is not unauthenticated as a whole, whose only research consent record is a valid-floor grant for subject X whose `expires_at` is in the past relative to the injected `now` (expiry-only; no withdrawal), and that also contains a live research payload for X, withholds X under the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table. `withheld_count` counts X. The same fixture with `now` before `expires_at` (and no withdrawal) shows X. Withholding is re-evaluated at a later render after the injected clock advances past `expires_at`.
*   **AT-206** (fail-first). A wiki or HTML page opened from bytes (pack `open` or `open_verified`, no filesystem path) contains a relative `Link` href that includes a `#fragment`. `hit_test` and the corresponding `SemanticNode` return the raw attribute with the fragment stripped, not a resolved filesystem path.
*   **AT-207** (fail-first). The same relative path `papers/SRC-XXXX.md` is a qualifying item-1 candidate under both `--wiki-root` and `--corpus-root`. The host passes `shadowed_by_wiki: false` for the wiki file and `shadowed_by_wiki: true` for the corpus copy. `open_path` of the wiki file yields `Some(stem)`; `open_path` of the corpus copy yields `None` (the pack does no path I/O). `GET /v1/candidates` lists one entry whose `page_path` is the wiki path; `POST /v1/open` of that `page_path` yields `paper_identity` equal to that `id` (AT-101). In a second case the wiki path exists but is not a qualifying candidate (a symlink, or a regular file with missing or unparseable YAML): `GET /v1/candidates` lists the corpus copy (one entry) with `page_path` equal to the corpus path; `open_path` of the corpus file is called with `shadowed_by_wiki: false` and yields `Some(stem)`; `POST /v1/open` of that `page_path` yields `paper_identity` equal to that `id`.
*   **AT-208** (fail-first). Scholar start with `--consent-state-file` or `--working-archive` whose canonical path is equal to, or inside, `--data-dir/.lock`, `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl`, `--data-dir/revoke-pending`, `--data-dir/lrs-retry/`, or a reserved sidecar `*.lock` is a startup error that names both flags, binds nothing, and mutates none of those paths. Scholar start with `--consent-state-file` equal to `--working-archive`, or with `--working-archive` or `--consent-state-file` equal to `--auth-token-file`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--working-archive.lock`, `--consent-state-file.lock`, `--data-dir/.lock`, `--snapshot-dir/.lock`, or `--download-dir/.lock`, is the same kind of error. These collision checks run in startup step (1) of §5.2.1, before any sidecar lock file is created: a colliding start creates no `.lock` file at the reserved path and mutates none of those paths. A second Scholar host whose `--consent-state-file` canonicalizes to the same path as the first (distinct data dirs otherwise) is a startup error that names the flag, binds nothing, and does not mutate the consent file (the first host already holds `--consent-state-file.lock` from its step (2)). Scholar start with `--working-archive` or `--consent-state-file` whose basename matches `s3a-kite-tmp-*` is a startup error that names the flag, binds nothing, and mutates none of those paths. Scholar start with a FIFO, symlink, or other non-regular file at `--data-dir/.lock`, `--working-archive.lock`, `--snapshot-dir/.lock`, `--download-dir/.lock`, or `--consent-state-file.lock` is a startup error that names the path, binds nothing, and mutates none of those paths (it does not hang).
*   **AT-209** (fail-first). After a successful download that recorded a non-null `snapshot_sha256`, the file at `--snapshot-dir/{sha}.s3a` is replaced with contents that fail the DD-103 reuse checks. A repeat `POST /v1/select` of that identity writes no snapshot, starts no fetch, and does not overwrite the planted file. A repeat `POST /v1/download` of that identity returns HTTP 200 with `download_error: "snapshot_invalid"`, `snapshot_error: "snapshot_invalid"`, `path: null`, `snapshot: null`, and `lrs_status: "skipped"` when `--lrs-url` is configured (otherwise `not_configured`), writes no file in `--download-dir`, keeps the inclusion record, and starts no fetch. A usable `drive_path` for the same identity is not copied in place of the invalid snapshot. This AT does not cover a **missing** `{sha}.s3a` (the file is present but corrupt in the cases above); the missing-file re-fetch is AT-228 / DD-152.

*   **AT-210** (fail-first). An Edge fixture is Verified (or Self-signed) with authentic type-70 records through `tile_index` *k*, and a later CRC-valid tile *m* > *k* carries a type-71 research grant (and no later denial, withdrawal, or expiry on any parseable tile). Tile *m* is not a valid floor, is in `invalid_tile_indices`, and that grant does not release (event-log payloads of that subject are withheld unless an earlier trusted grant already releases them). If the last authentic type-70 record lives in a container tile at index *c* with *k* < *c* < *m* and those records all verify, tile *c* is a valid floor and is not reported as unsigned tail. The file-level `status` remains Verified (or Self-signed); the banner reports the unsigned tail (tile *m*, not the verifying container). This fixture is in the AT-165 set. MICRO-VEC-03 (a normally signed file whose type-70 container follows the tiles it signs) does not show an unsigned-tail banner solely for that container.

*   **AT-211** (fail-first). `open_verified` returns `KiteError` with code `invalid_report` when the supplied report has an index in `invalid_tile_indices` or `torn_tile_indices` outside `0 .. tiles_total-1`, or `torn_tile_indices` is not a subset of `invalid_tile_indices`, or `break_index` is `Some` and is `>= tiles_total` or any index at or after `break_index` is missing from `invalid_tile_indices`, or `quarantine_reason` is `signer_not_pinned` or `missing_signature` and `invalid_tile_indices` is not the full range, or `status` is `quarantined` with `quarantine_reason` `None`, or `quarantine_reason` is `Some` and `status` is not `quarantined` (including `verified` or `unsigned` with `signer_not_pinned`), or `quarantine_reason` is `chain_break`, `signature_known_signer`, or `crc` and `break_index` is `None`, or a hyper-tile report uses `quarantine_reason` `signature_known_signer`, `chain_break`, `missing_signature`, or `signer_not_pinned`, or a hyper-tile report has `status` `verified` or `self_signed` (with or without a reason), or an Edge report uses `quarantine_reason` `seal_trailer`, or the report is supplied for `wiki` / `html` / `other` bytes. A report whose `status` is `unverified` remains the existing `KiteError` (AT-154), not `invalid_report`. A well-formed report still applies the DD-148 predicate to the supplied report (AT-137).

*   **AT-212** (fail-first). After step 1 has succeeded, hook points `fail_snapshot_write_temp`, `fail_snapshot_write_fsync`, or `fail_snapshot_write_rename` with `fail_once_with_errno=N` (§5.2.7) fail the step-2 snapshot temp write, fsync, or rename respectively, and only that first matching operation. The response is `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and an `lrs_status` (step 3 still ran on the request that wrote the inclusion line; with `--lrs-url` configured this is not omitted). The inclusion record is kept, no snapshot is published, the temp is deleted, and the in-flight registry entry is gone: a later select of the same identity does not hang waiting for that GET and retries the fetch (no `snapshot_sha256` was recorded); because the hook is one-shot, that later select's snapshot write is not failed by it. A concurrent waiter of the first request receives `500` `write_failed` with its own `included` / `lrs_status`.

*   **AT-213** (fail-first). After step 1 has succeeded, the snapshot temp is written, fsync'd, and renamed, and hook point `fail_snapshot_index_append` with `fail_once_with_errno=N` (§5.2.7) then fails the snapshot index-line append, and only that first matching operation. The response is `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and an `lrs_status` (step 3 still ran). The published `{sha}.s3a` file is left in `--snapshot-dir` for DD-103 reuse; no `snapshot_sha256` is recorded. The registry entry is removed (a later select does not hang). A later select of the same identity, with captured bytes hashing to that `sha`, reuses the left file if the DD-103 checks pass, or returns `409` `snapshot_conflict` if they fail, and does not overwrite the file; because the hook is one-shot, that later select's index append (when reuse records the sha) is not failed by it.

*   **AT-214** (fail-first). After step 1 has succeeded, hook point `fail_download_write` with `fail_with_errno=N` (§5.2.7) fails **every** step-2b download temp write, fsync, or rename in the test (including a waiter's own 2b). The request whose 2b failed gets `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and an `lrs_status` (step 3 still ran). No published file appears in `--download-dir`, the temp is deleted, and the inclusion is kept. A waiter of the GET first received the step-2 snapshot outcome and then ran its own 2b; that waiter's failed 2b also returns `500` `write_failed` with its own `included` / `lrs_status`. A later request does not hang.

*   **AT-215** (fail-first). A planted FIFO, symlink, or other non-regular file already sits at `--snapshot-dir/{sha}.s3a`. `POST /v1/select` or `/v1/download` of a candidate whose newly fetched captured bytes would have that `sha` returns `409` `snapshot_conflict` with `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1 (AT-180). When that sha is already recorded, a repeat select or download returns HTTP 200 with `snapshot: null` and `snapshot_error: "snapshot_invalid"` (AT-209). In both cases the host does not follow the symlink, does not hang on the FIFO, does not overwrite the planted path, and a concurrent `GET /v1/consent` is not blocked indefinitely (no hang on a FIFO or symlink).

*   **AT-216** (fail-first). A planted FIFO, symlink, or other non-regular file already sits at `--download-dir/{sha}`. `POST /v1/download` of the paper that would use that name returns `409` `download_conflict` with `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion record exists for the key after step 0/1. The host does not follow the symlink, does not hang on the FIFO, and does not overwrite the planted path. A regular file at that path that is larger than `--max-open-bytes` is the same `409` `download_conflict` (AT-223).

*   **AT-217** (fail-first). `/v1/open` of the Scholar working-archive path uses the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule and pack `open` (not `open_path`); on success `paper_identity` is `None`. When the test has made the file larger than `--max-open-bytes` before the open (ordinary filesystem operations; no host hook), the host returns `409` `file_too_large`. When the test replaces the final component with a symlink, or the `(dev, ino)` no longer matches the recorded startup or create identity, the host returns `409` `path_escape` and does not follow the symlink. When the final component is a FIFO, directory, or other non-regular file, the host returns `409` `not_regular_file` and does not hang. A concurrent `GET` or `POST /v1/consent` is not blocked indefinitely by a hanging open (it may wait for the write mutex for a bounded copy).

*   **AT-218** (fail-first). After a first `GET /v1/candidates` has listed a dossier under the item-1 glob and a corpus `*.s3a` that contributes an item-2 candidate, the test replaces each of those paths with a FIFO, and, in a second variant, with a symlink to `--auth-token-file` (ordinary filesystem operations between the two requests; no host hook, §5.2.7). A second `GET /v1/candidates` returns 200 promptly (it does not hang), omits those paths, and the response and `--log-dir` contain no token bytes. A concurrent `POST /v1/select` or membership check of an already-known identity also does not hang.

*   **AT-219** (fail-first). After a successful Scholar start on a real regular `--working-archive` file, hook point `pause_before_name_binding_check` with `pause_until_signal` (§5.2.7) pauses the host after the writer is attached to the hold's re-opened fd and immediately before the name-binding `fstatat(parent_dirfd, basename, AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` re-check. The test then replaces the final component with a symlink into `--corpus-root` and writes CONTINUE. `POST /v1/select` returns `500` `write_failed`, writes no tile-10, does not follow, append to, or truncate the symlink target, and leaves the target byte-identical. The name-binding check fails (the directory entry is no longer the recorded regular file).

*   **AT-220** (fail-first). A chain-inactive Edge fixture (`FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` clear) carries type-70 records. One mid-file committed tile has been edited and its CRC recomputed so the stored `seal_hash` no longer equals the recomputed `compute_seal_hash`, while the type-70 signatures still verify over the stored (stale) hashes. Pack `open` / `verification_report` and a reader-core `verify` mapped by the §5.1 table agree: `status` is `quarantined`, `seal_status` is `unsealed`, `quarantine_reason` is `chain_break`, `break_index` is (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none, and every tile at or after that index is in `invalid_tile_indices` and untrusted for consent (a later type-71 grant on a post-break tile does not release). In a **sparse** variant, authentic records sit at tiles *s* and *t* (*s* < *t*), and tile *s*+1 has been edited (CRC recomputed) so the mismatch is first observed at *t*: `break_index` is *s*+1, tile *s*+1 is untrusted, and a planted type-71 grant on *s*+1 does not release. In a **dense** variant (a verified record at every committed non-container tile), a mismatch at tile *k* has `break_index` *k*. A fixture whose stored hashes all match and whose signatures verify under a pinned key is Verified with `seal_status` `unsealed` (not auto-quarantined solely because the chain is inactive). These fixtures are also in the AT-165 set.

*   **AT-221** (fail-first). On a Scholar host with consent `on`, a `POST /v1/select` of a current candidate starts a slow `egress_test` GET (the request has already passed candidate membership and step 1). The starter's client closes the socket during that GET. A concurrent waiter for the same `(file_uuid, identity)` and a later `POST /v1/select` of the same identity both complete (neither hangs). The in-flight registry entry does not remain. With consent on, **exactly one** LRS statement is sent or queued for that identity (step 3 ran on the starter; the later request skips step 3 because step 0 matches). Step 1 is complete: the inclusion index has the line, or the defined `500` `write_failed` with `included: true` was already produced; there is no half-finished tile-10-without-index left without that defined response.


*   **AT-222** (fail-first). A host binary compiled without the `test_hooks` feature (including a `--release` distribution build) started with `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS` set to any value, including a well-formed `POINT=ACTION` list from the §5.2.7 table, is a startup error that names `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS`, binds nothing, and performs no hooked action. The same error occurs if only `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOK_READY` or only `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOK_CONTINUE` is set. A `test_hooks` build started with an unknown hook point, a malformed value, or `pause_until_signal` without both signal-path variables, is a startup error that names `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS` and binds nothing.

*   **AT-223** (fail-first). Scholar start with `--max-fetch-bytes` greater than `--max-open-bytes` is a startup error that names both flags, binds nothing, and creates no sidecar lock file. After a start with `--max-fetch-bytes` ≤ `--max-open-bytes`, a download whose destination path already exists as a regular file larger than `--max-open-bytes` returns `409` `download_conflict` with `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion exists, does not overwrite the file, and keeps the inclusion (the existing-file hash check cannot complete under the cap).

*   **AT-224** (fail-first). Scholar start with a well-formed `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl` whose size is greater than `--max-open-bytes` succeeds, binds, and uses the existing lines (it is not a startup error for size). A present index that is a symlink or FIFO is still a startup error that names the path, binds nothing, and does not follow or hang. The same startup error (names the flag, binds nothing, does not follow or hang) applies to `--auth-token-file` when that path is a FIFO or a symlink, or is larger than `--max-open-bytes`.

*   **AT-225** (fail-first). Two non-quarantined corpus `*.s3a` files each contain a live, un-withheld `AcademicPaperRecord` with the same `paper_id` (and no mark withholding). After sorting the containing `.s3a` paths in bytewise ascending order of their canonical absolute paths, `GET /v1/candidates` lists that `paper_id` **once**, taken from the first record in tile order in the first of those files (DD-61). The listed candidate's `page_path` is `null` (AT-101). Its `archive_path` is the canonical path of that first file, not the later path.

*   **AT-226** (fail-first). A parameterized deny-list fixture, using literal addresses and no resolver override (DD-140), denies at connect time each of: a CGNAT address in `100.64.0.0/10`, an IPv6 site-local address in `fec0::/10`, and a NAT64 / 6to4 translated form of an RFC1918 range. A select or redirect hop to each is `fetch_error: "egress_denied"`, writes no snapshot, and makes no TCP connection to that address. A select or redirect whose URL host is the name `metadata.google.internal` is denied **before any DNS query**: the test observes zero DNS queries and zero connects for that name, and the result is `fetch_error: "egress_denied"` with no snapshot. The newly-observed-LRS-IP union is a unit test, living in `crates/s3a-kite` (no network), of `s3a_kite::egress::lrs_deny_union(startup_ips, connect_time_ips, addr) -> bool`: an address present only in `connect_time_ips` is denied even if it is global unicast and was not in the startup set; an address in neither set is not denied by this function alone. A Scholar start with `--lrs-url` set to an `https` hostname that does not resolve is a startup error that names `--lrs-url` and binds nothing (host-spawn; the test may use a reserved TLD name that is not in `/etc/hosts`). The "zero DNS queries" observation for `metadata.google.internal` is made by running the host in a network namespace whose `/etc/resolv.conf` points at a counting stub resolver; this is not a host flag and is not a resolver-override flag (DD-140).

*   **AT-227** (fail-first). With `--max-fetch-bytes` ≤ `--max-open-bytes`, a select of a current candidate whose captured body is small enough to fetch, but whose sealed snapshot file is larger than `--max-open-bytes`, returns `200` with `fetch_error: "snapshot_too_large"`, keeps the inclusion, records no `snapshot_sha256`, publishes no `{sha}.s3a`, still runs step 3 when consent is on, and removes the registry entry. A later select of the same identity may retry the fetch. The test uses a body size and cap pair that produces this overhead case (it does not invent a numeric formula in the implementation). A download of the same candidate returns `200` with `fetch_error: "snapshot_too_large"`, `download_error: "fetch_failed"`, `path: null`, `snapshot: null`, skips 2b, keeps the inclusion, still runs step 3 when consent is on, and removes the registry entry. A waiter of an in-flight GET that ends in `snapshot_too_large` receives that same fetch-failure outcome (a download waiter has `download_error: "fetch_failed"`, `path: null`, `snapshot: null`, and skips 2b).

*   **AT-228** (fail-first). After a successful download that recorded a non-null `snapshot_sha256`, the file at `--snapshot-dir/{sha}.s3a` is **deleted** (missing, not replaced with failing contents). A repeat `POST /v1/download` of that identity, with the same `source_url` fixture returning the **same** bytes, starts a fetch, publishes `{sha}.s3a` again (a create, not an overwrite), returns `200` with that snapshot path, does not return `snapshot_invalid`, and does not write a second tile-10. A second case deletes `{sha}.s3a` and has the fixture return **different** bytes hashing to `new_sha`: the host publishes `{new_sha}.s3a` under the usual new-snapshot rules, appends a new inclusion-index line for that verb with `snapshot_sha256 = new_sha` (no second tile-10), and returns `200` with the new snapshot path; the last matching index line has `new_sha`. A download in either case delivers from the published snapshot (`path` and `snapshot` are non-null; 2b runs). A third case, after the second case has recorded `new_sha` and published `{new_sha}.s3a`, plants a corrupt file at the earlier `{sha}.s3a` path: a later download of that identity delivers the usable `{new_sha}.s3a` (the last recorded sha), and is not `snapshot_invalid`. A present-but-failing file for the **last** recorded sha remains AT-209 and is not this test.

*   **AT-229** (fail-first). After a Scholar host with consent `on`, hook point `fail_revoke_pending_remove` with `fail_once_with_errno=N` (§5.2.7) fails the runtime step-4 unlink of `revoke-pending` and only that first unlink. The revoke returns 200, leaves the file in place, and the process stays up (this is not a DD-138 fail-closed event). A later qualifying `POST /v1/consent` `{ "state": "on" }` in the same process then succeeds at the unlink, `fsync`s the parent, and persists `on`; a subsequent restart with the same flags keeps consent `on` and does not rewrite the file to `off`. A further case uses `fail_with_errno=N` (process-lifetime, not one-shot) so the later `on` also sees the hook: the unlink fails, the host does not write the consent file `on`, stops LRS, and exits non-zero (treated as a DD-138 fail-closed event).

*   **AT-230** (fail-first). On a Scholar (or viewer-only) host bound to a non-loopback address with TLS, an unauthenticated peer that does not finish sending the request header block within `--handle-idle-secs`, or that goes idle between header bytes for that long, has its connection closed without a response. The same bound runs from TCP accept and includes the TLS handshake: a peer that completes TCP and never sends a ClientHello, or that stalls mid-handshake, is likewise closed without a response when `--handle-idle-secs` elapses. A header block that arrives in time but exceeds `--max-request-bytes` is still `413` `request_too_large` (AT-171). This AT uses `S3A_KITE_BIN`. The concurrent-connection cap is AT-231.
*   **AT-231** (fail-first). On a Scholar (or viewer-only) host bound to a non-loopback address with TLS, at most 8 connections may be open at once. A connection occupies a slot from TCP accept until close (including during the TLS handshake, header-read, request handling, and any keep-alive idle). With 8 slots occupied (for example eight peers that have completed TCP and are stalled in the handshake or are holding an established connection), a ninth incoming connection is accepted at the TCP layer and then closed immediately, without a TLS handshake and without an HTTP response. When one of the 8 closes, a later incoming connection may occupy the freed slot and complete a handshake. A loopback IP bind and a Unix-socket bind have no such cap. This cap is fixed (not a flag). This AT uses `S3A_KITE_BIN`.
*   **AT-232** (fail-first). An Edge file that contains a valid type-75 floor, or a valid floor of any type other than 64, 70, or 71, is shown as a metadata-only floor (type, size, `record_count`) and never paints raw or decoded payload fields, both when a research denial is present on the file and when a research grant is present. The §5.8 render-model pixmap oracle is applied: a control that replaces those payload field values after decode, before paint, yields a byte-identical pixmap (the withheld payload is not a painted input). Type-64 32-byte and 96-byte layouts, type 70, and type 71 keep their existing tests (AT-19, AT-67, AT-134, and the type-71 consent rules). Lives in `kite-pack-tests` with the other floor-display ATs.

*   **AT-233** (fail-first). `render_page` of a corpus or wiki Markdown file that contains a GFM table and a relative CommonMark image whose file sits beside the page under the same allowed request root produces a page view in which: the table cell text appears as contiguous substrings of the text layer; a `SemanticNode` (or equivalent image semantic) is present for the image; and the pixmap is not identical to a control that omits the image file (the image is actually painted). A second case whose image destination is an `https://` URL, and a third case whose destination resolves outside the allowed root, paint the page without loading those bytes (no network, no escape read); the table text still appears. Tables are parsed with `pulldown-cmark` `Options::ENABLE_TABLES` (Q-04, DD-13, DD-118).

*   **AT-234** (fail-first). `POST /v1/select` of a current candidate whose `source_url` is a successful `egress_test` fixture writes no tile-10 inclusion record, creates no file under `--snapshot-dir`, appends no inclusion-index line, and does not fingerprint the payload. The LRS statement, when consent is on, uses verb IRI `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/opened`. A following `POST /v1/download` of the same identity writes one tile-10 inclusion record and one snapshot, and the LRS statement uses verb IRI `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/saved`. Both IRIs exist in `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs` at s3a-storage `b7bdcda0df63524ffe205f2ad92cd6a5b69a40f6`; the test does not invent a new IRI (Q-07, DD-154, AT-83).

*   **AT-235** (fail-first). After a `POST /v1/select` of a current candidate (no download), a `POST /v1/deselect` of that identity with body `{ "paper_id": "..." }` only returns `200` and, when consent is on, an LRS statement with verb IRI `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/removed`. No snapshot file is created, no fingerprint is written, no tile-10 inclusion record is written, and no inclusion-index line is appended; the paper is not in the corpus. A body that includes a `reason` field is `400` `invalid_request` and still writes none of those artifacts. A `POST /v1/skip` of a different current candidate still writes nothing (no event, no LRS statement). `POST /v1/deselect` of an identity that was never selected is `404` `not_selected`. After a successful download, `POST /v1/deselect` of that identity is `409` `already_included` and does not delete the inclusion, snapshot, or fingerprint (Q-07, DD-154).

*   **AT-236** (fail-first). With consent on and an LRS fixture that fails transiently on every POST, a `POST /v1/download` returns with `lrs_status: "queued"` without waiting the backoff interval (the handler is not blocked for 10 seconds). The host then retries in the background: three tries in total, 10 seconds apart, still re-checking consent before each send. After the third failure, a local failure record names the affected entry ids and the LRS error. A diagnosis export or request, if exercised, contains only the error code and message and does not contain the xAPI statement body, `--researcher-id`, paper identity, or source URL (Q-09, DD-51, DD-156).

*   **AT-237** (fail-first). A Scholar host started with `--lrs-url` and without `--lrs-actor-homepage` builds the actor homepage itself: in lab mode (no `--study-id`) it is `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/scholar/lab`; with `--study-id` `PROJ-42` it is `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/scholar/study/PROJ-42`. Passing `--lrs-actor-homepage` is a startup error that names the removed flag and binds nothing. In lab mode a `--researcher-id` that is a username or an email is accepted. In study mode a `--researcher-id` of `P-017` is accepted; a value containing `@` or a space (a name or email) is a startup error that names `--researcher-id` and binds nothing (Q-08, Q-13, DD-155, DD-157).

*   **AT-238** (fail-first). A hyper-tile fixture whose seal trailer has been completely stripped, leaving a recognised remnant, is tagged as a damaged sealed remnant, is already restricted (`seal_failed`, unauthenticated as a whole), and is not silently treated as a never-sealed file. A repair attempt on that remnant that would re-seal it is refused. When no verified copy is available from which to restore, the remnant is flagged unrepairable. This AT does not require the later repair agent to exist; it asserts the tag, the refusal to re-seal, and the unrepairable flag (Q-18, DD-141, DD-159).

*   **AT-239** (fail-first, and a guard once the fd-taking engine API exists). A snapshot temp is created, appended, and sealed through an fd-taking `s3a-engine` API on the same file description that holds `LOCK_EX`. The test holds that lock and shows there is no second open of the snapshot path (no `open`/`openat` of the path, and no `/proc/self/fd` reopen) between create and seal (F-13).


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------


## 8. Draft decisions

Ashley may accept a draft, change it, or leave it open. Draft values follow. DD-01–DD-152 keep their topics except **DD-63, which remains retired** (use DD-67 / DD-147); **DD-126 remains unused**. Changes of meaning since v22 are noted in the "Notes" column and in the round-22 disposition. DD-128 (phone in-scope role), DD-138 (fail-closed consent persist), and the intent of DD-142 (consent transitions serialized; revoke wins), DD-143 (no candidate cache), DD-144 (lock-free inhibit), DD-145 (Edge signer attribution), DD-146 (open-then-validate via fd), DD-147 (single consent table), DD-148 (unauthenticated-as-a-whole predicate), DD-149 (freeze after the first clean goldfish round), and DD-150 (total report-field derivation) are kept.

| ID | Topic | Draft value | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **DD-01** | Pack location | `crates/s3a-pack-kite` in the s3a-storage workspace, with the Kitesurf Rust core vendored from `ashleycribb/s3a-Kite` at commit `235111c6fe10f2d63f3476bf5e3d19b4c7089415`. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-02** | Host compile mode | Link `kitesurf-core` and `renderer-core` with `native-host` as native rlibs. Wasmtime and Boa are not used in v1. | Unchanged in meaning. |
| **DD-03** | Bind address and port | Required flags `--bind` and `--port`, except `--port` is forbidden when `--bind` is `unix:`. No silent production default. Docs may show `127.0.0.1:8787` as an example. | Tightened (F-24). |
| **DD-04** | Visual metaphor | Call the UI a warehouse. Data model remains lobby + floors. Bays map 1:1 to floors. Bay *area* is approximately linear in stride (sides rounded to the nearest integer; DD-70). | Tightened (F-19). |
| **DD-05** | Device-side stored manifest | Not written on the MCU in v1. The hub/PC/phone shows the derived header sheet whose fields are listed in §5.4. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-06** | Slimmed renderer / MCU | v1 uses the same `native-host` + `vello_cpu` renderer on hub, PC, and phone. No second renderer until a measured memory budget on a named device fails. Never on the microcontroller. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-07** | Event colors | Color by severity using the Okabe-Ito palette (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-10): 0 (normal) `#0072B2`, 1 (warning) `#E69F00`, 2 and 3 (critical) `#D55E00`. Print `event_type` as the EDGE §5.2.2 name, and always show the non-color category label and the `severity: N` label in §5.4 so colour is never the only signal (AT-19). | Amended (v24.1, Q-10). |
| **DD-08** | Pass 1 timer | Show a 15-second skim timer in the native UI. It is client-side. Expiry does nothing to records and the host has no write-on-timer endpoint. | Tightened (F-31). |
| **DD-09** | Snapshot and inclusion vs consent | Snapshots and inclusion records of **downloaded** papers are local writes (the working archive and `--snapshot-dir`) and do **not** require tracing consent. Select and deselect do not snapshot or write inclusion. Skip does not snapshot. Tracing consent gates only LRS xAPI. Formal confirmation of this DD is still outstanding, as left at the end of the Q review; only the select-equals-inclusion wording is corrected here. | Amended (v24.1, DD-154). |
| **DD-10** | Device transport | v1 opens files already on the hub, PC, or phone. BLE/USB/SoftAP stay Edge Phase 2. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-11** | Host authentication | Fail closed. `--auth-token-file` points at a token that is not in the repository. Unauthenticated requests are 401 except the tokenless health case in §5.2.1. | Tightened (DD-71). |
| **DD-12** | LRS URL | Must be configured (`--lrs-url`) to emit. No invented localhost default. The v3 "consent on and URL missing, statement fails closed" state is unreachable: that combination is a startup error (F-23). | Amended. |
| **DD-13** | Markdown | The pack parses CommonMark with `pulldown-cmark` (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-04), with GFM tables enabled (`Options::ENABLE_TABLES`), raw HTML disabled or escaped, to HTML, then native-host Blitz lays out that HTML. No JS and no remote subresources on wiki/corpus pages. Academic papers need tables and images: a relative Markdown image that resolves inside the allowed request root of the opened page may be loaded as in the DD-118 exception (AT-233). | Amended (v24.1, Q-04). |
| **DD-14** | PDF pages and `kind: "other"` | v1 does not rasterize PDF input and invents no title or abstract. A file that is not S3A and whose name hint is not `.md`/`.html`/`.htm` is `kind: "other"`, `first_view: "page"`, and `render_page` shows the exact label `no text conversion in archive` (AT-148). | Tightened (F-39). |
| **DD-15** | New tile IDs | None in v1. Snapshots use existing BLOB + PROVENANCE. Inclusion uses Scholar tiles when `ADDON_EDU` is set. The inclusion index is a file under `--data-dir`, not a new tile. | Clarified (F-05). |
| **DD-16** | Addon mask | No new addon bit in v1. Scholar writes require `ADDON_EDU`. A missing bit on an existing working archive is checked in the step-0/1 hold on every select/download, whether or not step 1 is skipped (not at startup), and is `409` `addon_missing` with no write and no `lrs_status` (AT-46). | Tightened (v19, F-21); intent kept. |
| **DD-17** | Upstream pin | Kitesurf / s3a-Kite `235111c6fe10f2d63f3476bf5e3d19b4c7089415`. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-18** | Animation timing | Match `tour_html_template.html`: 0.45s `cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)` from the bay rect at the current scroll to `0.06w / 0.08h / 0.88w / 0.84h`, skipped when `instant` is set. `1vw = width/100`, `1vh = height/100`. | Tightened (F-20). |
| **DD-19** | App relationship | s3a-kite is the viewer inside `s3a-reader`. Verification exists in both the pack and `s3a-reader-core`. The pack algorithm in §5.4 is normative for the host. The host always runs that algorithm itself and does not prefer `open_verified`. A mapped reader-core report may still be passed to `open_verified` by the app (DD-115, AT-165). | Tightened (v7, F-10). |
| **DD-20** | Quarantine UX | A quarantined file can be opened with a persistent banner. Failed-CRC, torn, and chain-broken tiles are not valid floors (§5.4 item 2). Seal-trailer failure is AT-90. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-21** | GPU | v1 stays on `vello_cpu`. No wgpu, Vulkan, Metal, or GLES backend at all in the pack dependency tree. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-22** | Mobile fonts | Ship Noto Sans, Italic, Serif, Mono, and Symbols. `system_fonts` remains false. Color Emoji and CJK SC are optional bundled asset packs, not downloaded. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-23** | Android ABIs and UI | minSdk 26, `arm64-v8a` and `x86_64`, UniFFI + Kotlin, Jetpack Compose. No 32-bit ARM in v1. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-24** | Kotlin deck plan | The kite warehouse is the primary view and the default screen after open. `MapLayout` may remain as a secondary diagnostic screen, not the default. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-25** | iOS | Feasible. Ship Android first. iOS follows when AT-40 stays green. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-26** | Page JS on a native host | **v1 does not run page JavaScript.** HTML is laid out with JS disabled. Wasmtime is not required. There is no general open-by-URL in v1 (DD-59). | Clarified (F-02). |
| **DD-27** | Write target | Inclusion records go to `--working-archive`. Snapshots go to `--snapshot-dir`. The corpus is read-only to kite. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-28** | Write API, seal, lock | The host re-opens the working archive in every step-0/1 hold (`openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK)` from the parent dirfd; no startup-lifetime fd). Create with `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` (plus `enable_addon` and the create-time `ConsentRecord`) is startup-only; a failed startup create is a startup error that names `--working-archive`. Immediately before each tile-10 write, `fstatat(parent_dirfd, basename, AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` must match `fstat(writer_fd)` and the recorded identity (AT-203, AT-219). Header reads for `file_uuid` and `addon_mask` use that hold's fd. A mid-process identity change is `write_failed` until restart. Fd-taking `TileWriter` in `s3a-engine` or `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` (working-archive hold only; snapshot and download temps must use an fd-taking API on the same file description that holds `LOCK_EX`, not `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` or a path reopen). A swap after the final name-binding check may write the already-open inode, never the symlink target. New archives get `ADDON_EDU` and an active research-purpose `ConsentRecord`; snapshots sealed with `seal_archive`. | Tightened (v24, F-13); intent kept. |
| **DD-29** | Health, token, TLS | Health is 200 `text/plain` `ok\n`; token rules in DD-68; TLS required for non-loopback binds. | Token charset tightened (F-14). |
| **DD-30** | Roots | Roots and working-archive parent canonicalization as in §5.2.2. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-31** | Host-fetch egress | Deny list always applies, at connect time and every redirect hop, including mapped and non-global unicast forms (DD-64, DD-76). | Tightened (F-09, F-29). |
| **DD-32** | Human control | Only the UI token may call write routes. The native window is an HTTP client that sends that token. Custody of the token file remains the control point. | **Amended** (F-04, DD-60). |
| **DD-33** | Tracing consent model | `--consent-state-file`; `--researcher-id`; revoke drops LRS and the retry queue, keeps local records. Retries re-check consent (DD-66). | Tightened (F-11). |
| **DD-34** | Inclusion side effects | These steps apply to **download** (the inclusion decision, DD-154). Order: step 0 index lookup, then local inclusion record and index, then fetch/snapshot, then LRS. Idempotency key is working-archive `file_uuid` + identity + verb. A matching index line for the download verb skips step 1 and step 3 (no new LRS statement). Failure of step 1 skips 2, 2b, and 3. Fetch failure keeps the inclusion record. Select and deselect log their own events and may send LRS, but they skip inclusion steps 1, 2, and 2b (no snapshot, fingerprint, or index line). | Amended (v24.1, DD-154). |
| **DD-35** | Missing optional fonts | The first Play/App build does not ship the optional CJK and emoji font packs (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-05). Open succeeds, missing glyphs show `.notdef`, no system fonts, no network. Those packs may be added later if someone asks. | Amended (v24.1, Q-05). |
| **DD-36** | Manifest-to-tour trigger | Explicit `proceed_to_warehouse` / `POST /v1/proceed` only; no timer. A Continue `hit_test` is pure and does not change state (DD-97). | Tightened (F-05). |
| **DD-37** | Warehouse scale and frame API | The §5.1 functions, including `render_page`. Bay sides 32 / 45 / 181 CSS px (DD-70). `device_pixel_ratio` is on every signature (DD-69). | Amended (F-01, F-18, F-19). |
| **DD-38** | Text and semantics layer | Every frame returns `text_runs` and `semantics` with the schema in §5.8. | Schema now inlined (F-03). |
| **DD-39** | Phone Scholar writes | **Out of v1.** Phone builds are viewers only. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-40** | Inconsistent Edge headers | Quarantine / `kind: "unsupported"`; do not guess a stride. Other strides: AT-97. | Tightened (F-27). |
| **DD-41** | No CDP, no agent tokens | v1 does not reimplement CDP and does not mint or accept an agent token. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-42** | Native UI and Host HTTP API | The native window is optional and is a client of §5.2.1 (DD-60). | Amended (F-04). |
| **DD-43** | `KiteError` | Every fallible pack function returns `Result<_, KiteError>`. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-44** | Page JS off in v1 | No Boa, no Wasmtime, no wasm32 page-isolate ABI in v1. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-45** | Phone is viewer-only | v1 phone UniFFI is an S3A / Edge-tile viewer: `KiteContext::new`, `open`, `open_verified`, `verification_report`, `close`, `proceed_to_warehouse`, render, `hit_test`, and the `KiteVerificationReport` type. It does not export `open_path`, `paper_identity`, or any Scholar 3-pass write. Later phone research scope is DD-128. Phone `max_frame_pixels` / `max_open_bytes` are Q-17. | Tightened (v13, F-04). |
| **DD-46** | Paper identity and inclusion record | Identity is the `SRC-NNNN` stem or a decimal `paper_id` (DD-61). `object_hash` is CRC32C of the UTF-8 identity (`u32`). `actor_hash` is `hash_iri(--researcher-id)` (`u64`). Verb mapping is Q-07. | **Amended**: actor_hash is no longer CRC32C; idempotency is not by `object_hash` alone. |
| **DD-47** | Snapshot chunking | 40-byte prefix; `blob_key` is SHA-256 of the complete captured bytes. Provenance document is the three-line text in §5.6. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-48** | One researcher, durable disk | One Scholar-mode process serves one `--researcher-id` and one working archive. Durable local disk is required in Scholar mode, not in viewer-only mode (DD-78). Cloud Run is not a v1 target. | Tightened (F-36). |
| **DD-49** | Fetch timeout | Scholar mode requires `--fetch-timeout-secs`. No silent default. | Unchanged. |
| **DD-50** | Consent file writer | Required when `--lrs-url` is set; forbidden when it is absent. Host is the only writer, including first-run bootstrap (DD-96). Toggle after start is `POST /v1/consent`. | Tightened (F-03, F-30). |
| **DD-51** | LRS retry queue | Files under `--data-dir/lrs-retry/`. Revoke deletes the directory. Every send re-checks consent (DD-66). A send is at most 3 tries, 10 seconds apart (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-09, DD-156). The first try may run in the request, bounded by `--lrs-timeout-secs`; the HTTP response is never delayed by the 10-second backoff. Tries 2 and 3 run in the background. After the third failure, the failure and the affected entry ids are recorded for a future AI Teacher's Assistant; only the error code and message may be sent off for diagnosis, never the xAPI statement or researcher data (AT-236). | Amended (v24.1, Q-09). |
| **DD-52** | `--behind-proxy` | Accepted only with a loopback or Unix-socket bind. With the flag, `/health` also requires the token. | Tightened (F-24). |
| **DD-53** | Consent filtering | A file is unrestricted only if authentication is trusted and there are zero consent records of any kind in any parseable tile (DD-147). Header sheet and `DeviceIdentityRecord` are exempt. Create of the working archive still writes a research-purpose `ConsentRecord` (DD-105); snapshots get none (AT-107). No snapshot exemption (DD-63 retired; DD-147 applies). The only normative rule is §5.3 item 4 / DD-147. | Tightened (v13, F-01, F-02, F-09). |
| **DD-54** | Product name | The product will be renamed to Navigator: crate/binary `s3a-navigator`, display name "S3A Navigator" (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-11). The GitHub fork is now [ashleycribb/s3a-Navigator](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-Navigator) (the former `s3a-Kite` URL redirects). This revision records the decision only. Crate, binary, flag, and environment-variable names in this spec stay `s3a-kite` / `S3A_KITE_*` until a later dedicated rename build step. | Amended (v24.1, Q-11). |
| **DD-55** | Required dirs and ephemeral temp | `--log-dir` is required in both modes; `--data-dir` is required in Scholar mode and **not accepted** in viewer-only mode. Ephemeral temp is deleted on exit. | Tightened (F-36). |
| **DD-56** | Download directory | `--download-dir` must exist in Scholar mode. Names are `{sha256_hex}`. Same-bytes re-download is a success no-op. A planted name with different bytes fails and does not overwrite. Delivery (step 2b) runs only after a successful step 1; a failed inclusion leaves `--download-dir` unchanged. The earlier "keep a delivered file if inclusion fails" sentence is deleted as unreachable. | Tightened (F-05). |
| **DD-57** | Edge signature failure | A failed `DeviceSealRecord` signature invalidates every tile at or after `break_index` = (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none, as specified in §5.4 item 2 / DD-145 / DD-150. Gap tiles in that range are untrusted. | Tightened (v24, F-04); intent kept. |
| **DD-58** | Page render and links | `render_page` and `view=page` are the wiki/HTML API. `hit_test` may return `Select` or `Link`. Links are not auto-fetched. Writes go through the Host HTTP API. | New (F-01). |
| **DD-59** | Source model and fetch trigger | Decision table in §5.5 applies to **download** (DD-154): source_url only → fetch+snapshot; on-disk only → copy, no snapshot; both → prefer on-disk, no fetch; neither → inclusion written, `download_error: "no_source"`. Select does not fetch or snapshot. Relative `drive_path` is resolved against the dossier parent. The dossier YAML key for a remote URL is `source_url` (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-12). The dossier `.md` is not the on-disk source. No open-by-URL in v1. | Amended (v24.1, Q-07, Q-12). |
| **DD-60** | Native window and headless | Cargo feature `native-window` plus runtime `--headless`. A build without the feature must pass `--headless`. The window is a Host HTTP API client that sends the UI token (except the tokenless health case) and is allowed only on loopback or Unix binds, which never need TLS. The HTTP listener always starts. | Tightened (F-31). |
| **DD-61** | Identity, candidates, index | SRC stem vs decimal `paper_id` are distinct identities (no join). Duplicate SRC paths: wiki-root wins if and only if a qualifying item-1 wiki candidate exists at the same relative path; the host passes that fact as `ScholarRoots.shadowed_by_wiki` and the pack does no path I/O (AT-207). The SRC glob is exactly `{root}/papers/SRC-*.md` (not recursive). Duplicate `paper_id`s: first `.s3a` path in bytewise ascending canonical-absolute-path order, then first in tile order. The candidate set is recomputed per request. The index stays in `--data-dir` and stores `file_uuid`, canonical archive path, and nullable `snapshot_sha256`. It is not bounded by `--max-open-bytes` (AT-224). Idempotency is current archive `file_uuid` + identity + verb. The winning corpus file is exposed as `archive_path` on the `AcademicPaperRecord` candidate (`page_path` stays `null`; AT-225, AT-101). | Tightened (v23, F-01); intent kept. |
| **DD-62** | Download filename | `--download-dir/{lowercase sha256 of delivered bytes}`. The existing-file check uses the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule. Same bytes on a regular file: success no-op. A symlink, FIFO or other non-regular file, or a regular file with different bytes at that name: `409` `download_conflict`, no overwrite (AT-94, AT-216). | Tightened (v17, F-03); intent kept from v4. |
| **DD-63** | ~~Kite-written snapshot criterion~~ **Retired** | Retired in v5. A BLOB+PROVENANCE snapshot has no `ConsentRecord`, so DD-67 already leaves it unrestricted. Sitting in `--snapshot-dir` grants no exemption (AT-107, AT-108). | Retired (F-22). |
| **DD-64** | Allowlist vs deny list | The deny list always wins. `--egress-allowlist` never overrides it. Loopback fixtures use the `egress_test` feature and `--egress-test-loopback-port`, rejected in builds without the feature. | New (F-09). |
| **DD-65** | LRS client | HTTP Basic from `--lrs-credentials-file`, `X-Experience-API-Version: 1.0.3`, statement schema in §5.5. `--lrs-url` is https except loopback http. The lab/production endpoint is `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/xapi/` (live, xAPI 1.0.3; Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-02). The statements URL is built without a doubled `/xapi` path: if the configured `--lrs-url` (after stripping at most one trailing `/`) already ends with `/xapi`, POST `{lrs-url}/statements`; otherwise POST `{lrs-url}/xapi/statements` (so existing test fixtures that pass a host root still work). `--lrs-actor-homepage` is not a flag; the host builds the actor homepage as in DD-157. | Amended (v24.1, Q-02, Q-13). |
| **DD-66** | Retry re-checks consent | Every LRS send, including startup retry, requires `lrs_inhibit` clear (DD-144) and in-memory consent `on`. Consent `off` at start (after sidecar locks) deletes the queue. A failed `lrs-retry/` delete on revoke is fail-closed process exit (DD-138), not an in-process send barrier. Leftover `{id}.claimed` files are renamed to `{id}.json` at start after the locks are held, before replay (AT-91). | Tightened (v12, F-01, F-04). |
| **DD-67** | Consent filter (points at DD-147) | The §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table is the only withholding rule for hyper-tile `ConsentRecord`s and Edge type-71 records. Type 82 is never consent. `AcademicPaperRecord` is mark-only (no `subject_id`). Also covers kite-written snapshots (replaces DD-63). Restrict-only wording lives with DD-111 / DD-147. | Tightened (v13, F-01, F-02). |
| **DD-68** | Token file charset | One line of ≥32 characters from `A–Z a–z 0–9 _ -`, optional single trailing newline stripped at load. Bearer is the secret without that newline. | New (F-14). |
| **DD-69** | Frame parameter defaults | `device_pixel_ratio` is on every `render_*` signature and on `/v1/frame`. Contract defaults when omitted: `scroll_x=0`, `scroll_y=0`, `instant=false`, `device_pixel_ratio=1.0`. `w` and `h` have no default. Types and ranges are in §5.2.1; a malformed unused field is `invalid_param`. This is an explicit exception to the no-defaults rule. | Tightened (v19, F-17); intent kept. |
| **DD-70** | Bay sides | Integer sides 32, 45, and 181 CSS px at scale 1.0. Area is approximately linear. Mixed-stride warehouses are out of v1. | New (F-19). |
| **DD-71** | Auth status order | Authentication first: 401 without a token (except the tokenless health case); 404 with a valid token on an unknown path. | New (F-21). |
| **DD-72** | Unix-socket bind | `--bind unix:/absolute/path.sock`; `--port` is forbidden. Token still required. | New (F-24). |
| **DD-73** | Host is Unix; token privacy | v1 host is specified for Linux/Unix only. Token file owner is the process euid, mode `0600`. Secret and identifier logging is extended by DD-85. | Tightened (F-18). |
| **DD-74** | Handles | `POST /v1/close`; required `--max-open-handles` ≥ 1; 429 when exceeded. Per-handle serialization. Idle expiry is DD-92. The last-frame cache and `hit_test` lookup, including floor and first-view alias normalization, are DD-125. | Tightened (v13, F-18); floor/alias rules superseded by DD-125. |
| **DD-75** | No path mmap in v1 | `open_path` reads from the host-validated fd and uses `from_bytes`. No SIGBUS handling is required. | Tightened (v16, F-01). |
| **DD-76** | Fetch size and deny-list completeness | Required `--max-fetch-bytes` (applied to decoded bodies and, separately, to the fetch response header block; DD-86). Deny all non-global unicast, including mapped and translated forms, and every IP of the LRS host. Ignore `HTTP(S)_PROXY` / `ALL_PROXY` even for an allowed `egress_test` fixture. | Tightened (v21, F-15); intent kept. |
| **DD-77** | No HTML subresources | v1 `render_page` loads no subresources of any kind (network or local): no `<img>`, `<link>`, `@font-face`, or Markdown image/file includes (DD-118, AT-121, AT-167). | Tightened (v7, F-13). |
| **DD-78** | Viewer data-dir and locked reads | `--data-dir` is not accepted in viewer-only mode. Durable disk is required only in Scholar mode. `/v1/open` of the locked working archive uses the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule under the write mutex and pack `open` on those bytes (not `open_path`), so `paper_identity` is `None` (AT-217). | Tightened (v17, F-10); intent kept from v4. |
| **DD-79** | Pinned keys as the Edge trust anchor | A public key embedded in the file is never a trust anchor. Trust is an optional `--pinned-keys-file` (hex 32-byte keys, one per line, euid+0600). Empty or omitted list: at best Self-signed. Non-empty list and signer not in it: Quarantined. Labels: Verified / Self-signed / Unsigned / Quarantined, matching `s3a-reader-core` `verify(pinned_keys)` and vault contact pinning. `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` with no type-70/key is Quarantined. | New (F-13). |
| **DD-80** | `open_verified` and `KiteVerificationReport` | Pack type `KiteVerificationReport`; the app maps a reader-core report. If a report is supplied, kite uses it even when a fresh verify would disagree. The pack does not depend on `s3a-reader-core`. `/v1/open` runs the same algorithm with `--pinned-keys-file`. | New (F-14). |
| **DD-81** | `view=banner` | Alias of `render_manifest`. `GET /v1/frame?view=banner` returns the banner / header / manifest text layer. | New (F-09). |
| **DD-82** | `floor_index` is the tile index | `floor_index`, `Floor(n)`, and `?floor=` are the tile `index` after invalid tiles are hidden. Omitted (where required) is `400` `missing_param`; present but not a valid tile index is `409` `invalid_floor`. The invalid-list is banner text only. | Tightened (F-04). |
| **DD-83** | Error catalogue | The status/code table in §5.2.1 is normative. Unknown `view` is `400` `invalid_view`. ATs assert the listed codes. | New (F-10, F-26). |
| **DD-84** | Write mutex and numbered steps | Superseded in part by DD-102: the mutex is held only for local disk I/O and consent-state access, never across network I/O (fetch or LRS); skip is not in the mutex. Steps remain (1) inclusion+index, (2) fetch+snapshot, (2b) download delivery, (3) LRS. | Tightened (v12, F-22). |
| **DD-85** | Secrets, TLS store, no-logging | `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--tls-key`, `--pinned-keys-file`, and an existing consent file must be euid+0600. Host-created index, retry-queue, and consent writes are 0600. No logging of Authorization values, LRS passwords, paper ids, source URLs, or `--researcher-id`. LRS HTTPS uses the system / webpki root store; v1 does not pin LRS certificates. | New (F-18). |
| **DD-86** | Fetch 2xx and content encoding | Required `--max-redirects` ≥ 0. Only a final 2xx is stored. The hashed body is decoded `Content-Encoding` (`identity`, `gzip`, `deflate`); unknown encoding or a non-2xx is `fetch_error` and no snapshot. `--max-fetch-bytes` applies to decoded bytes and, separately, to the fetch response header block. | Tightened (v21, F-15); intent kept. |
| **DD-87** | Kind detection and name hint | The ordered table in §5.2.5. `open` / `/v1/open` take an optional name hint (or the path basename). Bad S3A version is an error, not `other`. | New (F-08). |
| **DD-88** | Logs and ephemeral temp | `--log-dir` records startup (flag names only), shutdown, and identifier-free errors. No access log of paper ids. Ephemeral temp is `mkdtemp` 0700 under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or `/tmp` with prefix `s3a-kite-{pid}-` and a flock'd marker; cleaned on exit and, at startup, of leftover same-euid directories whose marker flock can be taken. Names whose pid cannot be parsed are skipped. No delete on "pid not running" alone (AT-187). Pid-aware snapshot/download temps and dir locks: DD-136. | Tightened (v12, F-17). |
| **DD-89** | Path-rule scope | The inside-root rule applies to request paths, `Link` follow-ups, and candidate files, not to `drive_path`. A `drive_path` that is missing, fails DD-146 open-then-validate (opened once per request, before step 0, outside the mutex), escapes those roots, or lands in `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir` is silently no usable on-disk source (`on_disk: false`), not `409` `path_escape` (AT-150, AT-202). Host config and state files may sit outside request roots and must not sit inside one (DD-129). Open-then-validate: DD-146. | Tightened (v13, F-06). |
| **DD-90** | Frame pixel cap and CSS vs pixmap | Required `--max-frame-pixels` and UniFFI `max_frame_pixels`. `w`/`h`/`x`/`y` are CSS px. Pixmap is `ceil(w*dpr)` by `ceil(h*dpr)`; that product must be ≤ the cap. `dpr` must be finite and > 0. Else `400` `invalid_frame_size`. `TextRun` / `SemanticNode` `x`/`y` are `i32` and may lie off-pixmap. | New (F-17, F-29). |
| **DD-91** | `select_enabled` | `render_page(..., select_enabled)`. True only in Scholar mode when `/v1/select` is available. False in viewer-only mode and on the phone; no Select control is painted. | New (F-30). |
| **DD-92** | Handle idle | Required `--handle-idle-secs` ≥ 1. An idle handle is closed; the next use is `404` `unknown_handle`. | New (F-34). |
| **DD-93** | Disjoint write and read locations | After canonicalization, snapshot, download, data, log, and the working-archive parent are pairwise-disjoint from wiki, corpus, and view roots (equal to, or an ancestor directory of: a path-component prefix, so `/x/a` and `/x/ab` are disjoint; AT-179). `--data-dir` and `--log-dir` are also pairwise-disjoint from `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir`. `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir` are pairwise-disjoint from each other. The working-archive parent is also pairwise-disjoint from snapshot, download, and log dirs. Viewer-only: `--log-dir`, `--pinned-keys-file`, the token file, and any TLS files versus `--view-root` (AT-179). A violation is a startup error that names both flags (AT-179). Secret files: DD-129. | Tightened (v24, F-18); intent kept from v10. |
