| **DD-94** | Host fonts | The host embeds the same required UA font bytes with `include_bytes!` and passes them to `KiteContext::new` (DD-101, DD-121). AT-42 applies to the pack linked into the host, via an empty font set passed to `KiteContext::new`, not by stripping compiled-in bytes. | Tightened (v13, F-18). |
| **DD-95** | CLI shape | The host is `s3a-kite host [flags...]`. `host` is the only v1 subcommand. | New (F-01). |
| **DD-96** | Consent-file bootstrap | When `--lrs-url` is set and the consent file is missing, the host creates it as `off` plus timestamp, mode 0600. Malformed existing files remain a startup error. `--consent-state-file` without `--lrs-url` is forbidden. | New (F-03, F-30). |
| **DD-97** | `hit_test` is pure | A Continue hit does not enter the warehouse. The client calls `proceed_to_warehouse` / `POST /v1/proceed`. | New (F-05). |
| **DD-98** | `pinned_keys` on `open` / `open_path` | Both take `pinned_keys: Vec<[u8; 32]>`. Empty means the omitted list. The host passes keys from `--pinned-keys-file` (or empty). The pack does not read the host flag. | New (F-06). |
| **DD-99** | Verification labels and `seal_status` | Table in §5.4. `KiteVerificationReport` has `status`, `seal_status`, `tiles_crc_failed: Vec<u32>` (increasing CRC-failed tile indices), `invalid_tile_indices`, `break_index`, `torn_tile_indices`, `quarantine_reason`, and `detail: String`. `unverified` is a legal field value (verify not yet called) but is rejected by `open_verified` (DD-116). A sealed-OK hyper-tile is Unsigned + sealed, not Verified. An open handle exposes the report via `verification_report` (F-04). Report mapping: DD-130. | Tightened (v18, F-10); intent kept from v13. |
| **DD-100** | Torn vs committed CRC vs mid-chain signatures | Torn/uncommitted: ignored, no quarantine; a torn-only stop leaves `seal_status` `sealed` when the chain is active (`unsealed` when it is not) and sets `break_index` to the torn tile whether or not the chain is active (DD-150, AT-17, AT-165). A seal-chain break is `SealedChainBreak`, reordered tiles, or a seal-hash mismatch that is not a mere signature failure and not a mere CRC (`chain_break`). A signature failure after an earlier authentic record is `signature_known_signer` (DD-145 rules 3/4), not `chain_break`; earlier valid floors may remain. Committed CRC fail: file Quarantined, reason `crc`, later tiles invalid; hyper-tile `seal_status` is `seal_failed` if a trailer or remnant is present, else `unsealed`; Edge `seal_status` is `seal_failed` when `FLAG_SEAL_CHAIN_ACTIVE` is set, else `unsealed` (AT-35). Unpinned signer on a non-empty list: file Quarantined, no valid floors. Signer attribution is DD-145; the total derivation table is DD-150. | Tightened (v15, F-02, F-06). |
| **DD-101** | Injectable font set | The font bytes are an argument of `KiteContext::new` / `KiteContext::new_with_clock` (there is no separate `register_ua_fonts` in the v1 pack API). Shipped binaries pass `include_bytes!` into `KiteContext::new`. Tests pass an empty set to `KiteContext::new` and expect `KiteError` (AT-42, DD-121). | Tightened (v13, F-18). |
| **DD-102** | Mutex, LRS timeout, and revoke | Mutex is held only for the local disk I/O and consent-state access listed in §5.2.1, never across network I/O, and also covers the working-archive read-copy for `/v1/open` and consent GET/POST. The in-memory consent flag is read and written only under that mutex (DD-142). Immediate LRS inhibit on revoke is the lock-free `lrs_inhibit` latch (DD-144), separate from the flag. Writing a retry file, and the consent / inhibit check that allows that write, run in the same mutex hold as the write; if `lrs_inhibit` is set or the flag is `off`, write nothing and return `lrs_status: "skipped"` (AT-156). Required `--lrs-timeout-secs` when `--lrs-url` is set. HTTP classification is DD-134. Fail-closed persist is DD-138 / DD-113. | Tightened (v12, F-01, F-22). |
| **DD-103** | Snapshot reuse on identical bytes | If `{content sha}.s3a` exists, do not overwrite. The reuse open uses the §5.2.2 shared safe-open rule. Reuse only after `verify_sealed_archive` and a BLOB reassembly whose bytes hash to `sha`. A **newly fetched** capture whose existing `{sha}.s3a` is a symlink, FIFO or other non-regular file, is oversize, or fails those checks is `409` `snapshot_conflict` (inclusion kept; AT-180, AT-215). A sha already **recorded** in the inclusion index that fails the checks, when the file is present, is HTTP 200 `snapshot_invalid` (step 0, AT-209, AT-215), not 409. A missing recorded `{sha}.s3a` is a re-fetch, not `snapshot_invalid` (DD-152, AT-228). A sealed snapshot larger than `--max-open-bytes` is not published (`fetch_error: "snapshot_too_large"`; on a download, `download_error: "fetch_failed"`, 2b skipped; waiters receive it as a fetch failure; AT-227). | Tightened (v22, F-04, DD-152); intent kept from v14. |
| **DD-104** | Inclusion-index crash safety | Append-only; last matching line wins. Each mutation is copy + append + fsync + atomic rename. There is no in-place rewrite: a later snapshot appends a new line with the SHA-256. Malformed index at start is a startup error. Step 1 is tile-10 then index; index failure is `500` `write_failed` with the record kept. | Tightened (v8, F-10). |
| **DD-105** | Create-time `ConsentRecord` fields | Every field listed in §5.6, using `ConsentRecord::new`, `hash_iri`, and `actor_id_to_subject_id`. `HumanLrsRecord` has no `subject_id`; inclusion records are not withheld by `ConsentRecord`. AT-79 requires `withheld_count == 0`. | New (F-13). |
| **DD-106** | Flag inventory | The table in §5.2.2 is the complete flag list (type, range, mode, dependents). ATs 04, 76, 77, and 132 are built from that table. Test hooks are the `test_hooks` Cargo feature and environment variables in §5.2.7 / DD-151, not flags. | Tightened (v20, F-01); intent kept. |
| **DD-107** | View validity matrix | The kind x state x view table in §5.2.6 is normative. `manifest`, `header`, and `banner` are aliases of `render_manifest` on archive kinds. | New (F-24). |
| **DD-108** | Transport hardening | Unix socket 0600, euid; stale path is a startup error (no unlink). TLS 1.2 or newer. Required `--max-request-bytes` caps the inbound request header block and, separately, the request body; either excess is `413` `request_too_large` (header overflow even without a token). `--handle-idle-secs` also bounds time from TCP accept, including the TLS handshake, header-read, and inter-byte idle time, on every connection, including unauthenticated peers, on every bind including non-loopback TLS; the connection is then closed without a response (AT-230). On a non-loopback bind, concurrent connections are capped at a fixed 8 (DD-153, AT-231). The LRS response header block and body are each bounded by `--max-open-bytes`; a known status line with an oversized remainder follows the DD-134 class, and an oversized header block before a status line is permanent (AT-191). | Tightened (v24, Q-20, F-08, F-09); intent kept. |
| **DD-109** | Phone UniFFI surface | Phone builds export `KiteContext::new`, `open`, `open_verified`, `verification_report`, `close`, `proceed_to_warehouse`, render, `hit_test`, and the `KiteVerificationReport` type, and take SAF/share-sheet bytes. `open_path` and `paper_identity` remain host pack APIs. No Scholar writes (DD-45, DD-128, AT-99). Phone `max_frame_pixels` / `max_open_bytes` are Q-17. | Tightened (v13, F-04). |
| **DD-110** | Consent ordering and test clock | In the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table, a record is **later** than another when it has a greater tile index, or the same tile index and a greater record index. Pack tests inject `now` via `KiteContext::new_with_clock` (Unix UTC seconds). Production `KiteContext::new` uses the process clock. `new_with_clock` is not exported on phone UniFFI. Withholding and Edge expiry are evaluated at each render and at each candidate recompute. | Tightened (v14, F-13). |
| **DD-111** | Seal-chain break, signature failure after an earlier authentic record, and restrict-only consent | Any seal-chain break or a signature failure after an earlier authentic record (DD-145 rules 3/4) sets file `status` Quarantined. In an Edge file that contains at least one type-70 `DeviceSealRecord`, a tile is trusted only if it is a valid floor and is covered by a verified seal record: an authentic record at *s* covers `0 ..= s`. `break_index` for `chain_break` and `signature_known_signer` is (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none; tiles at or after it are untrusted (AT-220). Edge writers MAY sign every N tiles (sparse records; N is writer policy, no default N). In a hyper-tile, or an Edge file with no type-70 records, a valid floor of a file that is not unauthenticated as a whole is trusted. When a sealed hyper-tile also has a committed CRC failure, the CRC rule decides the valid floors (AT-35); AT-90 lists CRC-valid tiles only when every committed tile passes CRC. For Edge, a chain break makes only tiles at or after `break_index` unauthenticated; verified pre-break covered tiles stay authenticated and their grants may count as trusted under DD-147 (AT-194). Unauthenticated-as-a-whole is the single §5.3 item 4 / DD-148 predicate (used identically by `open` and `open_verified`): a hyper-tile with `seal_status == seal_failed`, or `quarantine_reason` `signer_not_pinned` or `missing_signature`. Edge `missing_signature` and `signer_not_pinned` files are unauthenticated as a whole and have no valid floors (AT-195). An unsealed / Unsigned file, including an Unsigned Edge file with a committed CRC failure (`quarantine_reason: crc`), is not unauthenticated as a whole (AT-136, DD-141). Consent withholding is exactly the §5.3 item 4 / DD-147 table: untrusted grants never release; any **later** parseable denial, withdrawal, or expiry, later than the latest trusted grant, restricts (an earlier expiry or withdrawal does not withhold a later trusted grant, AT-185); a file never becomes unrestricted via tampering (AT-184, AT-200). Applies to hyper-tile `ConsentRecord`s and Edge type-71 records. | Tightened (v20, F-02); intent kept from v13. |
| **DD-112** | In-flight fetch registry (removed on write failure too) | Concurrent downloads for the same `(file_uuid, identity)` (not verb) share one GET. Select does not fetch. Snapshot-exists lookup uses the same key. The verb remains the inclusion-record idempotency field only. A request inserts a registry entry only after step 1 has succeeded or been skipped, and only if it will start the GET. The entry is removed in the same mutex hold as the snapshot publish and index append, or the fetch-failure / `snapshot_conflict` / step-2 or step-2b disk-write-failure (`write_failed`) outcome. Waiters receive the starter's outcome (snapshot path, a fetch failure including `snapshot_too_large`, `snapshot_conflict`, or `write_failed`, with the waiter's own `included` / `lrs_status`) and never start a GET in the same request. A publish or index-append failure always removes the entry so waiters cannot hang (AT-212, AT-213, AT-214). Once the request has passed candidate membership, the handler runs steps 0-3 to completion even if the starter's client disconnects: the registry entry is always removed, waiters are woken with the starter's defined outcome, step 1 completes (or the defined `write_failed`), and step 3 still runs so a consented LRS statement is not lost (AT-221). The write mutex is not held across the GET. AT-126 counts GETs (exactly 1). | Tightened (v22, F-04); intent kept from v12. |
| **DD-113** | Revoke persist and fail-closed exit | On revoke: set `lrs_inhibit` on receipt (DD-144), then under the mutex set the flag `off` and write `revoke-pending` **only when the flag was `on`**; a same-state `off` performs no persistent writes (DD-144). When the flag was `on`, write `revoke-pending` (temp + fsync + rename, 0600, present means a regular file), write the consent file `off`, delete `lrs-retry/`, then remove `revoke-pending`. A failed step-4 unlink at runtime after steps 1–3 succeeded is logged; the revoke returns 200 and leaves the file (AT-229). A later qualifying `on` must unlink that leftover and `fsync` the parent before persisting `on`; failure of that unlink is treated as a DD-138 fail-closed event and does not write `on` (AT-229). If no later qualifying `on` removed it, the next start handles the leftover as in AT-181. The failed step-4 unlink itself is not a DD-138 fail-closed event. Any consent-file write, `revoke-pending` write, or `lrs-retry/` delete failure stops LRS immediately and the process exits non-zero (DD-138). If the `revoke-pending` write fails, still best-effort write consent `off` and delete the queue, then exit (AT-189). Startup with `revoke-pending` present (after sidecar locks) treats consent as `off`, retries, then removes `revoke-pending` (AT-181); failure to remove it, or a failed consent-file write or queue delete at that startup, is a startup error and binds nothing. There is no in-process `409` `revoke_pending` and no retired "refuse LRS for process life" latch (`lrs_inhibit` is the revoke send inhibit, not that mode). | Tightened (v23, F-06); intent kept from v12. |
| **DD-114** | Process sidecar locks and the engine archive lock | (1) Process-lifetime sidecar `flock`s, taken with `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`, on `--data-dir/.lock`, `--working-archive.lock`, `--snapshot-dir/.lock`, and `--download-dir/.lock`, and, when `--consent-state-file` is configured, `--consent-state-file.lock` (host vs host; AT-164, AT-208). Startup still runs the §5.2.1 order: canonicalize/`lstat`/checks with no creation, then these locks, then other reads. Startup cleanup, bootstrap, and replay run only after the locks are held. A colliding host, including one that shares only the consent file, mutates nothing (AT-164, AT-208). AT-46 uses a distinct path for each of the four write locations. `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir` are pairwise-disjoint (DD-93, AT-179). (2) Engine `try_lock_exclusive` on the archive file, taken per tile-10 write and on create. An external holder of (2) yields `500` `lock_failed` at step 1 (AT-73, not a second host). `/v1/open` of the working archive copies under the write mutex. Pid-aware temps: DD-136. `--working-archive` writes re-open the file in every step-0/1 hold and apply the name-binding `fstatat` re-check immediately before each tile-10 write (DD-146, AT-203, AT-219). | Tightened (v21, F-01); intent kept from v12. |
| **DD-115** | Pack verification is normative for the host | The host always runs the §5.4 algorithm via pack `open`. It does not prefer `open_verified`. After pack `open` / `open_path`, `verification_report(handle)` returns the pack-computed report (floors in `0 .. tiles_total-1`). After `open_verified`, it returns the supplied report, not a recomputation. Pack `verification_report` after `open` and a mapped reader-core `verify` must agree on `status`, `seal_status`, `invalid_tile_indices`, `break_index`, `torn_tile_indices`, and `quarantine_reason`. Pack `open` uses the same `quarantine_reason` priority as the §5.1 table. The mapping is DD-130. AT-165 lives in s3a-reader. | Tightened (v13, F-04). |
| **DD-116** | `unverified` in the report enum | `KiteVerificationReport.status` includes `unverified` (verify has not been called). `open_verified` returns `KiteError` for that status (AT-154). It is never painted as a UI label. | New (v7, F-11). |
| **DD-117** | Candidate consent and trust filter | Candidate extraction applies the §5.3 item 4 / §5.4 filter and valid-floor rules. Withheld or invalid records are not candidates. A Quarantined corpus file contributes no `AcademicPaperRecord` candidates (AT-166). | New (v7, F-12). |
| **DD-118** | No local or network subresources | v1 page view loads no HTML subresources (`<img>`, `<link>`, `@font-face`) and no `http`/`https`/`file://` images (DD-77, AT-121, AT-167). Narrow exception (Q-04, Ashley, 2026-11-16): on `view=page` of a wiki or corpus Markdown document, a CommonMark image whose destination resolves like a `Link` href (fragment and query stripped, percent-decoded, relative to the page parent, then canonicalized) and lies inside the allowed request root of the opened page may be loaded through the same safe-open / `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` path as `POST /v1/open`. Remote and `file://` image destinations stay unloaded (AT-233). | Amended (v24.1, Q-04). |
| **DD-119** | Expanded error catalogue | Adds `invalid_param`, `method_not_allowed`, `unsupported_media_type`, `not_regular_file`, `unreadable`, `file_too_large`, `consent_write_failed`, `render_failed`, and `internal` as in the §5.2.1 table (AT-171, AT-169, AT-170). `download_conflict` and `snapshot_conflict` include `included: true` whenever a matching inclusion exists after step 0/1. | Tightened (v14, F-08). |
| **DD-120** | Idempotent step 0 | A last matching index line skips step 1 and step 3. Step 2 runs if no **usable** snapshot exists for `(file_uuid, identity)` (any verb) and a fetch is required, including on a repeat after a failed fetch (AT-186) and when the only recorded `{sha}.s3a` files are missing (DD-152, AT-228). Usability looks only at the last line for the key that has a non-null sha (DD-104). A repeat whose last recorded snapshot is **present** and fails the DD-103 reuse checks is `snapshot: null` plus `snapshot_error: "snapshot_invalid"`, inclusion kept, no re-fetch and no `drive_path` fallback (AT-209). Only the GET starter appends a snapshot index line, under its own verb. Step 3 still runs on the request that first wrote the inclusion line, including after `snapshot_conflict`, `download_conflict`, or a step-2 / step-2b disk-write failure (`500` `write_failed` with `included: true` and `lrs_status`; AT-94, AT-180, AT-212, AT-213, AT-214). A snapshot file published when the index append then fails is left for DD-103 reuse (AT-213). A later request after `409` `snapshot_conflict` has no recorded sha, so it may re-fetch and hit `409` `snapshot_conflict` again with `lrs_status: "skipped"` when step 0 matched (AT-180). A recorded sha that later fails reuse is 200 `snapshot_invalid` with step 3 skipped (AT-209). If a usable snapshot exists for the key, download delivers from it, not from a later-usable `drive_path`. | Tightened (v23, F-03, F-04); intent kept from v14. |
| **DD-121** | `KiteContext` fonts and `kite-acceptance` | No process-global font set. `KiteContext::new(ua_fonts)` takes the bytes; an empty set is `KiteError` (AT-42 is a library test on the crate the host links). `kite-acceptance` is a workspace member with no pack dependency (AT-22, AT-37, host-spawn, and cargo-tree/guards that do not link the pack run today). Pack-linking library tests live in `kite-pack-tests`, an out-of-workspace crate with its own `Cargo.toml` and lockfile and path deps; a compile failure of that crate is the documented fail-first result until the pack exists, and the workspace stays green with the pack absent (`cargo metadata`, `cargo test`, and other workspace-wide cargo commands resolve). s3a-reader tests that need the pack (AT-38, AT-39, AT-43, AT-63, AT-165) live in a separate crate outside the reader workspace, not as a feature-gated member. The `s3a-kite` host library and binary live in `crates/s3a-kite`, which is excluded from the s3a-storage workspace (same rule as `kite-pack-tests`). Until the pack exists the crate has no pack dependency, so AT-183's unit-test half (`egress_allowlist_matches` and `parse_allowlist_entry`) and AT-226's `lrs_deny_union` test land in that crate in §11 step 1. The host-spawn halves of AT-183 live in `kite-acceptance`. Host-spawn cases in `kite-acceptance` find each feature build through the `S3A_KITE_BIN*` environment variables in §7 (no `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*`). Any host-spawn case that needs a successful source fetch uses an `egress_test` build. | Tightened (v22, F-02, F-12); intent kept from v7. |
| **DD-122** | Atomic download and temp cleanup | Snapshot, download, inclusion-index, consent-file, and `revoke-pending` publishes use a temp named `s3a-kite-tmp-{pid}-*` in the parent directory, `fsync` of the temp, atomic rename, then `fsync` of the parent directory, before the response. A basename matching that pattern directly inside `--snapshot-dir` or `--download-dir` is not openable via `/v1/open` (`409` `not_regular_file`, after the walk and before the read; AT-171). Every such temp (snapshot, download, inclusion-index, consent-file, and `revoke-pending`) is held `LOCK_EX` on its own fd from create until publish or delete. After sidecar locks are held, a stale same-euid `s3a-kite-tmp-*` file in `--snapshot-dir`, `--download-dir`, `--data-dir`, or the consent-file parent is deleted only if `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB` on it succeeds; names whose pid cannot be parsed are skipped; the configured `--working-archive` and `--consent-state-file` paths are never deleted by this cleanup, and those basenames must not match `s3a-kite-tmp-*` (AT-187, AT-208). Snapshot and download temps are written and sealed on the same file description that holds `LOCK_EX` (not a `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` or path reopen). The `lrs-retry/` directory is mode `0700`. | Tightened (v24, F-13, F-16); intent kept from v14. |
| **DD-123** | `--max-open-bytes` | Required in both host modes and as FFI `max_open_bytes`. Exceeding it is `409` `file_too_large` / `KiteError` (AT-170). | New (v7, F-26). |
| **DD-124** | SemanticNode CSS pixels | `SemanticNode` `x`/`y`/`width`/`height` are CSS pixels, the same space as `hit_test` x/y and the request's CSS `w`/`h`, not the pixmap `width`/`height` in the frame response (AT-168). | Tightened (v8, F-11). |
| **DD-125** | Hit-test view matrix | The §5.2.6 matrix applies to `/v1/hit-test` and pack `hit_test`. For `/v1/frame` and `/v1/hit-test`, errors are evaluated in the same §5.2.1 order as every other route: authentication, then `405`, then `413` / `415`, then `409` `viewer_only`, then JSON / param errors (`malformed_json`, `missing_param`, `invalid_param`), then route-specific errors. The same header-block exception applies: a header block over `--max-request-bytes` is `413` before authentication; body overflow is `413` after authentication. The route-specific tail is: `invalid_view`, then `unknown_handle`, then the matrix, then `invalid_floor`, then `invalid_frame_size`, then `no_frame`. When `view` is not in the enum, `invalid_view` is returned **before** any `floor` presence or type check. A well-typed optional field that a view does not use is ignored; a malformed one is `400` `invalid_param` (DD-69, AT-171). `hit_test` takes `floor: Option<u32>`: required (`Some`) on floor-scoped views (`None` is `missing_param`); on a non-floor view it must be `None`, and `Some(_)` is `400` `invalid_param` (AT-196). The last-frame cache, and `hit_test`'s lookup, are keyed by `(handle, view, floor)` everywhere, with first-view aliases normalized to `first`. `/v1/proceed` and `/v1/close` share the same per-handle lock as frame and hit-test (AT-172). | Tightened (v14, F-09). |
| **DD-126** | (reserved alignment) | Hit-test matrix and cache rules are DD-125. This number is not used for a second topic. | Unused (v7). |
| **DD-127** | Startup error precedence | When more than one required flag is missing or invalid, the host names one failing flag (no prescribed order), exits non-zero, and binds nothing. An AT that asserts a named flag supplies every other flag valid for that mode. | New (v7, F-27). |
| **DD-128** | Phone in-scope role (Ashley, 2026-11-16) | The phone's in-scope role in this spec is an S3A / Edge-tile viewer only. There is no phone Scholar Explorer and no Scholar 3-pass / literature-review flow on the phone (no candidate list, select, download, inclusion, snapshots, consent, LRS, or `paper_identity`). The AI Teacher's Assistant agent has not been built. AI Teacher's Assistant chat and phone paper search are future, unbuilt, and out of scope for this spec: no ATs, exports, or interfaces are added for either. Q-15 and Q-16 are kept as future questions; this spec invents no defaults for them. | New (v7, Ashley). |
| **DD-129** | Secret and state files vs request roots | At startup, `--auth-token-file`, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--consent-state-file`, `--data-dir`, and `--log-dir` must not canonicalize inside any request root. `--data-dir` and `--log-dir` are also disjoint from `--snapshot-dir` and `--download-dir`. The working-archive parent is disjoint from snapshot, download, and log dirs. Viewer-only applies the same rule to `--log-dir` / `--pinned-keys-file` / token / TLS vs `--view-root`. A violation names both flags and binds nothing (AT-179). | Tightened (v9, F-17). |
| **DD-130** | Reader-core report mapping | The field-by-field table in §5.1 maps `s3a-reader-core` `VerificationReport` / `SealInfo` / `SignatureCheck.detail` (`792742c`) onto `KiteVerificationReport`, including `break_index`, `torn_tile_indices`, and `quarantine_reason`. The index range is always `0 .. tiles_total-1` (`tiles_total` is every index the reader enumerates, including torn and uncommitted tiles, and may differ from header `tile_count`). `invalid_tile_indices` is the union of torn, CRC-failed, uncommitted, and at-or-after-`break_index` indices in that range, and, when any authentic type-70 record exists, the unsigned tail (verifying container exempt) (every index when `quarantine_reason` is `signer_not_pinned` or `missing_signature`). `torn_tile_indices` uses the §5.4 / DD-100 predicate, not `tile_at` `Err`. AT-165 compares `verification_report` after pack `open` to a mapped reader-core `verify` and lives in s3a-reader. | Tightened (v13, F-04, F-05, F-20). |
| **DD-131** | `lrs_status` values | `sent`: LRS accepted. `queued`: attempt failed and a retry file was written. `failed`: attempt failed and no retry file could be written (including a retry-file write, fsync, or rename failure, which is logged and is not fail-closed), or the outcome was permanent (`{id}.permanent` written, including an oversized LRS header block before a status line is read). `skipped`: consent off at pre-send, step 0 skipped step 3, or an in-flight LRS POST was aborted because consent became `off` (AT-156, DD-134). `not_configured`: no `--lrs-url`. | Tightened (v11, F-02, F-13). |
| **DD-132** | Pinned-keys and LRS credentials file validation | After comments and blanks, a pinned-keys line that is not 64 hex characters is a startup error naming the file; zero remaining lines is a valid empty list. A credentials file is parsed exactly as `s3a-lrs-server` `load_credentials_file` at s3a-storage `b7bdcda`: `line.split(':')`; username is `parts[0]`, password is `parts[1]`; further fields (scopes, archive) are ignored by kite. Passwords cannot contain `:`. A line with fewer than two fields, or with an empty username or password, or a file with no credential line, is a startup error naming the file (AT-182). | Tightened (v9, F-06). |
| **DD-133** | Egress allowlist effective port (either scheme at that port) | Matching uses the URL's effective port (explicit, or `80` / `443` by scheme). An entry without a port matches only port 443 with scheme `https`. `host:PORT` matches either scheme at that effective port. `host:80` matches `http://host/`, `http://host:80/`, and `https://host:80/`, and not `https://host/` (effective port 443) (AT-183). IPv6 ports require brackets (`[2001:db8::1]:443`). An unbracketed entry that parses as an IPv6 address is a portless exact address (`2001:db8::1:443` is that address, not a port). An unbracketed entry that does not parse as hostname, IPv4, or IPv6 (for example `2001:db8::1::443`) is a startup error that names the flag. | Tightened (v14, F-03; v15, F-01). |
| **DD-134** | LRS HTTP classification and serialized replay | Any 2xx is success. A `409` is success for every emit, first or retry. 408, 429, and 5xx are queued. Any other 4xx is permanent (`{id}.permanent`). No HTTP response (timeout, connect refused, DNS, or I/O/reset) is treated like 5xx: queued if inhibit is inactive and the flag is still `on` in the mutex hold, else `skipped`. An LRS POST aborted because inhibit became active or consent became `off` is `skipped` (AT-156, DD-131). A TLS certificate-validation failure is permanent (AT-192). If a complete status line has been read, an oversized LRS remainder follows this same status class; an oversized header block before a status line is permanent (AT-191). Queue names are `{id}.json`, `{id}.claimed`, and `{id}.permanent`. At start with consent `on`, leftover `.claimed` files are renamed to `.json` before replay (AT-91). A retry-file write failure is `failed`, is logged, and does not exit (AT-191). | Tightened (v24, F-08); intent kept from v11. |
| **DD-135** | Bind literals and TLS-on-loopback | `--bind` accepts any IP literal (loopback, `0.0.0.0`, `::`, or a specific interface address) or `unix:/absolute/path.sock`. A non-loopback, non-Unix bind requires TLS and `--headless`. `--behind-proxy` remains loopback/Unix only (AT-87). Passing `--tls-cert` or `--tls-key` with a loopback or `unix:` bind is a startup error that names the flag (AT-190). | Tightened (v11, F-20). |
| **DD-136** | Pid-aware temps and snapshot/download dir locks | Ephemeral temp **directories** (`s3a-kite-{pid}-*`) include a flock'd marker file. Snapshot/download temps are single files (`s3a-kite-tmp-{pid}-*`); the host holds `LOCK_EX` on the temp file's own fd until publish or delete (DD-122). Startup cleanup deletes only same-euid leftovers: a directory whose marker flock can be taken, or a `s3a-kite-tmp-*` file on which `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB` succeeds; names whose pid cannot be parsed are skipped. Scholar start also takes process-lifetime `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB` `flock`s on `--snapshot-dir/.lock` and `--download-dir/.lock`, **before** any state change, so two Scholar hosts cannot share those directories (AT-164, AT-187). The two dirs are pairwise-disjoint (DD-93). | Tightened (v14, F-11). |
| **DD-137** | `EXT_CONTENT_SHA256` presence | The inclusion statement includes `EXT_CONTENT_SHA256` if and only if, at step 3, the inclusion index has a non-null `snapshot_sha256` for `(file_uuid, identity)`, and the extension value equals that digest. An on-disk-only download with no snapshot omits it. | New (v9, F-20). |
| **DD-138** | Fail-closed host exit on consent persist failure (Ashley, 2026-11-16) | Any consent-file write failure, `revoke-pending` write failure, or `lrs-retry/` delete failure on revoke stops all LRS sending immediately and shuts the host down (fail closed, non-zero exit, logged). There is no in-process recovery or degraded mode: no "refuse LRS for the life of the process" latch, no later request in the same process, and no `409` `revoke_pending`. After the failing hold releases the mutex, in-flight requests receive no further disk writes; they may get `500` `internal` if that needs no disk I/O, otherwise their connections are closed without a response (AT-169). Persist uses temp + fsync + rename. If the `revoke-pending` write fails, the host still best-effort writes the consent file `off` and deletes the queue, then exits. Startup with `revoke-pending` present (a regular file) treats consent as `off` and retries. AT-169 injects failure by making the consent-file parent (distinct from `--data-dir`) non-writable, so the fixture defeats temp+rename. | Tightened (v14, F-16); intent kept from v10. |
| **DD-139** | `ScholarRoots` on `open_path` | `open_path` takes a host-validated fd, the proved `canonical_path`, the `validated_root` of that walk, `scholar_roots: Option<ScholarRoots>` with `wiki_root`, `corpus_root`, and `shadowed_by_wiki: bool`, and `name_hint: Option<String>` (the optional `/v1/open` `name`, else the basename of `canonical_path`). The pack reads from the fd only and does no path I/O. The host sets `shadowed_by_wiki` true iff a qualifying item-1 wiki candidate exists at the same relative path. The pack uses those roots, `canonical_path`, `validated_root`, `shadowed_by_wiki`, the YAML in the fd bytes, and `max_open_bytes` to compute `paper_identity` under the §5.5 dossier checks (host-only), with no second `lstat`. `None`, or `open` of bytes, yields `paper_identity` `None`. Phone UniFFI does not export `open_path` or `paper_identity` (DD-109, DD-128). | Tightened (v18, F-01); intent kept from v11. |
| **DD-140** | LRS and `egress_test` fixture addresses | Tests that need a successful fetch and an LRS in the same host bind distinct addresses (fetch on `127.0.0.1`, LRS on `[::1]`). AT-54's denial case uses the same loopback IP on purpose (`http://127.0.0.1:<lrs-port>/` vs `http://127.0.0.1:<egress-port>/`) to show the LRS-host deny rule is not overridden by `egress_test`. No resolver-override flag is added, and no global-unicast destination is used. The newly-observed-LRS-IP union is tested as the pure function `s3a_kite::egress::lrs_deny_union` (AT-226), not by changing the system resolver. An `https` `--lrs-url` that fails to resolve at start is a startup error that names the flag. The hostname `metadata.google.internal` is denied by name before any DNS query. AT-226 observes that deny by running the host in a network namespace whose `/etc/resolv.conf` points at a counting stub resolver (not a host flag). | Tightened (v22, F-13); intent kept. |
| **DD-141** | Unsigned downgrade after signature strip, and hyper-tile trailer-strip residual | An Edge file with `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear and no signatures is Unsigned; its valid floors are authenticated for consent. Clearing the flag and dropping type-70 tiles is indistinguishable from a never-signed file; this pack accepts that downgrade. A file that keeps the signed flag but drops signatures is `missing_signature` and unauthenticated as a whole (AT-195). `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` and type-70 tiles are Edge-only; this spec does not define them on a hyper-tile. For a hyper-tile, a recognised end-cap magic or a partial/truncated trailer remnant means the file claims to be sealed: if `verify_sealed_archive` fails, `seal_status` is `seal_failed`, `quarantine_reason` is `seal_trailer` (subject to DD-150 priority), and the file is unauthenticated as a whole (restrict-only). A complete trailer absence (no remnant) is indistinguishable from a never-sealed file and from a legitimate first append: s3a-engine leaves a sealed archive unmodified on a read-only open, and the first append unseals it (drops the trailer). This pack does not invent a durable "has been sealed" header flag. The residual complete-strip case is therefore accepted and recorded here; it does not make every unsealed hyper-tile unauthenticated as a whole (AT-72, AT-112, AT-136). A recognised remnant is tagged so a later agent can attempt repair, and only by restoring from a verified copy (never by re-sealing). If repair is impossible, the remnant is flagged unrepairable. The durable header flag remains deferred engine work (Q-18, DD-159, AT-238). | Tightened (v15, F-17). |
| **DD-142** | Consent transitions serialized; revoke wins | The in-memory consent flag is read and written only while holding the write mutex. Consent GET and POST take that mutex. `off` is final until a later explicit `POST on` that starts after the revoke has completed. An `on` that started before that point must not set the flag or write `on` (AT-198). A received revoke inhibits LRS immediately, but only after the `POST /v1/consent` has passed §5.2.1 error-precedence (auth through `invalid_param`) and is a valid Scholar `{"state":"off"}`. An `on` qualifies only when `revokes_in_flight` was 0 at receipt and is 0 at the CAS, and its observed epoch is still current. A same-state `on` never touches `cleared_epoch`. Every revoke decrements `revokes_in_flight` at the end of its mutex hold, including a same-state no-op and a client disconnect mid-hold; only a fail-closed exit skips the decrement. Fail-closed persist remains DD-138. The latch mechanics are DD-144. | Tightened (v18, F-07); intent kept from v11. |
| **DD-143** | No candidate cache in v1 (scope cut) | v1 does not cache the candidate list or membership result. Every `GET /v1/candidates` and every select/download/skip membership check is a fresh walk plus a current-clock consent filter (DD-110, AT-161). Ashley may reinstate a cache later. | New (v11, F-03). |
| **DD-144** | Lock-free revoke-epoch latch | Immediate LRS inhibit is a lock-free pair of monotonic counters, `epoch` and `cleared_epoch`, initially equal, separate from the mutex-held consent flag (DD-142). Inhibit is active iff `epoch != cleared_epoch`. `epoch` only increases. A revoke increments `epoch` and `revokes_in_flight` only after the `POST /v1/consent` has passed §5.2.1 error-precedence (auth through `invalid_param`) and is a valid Scholar `{"state":"off"}`, then, before the mutex is taken, cancels in-flight LRS POSTs through the current cancellation token (`lrs_status: "skipped"`), and then swaps in a fresh token. Inside its mutex hold the revoke always cancels the current token again and swaps it (including a same-state no-op). If the flag is `on`, it is set `off` and the four persist writes run; if it is already `off`, there are no persistent writes. Every revoke decrements `revokes_in_flight` at the end of that hold, whether or not it persisted, even if the client disconnects mid-hold; only a fail-closed exit (DD-138) skips the decrement. Every LRS sender (1) registers with the current token, (2) re-checks that inhibit is not active and the in-memory flag is `on`, then (3) starts the POST. A qualifying `POST {"state":"on"}` (none pending at receipt and at the CAS, observed epoch still current) clears inhibit only by a compare-and-swap of `cleared_epoch` to the `epoch` it observed (AT-198). A same-state `on` never touches `cleared_epoch`. Fail-closed exit (DD-138) also increments `epoch`. The shorthand `lrs_inhibit` in this document means "inhibit is active". This is not the retired in-process degraded-mode latch. | Tightened (v18, F-07); intent kept from v12. |
| **DD-145** | Edge signer attribution | The §5.4 item 2 algorithm is total: every type-70 record maps to exactly one of authentic, `signature_known_signer`, `signer_not_pinned`, `missing_signature`, or `chain_break`. Candidate keys are the pinned list if non-empty, else every embedded `DeviceIdentityRecord` key; a record is authentic if it verifies under any candidate key **and** its stored `seal_hash` equals the recomputed `compute_seal_hash` at that `tile_index` (checked whenever any type-70 record exists, whether or not the chain is active; a mismatch is `chain_break` at `break_index` = (highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if none, AT-220). An authentic record at *s* covers `0 ..= s`; writers MAY sign every N tiles. A record that fails every pinned key and verifies under an embedded key is `signer_not_pinned` whether or not its stored hash matches. With an empty pin list and at least one embedded key, a record that fails every embedded key with no earlier authentic record is `missing_signature` (no valid floors); `signer_not_pinned` is not used when the pin list is empty. `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` set with no type-70 or no usable key is `missing_signature` (disjunction). Flag clear with type-70 present applies the signed-file rules. AT-201 / AT-165 / AT-220. | Tightened (v20, F-05, F-06); intent kept from v12. |
| **DD-146** | Open-then-validate via fd | Every request path (`/v1/open`, `Link` follow-up) is lexically normalized; a normalized path that lies under no allowed root, or under more than one, is `path_escape` (a `..` that crosses from one root into another is resolved in that absolute lexical step, then matched; AT-52). The path is then opened from the chosen root dirfd with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` (final component `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`) and validated on the fd: `fstat` requires a regular file, and at most `max_open_bytes` + 1 bytes are read. `ELOOP` / `ENOTDIR` from a symlink is `path_escape` for a request path. The same shared safe-open rule (startup dirfd, `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, `fstat` regular file, read cap `--max-open-bytes` + 1) applies to a `drive_path` used as an on-disk source (once per request, before step 0, outside the mutex; unusable → `on_disk: false`), a snapshot-reuse open, a download-dir existing-file hash check, a step-2b snapshot-reassembly open, the working-archive `/v1/open` copy, a candidate item-1 dossier front-matter read, a candidate item-2 corpus `*.s3a` open, startup leftover-temp cleanup, and the config and state files listed in §5.2.2 (`--auth-token-file`, `--lrs-credentials-file`, `--pinned-keys-file`, `--consent-state-file`, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`, and `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl`) (site-specific errors in §5.2.2; AT-197, AT-215, AT-216, AT-217, AT-218, AT-223, AT-224). Config and state files must be regular; leftover-temp cleanup skips a FIFO or symlink. The inclusion index is not bounded by `--max-open-bytes`. An oversize existing download file is `409` `download_conflict`. The YAML parser rejects aliases and anchors (AT-188). After lexical normalization of an absolute request path, the host picks the unique allowed root that is a path-component prefix of that path; no match or more than one match is `path_escape`. A non-canonical symlink prefix of a stored canonical root is `path_escape`. Step 2b and `/v1/open` of a request path use the already-validated fd and do not reopen the path by name. `/v1/open` and `Link` follow-up of a request path pass the fd, the proved canonical path, and the validated root into pack `open_path`; the pack reads from the fd and computes `paper_identity` from that path and the fd bytes, with no second `lstat` (AT-101). Mid-copy cap overflow is `download_error: "source_changed"`, inclusion kept, temp deleted, no mid-request fetch switch. `--working-archive` writes re-open the final component in every step-0/1 hold (no startup-lifetime fd; create with `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` is startup-only). Immediately before each tile-10 write, `fstatat(parent_dirfd, basename, AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` must match `fstat(writer_fd)` and the recorded identity (AT-203, AT-219). A swap after that final check may write the already-open inode, never the symlink target. Header reads for `file_uuid` and `addon_mask` use the hold's fd. `ENOTDIR` from a non-symlink intermediate on a request path is `404` `not_found` (AT-171). `/v1/open` of that path is not `open_path` (the parent is not a request root): the host copies under the shared rule and calls pack `open`, so `paper_identity` is `None` (AT-217). When `--view-root` is set in Scholar mode, it is pairwise-disjoint from `--wiki-root` and `--corpus-root` (AT-179). | Tightened (v21, F-01, F-06); intent kept from v12. |
| **DD-147** | Single per-subject consent table | The §5.3 item 4 research-consent evaluation is the only withholding rule for hyper-tile `ConsentRecord`s and Edge type-71 records. A subject's data is shown only if the latest trusted record (on a valid, authenticated floor of a file that is not unauthenticated as a whole) for that subject is an unexpired grant AND no parseable record from any tile (valid or invalid) for that subject is a later denial, withdrawal, or expiry. Any **later** denial, withdrawal, or expiry from any parseable tile, later than the latest trusted grant, restricts; untrusted grants never release. The file is unrestricted only if authentication is trusted and there are zero consent records of any kind in any parseable tile; any tampering or untrusted state with consent records present is default-withhold. "Unauthenticated as a whole" is the single DD-148 predicate. DD-67 and DD-111 point here. | Tightened (v19, F-04); intent kept from v13; pointer added v14. |
| **DD-148** | Unauthenticated-as-a-whole predicate | A file is unauthenticated as a whole if and only if (it is a hyper-tile and `seal_status == seal_failed`) or `quarantine_reason` is `signer_not_pinned` or `missing_signature`. Pack `open` / `open_path` apply this to the pack-computed report; `open_verified` applies the same predicate to the supplied report. An Unsigned Edge file with a committed CRC failure is not unauthenticated as a whole. An Edge chain break unauthenticates every tile at or after `break_index` = (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none; it does not by itself make the whole file unauthenticated. An unsigned tail does not make the whole file unauthenticated (AT-210). A hyper-tile trailer remnant whose verify fails is `seal_failed` and is unauthenticated as a whole; a complete trailer absence is not (DD-141). | Tightened (v15, F-03, F-17). |
| **DD-149** | Freeze after the first clean goldfish round (Ashley, 2026-11-16) | Ashley decided (2026-11-16): after the first subsequent goldfish review that reports 0 blockers and 0 majors, freeze this spec. Remaining minors then go to the test-first build. None of v15–v24 is itself frozen (rounds 14–23 still had majors). The freeze is recorded here as a decision by Ashley; it takes effect when that clean review is accepted, and is not a license to invent defaults or reopen DD-128, DD-138, or DD-142–DD-148 intent. | New (v15; Ashley, 2026-11-16). |
| **DD-150** | Total report-field derivation | The §5.4 derivation table is the single total derivation of `status`, `seal_status`, `quarantine_reason`, and `break_index` for hyper-tile and Edge, used identically by pack `open` / `open_path` and the §5.1 mapping. Multi-cause priority is `signer_not_pinned` > `missing_signature` > `seal_trailer` > `signature_known_signer` > `chain_break` > `crc`, so `signature_known_signer` is reachable. `break_index` is the minimum over every applicable cause that sets one; for `chain_break` and `signature_known_signer` it is (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if there is none. Valid floors end before it. In an Edge file that contains at least one type-70 `DeviceSealRecord`, an authentic record at *s* covers `0 ..= s`; a tile not covered by a verified record is untrusted. Edge writers MAY sign every N tiles (no default N). In a hyper-tile, or an Edge file with no type-70 records, a valid floor of a file that is not unauthenticated as a whole is trusted. A torn-only stop is not a chain break for `seal_status`; `break_index` is the first torn tile whether or not the Edge chain is active, and later tiles are uncommitted (AT-165). Whenever any type-70 record exists, stored `seal_hash` must equal the recomputed `compute_seal_hash`; a mismatch is `chain_break` at that `break_index` (chain-inactive: `seal_status` stays `unsealed`; tiles at or after `break_index` are untrusted) (AT-220). An active chain with `FLAG_ED25519_SIGNED` clear, no type-70, and no CRC is Unsigned / sealed when the chain is intact (zero seal records are not a `chain_break`). An unsigned tail after the highest authentic signed `tile_index` is not a valid floor and is untrusted for consent, except a verifying type-70 container tile (AT-210). A hyper-tile torn/uncommitted tail (including a tile index greater than or equal to header `tile_count`) is not a quarantine by itself (AT-165). | Tightened (v20, F-02, F-03); intent kept from v15. |
| **DD-151** | Test hooks | In-process fault injection and pauses used by fail-first ATs are the `test_hooks` Cargo feature (not in the default set; absent from release builds) and the `S3A_KITE_TEST_HOOKS` / signal-path environment variables in §5.2.7. They are not flags (DD-106). The hook-point list in §5.2.7 is closed and includes `pause_before_name_binding_check`, `pause_before_lrs_retry_write` (AT-156 case 3), `pause_before_consent_rename` (AT-187), `pause_after_consent_receipt` (AT-198), `fail_lrs_retry_write` (AT-191), `fail_lrs_retry_delete` (every `lrs-retry/` delete, startup included), and `fail_revoke_pending_remove` (startup, runtime step 4, and a qualifying `on` that unlinks a leftover; AT-229). Hook actions are process-lifetime except `fail_once_with_errno=N`, which fails only the first matching operation at that point (AT-229 case 1, AT-212, AT-213). A build without the feature rejects those variables at startup and performs no hooked action (AT-222). Filesystem operations between requests, and `kite-pack-tests` mid-call fd swaps (AT-101), are not hook points. Host-spawn ATs select a feature build through the `S3A_KITE_BIN*` variables in §7. | Tightened (v24, F-01, F-02, F-08, F-12); intent kept from v20. |
| **DD-152** | Missing recorded snapshot is a re-fetch (Ashley, 2026-11-16) | Ashley decided (2026-11-16): "Q-19: re-fetch the paper when the recorded snapshot is missing." Usability and `snapshot_invalid` look only at the **last** index line for `(file_uuid, identity)` that has a non-null `snapshot_sha256` (DD-104). If that last recorded file is missing (ENOENT under the shared safe-open rule), as opposed to present but failing the DD-103 reuse checks, the host treats it as no usable snapshot and runs step 2 if the source table requires a fetch (AT-228), even if an earlier line's file is present but failing. If that last file is present and fails the checks, the result is `snapshot_invalid` (AT-209), even if an earlier line would be usable. If the captured bytes hash to the recorded sha, the host publishes `{sha}.s3a` (a create, not an overwrite) and the existing index line stands. If they hash to a different `new_sha`, the host publishes `{new_sha}.s3a` under the usual new-snapshot rules (reuse and `snapshot_conflict` still apply) and appends a new inclusion-index line for this request's verb with `snapshot_sha256 = new_sha` (step 1 remains skipped when a matching verb line already existed; no second tile-10). A download then runs 2b from the published snapshot. A present-but-failing **last** recorded file remains `snapshot_invalid` (AT-209) and does not re-fetch. | Tightened (v23, F-04); intent kept from v22 (Ashley, 2026-11-16). |
| **DD-153** | Non-loopback connection cap | When `--bind` is not a loopback IP and not a Unix socket, the host allows at most 8 concurrent connections. A connection occupies a slot from TCP accept until the connection is closed (including during the TLS handshake, header-read, request handling, and keep-alive idle). When 8 slots are occupied, a further incoming connection is accepted at the TCP layer and then closed immediately, without a TLS handshake and without an HTTP response; it is not left in the listen backlog waiting for a slot. When a slot frees, a later accept may occupy it. The cap is fixed at 8 (Ashley, 2026-11-16); it is not a flag and has no invented default. Loopback IP binds and Unix-socket binds have no such cap. `--handle-idle-secs` still bounds time from TCP accept, including the TLS handshake, on every connection (AT-230, AT-231). | New (v24, Q-20; Ashley, 2026-11-16). |

| **DD-154** | Select, download, and deselect verbs | Select, download, and deselect are three separate actions. The verb IRIs are existing entries in `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs` at s3a-storage `b7bdcda0df63524ffe205f2ad92cd6a5b69a40f6`; this spec invents no IRI: select `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/opened`, download `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/saved`, deselect `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/removed`. Select is a closer-read event and is not the inclusion decision: it does not snapshot, fingerprint, write a tile-10 inclusion record, append an inclusion-index line, or enter the paper in the corpus. Download is the inclusion decision (tile 10, snapshot, fingerprint, corpus). Deselect is logged as its own event after a select that has not been followed by a download; the paper is not snapshotted or fingerprinted and does not enter the corpus; there is no reason field. Deselect does not delete an already-downloaded inclusion. Pass 1 skips remain unrecorded. | New (v24.1, Q-07; Ashley, 2026-11-16). |
| **DD-155** | Researcher-id provisioning | In the lab, people use the system long-term, so `--researcher-id` is the user's own username or email. A study run by a PI and committee has a study ID (like a facilities project ID), passed as `--study-id`, and each participant gets a code ID under it (for example `P-017`); names and emails must not appear as `--researcher-id` in study mode (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-08). The identifier is still never logged (AT-117). | New (v24.1, Q-08). |
| **DD-156** | LRS failure diagnosis privacy | After the last LRS retry (DD-51), the host records the failure and the affected entry ids so a future AI Teacher's Assistant can tell the researcher. Until that assistant exists, the record waits. If the error is sent off for diagnosis (for example to a search or docs lookup of the error code), only the error code and message may leave the host. xAPI statements, `--researcher-id`, paper identities, and source URLs must not (AT-236). | New (v24.1, Q-09). |
| **DD-157** | Actor homepage built by the host | `--lrs-actor-homepage` is not a flag. The host builds the actor homepage from the mode: `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/scholar/lab` in lab mode, or `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/scholar/study/<study-id>` in study mode (Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-13). The prefix is fixed; the host may append a further folder path under that prefix, but this spec does not invent folder names. Passing the removed flag is a startup error (AT-237). | New (v24.1, Q-13). |
| **DD-158** | LRS endpoint and consent copy | The lab/production LRS base URL is `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/xapi/` (live, xAPI 1.0.3; Ashley, 2026-11-16, Q-02). The consent copy shown before tracing is the draft in §5.2.3. That draft is pending Ashley's review and IRB approval and must not be shown to study participants until then. Formal confirmation of DD-105 also remains outstanding, as left at the end of the Q review. | New (v24.1, Q-02). |
| **DD-159** | Seal-stripped remnant repair | A recognised hyper-tile seal-trailer remnant is tagged. A later agent may attempt repair only by restoring from a verified copy (for example an S3A backup). The agent must not re-seal the remnant itself, so a repair cannot hide tampering. If no verified copy is available or restore fails, the remnant is flagged unrepairable. The durable "has been sealed" header flag remains deferred engine work (Q-18, AT-238). | New (v24.1, Q-18). |


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 9. Open questions

Q-01 through Q-20 are answered or (for Q-15 and Q-16) recorded as future work out of scope. Numeric phone budgets in Q-06 and Q-17 will be filled in from measurements; this spec invents no numbers.

*   **Q-01.** Lab host authentication: token file (DD-11, DD-29) or an existing lab SSO? **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley):** keep the token file.

*   **Q-02.** Exact LRS base URL on the s3a-lrs VM, and the consent-copy shown before tracing. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-65, DD-158):** the live LRS endpoint is `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/xapi/` (xAPI 1.0.3). The consent copy is drafted in §5.2.3 and is pending Ashley's review and IRB approval; it must not be shown to participants until then.

*   **Q-03.** Whether a later phase should enable phone Scholar writes or phone network for LRS. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley):** the phone is not a literature-review surface. Scholar 3-pass stays off the phone (DD-45, DD-128). Q-15 and Q-16 are future questions about unbuilt work; they are out of scope for this spec.

*   **Q-04.** Preferred CommonMark library if not `pulldown-cmark` (DD-13). **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-13, AT-233):** keep `pulldown-cmark`, with tables enabled. Academic papers need tables and images. A relative Markdown image may be loaded only under the DD-118 exception.

*   **Q-05.** Whether the optional CJK and emoji font packs should ship in the first Play/App build or only as additional bundled / Feature Delivery packs. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-35):** they do not ship in the first build. Missing glyphs show `.notdef`. The packs may be added later if someone asks.

*   **Q-06.** Named minSdk-26 measurement device, and numeric budgets for peak RSS, time to first manifest frame, and APK size delta versus the current reader. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley):** measure first on Ashley's Pixel 9; budgets are set from those measurements. No numbers are invented here. The Pixel 9 is a fast phone, so the figures are a baseline and will look lighter than on older Android 8 (minSdk-26) devices.

*   **Q-07.** Which existing verb IRI in `s3a-pack-scholar` `xapi.rs` is used for select and which (if different) for download. This spec will not invent a new IRI. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-154, AT-83, AT-234, AT-235):** select and download are separate verbs. Select is not the inclusion decision. Deselect is logged as its own event; a deselected paper is not snapshotted or fingerprinted and does not enter the corpus; there is no reason field. Pass 1 skips are still not recorded. The existing IRIs are select `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/opened`, download `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/saved`, deselect `https://ashecribb.dev/xapi/verbs/removed`.

*   **Q-08.** How `--researcher-id` is provisioned in the lab (manual flag, existing Scholar session, or something Ashley names). **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-155, AT-237):** in the lab, the researcher ID is the user's own username or email. In a study, a study ID (like a facilities project ID) is used with participant code IDs (for example `P-017`); names and emails do not appear in the research data.

*   **Q-09.** LRS retry backoff schedule and a maximum attempt count. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-51, DD-156, AT-236):** 3 tries, 10 seconds apart, in the background, never stalling the app. After the last failure, record the failure and the affected entries for a future AI Teacher's Assistant. Only the error code and message may be sent off for diagnosis, never xAPI statements or researcher data.

*   **Q-10.** Exact RGB (or palette) values for the three severity badge colors in DD-07. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-07, AT-19):** Okabe-Ito 0 `#0072B2`, 1 `#E69F00`, 2 (and 3) `#D55E00`, with labels always present.

*   **Q-11.** Whether the `s3a-kite` / "Kite" name should change to reduce trademark overlap with Cloudflare Kitesurf, and if so to what. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-54):** rename to Navigator (`s3a-navigator`, "S3A Navigator"). The GitHub fork is [ashleycribb/s3a-Navigator](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-Navigator). The actual in-tree rename is a later dedicated build step, not this revision.

*   **Q-12.** Whether the optional dossier YAML key for a remote URL should be `source_url` (DD-59 draft) or another name Ashley already uses. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-59):** the field name is `source_url`.

*   **Q-13.** Whether `--lrs-actor-homepage` should later be a fixed Scholar IRI rather than a required flag (DD-65). **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-157, AT-237):** the host builds the address itself, not a flag. Lab: `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/scholar/lab`. Study: `https://34.74.163.88.sslip.io/scholar/study/<study-id>`. The path is otherwise the same; the host may append folder structure under that prefix.

*   **Q-14.** Timed `cargo-fuzz` duration for CI (seconds or a CI runner budget). **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; AT-71):** a timed `cargo-fuzz` job of 300 seconds per push, plus the required seed-corpus replay.

*   **Q-15.** (Future, out of scope for this spec.) Which AI Teacher's Assistant backend or model a later phone feature would use, and whether it would run locally on the device or via the hub. The agent has not been built. This spec invents no backend, model, or topology, and adds no ATs, exports, or interfaces for it (DD-128).

*   **Q-16.** (Future, out of scope for this spec.) Whether a later phone paper search would be read-only, and how results would hand off to the desktop or hub for Pass 2/3. There is no phone Scholar Explorer. This spec invents no search or hand-off design, and adds no ATs, exports, or interfaces for it (DD-128).

*   **Q-17.** What `max_frame_pixels` and `max_open_bytes` the phone app passes to `open` / `open_verified`. The host takes these from required flags; the phone has no flags. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley):** measure first on Ashley's Pixel 9, the same way as Q-06. This spec invents no numeric defaults; the values are supplied by the app at the call once those measurements are set (DD-45, DD-109).

*   **Q-18.** Whether a later durable hyper-tile "has been sealed" header flag should close the complete-trailer-strip residual recorded in DD-141. **Answered 2026-11-16 (Ashley; DD-159, AT-238):** damaged remnants are tagged. A later agent attempts repair only by restoring from a verified copy (never by re-sealing). If it cannot, the remnant is flagged unrepairable. The header flag remains deferred engine work. A recognised remnant already restricts (`seal_failed`, unauthenticated as a whole).

*   **Q-19.** (Answered 2026-11-16, Ashley; DD-152.) If a recorded snapshot file is missing, a later download of that `(file_uuid, identity)` re-fetches the paper. It is not permanent `snapshot_invalid`. If the re-fetched bytes hash differently from the recorded sha, the host publishes the new snapshot and appends a new index line with the new sha (no second tile-10). See DD-152 and AT-228.

*   **Q-20.** (Answered 2026-11-16, Ashley; DD-153.) Concurrent connections on a non-loopback bind are capped at a fixed 8. Connection 9 is accepted at the TCP layer and then closed immediately, without a TLS handshake and without an HTTP response, until a slot frees. It is not a flag. See DD-153 and AT-231.

Lawyer review is not required for the fork itself (Apache-2.0 + MPL file-level terms are listed in §6), but distribution of the app's font files must keep OFL notices (AT-02, AT-70). Q-11 is answered as a product rename to S3A Navigator, recorded here and carried out in a later build step, not as a license-term change.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 10. Risks

1.  **`native-host` is a thin feature flag.** Much of Kitesurf's DOM and JS is `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`. The tour and JS-disabled HTML layout path should use paint/layout entry points that already have native-host or non-Wasm coverage (`scene_replay`, `VelloCpuImageRenderer`). Additional native entry points may still be needed in the fork.
2.  **Wasm-oriented dependencies.** Workspace `getrandom` uses `js` / `wasm_js`. `fonts.rs` uses `wasm_bindgen`. Android and iOS builds fail until the pack forks those bits (AT-44), even though Wasmtime and Boa themselves are out of v1.
3.  **Today's `s3a-cli tour` is not a drop-in Edge tour.** It steps 128 KiB tiles and has a type-82 consent *branch*. Kite must use `MmapReader` for all file types, compute `withheld_count` itself, and must not build the warehouse walk on a full `tour_file` output (§11 step 3).
4.  **Type-number reuse** (64, 71, 75) between hyper-tile and Edge layouts. Branching on `is_edge_archive` is mandatory.
5.  **Phone memory and binary size.** Full Stylo + Blitz + UA fonts is heavier than the current Kotlin map. The DD-06 trigger cannot fire until Q-06 is answered.
6.  **No PDF input renderer** in the Kitesurf tree (Krilla is output). Pass 1 can look incomplete for PDF-only papers (DD-14).
7.  **Kitesurf is experimental** (README; last grounded commit 2026-11-16, `235111c`). Upstream will move. The pin is explicit (DD-17).
8.  **Host auth.** There is no CDP port in v1 (DD-41). AT-06, AT-50, AT-51, AT-87, and AT-115 remain gates. Token-file custody is the human-control point, including for the native window (DD-32, DD-60).
9.  **License completeness.** Forgetting NOTICE, MPL source, the third-party inventory, or OFL/IJG texts is a release blocker (AT-02, AT-70). The `s3a-kite` name overlaps "Kitesurf"; the recorded rename to S3A Navigator is a later build step (DD-54, Q-11).
10. **Working-archive durability and the inclusion index.** Unsealing or locking incorrectly could damage records. If `--data-dir/inclusion-index.jsonl` is lost, a later download may write a duplicate tile-10 record (DD-61 chooses that over silently dropping a real inclusion). AT-46, AT-47, AT-73, and AT-89 are gates.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 11. Implementation notes (after tests exist and fail)

Order of work. Ashley authorized a local test-first build on 2026-11-16, and later the same day authorized pushing a green build step to a **build branch** on [ashleycribb/s3a-Navigator](https://github.com/ashleycribb/s3a-Navigator) (the renamed fork; the former `s3a-Kite` URL redirects) — never to `main`. Do not push to s3a-storage or s3a-reader until she says so. A red step is not pushed. The ordered step list is in `BUILD-PLAN.md`. "Land" means local branches, plus a push to that build branch only when the step is green. Step 5 depends on an fd-taking `s3a-engine` API (F-13): `TileWriter` create/append and `seal_archive` on the same file description that holds `LOCK_EX`; `/proc/self/fd` and path reopen are forbidden for snapshot and download temps (AT-239). Engine work stays on a local branch until a push is authorized.

1.  Land the fail-first acceptance tests as specified in §5.1 / DD-121: `kite-acceptance` (workspace member, no pack dependency) so AT-22 and AT-37 stay green and host-spawn tests fail at runtime if the binary is missing; `kite-pack-tests` (out of the workspace, own lockfile) so pack-linking tests fail by a documented compile failure of that crate until the pack exists, without breaking workspace cargo commands; the s3a-reader tests (AT-38, AT-39, AT-43, AT-63, AT-165) in a separate crate outside the reader workspace (not feature-gated); and `crates/s3a-kite` (out of the s3a-storage workspace, no pack dependency until the pack exists) so AT-183 can land in this crate's own tests. Names in §5.1 are already fixed, so this step does not wait on "names as implemented."
2.  Vendor the Kitesurf Rust core from `ashleycribb/s3a-Kite` at `235111c`, add LICENSE/NOTICE, enable `native-host`, fix getrandom/wasm_bindgen for native and Android targets, and keep Apache change notices (AT-03).
3.  Implement hyper-tile **and Edge** manifest / header + warehouse paint (text layer, §5.1 / §5.8 animation, `hit_test`, `render_page`, pinned-key verification) using **`MmapReader` for every file type**. Reuse only safe pieces of tour data — stored-manifest bytes and the floor-list fields `index`, `type_id`, `record_count`, `size_bytes`, `payload_hash_short`. **Do not** feed `tour_file` output into withheld_count or into the unsealed walker (that path includes the type-82 branch and a stride-blind 128 KiB scanner). Compute `withheld_count` in kite with the §5.3 item 4 algorithm. Do not treat an embedded device public key as a trust anchor (DD-79).
4.  Host binary (`s3a-kite host`) with the §5.2.2 inventory (including `--max-frame-pixels`, `--max-redirects`, `--handle-idle-secs`, `--max-request-bytes`, `--max-open-bytes`, and `--lrs-timeout-secs` when LRS is on), Host HTTP API (including `view=page`, `view=banner`, `/v1/close`, and floor-transition scroll), token, TLS 1.2, health, path rules, secrets mode 0600, consent-file bootstrap, process-lifetime data-dir, working-archive, snapshot-dir, and download-dir locks, and optional `--headless`.
5.  Write path, Scholar 3-pass gates, source-url fetch (2xx, decoded bodies), BLOB/PROVENANCE snapshots (including payloads larger than 128 KiB; reuse on identical bytes), inclusion index (`file_uuid` + `snapshot_sha256`, append-only, step 0), disk-only write mutex, in-flight fetch registry, consent/LRS client (revoke-pending, `consent_write_failed`, pre-send consent check, LRS timeout, abort on revoke including during an LRS POST), and host-fetch egress. HTTP snapshots stay in this step; there is no Wasmtime step in v1.
6.  UniFFI + Android Compose viewer surface in `s3a-reader` (tests already landed in the out-of-workspace reader test crate, in step 1), including `KiteContext::new`, `open`/`open_verified` with `pinned_keys` or a report, `verification_report`, `close`, `proceed_to_warehouse`, and `KiteVerificationReport`; `open_path` and `paper_identity` are not exported on the phone; then the iOS `staticlib` link gate. No phone Scholar Explorer or 3-pass flow in this step (DD-45, DD-128). AI Teacher's Assistant chat and phone paper search are out of scope for this spec (Q-15, Q-16): this step adds no interfaces for either.
7.  Land the `open_and_render` fuzz target with a checked-in seed corpus and a corpus-replay CI job (AT-71). Package LICENSE/NOTICE and font OFL notices with the host and mobile artifacts (AT-02, AT-70).

Do not "temporarily" call the old WebAssembly viewer, invent a manifest, walk files with `HYPER_TILE_SIZE`, treat type 82 as Edge consent, gate local inclusion on tracing consent, invent a new `TileType` or verb IRI, stand up a CDP server, or add Wasmtime "just in case."

Immediate LRS inhibit is a lock-free `lrs_inhibit` latch checked before every send and used to cancel in-flight POSTs (`skipped`), separate from the mutex-held flag (DD-144, AT-198). When grants are untrusted or a file is unauthenticated as a whole, subjects without a trusted grant are withheld (AT-200). Edge signer attribution is defined (DD-145, AT-201). *   2026-11-16: v13. Addresses goldfish round 12 (F-01–F-20; 0 blockers, 5 majors, 15 minors). One normative per-subject consent table (DD-147) is defined once in §5.3 item 4 and referenced everywhere: show a subject only when the latest trusted record is an unexpired grant and no later parseable denial, withdrawal, or expiry exists; unrestricted only with trusted authentication and zero consent records of any kind; default-withhold whenever consent records exist in an untrusted or tampered state. The lock-free latch is a monotonic revoke-epoch pair cleared by compare-and-swap; senders register, re-check, then send (DD-144, AT-198 black-box). Pack `verification_report(handle)` returns the pack-computed `KiteVerificationReport` (or the supplied report after `open_verified`). `invalid_tile_indices` uses `0 .. tiles_total-1` everywhere. Acceptance tests: AT-01–AT-209. Draft decisions: DD-01–DD-147. Open questions: Q-01–Q-17 (unchanged).

IPv6 allowlist: ports require brackets; a bare IPv6 that parses is a portless address. `snapshot_conflict` is for a newly fetched capture; a recorded sha that fails reuse is 200 `snapshot_invalid`. Wiki wins iff a qualifying item-1 wiki candidate exists; the host passes `ScholarRoots.shadowed_by_wiki` and the pack stays fd-only (AT-207, AT-101). The shared safe-open rule covers candidate item-1 and item-2 reads (AT-218). Working-archive writes operate on the already-validated fd, with a mid-request swap AT-219. Chain-inactive Edge files with type-70 records recompute `seal_hash`; a mismatch is `chain_break` / unsealed, and grants after the break are untrusted (AT-220). A revoke runs to completion, decrement included, even if the client disconnects (AT-198). This v18 is not frozen (DD-149). Acceptance tests: AT-01–AT-220. Draft decisions: DD-01–DD-150. A tile is trusted only if it is a valid floor covered by a verified seal record (an authentic record at *s* covers `0 ..= s`); `break_index` for `chain_break` and `signature_known_signer` is (the highest preceding authentic `tile_index`) + 1, or 0 if none; Edge writers MAY sign every N tiles (no default N) (F-02, AT-220). After candidate membership, select/download runs steps 0–3 to completion even if the client disconnects: the fetch-registry entry is always removed, waiters are woken with the starter's defined outcome, step 1 completes, and step 3 still runs (F-03, AT-221). Minors: later-than-latest-trusted-grant wording (F-04), `chain_break` in the total outcome list (F-05), `withheld_count: N` paint (F-06), `payload_hash_short` definition (F-07), warehouse-floor start rectangle (F-08), AT extensions for mid-copy `source_changed`, max-redirects, revoke-pending directory, and deflate (F-09), Link strip-then-decode (F-10), normalize-then-unique-prefix roots (F-11), startup cleanup and config/state in the shared safe-open rule (F-12), host B distinct bind/consent (F-13), no hang on FIFO or symlink (F-14), AT-183 in host-crate tests (F-15), glossary and pointer consistency (F-16), `/v1/frame` field types (F-17), §5.2.6 codes on FFI `proceed_to_warehouse` and `render_*` (F-18), YAML aliases rejected (F-19), hyper-tile torn tail in AT-165 (F-20), `ADDON_EDU` on every step-0/1 hold (F-21), and AT-171/AT-24/AT-125 runner notes (F-22).
